Prologue: The Attack
     A baby’s scream pierced the warm summer day.
     The old man was impatient as he walked up the pathway leading towards the royal manor.  At long last after eight and a half months the baby was finally born on that warm summery day.  As he arrived at the elaborate entrance the guards instantly stepped aside to allow him entrance.  He was the royal mage and he found that life with magic is interesting enough without having any hindrance, and also, even the smallest hindrance could be fatal at any time in case of an emergency, so he himself trained the guards stationed at the gate to examine the minds of anyone that requested entrance.  It was tiresome, and a well-trained assassin could either block his mind or find another way inside, but it served well as a precaution and had prevented many incidents from occurring.
     As he proceeded down the hallway that led to the birthing chambers he reflected on the daily responsibilities of the title of royal mage.  At any one time there is only allowed to be one fully trained mage in the royal house, and that position was always upon the eldest son.  At birth the boy was to be examined by the mage for the traces of magic in the child.  Everyone has “magic” though without training it will stay passive, but even with training it is limited.  “Magic” can be expressed as unnatural intelligence, or in strength, or reflexes.  But in the males of this bloodline it is always great.  The eldest female, having not the ability to become high mage, was almost always chosen to become queen and to rule the lands.
    The old man embraced his position and admired the manor fervently.  He admired the lamps hanging on either side of every doorway, and the intimate patterns on them all, he admired the grounds, and the life that sprung forth of every nook and cranny of the capital city. But when he walked into the birthing chambers and saw the queen nursing her son, and her refusal to submit him to nursemaids.  He admired her even more. After a moment his sister silently gazed at him and spoke.
     “He’s beautiful isn’t he?” she said, and as she said it the old man’s eyes automatically were locked on the child.
     “May I see him,” he said, pleased that it was to be his student, and nephew.
     “Of course, but only for a minute, he cries when he’s away from me.” 
     He took the child wordlessly and examined him.  When his eyes met those of the newborn his mind seemed to explode with the magic contained in this one child. The boy had dark hair and a clear complexion, his eyes spoke of a potential for power unique, and potent.  He quickly glanced at his sister, the queen, and wondered how she could have carried that kind of magical potency for almost nine months.  This was yet another example of the strength of royal blood. The old man turned his gaze back to the child and began to delve cautiously into the boy’s essence to determine the source of the power. 
   Before he got anywhere he heard shouts that quickly progressed into blood curling screams of terror.  He leapt to his feet, and set the child on his mother with grace that one would not have expected from him.  The old man sprinted down the hall followed by soldiers that he outran, and when he arrived at the doorway he was horrified.  
    Bodies littered the streets outside the gateway where twenty-five of his best soldiers stood confronting an immense mass of enemy men.  He led the men following him to the lines and strengthened them to a good hundred strong.  As he was gathering his men together the enemy lines surged onto the royal guard, and he realized that his hopes of gathering men from the town were lost in a mass of blood and confusion.  As the enemy line crashed into the guards he threw himself into the fray wielding a mighty brand of white-hot energy and brought it down onto a soldier’s head, cleaving it in two.  Then he was assaulted by three soldiers fiercely intent on killing the old man, and were all parried by a wall of air that immediately after contact exploded into shards of razor sharp glass, killing all three men and a dozen other enemy soldiers that happened to be caught within range.  
   As the day progressed he lost fifty men amid the confusion and realized that, even though they had killed over three hundred of the enemy, they stood no chance against the overwhelming odds and were slowly being flanked by the enemy.
   “Get back to the hall!” The old man yelled, and was rewarded with looks of confusion.
   “Get the hell back to the hall, you heard me!” He yelled again, and was followed by his men this time as they retreated and regrouped inside the manor gates.
   “How many fit men do we have captain?” he asked his commander of the guards.
   “Not enough.  Damn, not nearly enough, maybe around fifty men.” He replied.
   “Would you advise me on any tactics that might get us out of this hole alive?”  The old man seemed so calm in the face of such certain danger, it was comforting to most of the men.
   “Not any that you don’t already know m’lord.” His voice quivered as he tried to match the mage’s cool disposition.
   “I suppose you’re right, but I didn’t want to hear that.  Send somebody to get the queen and her son out of here as quickly as possible.” He said, his face resolute.
    Suddenly a huge mass of bodies broke through the gate that had been barred and hurled themselves against the guards.  When the men in the front realized how badly they were outnumbered they broke rank and tried to run, but the men in the back had yet to realize what those in front had, and so they did not run, and all were caught in the slaughter.  The old man, seeing this, let lose all walls of his body that held his magic down and it took him.
   His vision turned white until he stopped seeing at all.  Energy coursed through his veins, filling them with a deadly light, and the blade of energy fluttered into existence below his right hand.  His other hand was bathed in a blue flame that continued up the whole arm.  He was a terrible sight to behold.
   He caught the first soldier in the throat with the blade of his sword, twisting it to sever his spine while releasing the head from the man’s shoulders.  Another swung his axe at his back, but he released a jet of flame towards him so hot that it vaporized him into a pile of ash.
   The mage began cutting a swathe through the center of the enemy mass, large flashes of blue light were illuminating the walls around them every second or two, and every time it did a sound like lighting would shoot through the hall.  
   He reached a point where he began to falter, he was growing very weak.  He was not the young man he used to be anymore.
   His next step was onto the stairs at the end of the hall, he missed it and stumbled down them.  His light went out and the opposing soldiers stabbed at him repeatedly until they were sure he was dead.
   With screams of glee the enemy soldiers leapt over the body of the dead mage and began the long awaited looting of the royal household.  Amid the frenzied looting, the enemy lord Vallech, who is a rider of the enemy air cavalry that ride venomous wyverns, stepped into the birthing chamber.  The queen stood in the center of the room, hands outstretched in an effort to shield her baby from him.  In two long strides he drew his lance and hurled it into the queen who collapsed instantly into an eternal sleep.  
   Vallech strode around the room seeking for small treasures when he heard the child on the floor screaming.  The lord’s first thought was to kill the child, but then a second thought entered his mind.  What kind of an abomination it would be to take his enemy’s child and train it as a warrior of his cause?  The thought pleased him, but the new emperor would not allow it, but then he didn’t have to know.  He picked up the child and walked away from the ransacked manor that would have been its home.














 Chapter 1: The Tourney 


          FIFTEEN YEARS LATER


      “Put some effort into it arseling!”  The general shouted at the top of his lungs, as the youth struggled against another soldier that was at least four years older than him.  The fact that he was being slaughtered in the wrestling match wasn’t being helped by being insulted, and if they were using weapons it wouldn’t matter anyway.  He was always talented with the lance, but any weapon would do.  He was unmatched at the sports of war, including ploy armies clashing against each other.  But in a duel, one-on-one he could not be touched by another recruit.  He was fifteen years old, almost into manhood, and he had long wavy brown hair that came down to his chin, he had always been slim, not stick-thin, just very fit.  He was about five-foot ten inches, and usually didn’t care about anything but the war.  His name is Eric son-of-none.
       The war, fifteen years earlier the ruling emperor had simultaneously attacked all six countries sharing the continent of Mercolas, under name of the country Trojia. Mercolas was roughly round, with a panhandle extending south of the mainland, and an island on the western shore.  The six countries from largest to smallest are.
  Vervadia, where the horselords and the Vervians ruled in the flat plains of northeastern Mercolas.  Trojia, who betrayed all nations and now rules with an iron fist.  Mercia where the hills hide the last remnants of rebellion.  Frandia, where the war hungry Frelians lived in their huts. Rema where political freedom and justice reigned supreme.   The island Fiora was where the pegii riders flew freely in the past. But all fell to the fierce might of an unprovoked attack by Gunthred the king of Trojia, and are now lost amid the rubble.  
       “All right, all right, get your arses off the ground.” The general sighed at the two boys.
      “Joshua, great job you may return to your barracks for whatever’s left of the day.” He said as the youth left silently.  Then the general turned to the remaining boy.
   “Okay arseling; allow me to let you in on a secret, if it were me I’d leave you here for at least two more years of training.  But unluckily for you I’m outranked by our most gracious lord Vallech, and he seems to think that you deserve a chance to see battle. So you’re to join the ranks of the fifth infantry in four days’ time, and you are going to march with them on a final conquest against our valiant Vervadian rebels.” The general stated.  At the sound of those words Eric’s heart leapt for joy, for the only thing he had wanted for as long as he had known was to march against the remaining rebels in Mercia in the name of the emperor.  
     The sky was dimming as Eric raced to his tent, and hastily packed his few belongings into a sack that the general had given him for that purpose.  Then he left his tent for the next recruit to lodge in for the three-year period that the boy would be trained, and started off towards the barracks.  The encampment that he had been trained, and had grown up in was a very plain, unfortified stretch of grassland on the northern border of Trojia.  The buildings were wooden, and not built to last for any more than maybe five years.  Eric made his way towards the barrack official and was given his station number, and since he knew how to read he didn’t have to have a guide to take him there.  When he arrived at his room he spent a brief amount of time unpacking his things, then he examined his room.
      The room was very plain, with barren walls that bore no decorations, and one bunk that would hold two people.  The walls had been white-washed six-months before and because of usual wear and decay almost all of it had been stripped off, leaving bare planks showing.  The floor was also whitewashed and was covered in muddy boot marks from the dozens of men that had housed there before.
      Eric left the room and proceeded down the hall back to the main lobby.  When he arrived there his eyes scanned the room briefly and something caught his eye.  It was a stack of flyers that were lying on the official’s desk.  He walked closer for a better look, and saw that they were for a tournament.  He was immediately interested and set off to the arena to sign up.
      Eric walked through the camp towards the arena hastily to try and make sure he arrived before all the entrants’ spots were full.  He walked through the tent grounds to make better time.  He skirted through the tents that were mostly empty in his attempt to get to the arena as fast as possible.  
      After he was out of the tent grounds it was a straight shot to the arena.  It was about one-hundred yards away, and he rushed towards it. The arena was just open ground that was a dirt pit in the middle surrounded by rows of benches.  There were about two-hundred benches around the pit, and they seated about six-hundred men.
      At a small opening in the benches there was a man sitting on a chair that had a list of entrants for the tournament.  He was short, skinny, and nearly bald and he seemed to have the look of a grumpy old man.
    Eric walked up to the organizer, and cleared his throat.  
    “What do you want Eric?” The organizer said.
    “Good evening to you too.  I’d like to be placed in the tourney.”
    “I’m sure you would.  Give me your name."
    “Eric Son-of-None” He hazily replied.
    “Please spell it.”  This was an extremely unfair action that the old man would use to keep men out of the tourney, and it usually worked if they couldn’t read.
    “E-R-I-C” He replied, with a sarcastic tone added to reinforce his words.
    “Okay then, now all I need is your spear head to ensure that no one gets hurt, and you’re set to go.” 
    “Why, in the world would I give it to you, everyone here knows you’re a cheap bastard?”
    “Because it’s the rules now give it!” 
    “Okay, but if it’s not taken care of, or if it goes missing it’s your head.”
    With that closing statement Eric reluctantly handed over his lance and began to walk towards the entrance to await the calling of his name.
    After about an hour of waiting for the first of three rounds in the tourney, an attendant entered the room with his wooden weapon and motioned to him.  When he had walked over he was handed his weapon.
   “You’d better get going.” The attendant told him.
   He answered with a nod and entered the ring.  When he entered he was welcomed with cheers and happily nodded to the crowd.  After the announcements were made Eric turned to his opponent, and with a crossing of weapons the first round began.
   His enemy rushed at him swinging his sword in a sharp arc downward at his face, the wooden blade weighed about 12 pounds and could easily bruise muscle, or even break a bone.  As the sword came down he just stared. At the last second, just when the crowd was sure he had lost, he twisted his body to the side.  At that instance the battle-calm overtook him, and the world slowed.  He instantly stabbed out with the wooden tip.  The stab was parried, but clumsily and then the battle was on.  The enemy slashed to the left and Eric dodged, then he countered with a kick in the enemy’s knee.  The man fell to one side, but defended Eric’s finishing stab, and attacked by sweeping his leg along the ground to trip him, Eric was expecting this and jumped in the air over his opponent, and landed with his spear at the other man’s throat.
    “I submit” The older man panted.
    Eric nodded and left for the waiting benches.  The attendant took his spear and he began the wait.  Almost immediately he was summoned, and he crossed spears with the second enemy.
   This time was too easy, the youth stabbed at him with his spear and Eric twisted to the side and smacked him in the head with the butt of his lance and his opponent was out cold.
   He returned to the waiting benches which were usually packed to find one person left.
   That person was his idol, the man he had looked up to his entire life, only to find that he was his opponent.  The man was Lord Vallech.
   The attendant entered the room and both combatants rose to their feet, and with trembling hands Eric took his lance from the attendant.
   As they walked to the ring bile rose into Eric’s throat as the announcer shouted their names the crowd went wild as they crossed spears.
     Vallech glanced into his eyes and then attacked ferociously, and Eric’s arm automatically parried the blow that jarred his senses.  Vallech attacked again with overwhelming intensity, and blow after blow fell, but blow after blow the youth always found a way to parry until both combatants were exhausted to the point of nearly fainting. As the battle waged on, a wave of hopelessness washed over him, threatening to drown him in his overwhelming desperation.  When Eric felt as if his last hope of beating the veteran was gone, a sensation began to build in his gut.  As it slowly built he was able to identify it as energy.  As the wave of the pure adrenaline hit him the world slowed, it seemed as if Vallech had the reflexes of a two-year old.  Now Eric was the one attacking, and slowly the battle began to shift in the boy’s favor until Vallech desperately thrust his lance at Eric’s chest.  But the energy took hold of him and he flipped over Vallech while corkscrewing through the air, ending with his spear on the man’s throat.
     Both men stared at the other panting as the older said.  “I submit.”
     The crowd was silent as Eric collected his prize money, and then began to walk back to his barrack.  On the way he marveled on the fact that he had beaten the lord of the sky knights.  The very man who singlehandedly commanded the troops that took Draland, which is the capital of Frandia, and then immediately joined the onyx knight J’harra in his conquest against the horse-lords of Vervadia.  With which he and his troop of sky knights excelled against the horse riders.  The Vervians discovered that horses were inferior to the wyverns that could attack, and then be gone without a single chance at a counter strike.
    Eric had spent many nights up dreaming of the possibility of one day joining Lord Vallech and his sky knights in the city-island of Fiorra, which once was the home of the pegii riders who would be the only ones match for the sky knights of Trojia.  But the Riders of the great and ancient pegii are no more.  They perished along with their mounts when the Lord Vallech, along with his sky knights, utterly and totally destroyed them at the legendary sky battle that took place against the wyverns and the pegii.  Eric was actually glad that the pegii had perished against the wyverns, because he had no wish to ride the pegii, they must have been terribly cumbersome compared to the light aerodynamic sleekness of the wyvern.
    As he walked he realized that he had left his spearhead, which had been replaced with a wooden one, was missing.  He must have forgotten it after the finals of the tournament.  No wonder, he had been so shocked that he had also forgotten his casual clothes which he had exchanged for the official clothing of the tournament.  He quickly turned around and headed back towards the arena   
    When he arrived he noticed that his clothes and spearhead were gone.  After looking around the waiting area he decided to head to the official that he had told to keep watch over his spear.  Hopefully they would be there.  When he arrived outside, he immediately noticed that the groups of houses surrounding the west side of the arena were on fire, and there were about a hundred men around it.  They must be firemen he thought, and immediately started towards them to help them maintain the blaze.  When he was closer however he noticed that the men carried weapons, and decided to question the flag-bearer if it was an attack.  When he was about five steps away from the flag-bearer, all the men around him suddenly drew their weapons and started towards him with shouts.  
   When he heard them he made a break for the way he had come, however two men were blocking his way.  Without breaking his sprint he flung himself towards one of them, and with a scream of defiance he struck out at the man’s throat, breaking his windpipe with his knuckles.  After this the second man had drawn his sword and started toward the youth with a grin.  The boy turned and swept his leg across the ground, the way his unarmed specialist had taught him, and leapt out of the way when the man fell.  He then wrestled the man’s sword from his grasp and, with a shout, leapt up and swung the sword in a quick slice at another man that had tried to attack his back.  The man fell to his knees, making a loud gurgling sound, as Eric sped away screaming at the top of his lungs that the camp was being attacked.
  “Attack, attack” he cried.  “They’re attacking the western encampment!” 
  The camp was soon full of the sounds of shouts and ruckus as every man prepared himself for battle.  Before the general started shouting orders, Eric sprinted to the armory to arm himself for the battle.
  He chose a medium length lance that was specialized for ground work, along with a small dagger with many nicks along its edges. For his armor he slipped on a leather breastplate made for quick, light movements.  Even though the leather didn’t provide as much protection as the traditional chain-mail, he preferred it because it was more suited to his style of weaponry.
   After preparing, he set out to receive orders from the general, who could be heard bellowing orders halfway across camp.  When he arrived the general immediately sent him with the force of one-hundred-and-twenty men that were taking back the ruined housing that they had destroyed earlier.  When he caught up with the men he was ordered to the right side in the second line, where there were a couple younger recruits that he was worried about.  He left for his rank silently and when he arrived he began to march alongside the other recruits. 
     After about a minute the enemy came into view, and when they saw the recruits, they immediately drew up battle lines, and advanced towards them.  
    Both lines jeered and taunted each other for a few moments.  One man even stripped off his pants and ran around screaming about how he had shit bigger turds than those recruits.  When he bent over to pull up his pants Eric took a javelin a hurled it right up the man’s arse and into his guts.  The man screamed and fell to the ground convulsing in pain as the barbed spear tore further into his gut with every movement.  
    After that the Mercians charged.  When the two forces were about fifty feet from each other the back lines hurled spears at the other, and then met with a ferocious force.  The enemy soldiers began to hack away at the inexperienced recruits, who were showing great courage against the enemy veterans.
     Eric saw the lines meet, and almost immediately the boy in front of him fell to an axe that struck him in the head.  While the enemy soldier was trying to remove his axe from the poor youth’s head, Eric’s arm quickly snapped forward and stabbed the man in the temple.  He left his dagger there and followed up with a thrust of his spear into another man right behind the soldier he had just killed, he twisted the lance to keep it from being stuck in the soldier’s gut.  After wrenching his spear from the belly of the second man he was assaulted by a lancer from the enemy lines where he responded to the thrust by stabbing at the others shoulder, the man quickly jerked up his spear to block his strike but Eric had anticipated the move. He brought the back of his spear into the man’s groin and when he bowled over shortly took him in the heart with the tip of his lance.  After ripping the spear out of the man’s chest the unfamiliar energy once again took control of his body.  
    His eyes unfocused, then just as quickly refocused with the sight of a hawk.  Every muscle in his body seemed inferior to the strength, and reflexes of the pure adrenaline that now flowed through them.  He was able to see the weakness in the leg of a new man that had been injured in a similar raid, as he came to make a feeble attempt at taking his life.  Immediately, almost of its own accord his body twisted and while parrying the soldiers slash, also stabbed sideways into his throat, using his hip more so than his arm.  After that he noticed a slight hesitance in the man in front of him, and he took advantage of that by bashing him in the head with the blunt end of his spear.
   Even with his efforts the enemy was slowly advancing against the young recruits, and when he saw this he quickly stepped back from the lines and began to shout at the top of his voice.
   “Come on!  These are nothing but excuses of men driven by nothing but brutality.  Show them the might of Trojia; Show them the might of the empire!”
   After that the youths began fighting with a ferocious new valor, and beat back the enemy soldiers until they were forced to retreat.  When the soldiers escaped into the woods the boys began to give chase, but were then called back by the general.  
       When they arrived at the headquarters of the camp they received orders that half the recruits were to stay up as a watch, to insure that there would be no new attack.  Luckily Eric wasn’t one of the chosen to keep watch, so he returned to his barrack.  When he arrived he laid down his spear and dagger, and slumped into his bed fully clothed and slept a deep, dreamless sleep.
  
     




















                                                           








                                     
   Chapter 2: flying east
    
      When he awoke he set out to help with the repairs that would be essential, after the damages done by the enemy soldiers the night before.  When he arrived on the western side of the camp, which had been attacked last night, he saw that the repairs were nearly complete.  Confused Eric headed towards the general that was supervising the repairs being done.  
      Eric approached the general and spoke.  “Sir, how did the repairs get completed so fast?  I woke up believing that I was heading here to spend all day rebuilding from last night’s rubble.”
     When he heard this, the general burst out into laughter, and said sarcastically.  “Arseling, if half the recruits in this camp had the brains you have, we’d be living like the barbarians in Frandia!”  The general said as he continued to laugh.
     “But sir,” Eric said.  “I still don’t understand how all this work was done in one night, with half of us keeping watch, and the other half sleeping?” He asked.
     “Are you serious arseling?  Are you telling me that you recognize the men out there, rebuilding this hellhole of a place?”
     Immediately Eric began to examine the men, while looking for the source of this mystery.  The men were all dressed in the regular casual clothes of the camp, but strangely, he didn’t recognize any of them.  It was like they had popped up from the ground, which he found was close to correct.
    “Sir,” he asked.  “Where did these men come from?”
    “Are you blind?” He retorted. “Damn, arseling you remind me of when I went to my pigpen this morning, and saw all the shit in the bottom!  That’s what you remind me of arseling!”  Eric still looked confused.  So the general shouted. “They’re slaves, arseling, slaves.  Damn you’re stupid, we keep them in pens about a mile away from camp, what the hell do you think all that food we send there is for, the animals!”
    Suddenly Eric remembered.  Every other morning, about twenty of the camps servants were sent with several bags of food, off towards the western forests, now he realized that the food was going to slaves.  With horror he looked around, and began to examine the slaves.  Every one of them was tied together by ropes, and also shackled by the feet, with chains.
    Eric looked at the general, and quietly asked.  “Does Lord Vallech know about this, sir?”  The lord of the sky knights had a hate towards people lazy enough to force slaves to work.  He always said it was because they were too lazy to work themselves.
    “Hell no, of course not!” He shouted.  “What the lord doesn’t know, we’ll take advantage of,” He said.  “But you don’t need to work, because you can fight.  So, because of that, you’re being sent in the ground troops being led against the rebels in southern Vervadia.” He said.  “You’ll be traveling, and fighting under the command of the famous Lord Vallech.  You’ll be attacking the city of Shan, along with the sky knights.  If you have any more damn questions, give them to the wonderful Vallech,” he said. “Now, get going arseling!”
    Eric sped towards the commander’s quarters, and when he arrived he asked the attendant if he knew were the lord of the sky knights was.
    “Saw him leave this mornin’, said that if anyone was looking for him, then to send ‘em to the eastern camp boundary.”  
    “Thanks,” Eric said quickly, and set off towards the area.  When he arrived he saw about eighty men there, all of which was scrubbing the scales of armored wyverns.  This was a dream come true, and he stood awestruck until one of the knights piped up and said.
    “Hey boy, did you lose track.  This is Lord Vallech’s troop, and he doesn’t choose boys to ride with us.”  Eric just stared until Vallech took notice and looked up from his wyvern.  
    “Come over here, Eric.” Vallech said, and the boy slowly walked over.  “Come down here, let me show you something,” he said from a crouching position.  When Eric crouched down beside the lord, Vallech pointed at the scales of the wyvern. “See how the scales are long?  They’re that way because the air is caught between the scales, but only when the wyvern tucks its wings in.  See where the scales break off at the wings, that’s so that after the wyvern strikes it can tuck its wings in and be off immediately.”  
    Over the next hour the lord continued with explaining the anatomy of the wyvern until Eric asked. “Sir, this is very interesting, but I’m a foot soldier so I won’t be fighting on a wyvern.  Why aren’t you telling me how to defend the knights, instead of taking care of a mount?”  He regretfully asked
      “Because there are no ground troops in my army Eric, and because you are, as of now, in training to become one of my knights.”  He stared at the youth and then said. “Unless you’d rather be in the general’s pathetic troops, but you’re the only reason that I stopped here, so if you chose to come I have two things to tell you.”
      Eric quickly nodded in response to what Vallech was suggesting. “All right then,” Vallech said. “Come with me.” Vallech stood up and walked over to a wyvern that, instead of a knight, had a pack in the saddle.  When they arrived at the wyvern Vallech silently handed Eric the pack, and motioned towards the saddle, and said. “This is you’re training wyvern, you will ride it until that wyvern in the egg I gave you hatches, and is big enough to ride.” He glanced at the boy “Any questions?”
      “Yes sir, where is the egg that you mentioned?” Eric hesitantly asked.  Vallech began to laugh.
      “Have you checked your bag?”  When he said this Eric looked inside the pack, and gasped.  Inside the pack was an egg, just as Vallech had said.  It was a dark brown color, and about the size of a loaf of bread, but had no spots, or blemishes on it.  Eric thought it was the most beautiful thing in the world.
      “Thank you,” was all he could say.  
Vallech nodded and said.  “Get on your mount, we’re leaving shortly.” Eric had turned away but Vallech had not finished.  “And Eric, do not think anything of my letting you beat me.”  His eyes flashed for a moment, “It is always mindful that you should never forget your place.” He did not wait for a response before walking off towards his smoke-colored wyvern, that was strangely more snakelike than the others.  
   When Eric leapt onto his mount, slightly troubled, the wyvern quickly moved its tail to motion towards the straps at either side of the saddle.  Confused, Eric spurred the sides of the wyvern, but the beast’s tail wrapped around the straps, and lightly held it to the boy’s leg as it began to buck up, and down.  Eric realized, after he almost fell off that the straps must be for his legs, that they must be to prevent him from falling off.  Under consistent watch from the wyvern, he knotted them in three places on the shin, knee, and thigh, on both legs.  Satisfied the wyvern waddled into formation with the rest of the sky knights.  After inspecting all of the knights, Vallech walked over to Eric. “Don’t worry,” he said to the boy. “She’ll do all the work; just get comfortable in the sky.” Then he smiled at the boy, and leapt onto his wyvern.
     With a shout Vallech’s wyvern pushed against the ground, tearing the earth in four places, and vaulted into the sky.  Immediately all other knights flew off after him.  Eric’s mount was the last to leave, and she stayed behind the rest, so that Eric could examine the riders in front.  As soon as the wyvern took off, bile rose into his throat and he vomited into the air that was falling down below him.  They continued to rise until they reached just below the cloud level.  Eric vomited again when they leveled off, and afterword was left very, disoriented.  After a minute, or so passed, his stomach stopped expelling its content.  Whether it was because of it being empty, or him being used to the air, didn’t matter, because he was finally able to enjoy the environment around him.
     The clouds were just above him, and seemed like he could touch them.  The land was a blur below him, that was nothing but plains as far as the eye could see.  The knights flew for about eight hours, but after three, the plains of Trojia fell away, to the thick forests of Rema.  Eric had never before left the Trojian country, and hardly noticed when Vallech and the knights began to slowly decline before him.  His stomach lurched as his wyvern declined sharply to match the formation of the knights, but luckily his stomach was empty.
    When they reached the clearing, that Vallech had spotted, his wyvern drew itself up sharply, to prevent a heavy impact when they landed.  After untying the knots holding his legs to the saddle, he approached Vallech. “Sir, I was wondering, how far are we away from Shan?” He asked the wyvern lord.
    After staring at him for a second, he replied, “We’re about three hours away from the enemy city.” He said, and then turned around to tend to his wyvern.
    “But sir, we’ve only travelled for eight hours, how could we have travelled so far, in such a small amount of time?” 
    “You’ve never travelled by wyvern, have you boy?  We could have been there this evening, but we want to arrive at dawn tomorrow, before the watch has changed.”  He said.  “Just follow us, and take notes because you won’t be fighting tomorrow.”  The boy looked at him quizzically.  “You’re still in training boy.  You won’t fight until we join up with some ground troops and continue on to the city of Tora, on the eastern edge of Vervadia, close to the boarder of Mercia.”  Eric nodded silently, and after eating some venison that some of the knights had hunted, he rolled onto his mat, and fell asleep.






      He awoke early the next morning, by one of the disgruntled knights, and began to quickly roll up his mat, and stuff it into his bag.  The other knights were already mounted, and seemed irritated that the boy was taking so long.  Vallech however, seemed content with waiting for the lad, and was even smiling while humming a cheery tune.
     After he had loaded up his things, he jumped onto the training wyvern, and nodded towards Vallech.  The knights began to take formation, with Eric in the middle.  The formation was unlike any other that he had seen.  It was made up of a large central V-shape, with two smaller V’s coming off of it.  Vallech was the point of the central V, and Eric was about ninety feet behind him.
     The knights took off, and the training wyvern with them.  The flight was very dull compared to the flight the day before.  After a few minutes of flying the forest that was beneath them fell away to an infinite plain.  He saw several deer below him as they sped along.  
     As he was looking around he heard a man shout over the sound of the wind. “THERE IT IS, THERE’S SHAN!”  Eric quickly began to search for the city and found it about forty-five degrees, off to the right.  He wondered if Vallech had missed it in his early morning sleepiness, until the wyvern lord began to incline sharply.  All of the knights matched his formation, until they broke through the clouds.  As soon as they had cloud cover, Vallech took a sharp right turn and headed towards the city.  They kept formation as Vallech stopped, and hovered in mid-air.
    “ALRIGHT KNIGHTS, WE’RE GOING TO HIT ‘EM HARD, AND FAST!  AS SOON AS WE BREAK COVER I WANT THE LEFT AND RIGHT FLANKS TO SEPARATE, AND THEN HEAD TOWARDS THE WATCHES ON EITHER SIDE.  ERIC!  I WANT YOU TO STAY IN FORMATION UNTIL WE’RE ABOUT EIGHTY FEET OVER THE GROUND, THEN JOIN FORMATION AGAIN WHEN WE COME BACK UP FROM THE DIVE!”  
   Eric nodded along with every other knight, and they started flying towards the city.  When they were close to the city, the left and right flanks broke formation and dived out of the clouds.  After about a minute Vallech motioned, and dived through the clouds.  As he dived through the clouds he submerged into what felt like powder that got into his eyes and mouth, leaving him temporarily blinded.  After shaking his head, the knights dove towards the ground faster than he’d ever gone before.  When he was eighty feet above ground he broke formation, just like Vallech had said, and hovered in mid-air.  He watched as the knight tore into the men around the keep, and hurled spears at the startled soldiers.  The wyverns bit, and tore into the enemy, and as they inclined each picked up a soldier.  When he saw them rising Eric flew forward and joined formation just when the wyverns were dropping the men.  
    The knights circled the buildings, and continued to dive bomb the civilian houses.  However every time they dove, Eric broke formation and hovered eighty feet above the ground.  He examined as the knights broke through the lines of men that were beginning to form inside the center of the city, and by doing this kept them disorganized. 
    Without being able to form up, the enemy men rushed into their houses, and returned with hunting bows.  Each of them stood together in the center area and faced the middle formation with bows drawn.  
    Eric instinctively inclined another eighty feet, to keep out of range of the deadly arrows, and watched as the right flank dove from behind the enemy archers.  Instead of rising from the dive, the knights jumped off their mounts, and began to slaughter the poorly armed archers.  The knights hacked through the hundred-so archers that had once posed a threat.  The middle, and left flanks then dove towards the keep, shouting war cries.  The knights dismounted at the gate, and Vallech motioned to Eric.  Eric quickly dived, and leapt off his mount.  “Okay Eric!” The lord shouted over the cries of dying men.  “I want you over there!  On the right side, third rank fifth column,” Vallech shouted.
     “Yes sir,” Eric said, and took his designated position in the newly drawn up battle lines, in the third line of their shield-wall.  
     When he was in formation Vallech yelled out complicated instructions to the knights, and then they stormed the keep.  When they arrived at the gate, Vallech yelled “bring me the key!”  When a soldier handed a bright red object to the lord, Eric realized that the “key”, was actually a tool designed by Reman engineering, that would heat up to scorchingly high degrees, at the press of a button.  Vallech pressed a lump on the “key,” and the tip of the object turned white-hot.  Then he stabbed it into the gate.  Instantly that section of the gate caught on fire.  When the fire spread over the entire gate Vallech kicked it, and the gate fell inward onto a small group of soldiers.  The knights leapt over the flaming gate, and formed up just in time to meet the house guard.  The guard all wielded wicked looking battle-axes.  The axes curved up an extra foot from where they would usually end, and curved to a point that the soldiers could either slash, or stab with.  The forces met and the screams began.
     Eric watched as the first knight fell by unseen blow to his head.  The knight in front of Eric moved to the front rank, and Eric fell in behind him.  As the knight fought, Eric was stabbing over his shoulder, helping to protect him from the fierce blows falling all around them.  After about four minutes the knight in front of Eric took a fierce blow to his arm.  Eric quickly jerked the man back, and took his place at the front line.  When he stepped forward he was immediately forced to parry a blow by an enemy soldier to his left.  The knight at his left shoulder locked weapons with the man, and bore him to the ground.  Eric’s gaze was forced onto the soldier in front of him, as he parried, and countered the man’s blow and quickly stabbed him in his neck.  The man fell to the ground as Eric twisted his spear out of the man’s throat, and then stabbed the soldier that was leaping over the dying man.  When he died the man fell on him, the man was heavy and he could feel his breath fade and falter as the fatal wound did its work.
     Eric rolled the man off him as a knight took his place on the front line.  Then he leapt up from the ground, and picked up his spear.  As he went to take his place back on the line, the man shoved him back.  He was disgruntled until he noticed that the man was the same one that he had saved by stepping over him into the fray.  He looked at the back of the man as he fought through three enemy soldiers, and realized that, there was blood staining his armor, but he was using his arm as if it had never been damaged.  He glanced at the man’s shoulder again, but was forced to protect him from a slash that was coming from to his right.  
     The fighting continued for another hour, but from the start they had won.  The last man stood his ground, and was ripped to pieces by five men that were in the front lines.  As the men began to loot the household, Eric found Vallech, and continued the raid with him, taking a mental note of everything, as a lion cub watches its mother in its first hunt.
     After the looting was over with the knights made their way back to their mounts, and took off to an empty stretch of plains to camp for the night.  He had heard tales of how, during raids, the soldiers usually took the women also.  Vallech however had forbidden his men to do such, not because of his mercy, but because of infections and plagues that the people of this area often carried. When they landed Eric began to unpack his bags, and as he did so he noticed that some blood had gotten on his gloved hand.  He wiped it off, and picked up the egg that was in his bag, and placed it by the fire offhandedly.  After a minute he unfolded his sleeping mat, and laid down on it.  However, when he had lain down he noticed the man that he had fought with at the gate, had laid his mat right next to him.  Eric rolled over, and closed his eyes to fall into some badly needed sleep as the man spoke.  “Thanks for the help out there kid.” The soldier quietly said after he had also lain down onto his mat.
   “Sure, it was nothing,” Eric replied sleepily.
   “No, it wasn’t nothing,” the man said.  “You saved my life.”  Eric was silent as the man spoke again softly. “My name’s Gerik, and anytime you need anything, just ask.”  
   Eric was silent as he thought about the fight earlier that day, and then decided to ask.  “Excuse me for asking sir, but can I ask you a question?”
    “Of course,” the man replied.  “What is it?”
    “It’s just, that I was wondering how your arm was doing, you got hit pretty hard, and there was a lot of blood?” Eric asked nervously.
    “Oh that, that was just a scratch, but thanks for your concern,” he said, almost as if he was shrugging off the wound.  “But in thanks for watching my back, I’ll give you a bit of advice.  You know that egg by the fire, well if it stays real warm, close to you for a while, that might encourage it to hatch a bit.”
    Eric glanced at the egg, wordlessly took it from the edge of the fire, and tucked it into his arms.  He began to think of the hatchling, until his tiredness caught up with him, and he fell to sleep.
  




















     
Chapter 3: the rising of a knight


    Eric woke to the egg shaking in his grasp.  It was nearly dawn, and he felt like all the energy had been sucked out of him.  He stumbled to his feet, and looked at the egg.  
      The egg had turned a bright red color, and had begun to shake.  As he was examining the egg he noticed that the spot where it had touched his skin was a darker red than the shell around it.  Then he noticed that his chest had turned red also.  With a shudder he began to think of what could have caused this.  He examined his chest more carefully, and it seemed like his blood had gathered around the spot where the egg touched him.  His blood must have centered there for a reason, but before he could figure out why, he heard a cracking sound.  He quickly looked down, and saw that a crack had run along the entire edge of the egg.  Eric leapt to the ground to examine the shell.  As he was examining it, the top half of the shell flipped open, and the hatchling emerged from it.
      The creature was about the size of a small cat, and had wings that were dripping wet from the insides of the egg.  The most astonishing feature however, was that its entire body was ruby-red, and its eyes were the color of onyx, and seemed to stand out against the rising sun, in such a way that one could get lost in their seemingly eternal depth.  
      Eric reached down, and picked up the creature, which was surprisingly light, and cradled it in his arms.  It immediately crawled against the stretch of skin that had turned red from the egg.  He was unperturbed by the hatchling, until he felt his strength beginning to drain away.  He looked down at the wyvern, and noticed that his chest was faintly glowing, from the touch of the hatchling.  After a moment of measuring his strength, he decided that the amount of energy being transferred wasn’t to dreadful, so he tucked his head against the wyvern, and went to sleep. 










     
Chapter 4:  loyalties of a lord


     He awoke next morning to a shout from one of the knights.  “Hey guys come over here, quick!”  There was a rustling sound as the ten knights closest to him struggled to get off their mats.
     “Look, the boy’s wyvern hatched,” he shouted.  “It’s got to be the strangest thing I ever saw!”
     “What the hell are you shouting about,” said a skinny man after he had risen to his feet.  Then he looked at the hatchling in the boy’s arms, and gasped.  When the other men got there they were silent.  After a few moments one of the men piped up and said.  “What happened to it?” he said.  “The egg was brown the baby should be the same color?”  The other men followed with similar questions.
    “Do you want to know what I think” said the first man, vehemently.  “I think that the little bastard’s a runt!”  The men behind him shouted in agreement with the man.  Then a voice shouted, louder than the men that were already yelling. 
    “QUIET!” The man said, and as he approached the crowd Eric saw that it was Gerik.  He glanced at Eric and then asked the men.  “What has the boy done wrong, that you should speak of him so?  Has he stolen something?” He said as he glared at the silent crowd of men.  “Well c’mon; you were speaking so much earlier, what has the boy done wrong?”  
    The man that had spoken first looked at Gerik and said.  “We were shouting because the boy’s wyvern hatched.”  
   Gerik looked at the hatchling in the boy’s arms, and then looked back at the man and said.  “I see nothing wrong with the thing, if anything I see nothing but right.  Look at it, it’s amazingly healthy, it’s got a good body-to-wing ratio, and it doesn’t look sick in the least.  I personally don’t see that the color could affect its capabilities at all.”  He looked at the man as if he was suggesting for him to respond.  Then after the man failed to do so, he walked to his bed and began to pack his things.  
       Eric watched as the crowd surrounding him slowly disappeared, as the men all went to pack their belongings.  After the last man was gone Eric stood up, all the time making sure that he had a good hold on the hatchling and he began to try and find a way to keep the thing from running away.  He searched his pack for a long piece of fabric, or something that could be made into one.  As he was searching he felt a tap on his shoulder, when he turned he realized that it was Gerik.  “What’re you doing?” He asked.  “It’s about time to leave.” 
      “I’m trying to find a way to make something that will keep this little thing from running away, and I’m not doing great.” He replied, as he continued to rummage through his pack.  Gerik stared at him inquisitively and then said.  “You don’t have to make anything.” Eric turned, and stared at him questioningly.  “The hatchling follows its mother to keep it safe and to teach it.  It will follow you first and foremost, but will follow the example of the adult wyverns around it, which is how they’re trained.  You see, the wyvern follows the rider as he rides the training wyvern into battle. It takes note of every aspect of the fight.  That’s why wyverns usually travel in packs, so that the hatchlings can experience many different aspects of the fight through the adults.  What the knights here are doing is hunting as a pack, thinking as a pack, and acting for the good of the pack.” After he said this he began to make his way back to his pack and mount, and Eric began to wonder why the veteran knight that had obviously been in the troop for a while, was giving him that kind of exceptional treatment, he was just beginning as a knight and, other than the fight at the gates he had never actually seen him fight.  
      He immediately ceased his wondering when he saw Lord Vallech approaching him swiftly.  Once he had walked near Eric he motioned for the boy to join him at his mount.  Eric immediately started towards the lord’s mount, and when he had arrived Vallech stepped towards him and spoke softly.  “Okay Eric I’m going to ask you once and I want no lies.  What happened in the camp this morning?” He stared at the boy with eyes that felt like they could cut through metal.  
  Then Eric answered by saying, “I woke up to the shout of one of the men, and when I sat up to see what was going on, the man was motioning for everyone to come and see my hatchling.  Then they all started arguing over my wyvern, until Gerik came and stopped them.”  
  Vallech continued to stare, and then spoke.  “What were you doing during all of this?  Why didn’t you stick up for your hatchling?”  
  Eric stammered as he spoke, “Because I was afraid that if I did then the men would be even angrier, and also because I didn’t want them to take my hatchling like they were talking about.”  Vallech sighed as he walked to the boy and put his hands on the boy’s shoulders.  “You don’t have to be afraid of the knights Eric, I’ve seen you in battle, and you could probably take on two or three of them yourself,” he said quietly.  “Also, I’m pretty sure that Gerik would have helped you, and he’s one of my finest knights.” Then he took his hands of the boy’s shoulders and whispered, “if you were in danger Eric, I would be there to protect you in a heartbeat.”  With saying that Vallech turned and began to walk away, Eric also turned and walked towards the training wyvern with his hatchling in toe.   
   After he finished packing his bed, Eric stood up and took a deep breath of the air around him.  He could see the trees that were swaying on the edge of the clearing, and how every leaf seemed to stand out against the sun that was being reflected by them.  The floor of the clearing was full of waist-high grass that seemed to change from light to dark green as it bent to the gentle breeze that was blowing through the valley.  
       He looked down to see his hatchling wyvern chewing on a bone that one of the knights had left from their meal of venison the night before.  He delighted to see that the hatchling’s teeth seemed to slice right through the bone as it enjoyed its feast.  The wyvern’s scales seemed to shine like rubies littering the things back, and every single one seemed as if it was forged by the greatest of gems in the lands.  He picked up the hatchling and held it close to examine it.  It was about two-feet in length, and was very lithe and sleek, almost like a snake with wings, only he had never seen a red snake.  The scales were a sharp v-shape, and were attached to the scales on either side.  Every layer was overlapped by another until they reached the tail, which then all the scales seem to mesh together into a point.  As he was examining it the creature turned its head and stared him in the eyes.  Each iris was an iridescent red and surrounded a pupil, whose depth seemed endless.  
      After he was done he set the wyvern down and began to attach his bags to the training wyvern.  While he was in the midst of this however Vallech strode up to him and spoke.  “Eric, our plans have changed.  We thought we were going to meet up with the ground troops and advance against the city of Tora.”  Eric nodded and Vallech continued to speak.  “The ground troops that are marching are a good three days behind, due to an attack by the Mercians.   Therefore I have decided to send a scout forward to examine the city for weak points and the like.  That person is to be you and you alone.  You are to journey the ten miles to the city and reside there for two days, until the day before the attack.”  Vallech stared into his eyes and then said.  “Also if you need provisions for the ride there you may speak to Gerik, it seems he has taken an interest in you.”
     With that Vallech walked away to assign other knights with orders.  Eric stood up set his wyvern down and walked to find Gerik for provisions.  He found him fifty feet away packing his things onto his wyvern.  He walked up to him and asked, “Gerik I’ve been sent to spy out Tora and would like to know what provisions to take.”
     Gerik turned and inquisitively said.  “Well you’ll need everything that would make it look like you’re travelling alone, not with a bunch of wyvern knights.  Which means you’ll need a bigger pack loaded with three days worth of food, but it must be preserved so that it looks like you’re prepared.  Also I’d give you a horse, but since we travel by sky we have none so that’s out of the question.”  Eric speedily went to gather the supplies, almost tripping over the hatchling wyvern that stayed between his feet.
     When he arrived back with all the supplies, Gerik gave him a large bag to put it in, and then showed him how to carry it around the waist so the weight of it wouldn’t wear him out, and then said a short farewell.  “Well boy you take care, and when you get there find the inn with the most drunkards as possible, and lock your door when you sleep.  Also drink, but not so much as to make you unfit to scout tomorrow.  Wait ‘till midday to leave the inn and take your lance with you, never give it up.  Hopefully you’ll last these next few days without arousing any curiosity, especially if you follow my directions.”  He smiled and then cuffed the boy’s ear in a fatherly manner.  “You take care boy and I’ll save the space behind me for you when we attack the mighty city of Tora!”  With that he beckoned the youth off, and returned to packing his saddle-bags.
      Eric walked to the edge of the encampment and turned for a final glance at his fellow soldiers then set off.  
      For the first hour the walking was great, the way the fresh breeze tickled his face as he hiked the lowlands, and the trees spread their pollen that filled the air with a wonderful flowery scent.  The second hour was more tiring, and he wasn’t able to take in the sights anymore, and by the third he was ready to collapse.  Now he was mostly swatting flies and stepping over roots that were in his path, and was always checking the map he had grown tired of checking over, and over again.  For what felt like the twentieth time he checked the sun over his head to find east and west, and then referred to his map to decide that he should modify his direction and head more towards north.  He walked for another hour and then collapsed onto a log that was on the ground a few feet from the deer trail he was following.
      When he sat down he pulled out some hard cheese from his pack, and then found a stream to drink from.  He also found a tin cup in his pack and he used that to catch water from the stream to drink along with the cheese.  
      After he had finished eating he rested for a couple minutes, and then set off again at a leisurely pace on the deer trail.  He referred to the map a few more times along the way and, just as the sun was about to set the city entered his field of vision.
      At first it was impossible to see the details of it, but after a half-hour he was only half a mile away and he could see all its features.  The city was built around a huge fortress that he could see even at his distance.  The fortress looked all but penetrable and as he got closer he could see that there were trebuchet mounted on the fortress walls with boulders that could easily destroy a house or, more importantly, any siege weaponry that was approaching it.  He could also see that there was a two-hundred yard long plain between the suburbs and the keep.  He approached the suburbs and followed Gerik’s advice in finding the grungiest inn within sight and entered it.
      He entered to find that the common room was full of men all of which had their eyes all locked on the three female dancers that danced in a corner on the far side of the room.  He skirted trough the crowd to speak with the bartender. He waved him down and the bartender spoke cheerfully.  “Well hello thar young sir, are ye enjoying the fine entertainment provided by yours truly, and I may say also that they are for hire if one so wee as yourself were interested.”
    Eric shook his head, “no thanks sir I’m just here for lodging.  How much would a room for two nights go?”
    The bartender replied as boisterously as ever, “well if it’s a room your lookin’ fer they go for three silvers fer the firs’ nigh and two fer every one after tha’.”  
    “Thank you sir I’ll take two nights.”  He said as he gazed out at the crowd, “and a pint of ale.” He added. 
   “Comin’ right up young sir” the bartender said as he filled up one pint from the ale in a barrel right next to him.  
    The bartender handed him the ale as Eric stood up and decided to join in the festive dancing to the tune of a fiddler that had just started playing.  He joined in a game of coin pitching, a game where two players try and pitch a coin across a long table into the other persons cup of ale.  They didn’t play with pints they played with small cups that were similar to the tin cup Gerik had given him.  As the night progressed Eric found that his trained dexterity won him many games and by the end of the night he was almost as drunk as the other men around him.  Things were harder to remember and much more enjoyable as he sang boisterously along with the men to the tune of the fiddler.  He even danced with one of the dancers, and that evening when he retired himself to his room he was accompanied by one of them.
  
         










Chapter 5:  the glory of Tora


    
       It was well past daybreak when Eric stumbled out of his bed still very woozy from the ale and excitement of the night before.  Another thing he noticed was a tingling in his fingertips and toes that felt as if there were a thousand red-hot needles stabbing at the soft tissue.  He finally managed to pick up his clothes until he had to fight back the urge to yell because of a head-splitting pain on his forehead, temples, and what felt like behind his eyes.  After a moment he was able to secede to the pain and slip on his trousers and tunic, he decided to leave his armor with the innkeeper, trusting the man’s resourcefulness extended to safekeeping.
     He left his room with his palm over his eyes to block out the light that seemed to set off every pain receptor in his head.  He proceeded down the dimly lit hallway into the common room that seemed strangely empty after last night’s ruckus.  He sat down on a barstool with his head down and rubbed his eyes with his fingers.  The bartender glanced at him and then proceeded to heat up some water from one of the barrels over his oven.  He approached the youth and said.  “Well lad it looks as if you’ve enjoyed y’ur firs’ nigh’ in a fine tavern, ehh?” The man chuckled as the youth was silent, “and I suppose that head of yours feels just great now doesn’t it?” The youth looked at the man and managed a smile before he was forced to lower his head again because of the light that felt as if it set his head ablaze.  The bartender just chuckled, “aye boy; I’ll fix ya right up, if I haven’t done this once then I’ve done it a thousand times.  Don’t you worry me special remedy will fix ya right up.”  The “special remedy” was just hot water with a drop of honey and lemon rind ground up in it, but from the moment the drink hit his mouth he began to feel much better.  After he had finished the glass he thanked the bartender and offered a tip, which was refused, then picked up his bag and lance, and exited the inn.
     The streets were cluttered as people rushed around this way, and that as men haggling prices for timber, women bought clothe, children scampered around looking for spare change that had been dropped on the ground.  All of them were so happy and oblivious to the fact that the day after tomorrow most of them would be dead.  The sound of a blacksmith’s hammer pounded hot metal into shape as Eric walked by his shop, there were skins being stretched by chords from the wooden beams of a man’s house that would be sold the coming spring.  There was a small group of women chatting and gossiping to the side of the house, and all were oblivious to the fact that everything they had ever worked for would be destroyed or plundered the day after tomorrow.
     Eric felt tears begin to form in his eyes, but he wiped them away and proceeded to go along with his task.  He had decided earlier that day that the first thing to do was make a rough estimate of the number of men in the city.  The Trojian army was about ten-thousand strong, and shouldn’t split up into raiding parties until they were sure that they wouldn’t be stopped.  He spent the next hour walking the streets counting the number of men, while making sure he didn’t walk in the same place twice to avoid any confrontation with the people.  At the end of the hour he had a rough estimate of two-thousand fit and unfit men in the suburbs, later he would make his way into the walled city and search for weaknesses in the walls, and count men.  The counting would take much longer in the city because of the vast number of men in there.  
    He began to make his way towards one of three gate entrances that stood guard to any opposing force that would attempt to seize control of the city.  The city was shaped like a half-moon and was backed into a mountain which provided extra protection against invaders, but as a drawback they also insured that if the city was taken then there would be no escape for the citizens inside.  This fact provoked a curiosity in him, why would people build a city backed into a small mountain valley without having some way to escape.  Eric decided that if there was time after he had counted men and inspected walls that he would investigate this.
     When he arrived at the gate entrance that entered the western side of the city he was pleased to see that the guard was slouched on a chair against the wall, and nodded to Eric as he passed by.  On the other side of the wall the city was divided into eight sections and a paved road made its way along the edges of each section.  All the roads met in a center area that was full of shops and merchants.  From the sky the city looked like a large pie that had been cut into eight separate pieces each piece representing a section. 
     The weather was sunny and warm as the sun warmed the back of Eric’s neck as he made his way through the south-western section making an estimate of the number of men as he made his way towards the center square.  When he arrived at the square he had already counted about three-thousand men so he could already make an estimate that the population of armed men in the city was of about sixteen-thousand.  As soon as he entered into the square he immediately took a hard left and entered the southern section.
     He made his way down the street counting men when something caught his eye, there was a man in colorful costume juggling six daggers in the air.  Eric stood transfixed at the man as he finished catching half of the daggers behind his back, and the other half in front.  Eric noticed a large bowler hat on the ground at the man’s feet and realized that the man was a beggar.  He was shocked at the thought of a man with this much talent not performing in the courts of royalty somewhere, he quickly walked up to the hat and deposited five silver coins into it.  The performer stared at him wide-eyed as Eric nodded at him and then returned to his business of head counting.  
     He made his way to the end of the street and returned with a count of four-hundred men.  When he arrived at the wall the number shocked him, so as he walked back to the city square he recounted the men, and he realized that every man was dressed in an extravagant robe and the houses were built as if they were for kings.  He realized that this must be the housing of the city council and mayor, so he began to quickly make his way out of there to avoid being asked of a favor from one of the men.  Just as he was about to the end of the street he saw a man walk up to the hat that was at the feet of the juggler and, instead of depositing coins into it, he snatched it up and began to sprint away with the coins.  Eric waited as the man ran towards him for the juggler to hurl one of his daggers at the man, but the juggler did nothing.  Seeing this Eric immediately shifted his lance into an attacking stance and slammed the blunt end into the thief’s fore head, which knocked him out cold.  When he was hit the hat with the coins flew into the air and the money was spread along the sidewalk.  Instantly like sharks in a feeding frenzy the persons walking along the street began to snatch up the money as fast as possible.  Eric was stunned and repeatedly shouted for the people to stop, but no matter how much he yelled in a moment all the money had been scavenged and not a copper was left.  
    As the people walked away Eric continued yelling, “What’re you doing, you greedy bastards!  That man earned those coins!  You’re thieves!  You bastards, you’re thieves!”  Eric continued to yell until the juggler walked up to him and silenced him with a grim look.
   “Thank you kind sir, but that won’t do you or me any good at all.  Actually it’ll probably just rouse the guards, but thanks anyway.”  Eric was fuming as he heard this,
     “Do you mean to tell me that this has happened before?”  He asked.
     “Oh, yes it happens about every other day, but no one’s ever said anything about it.  You see I used to perform in the homes of the councilmen here on this street, but a couple months ago one of the mayor’s sons began to beat me with a stick, and it angered the mayor when I asked the lad to stop.  After about a minute I just left the house and ever since no one will hire me, it’s a terrible fate to be resigned to, but I suppose that it belongs to me.”  He glanced at Eric and concluded the conversation by saying.  “Thank you for the coins earlier; I just wanted you to know that I appreciate it.” 
   He began to walk away until Eric ran up behind him and whispered into his ear.  “Get out of the city, take these coins.  Tell no one and get out, you don’t want to be here tomorrow.”  He slipped three more silvers into the man’s hand and returned to the square.
    He ended the day after he finished counting men and inspecting the wall.  He found that there were about six-thousand men in the city, plus the ten-thousand from the suburbs would make sixteen-thousand men.  Luckily there is no suspicion among the civilians of an attack so the suburbs would probably be taken before the men would be prepared.  Unluckily he had found that the wall was flawless with no openings in it that the troops could use to their advantage, which meant that the sky knights would be solely responsible for taking care of the gates and trebuchets, so they would have to attack just right so that the riders weren’t unevenly divided too thin.  But those were questions that Vallech could ponder tomorrow for now he needed sleep after a tiring day.
























Chapter 6: return to camp
  
   Eric woke to the sound of a cock crowing next door.  He pondered how to spend his time.  Eventually he decided to recount the men of the city, and then study and draw the gates for Vallech.  He spent the next three hours recounting, then he went to the gate on the eastern side of the city.  The guards were both wary and did not question him about his entrance to the city.  He took notice of the levers on the tops of the ramparts, on the right side of the gates.  Each gate had the same basic structure as the one before.  They were about thirty feet high and fifteen feet wide, and were made of thick, reinforced wood that looked like it had been replaced within the last year.
   After this it was late in the afternoon, and he decided to retire himself to the inn.  By the time he made it back the sun was beginning to set and the taverns were open for business.  He had no mind for drinking or whoring, he had to keep a sober mind for tomorrow.  He took a seat by the hearth and ordered some spiced wine, and sipped it slowly while staring into the fire.  An hour passed, then another and he closed his eyes for what felt like a second and when he opened them it was midmorning of the next day.  
   The day that Tora would fall.
   The day that thousands no, tens of thousands of people would die.
   The day that he would become a man of Trojia, someone who, upon returning to his homeland, would be given a small piece of land and a steady income for the rest of his life.
   If he wished of course.
   And he did not wish it to be.  He would become one of the veterans, and then an officer.  War was his only life, the only thing he dreamed of.  Women brought happiness, but that came with a price, and he had no patience for their gossiping and bickering.  War was for him.
   He left the inn and winded his way through the early morning vendors until he came to the edge of the suburbs, the area where the parasites of the world dwelt.  He couldn’t help but notice them creeping in the alleyways, or hiding in the shadows.  One would be strangled instantly if they left the safety of the crowd, it was one of the things about the city that Gerik had warned him of.  This area was heartless and without honor, therefore they would be the first to die in the assault.  It was the way of things, how could a building stand without a strong base?  Likewise how can a city stand without a strong center?  The weakest of people were forced outward to the edges of the city.  Everyone had their place, and anywhere else in society was like oil to their watery existence.
  He left the dark outlines of the city and headed for the woods.  He would go one mile into the ocean of trees until he came into contact with the ground forces.  Then he would relay information to Vallech, and Cornelian who was the leader of the infantry.  After reporting to them he would most likely be sent back to the knights where he would find Gerik and stick with him for the rest of this day.  He entered the corn fields that were outside the city at a steady pace.  Half way through he startled three deer feeding on the corn and, as they ran across the field, he heard a party of hunters afar off in the distance shout.  The next five minutes were filled with sounds of dogs barking, men shouting, and the rustling of corn as he ran through the fields away from the men.  He was not afraid of them; he simply had no desire to speak to anybody.  He didn’t want to listen of the tales of horror or hate towards the Empire from anyone, nor the tales of men coming in the night to steal women and children from their homes.
   He made it into the forest at around noon.  The attack was to commence just before dawn the next day and his orders were to meet up with Vallech no later than dusk.  He traversed the mile and began to look for sign of the troop.  After close to an hour he heard shouts and the sound of conversations just fifty yards ahead.  He crouched and slowly walked forward fearing the city watch that would inevitably be patrolling the area.  His fears were relieved when he saw through the bushes and recognized the Trojian attire and accent.  He confidently stepped into the clearing only to be roughly grabbed by the ten or twelve perimeter guards closest to him.
   “What in the name of the Gods are you doing here whelp eh?” The guard was tall, round, and extremely ugly.  Eric responded by falling to his knees in fear, and the guards responded quite well to the masquerade.  “Oh look at this piece of goose shit, I’ve had women more AHHH YOU PIECE OF…” He was cut short when Eric reached up to the guard leaning over him and ripped out a section of his beard.  When the man raised his face to the sky and shouted profanities Eric reached up and slammed his fist as hard as he could into his groin.  He did not wait for the others to respond.  He picked up his fallen spear and held it into the air in the Trojian form of the dove.  He held up his spear with one hand and crossed the spear over his other arm without grabbing it.  
   Immediately the guards stopped.  “What’s your rank soldier?”  The man asked.  “No wait, I’ll take you to the officer’s tent to be interrogated.”  The soldier roughly took hold of him and they set off through the camp.
   As they passed through the roughly pitched tents of the soldier the man spoke.  “So you really a soldier eh?”  Eric nodded, and the man responded by asking him his rank.
   “I’m under the tutelage of Lord Vallech; I’m to become a sky knight.  I served in the twenty second ground infantry near the border also.”  The man looked at him for a moment than asked.
   “You served in the twenty second?  I know the scumbag of a captain there.  Since you served you, could tell me his name.  Go ahead, tell me!”  Eric thought long and hard, but he had never heard the general’s name uttered before.  Another moment passed and he stammered.  
   “I don’t know, all we ever heard him called was ‘sir’ or ‘general.’  Oh and I suppose what the wenches call him, he was always called ‘Uncle Teddy’ don’t ask me what it means I don’t know.”  The man grinned at him.
   “Oh that’s him alright, okay I trust you there, so I guess I’ll just take you to Vallech and we’ll see what he says.”  
   So they turned left and headed towards the sky knight encampment.  When they reached Vallech’s tent he was greeted by Gerik.  
   “Eric!!!  Good to see ya back in one piece lad.”  He glanced at the man holding him.  “Meat do you really know who it is you hold?  You’re holding an apprentice of Vallech himself.  This boy could leave you as nothing but a charred husk of a human being, so I would recommend that you return to your post.”  The man stammered excuses.  “NOW!!!”  Gerik shouted and the man saluted and abruptly left without a word.  Gerik winked at him and opened the tent flap.  “Go ahead and go in lad, and I put in a word for ya and Vallech agrees, I’m to be your offensive crafting tutor from now on.”  Eric stared at him blankly.  What is he talking about? He wondered but kept quiet and entered the tent.  The room was round and centered around a table with a map of the city laid out on it.  There were two men studying the map and they turned as they heard him enter.  
   “Ahh Eric what a pleasant surprise, come come.  We’ve been waiting for your information for plenty long enough that we’ll not wait any longer.  Okay now report soldier”  The Lord addressed him as a close friend, with sincerity that held respect for the lord’s rank, and showed respect to a fellow soldier as well.  
   “Yes M’lord, I inspected the city thoroughly twice.  Both times the count of armed men was between eight and ten thousand.”  Vallech nodded for him to continue.  “The three main gates are each opened with the use of levers on top of the towers immediately to the left and right of the gate.  All three function using this method.”  Vallech nodded again impressed at how well the job was done.  Eric continued “The morale of the city is very lax, nobody is expecting an attack.  Guards sleep on their posts, men never carry weapons, and the town surrounding the city is full of beggars and the sickly.  The main armory is actually in the city, on the western side of the plaza.  I would recommend taking that first to cut them off from their weapons, but that of course is a humble suggestion sir.”  This time the other man took notice of him.  He took a step towards them and addressed Vallech.  
   “This is your apprentice straight from the recruiters Vallech?  I would’ve expected a speech like that from a prime agent of my own.  Good pick with this one.”  Eric blushed and was dismissed by them both.
   He exited the tent to find Gerik waiting for him outside.  “I heard that report lad and I think Vallech was pleased.  He’ll be taking your advice about the armory for sure, you should be honored lad.”  He patted him on the shoulder roughly as Eric smiled.  Gerik led him back to the sky knight’s camp.  
   As they progressed further Eric thought about spell casting that Gerik had mentioned earlier.  After awhile curiosity got the better of him.  “Ummm Gerik, do you remember when you mentioned crafting to me?”  The older man nodded, and he continued “Well I just…er wanted you to know that I’ve already been taught blacksmithing.”  The man stared at him blankly.  “I just… uhh didn’t want to waste your time with that... Okay?”  Gerik stared at him briefly and then burst into laughter.
   “No, no, no boy I’m not talking about smithing.  I mean CRAFTING, you remember that Reman key we used at Shan?”  Eric nodded.  “THAT was crafting, not machinery, it was changing nature not apart from it.  Where do you think all that energy came from, a piece of metal?”  Eric was still confused.
  “Wait so you mean like… magic?”  He was still confused, this was war not some magician’s show for children.
  “Okay lad do you remember when you asked me about my arm, when I’d taken that hit at Shan?”  Eric nodded.  “Well that was crafting, I used the energy of the enemy soldiers to heal myself.  I sped up nature by knitting the skin back together with heat energy from the enemies.”  Eric froze.
   “Wait so you mean that you took their energy?  I don’t understand.”
