   “I tell you what, as long as you’re not fond of sleep we can have our first lesson tonight.”  Eric nodded so the man continued.  “The common thought is that when you run or fight that you burn the energy in your body and it disappears right?”  Eric nodded so he continued.  “Well that’s not precisely true, when you use that energy to move your arm towards the enemy all that energy is focused into your arm.  When you make contact with the other man all the energy in your arm transfers to him…”
   “Wait,” Eric interrupted.  “That can’t be true, if you’re telling me that then nobody should ever die, they should get stronger when I hit them right?”
   The older man laughed “Of course not, too much energy hurts.  Plus you’re applying it to a small part of his body.  All of your energy wants to move down, so a small part of his arm, or head moves down.  That’s why your sword goes through his head because you force parts of it to move down.”
  “Okay I think I understand… a little.”  
   Gerik smiled “Well than we can move on and I’ll explain the basics of crafting.”  He made some vague hand motions as he spoke.
   “When you craft essentially you are transferring energy either from yourself to your opponent, or from your opponent into you, OR if you’re especially talented, from your opponent into another opponent.  But you won’t be doing that for awhile.”  
   Eric nodded enthusiastically urging him to continue.  “So you could take some energy from yourself and force it into, say, a man's heart.  It could be like hitting him in the heart correct?”
   Gerik nodded slowly, “You’ve got the idea boy, you’ve got the idea.  Now think.  Not only do you have to supply the energy to deal the blow, but also counter the effects of nature taking energy all the time.  You see energy always wants to go the easiest route, like a lazy recruit, always downhill.  So the more you transfer the more is lost to nature, also the further it travels the more is lost.  These effects have to be compensated from somewhere.”
  Gerik stared at him intently and asked.  “So what does this mean?”
  Eric took a moment to think and then answered confidently “It means that the further away the thing is the more energy it takes to transfer, and also the bigger the crafting the more is lost.”
   The older man nodded enthusiastically “that’s right boy you’ve got a real knack for this, so essentially it would be less taxing to do lots of tiny transfers than one big one.”
   “Right!”  Eric exclaimed.  He was enjoying this a lot, he couldn’t wait to get started.
   “Yes, although the theory is easy to talk about, the doing is rather difficult.”  Eric looked confused.  “Okay boy imagine, almost nobody can do this, almost nobody.” Eric pondered the words for a moment.  “A crafting requires a link between the two entities, normally this would be an arm or a blade, but you have neither of these so how do you transfer the energy?”  Eric was stunned for a second; Gerik was right it is one thing to talk and another to do.  Gerik smiled “You have to make one and to do that you have to have the gift.  Vallech thinks you have the gift, and he wants me to test you tonight before tomorrow.
  Eric was shocked “What do you mean… test?  You mean like you want me to try and do this?”  Gerik stared at him for a moment and then replied.
   “Yes and no, I’ll be aiding you.  I’ll teach you how to feel out a link and then help you make one.  If you can make one then I’ll be amazed, I just want to see if you can feel the link”  Eric nodded slowly as Gerik continued “But first you’re going to need to rest for a bit, I’ll wake you up an hour or so before everyone else if you don’t mind.”  The question was rhetorical, this had been commanded by Vallech but he nodded anyway.  “Good boy now go to sleep, your hatchling is progressing well by the way.  He’s waiting for you with your cot.  His training is also going well.” 
   Eric nodded and thanked him before he set off for his cot.  The wyvern had grown a little over the last few days and had definitely not forgotten him.  This time though it crawled up his back, which was quite painful due to its newly grown talons, and stood on his shoulder.  It was very light.
   “Hey there boy, it’s time to sleep now, c’mon.”  The animal looked into his eyes and glided to the ground where it waited for him to enter the cot.  
   When he did the thing slid up his shirt and pressed itself against the dark spot on his chest.  It pulsed and glowed mildly as they took slow breaths together and fell into a deep sleep.
























Chapter 7: preparation for Tora and a new power


   “C’mon boy wake up!”  Eric struggled to escape from his dreams.  After a moment he shook his head and stood up off his mat.  The little wyvern on his chest squeaked in displeasure as he rose, and it fell down to the ground landing with a grunt.  He looked around, it was the middle of the night and the only men that were awake were those that were following as suppliers of the army.  They would not be fighting so therefore they could stay up drinking all night long, like they were right now.  Gerik patted him on the shoulder; he was a big man, big but also lean.  He was easily six-feet tall, close to two-hundred pounds.  He had flaming red hair that he kept close-cropped to about an inch away from his scalp.  He wore standard legion outfitting, which composed of gray leggings along with a gray tunic that had one patch sewn onto it for every battle he had been in.  The shirt was covered, back-to-front in the patches.
   “Okay you still wanna do this?”  The man asked.  
   “Of course I do, when do we start?” 
   The big man chuckled “We can start right now if you like.”  Eric nodded so the older man went to one of the tents that surrounded the captains and returned with a small satchel.  He carried the satchel over to where Eric sat and did not open it.  “Alright lad could you go bring your hatchling over here for this, we’re going to need him.”  Eric stared for a moment then quickly stood up and called for the thing.  It had only been about five yards away, in the shadow of some tent gnawing on a bone in frustration after being waken up.  It stood up and lazily walked to him and laid down against his foot.  
   “Alright he’s here, now what?” Eric chuckled as the hatchling played with his leather boots; it had sunk its teeth into the boot and was using its back legs to attack the thing.  It was quite humorous.
   “Now you sit down and press him to that spot on your chest, that’s right I know about that, it happens to all of us.  That’s an energy link provided by both of you as a means of transferring battle experience to the hatchling.”  Eric stared down at the now still hatchling, and sat down next to it.  He took it tenderly in his arms and tucked it inside of his shirt.  As soon as it came in contact with the spot it stopped squirming and pressed its whole body against it, like it had always done.  “Alright, have you noticed that you feel slightly drained after sleeping with that thing against your chest?”  Eric nodded, he had noticed that.  “Well that’s because of the link, you’ve already made an energy link Eric you just never realized.”  Eric nodded again, this time slowly as he drank in the man’s words.  “So I want to see if you can identify the energy being transferred from you, where it’s going in the hatchling, and to identify the link that exists, meaning about how much energy is lost in the link and so on.”  
   Eric paused, how am I supposed to do this?  He thought, and he truly had no idea.  He analyzed the feeling of the hatchling against his chest and it was colder than it should have been.  It must be transferring heat from me to him.  He analyzed it more and felt a subtle tingling all over his body.  He hadn’t noticed it before, but now he did.  It felt like a flowing river that went through his body, and he could feel the deep and shallow parts, and the bends and curves.  It was strongest and deepest in his chest which was the source of the river, and next his head.  Now that he could feel it he realized that the hatchling next to him was part of the river too.  He could feel out of his own and into the wyvern.  Their heartbeats matched each other perfectly and they both felt at peace while sharing the contact.
   After a few moments Eric began to think, so, according to Gerik, this link should be able to extend through the air.  He turned to ask the man and saw that he was smiling.  “Great job lad you’ve recognized the link.  Don’t shake your head I can tell.”  Eric shook his head again.
   “Gerik you said that it could extend through the air, this only links like this when we touch.”  The older man nodded very slowly chewing on his words.
   “This link is a very unique type because you’re sharing your memories and your feelings with it also.  Essentially in one night that hatchling gets to know more about you than anyone else on the planet knows, and I’m not lying.”  They both glanced at the sleeping hatchling.  “I wouldn’t doubt that it already understands English by now too.”  This time Eric really stared at it and thought about waking it up to test this, but changed his mind.  It looked so peaceful while it slept.  
   “So how does this make a difference in the link?” Eric asked.  “I still can’t link to it now.”  Gerik shook his head.
   “Yes you can, you must simply extend yourself to the little creature.  With living things it’s easy, they kind of draw you into them.  It’s much harder with things that aren’t alive like a rock or a sword.”  Eric thought for a moment.
   “Can I try it, just once I have an idea of how to do it?”  Gerik nodded looking a little surprised and stepped away from them.  Eric took a step back away from the hatchling so that it laid five feet away from him on the ground.  Then he closed his eyes and almost instantly he felt the river again, that life-sustaining flow that flows through every vein of his body.  Now though he made a “breach” in the river.  A new river leading to the hatchling that laid in front of him.  He immediately felt a slight tiredness but kept going.  He extended the new “river” to the hatchling and came into contact with it.  Now it felt almost like when their chests were linked and he knew that he could give or take energy from it.  
   Wait what if I tried to link to one of the soldiers over there and give me and the hatchling more energy.  He thought some more and then heard a foreign thought in his mind.  It wasn’t in words but he could tell that it agreed with him.  The little hatchling on the ground had opened one eye and was staring at him.  But wait the men need their rest, I can’t take any from them.  Forgetting all the rules he looked at the campfire that they stood around.  He could feel the heat energy flowing out from it and instantly decided to link to it.  He made another breach and sent this new “river” into the hottest part of the flame.  Instantly he felt alive to his fullest extent, it was just like when the battle-calm took hold of him only this time it came from another source.  In the light of the fire that now flowed through him a new patch of energy was located.  It was a pocket located directly in his chest.  It was both familiar and not familiar, he couldn’t identify it as heat energy like the rest of his body, it was something else completely.  He made a third link, from the pocket to the fire.  He couldn’t think of anywhere else to put it, so the fire was the most reasonable place.  He made the river to it and the energy flowed so fast that it was overflowing the breach.  He could see sparks appear in the air in front of him and he quickly finished the link. 
   Instantly the fire exploded and he Gerik and the hatchling were blown back ten feet.  It had caught the tents that were closest on fire but those were quickly put out. Gerik stood up and stared with his mouth open at the boy.  “How… how did you…Blast it that’s impossible?!?!  That fire had more energy than you did, YOU should’ve been the one that exploded.  I tried to stop you but I just don’t…… understand.”  Eric didn’t move he had forgotten everything until he was sure his wyvern was okay.  He was and after a moment when everything was cleaned up Gerik told him to follow him and they left at a fast pace towards Vallech’s tent.
   When they arrived and spoke to the guards of their urgency they immediately stood aside and permitted them entrance.  Inside Vallech was still working at the battle-plans and looked up from his desk with a frown.  “What is the meaning of this Gerik, and why is the boy here?  I trust you but now is not the best of times.”  Gerik apologized and then spoke quickly.
   “The boy… he” they both glanced at him and after a moment Vallech spoke.
   “Continue Gerik.” It was not a request.
   “Sorry m’lord alright, I was testing him and he successfully made a link to his hatchling…”
   “Well this is a good thing, but not really worth bothering me for.  I expected nothing less from him, if this is all you have to tell me I must admit that I am slightly disappointed.”  He spoke calmly but with a tone of annoyance beneath the polite exterior.
   “No, no sir that’s not all.  The boy… well after he made that link he made another” at this Vallech’s gaze locked onto the boy, he nodded for Gerik to continue.  “Well sir you see… I recognized the first, it was a link to the campfire.  I think he was trying to rejuvenate himself and the hatchling.  Am I right?”  Eric nodded so the man continued.  “Now this is where I’m confused because he made another link after the one to the fire.  It was like he linked himself to the campfire twice, and the second link caused the campfire to explode which threw us all back.”  Gerik still looked slightly shaken from the incident and looked down at the floor in his confusion.  Vallech stared at the boy for a moment and then stood up.
   “Alright Eric here’s what I want.”  Eric nodded so the man continued “I want you to duplicate that link you made only this time control it.  I only want you to make the last one, oh and I want you to link to this.”  He held out the Reman “Key” that had been used at Shan.  “Go ahead take as long as you want.”  Eric was unsure but decided to obey the man.
   He felt the river and this time knew exactly where the pocket was, surprisingly it looked bigger than before.  Not sure what to think he chose not to think about it and opened a link from the pocket to the key in Vallech’s hand.  He felt the huge amounts of energy slosh at the sides of the breach but held it in so that, instead of sparks, only dim light traveled to the key.  Instead of exploding the tip of the key grew white hot and Vallech held it up amazed.  So that must be how the key works, but that must mean…
   Vallech told him to break the link, so he did.  With the breaking sparks popped and crackled in the air and then all was quiet.  Vallech was speechless, and Gerik was even more so if that was possible.  The red haired man’s mouth was hanging open in shock as Vallech spoke.  
   “He is to resume studies with you Gerik as normal.”  He turned to Eric and said.  “Eric I want you to become familiar with this energy.  It is a tool, the ability you have that makes you infinitely more valuable than most crafters.”  He smiled.  “As you’ve probably noticed you and I are the only ones who can work the Reman key, any other crafter would have to open a thousand links and kill fifty men to get as much energy as you or I have inside us.”  Eric nodded quite speechless.  “So I want you to treat it that way, become familiar with it, practice making links quickly and without so much of a firework display.”  He smiled.  “This energy can kill men and you should treat it thusly.  If you have a spear then you can stab someone.  If you have a knife you can cut someone.”  He smiled again, “but if you have an energy source as strong as a thousand men then you can do things that a thousand men could do.  Remember this, and I want you to use this power today.  You’re going to practice on some dummies, and I know it won’t be your primary weapon but…”  He looked directly into his eyes, “it could save your life if all you have to do to kill five men is open a quick link into the ground below them.”  Now he walked to Eric and put a hand on his shoulder.  “You will be a great crafter, and a greater sky knight.”  Now he looked him in the eye.  “Just don’t let the power corrupt you, you mustn’t treat others inferior it’s how you live not how you die that matters, and if you live like a tyrant swine than the Gods will only see pork when you die.”  Eric nodded.  “Good, let the preparation for the assault on Tora begin!”










Chapter 8: words of encouragement


   Wow, he thought.  This is fantastic!!  Once again the dummy exploded after about six seconds from making the link.  Vallech had told him that killing men would take much less time than simply making them explode, but it was good exercise for making the links.  He had run out of dummies and started to use archery targets, and he had started drastically altering the wood by putting pressure on various parts of it.  He could now successfully shatter a fist sized section of log from twenty feet away, and that could kill a man instantly even if he wore armor.  
   Now that he had discovered this power it was hard to restrain himself from using it.  It almost seemed tiresome to work with his spear after just one morning of practice, but he knew that this power could not block a blow, or at least he was not able to accomplish such a feat yet.
   He heard an officer shouting orders for the men to report to their seniors, so he finished up with the log he was working on and headed toward Vallech’s tent.  After a few minutes of winding and shoving his way through the crowd of soldiers he arrived at the Lord’s tent where he was greeting with enthusiasm by Gerik.
   “Hello thar lad, I think the whole camp’s in an uproar about you, they say you’ll take Vallech’s place after another ten years or so.”  The older man was smiling with his whole body; everything about him from the way he stood with his shoulders back, to his long confident strides screamed his pride in the boy.  “He wants you to report by the way, he’s giving a pep talk to the knights as we speak.”
   Eric thanked him and walked the rest of the way with him, trying to keep up with the large man’s pace.  After a few moments the rounded the next set of tents to see the sky knights preparing for battle.  Scales were being polished, as well as armor, the sound of a hundred swords being sharpened made him grit his teeth as he searched for Vallech in the crowd.  He found him talking to a few of his officers about the upcoming assault, and waited patiently for him to finish.  After he dismissed the men he approached Eric, his eyes blazed with some hidden excitement.  There was a skip to his step that he’d never seen before this, as if the man inside him had just awakened and been replaced with a lion.  
   “Eric!”  The lord said, as his lips parted into a smile.  “Are you ready yet?”  Eric nodded and smiled at the lord, it was so hard to be nervous when he was in his present mood.  “Splendid, I’m going to be dividing the troops into three squadrons, one for each gate.  I want you to stay near the officer of the central squad, so that he had someone to show him the central gate mechanism.  I’m hoping that, with your help, the man can manage to raise the central gate efficiently enough.”  Eric nodded again, ecstatic from the lord’s praise.  “Do you think you got that?”
   “Yes sir, I believe I do!”  Eric told him, he couldn’t believe it.  He wasn’t even a knight and he was being trusted with so much.
   “Great, enthusiasm I like it.  Be ready in a half hour, we’re going to hold the gates and then half of us are going to take that armory.  Just like you suggested I believe.  Correct?”  Eric’s heart skipped a beat, yes he had suggested that.
   “Yes, but you are the one who chose to put it into action m’lord.”  Vallech smiled at him, then put a hand on his shoulder.
   “Okay then Eric I think you have some things that you need to get ready for.  Role call’s in a half hour.”  Eric saluted him, “Dismissed” Vallech said.
   Eric rushed through his preparations and, after he had finished, he followed some of the men to the square where the ninety-so knights would meet.  Vallech stood on his mount and spoke vehemently to the men.  
   “Alright knights I’m expecting nothing more out of you than your title permits, each of you has specially trained for moments like those in front of us!  It is time for each of us to show everyone what it is like to fear!  It is time for each of us to prove to these rebellions that the Empire of Trojia is not to be trifled with!”  This was followed by a loud cheer from the men.  Vallech gazed quietly over the crowd.  “Each of you have received your orders, the ground troops are waiting for our signal, when we fly over that city I want you all to follow your orders to the dot and finish them all!”  The men shouted again and Vallech saluted them, “I wish all of you the Gods luck and speed.  May you rest in their bosom when your time comes.”  The men shouted then mounted their steeds and, with many shouts of enthusiasm, were off into the sky.












Chapter 9: the assault on Tora


   The ground fell away from him in a rush of swirling colors and the sound of wind roaring in his ears was almost deafening.  They did not rise to the clouds, they rose only to just barely above the tops of the trees, so that occasionally the training wyvern’s legs would brush against the foremost limbs of them.  They could immediately see the city just a little over a mile in front of them, with its white walls gleamed against the rising sun.  The rising ramparts of the keep towered above the rest of the city.  It was a glorious sight and a worthy prize for a Lord such as Vallech.  Eric was slightly worried though, the men he had counted were mainly trained men and they nearly doubled the numbers of the attacking force.  They would need to open those gates as fast as possible so that the ground forces could quickly move into the city.  He guessed that once the city proper was taken they would lay siege to the keep until its occupants were starved into desperation, if they made it that far that is.
   They were almost to the edge of the outlying villages that surrounded the city when shouts began to ring out.  They were expected but the cries of terror still struck his heart like a bell.  For a few seconds his heart rung out with mercy towards the people caught in the attack.  They were not trained, most of them had no weapons, and the ones that did stood no chance against the veterans from Trojia.  They would all die, but war had costs and the rulers only had power if the people chose to follow them.  This was the creed that Vallech lived by.  Slaves or those forced into service were always spared; they had no choice but to serve their masters and could not help the fact that they opposed the empire.  The free people of the land however were a different story. They would all die, and it was not customary for women or slaves to be taken by the Trojians.  Their honor took place above all else and they knew that, in their place, they would choose death over slavery.  
   They were now directly over the suburbs and the three groups began to break off and head towards each of their assigned gates.  Eric’s squad was the shortest distance from their gate and he could already see the men on the walls rushing to gather bows from the towers.  They knew they had little time and abruptly closed the distance between them and the wall in as little time as possible.  The squad split in half with each taking a side of the gate.  That left twenty men to take each side.  There were approximately sixty men on guard duty at the walls.  There were probably two thousand ready to rush to the armory and be on the walls within ten minutes.  That left them about five minutes to take the gate.  It sounded nearly impossible but he knew that he had yet to see the full potential of the sky knights.  If what Gerik had told him about crafting was true than most of the knights could likely kill many of them without lifting a finger.  
   True to what he thought, just before landing on the wall, five soldiers dropped dead clutching their chests and screaming in agony.  Eric quickly made a link to a man that was about to stab his mount, but before he could finish it the wyvern slashed out with its claws and rent the man almost in two.  The thought reassured him, that’s good, I had forgotten about the wyverns that doubles our fighting force.  It was much more comforting to know that an intelligent lizard twice the size of a horse, with poisonous fangs and lethal claws was covering your back in a fight.
   He jumped off his mount and threw his spear into the chest of a man fifteen feet away.  The man fell to the ground, convulsing and spewing blood from the wound.  He jerked his knife from its scabbard and dodged a sword that had just been slashed at him only to feel the world spin, and see stars when the man hit him with his shield.  He fell straight to the ground and just before the man delivered the killing blow he dropped his sword and screamed as blood gushed out of his ears and nose from a link one of the knights had made to his head.  He continued to scream as Eric stood up and observed the battle before him.
   The wall was close to thirty feet wide and already slicked with the blood of the dead.  It didn’t look like any of the knights had fallen and many of the enemy had died, or lay dying on the wall.  He found the officer and led him to the tower with the rest of the knights in toe.  Inside the tower they would not have the wyverns with them, but now that they matched the guards in numbers and could easily take it.  They confidently strode towards the door and when they were thirty feet from it two men appeared in the doorway with bows drawn back to their cheeks and arrows on the strings.  Without thinking he dropped to the ground and linked the two men together.  Then he linked both of them to the pocket of energy inside him and let the current flow. 
   The effect was devastating as both men simply exploded, spewing blood and gore like a fountain.  He turned around and saw the officer leaning on another knight with an arrow in his gut.  
   He couldn’t think he just yelled out “Who is the second in command here?”  That was answered with silence.  “Surely Vallech appointed someone here to lead if his officer fell?”  What they hell are we supposed to do without an officer?  He thought as the man holding the officer spoke up, it was Gerik.  
   “Eric all of the knights hold an equal position, we only have one head and that’s Vallech.”  He looked around quickly and then said “as long as there are no objections, I would say that we could follow your lead.  Lead us to the gate lever and we’ll figure out everything else when we meet Vallech at the armory.”  
   All the knights nodded in agreement and quickly fell into formation behind Eric.  One of the men drug the previous officer to his mount who quickly flew him away towards the ground army forces, then the knights followed Eric as he led them through the doorway.
   Eric ran up the stairs in the tower taking them two at a time, until he was met by the remaining guards halfway up the stairs.  They had nearly taken his head off before he jumped back and fell into Gerik who was directly behind him.  Gerik helped him to his feet then groaned as an arrow struck him in the leg.  The knights were in a bad position on the circular staircase, and knew they had little time to open the gate.  Eric stood up straight and linked the hearts of three of the archers to him and set the energy loose, which killed them instantly.  They fell down the stairs knocking over the men holding the staircase.  This was all the knights needed and they quickly rushed against the guards in a merciless fury.  The guards were cut down until only ten of them remained blocking the staircase with lances that were six feet long.  Eric watched amazed as the knights gathered and the energy of twenty full crafters was linked to a torch that one of the guards held in his hand.  The fire surged and caught the guard on fire as Eric linked his pocket with the torch on the wall by the spearmen.  The explosion killed all but two of the guards who laid down their weapons and dropped to their knees.  The knights beheaded them as two of the knights rushed over to open the gate.
   Now the squad split into two groups with twelve men staying behind, and the other seven headed towards the armory to meet Vallech.  When they reached their mounts they quickly launched into the air and headed towards the square.  When the square came into his field of vision he cursed and stopped immediately in mid-flight.
    The square was filled with soldiers and they were all headed towards the gate he had just opened and out towards the suburbs.  After a few seconds he saw Vallech and the other forty men on top of a house crouched down immediately to his left.  Vallech motioned for him to come and the seven knights quickly dropped to the roof.  After he dismounted he lay down next to Vallech, they looked at each other for a second before the lord asked, “What happened to Brendan I don’t see him with you?”  Brendan was the name of the officer who had gone down with the arrow in his gut.
   “He was shot in the gut, we sent him off with his mount before we took the tower.”  Vallech made a face.
   “One other was lost?”  Eric thought for a moment than said.
   “I don’t remember when he went down, I know that there were no bodies when I came back through.  Maybe one of the other men sent him off.”  Vallech nodded and then Eric asked.  “Lord what are we doing here, it’s evident that there’s no way we’re getting into that armory why don’t we just join the ground troops and aid them from the sky?”
   The lord smiled and quickly uttered under his breath.  “Because the enemy doesn’t know we’re here and as soon as they clear out we’re going to head right into the open gates of the keep.”  Eric gasped and looked towards the keep, amazingly the gates were open.  “The protocol here is to leave the gates of the keep open until the wall is taken, for the civilians to escape into.  They believe that the guards opened the main gates for them to march out of.  The lack of discipline among the officers amazes me.”  Vallech laughed under his breath for a moment before turning to Eric again.  “That means we’re going to have to deal with a small part of the royal guard and then nothing but fat senators and fatter royalty.”  Now he laughed openly and Eric joined him.  “Ready the men it looks like the last of the guard is clearing out.”  Eric looked and it was true, the gates now stood empty as the last of the guard walked by the building they laid on.  
   “Yes sir,” Eric whispered as he stood up and walked over to the knights who were treating wounds and chatting quietly.  Gerik walked towards him and wrapped him into an embrace.
   “You did great lad it’s almost over now.”  The man held on for another moment then let go and took a step back looking him in the eyes.  He frowned when Eric spoke.
   “Gerik, Vallech wants the men ready to fly right into that hall.  We’re gonna take the keep while the gates open.”  Gerik frowned openly and then whispered.
   “I don’t like the sound of that plan lad, I think the leaders here are a little smarter than we’re giving them credit for.”  Eric looked at the man contemplating what he had said.
  “I don’t know Gerik I’m just following orders, I don’t know what’s ahead.  I just know that Vallech hasn’t given us a reason to distrust him yet so I’m going to follow him.”  Gerik slowly nodded his head.
   “I suppose you’re right lad, I just have a bad feeling about this.  I’ll go and help you tell the men, we can’t be yelling up here.  We’d give away our position.”  With that the two men went and whispered the orders to the men.  There would be no splitting up this time; they were going to make a beeline straight to the bedchambers.  Hopefully they would arrive and listen as the pompous fools drowned in their own blood.
   Five minutes later the men were mounted and ready to fly into the keep.  The initial halls were large enough for the wyverns to fight alongside the men but after that the men would be alone.  Forty seven men were about to take the keep of the glorious city of Tora.  The largest stronghold on the eastern coast.
   They were ready when Vallech screamed and took off towards the open gate.  With cries of their own the knights followed his lead towards the gate.  No one opposed them as they flew through the open gate and the great hall.  
   The great corridor was mostly empty except for a few royals who had been halfway down the stairs.  They were impaled by the knights as they dismounted in the corridor.  The corridor was huge, close to eighty yards long and just as tall.  The ceiling rose higher than any other he had ever seen and stained glass windows filled the room with rainbow light.
   The knights rushed through the door at the end of the hall and the throne room until they came into the hallways leading towards the private rooms.  They traveled for a few minutes until they came to a hallway filled with guards who were protecting the doors at the end of the hall.   Perfect.
     The knights rushed forward and crashed into the guard with a roar.  Eric thrust his lance into the throat of the nearest guardsman and he fell to the ground and broke the spear as he fell on it.  He bent down to retrieve the man’s sword as he heard cries from behind him.  Oh God!!!  He thought as another sixty guards came pouring in from behind them.  That put forty seven, no now forty six against close to one hundred and fifty soldiers who were trained elites.  And who had closed them off in a corridor and were closing the distance quickly from both sides.
   Vallech screamed and ran back towards the way they came, shouting for the knights to follow.  He did not slow his pace as he neared the guards and threw his spear which flew directly through the guard and did not stop until it stuck into the wall behind them. Twenty guards dropped dead with holes in their chests as Vallech ran through the center of them.  
   Vallech escaped, but after the initial shock the guards reformed the line and the royal household protectors attacked.  The knights stood little chance as the guardsman crushed them like a louse between them.  They stood their ground and fought valiantly, but they had no chance from the start.  When there were twenty of them left the defenders backed off and someone shouted for them to surrender.
   Eric remained, and so did Gerik.  Other than that nineteen of the knights still stood and none of them were in fighting shape.  Half of them fell to their knees and dropped their weapons, and the others followed suit until every knight there was on his knees.  Their only hope for survival lay in the hands of the royalty that they had just been about to mercilessly slaughter in their bed robes.  
   The guards parted after they had taken the knight’s weapons and allowed three men to pass through.  One was a middle-aged man whose beard was flecked with gray.  One was a man with brown hair who was bristling with fury, he was very fat and if Eric could’ve laughed he would have found the man very amusing.  One had dirty blond hair that was wrapped in a ponytail, had a face that was accustomed to smiling (although it was not now) and looked to be about his own age.  
   Eric gasped.  The third man, the one with the ponytail, was the performer he had seen on the streets only two days ago.  The man looked him straight in the eyes, and held his gaze; he looked as if he had just been slapped in the face.








































Chapter 10: Imprisoned


   The beggar-man stared him in the eyes and held his gaze for almost the entire time the king was speaking.  Eric stared back at him, eyes pleading.  This man looked like he had some influence over the king that might just save Eric’s life.  Fate must have set him in the path of this man, this could be no coincidence.  Eric bowed his head and tried to look as somber as possible.  
  “Captain,” the king spoke to the commander of the household troops.  “Take these men to the imperial prison.  I want them confined and ready to be interrogated tomorrow by the royal inquisitor.”  The captain nodded.
   “You heard ‘em, Francis I want you and the twenty-second to take these prisoners to the prison pronto.”  The man holding Gerik nodded and jerked the man to his feet.  Gerik didn’t make one sound as the soldiers began tying rope around the prisoner’s hands.  
   Eric hadn’t stopped staring at the prince, his eyes were pleading to him.  Please, please was all he could think, but when he was wrenched to his feet the prince turned his gaze away and all his hopes were dashed.  He was hauled against the wall and his hands were tied behind his back.  Then he fell into place in the line of twenty-one men who, twenty minutes ago, had been sure of their victory.  Now emptiness filled their faces, and a black mood set over them as they exited the palace.
  They stepped into the courtyard and turned away from the armory and headed towards the side of the keep, the side of the keep where a dank prison was attached to the rock of the mountain.  The only visible portion of the prison was about the size of a small, one-story hovel.  But he knew that the prison extended into a network of tunnels that traversed through the mountain.  
  The surrounding soldiers signaled for them to stop and then removed every piece of clothing from their bodies.  They were left bare and naked in the streets for about five minutes until men came out of the prison and directed them inside.
   When he walked through the doors of the place a dark ominous feeling crept into his body.  They were herded down a small set of stairs lit by torches and into a small interior chamber where a man sat behind a desk and laughed openly at each of the men who walked by.  When Eric passed him he spat in his face.  It was a thick glob, thick and filled with mucus, and it landed right in his eye.  Eric stopped to try and wipe the stuff off, and the man slammed him into the ground.  Eric curled into a ball as the man kicked him with steel tipped boots, every kick made him twist and contort himself to get away.  He couldn’t make a sound until the man stopped, then dashed a bucket of ice-cold water on him that felt like it was liquid fire.
  “Get the up you steaming pile of shit!”  The man kicked him again, this time in the groin and Eric screamed.  “I can already tell we’re going to get along fabulously, now I would like you to get acquainted with one of my favorite toys.”  The man walked back to his desk and Eric stayed curled on the floor.  The man reached under his desk and pulled out something that looked like a potato peeler.  The only difference was that it was covered with blood instead of potato skins.
   Eric screamed again and tried to scramble to his feet, but a guard kicked him and he fell to down again twitching from the blow.
   “You little arseling, there’s nowhere to run.  Just me, and a couple of my special toys are the only things you’ll get to know for the rest of your life.”  The man laughed as he drug Eric by the arm to his desk.  There was a vice mounted onto the corner of it and, as soon as he saw it, Eric began to struggle like an animal in a trap.  Another man held him down as his forearm was placed in the vice and was clamped down until he heard the bone snap.
   “Ahhhh ohhhh GOD,” Eric screamed as the man laughed and brought the peeler down onto the palm of his hand.  He scraped it up and down, just like a potato would be peeled, and the palm of his skin was sliced and cut.  Eric twitched as his saliva turned to foam and he blacked out.
   Water was dashed onto him and he jerked up.  “Stand up turd,” the jailer yelled at him, and he put his hand down to push himself up and jerked away at the fresh wave of pain that jarred his jaw.  After he stood up a guard took him by the hair and led him along a hallway to the left of the room.  
   First they passed by large barred doors, surely that was where food and clothes were kept.  Then the doors fell away as the torches began to be spaced with more distance between them, so that there were long patches of dark before the ponds of light.  Finally after a few minutes they arrived at the cells.  They were thick wooden doors that were seemingly still green.  The wood was as thick as his arm and very, VERY hard.  
   He was led about halfway down the hallway to a cell marked with the number 53.  He shuddered as the door was opened and he was thrown into the room.  It was pitch black and he could see nothing.  He felt along the walls and stood up, but his head came just level with the ceiling and he found that he could barely take two steps before hitting the wall.
   Oh God, he could not get enough air.  He spread his arms and legs as far as they would go, and he had to bend his legs the chamber was so short.  He began to sob and cry, he wasn't sure why he should be thinking about how to escape.  Surely he could just light the door on fire, but he could not make the link.  The room was so cramped.  He had to breathE, if only he could…breathe!
   After a few moments he rolled onto his side and began to rationalize the situation.  He could escape, there was no question there.  The problem was that, if he did, there was no way he was going to get through the city.   The men out front would know the faces of the guards on duty inside the prison, and would sound an alarm if they suspected him.  
   Maybe if I could get Gerik free than we could find a way out together.  But even as he thought that he found that there was no way he could leave his brother knights.  The city is still under siege, possibly we could sneak along the side of the mountain until we came to the first gate, then we could pass through freely because the guard wouldn’t know us.  That sounded reasonable, he could break out of his cell find Gerik and walk right out the front door.  There were only around fifteen men that he had seen in his section of corridor and he could take them himself.  He found that his enormous energy was comforting.  He could go for days, weeks without sleep just by taking from the source inside him.  He would wait tonight; wait for the first circulation of the watch.  He would escape tonight, for he would not be subject to another session of the warden’s fun and games.  He was a sky knight, he was just below royalty, and the promise of his king was a piece of land and enough livestock to fill it.  He had a happy life back in Trojia, and it did not include this prison.
   He would escape.
   He turned so that he could sit, and he analyzed his wounded arm from earlier.  It was a clean break, not a jagged one, or a fracture.  He couldn’t move his wrist at all.  It was his left wrist so he could possibly try and set it when he got back to the camp.  Just one more reason I must leave tonight, if this isn’t set than I could have a crippled hand forever.  The thought resolved him and he reached out with his mind to feel the energy of the door.
   He could feel that it was room temperature and that it was very dense, there would be no feeling through that.   He sent his mind through the room just in case there was something he could use in the rocks as an energy source, but found nothing.  He wasn’t surprised so he began to feel for the weakest point in the door.  After a moment he discovered that the he could set it on fire around the lock and then kick the lock out.  If he had just set the thing aflame he would be roasted in seconds, hopefully he wouldn’t suffer any burns this way.
  He began to make the link and then stopped.  A wave of tiredness washed over him, and he felt too tired to link himself to the source.  He curled down awkwardly on the floor and slept.
   He awoke violently to the sound of the lock clicking.  I can’t have slept that long.  He had tried to limit his sleep, but had obviously failed.   
   He jumped up and rushed to the side of the door, he would make his break now.  The door opened but no light shone through.  
   That’s odd.  He thought and just then a man crept in through the open door.  He walked over to the bedroll and squatted down on the balls of his feet.  He reached for the mat and realized that nobody was in it; he leapt to his feet just as Eric shut the door behind him.
   “Come to kill me in my sleep eh?”  He asked the man in a sneering tone.  “That’s alright though I’ve killed plenty of you swine what’s one more.”  The man just stared at him, and then held up both hands in surrender.
   “I see that I’m hopelessly outmatched, why don’t I just leave and let you find your own way out then hmm?”  The man held out his hands again and shrugged.  “I’d have no chance against someone such as yourself anyway sorcerer” he coughed as he said the last word, then looked at him expectantly.
   Eric flinched as soon as the man said it, then he linked to the man and hit him with a burst of mechanical energy which sent him flying into the wall.  As soon as he had done it though he felt something strange, like he was in a whirlpool and he couldn’t stop moving forward.  The man stepped out from the wall and abruptly the feeling was gone.
   “What you did there, yes that was…quite peculiar.”  The man shook his head to rid himself of the vertigo and then held out a hand.  Abruptly Eric felt tiny tendrils of energy seeking through him seeking a hold.  Then one that had crept into his skull stopped then all the other gravitated towards it and the feeling went away.  This all happened in about two seconds.  The man shook his head and then stared hard at him.  “Alright then let’s get moving, I don’t have time to waste.”  And he walked confidently out of the room.
   Eric followed him cautiously.  He doubted that any of the guards would just let him walk out.  They left his cell and the man turned and headed into the tunnel that led towards the front door.  He could finally get a good look at the man and he gasped.
   He was garbed head to toe in black breeches and tunic, and he had a cowl over his face.  The bottom part of his face was also covered with some sort of turban so that the only parts of his body visible were his forehead and eyes.  He had two knives one on each hip, and another in his right boot.  He seemed to glide over the ground without a sound.
   Eric motioned quickly towards the man and pointed down the tunnel where the other prisoners were being kept.  The man shook his head and a voice entered his head, no not that way.  Eric stopped and motioned again this time the man shook his head again.  The captain is questioning the other prisoners you cannot save your friend.  Eric froze when he heard the words, Gerik wouldn’t have wanted him to risk himself like this especially if it increased his chance of being captured.  Eric reluctantly nodded and fell in step behind the mysterious man.
   They entered the tunnel and preceded through the halls, it should only take about three or four minutes to reach the entrance hall.  That is if they didn’t encounter anyone along the way.  The man stopped in front of him and held up a hand.  Then he flattened Eric against the wall and pressed a hand into his mouth.  Just then a guard rounded the corner up ahead and the man stepped out from the shadows.
  “What the bloody…” was all the man got out before a knife caught him in the throat.  He made an odd mewing sound just as the man reached him and put another knife under his ribs which silenced the still-twitching man.  
   The man motioned again for Eric to follow and they made their way to the entrance hall.  Just before they entered the doorway he motioned for Eric to stop.  Then he looked at him and he heard the voice again in his mind.  I will kill these men up ahead, count to twenty and then come in.  Then he left Eric alone.  
   Eric began counting and when he reached twenty he stepped into the room.  Every man was dead and the man stood confidently in the center of the room and exited through the door.  Eric followed him into the streets, the man had disappeared seemingly into thin air.  He looked around and he heard a chuckle in his head and then a voice.  Over here blind one in the shadow of that house and along with the words came a thought and he instantly saw the man crouched in the darkness motioning towards him to join him.
   Eric ran to join the man just as he walked into the open and towards the right side gate.  Eric followed him and saw him sprint towards the guards and then the man disappeared.  He reappeared twice while knocking the guards cold and then ran inside and opened the gate about four feet so that he could crawl under it.  Eric dropped to his knees and went through the opening and saw the man waiting at the other side.  
   The man motioned to him and then headed off towards the western city gate.   They made their way through the city and only encountered a few patrolling guards, then changed their direction to a spot on the wall that was covered in ivy.  The man walked to the ivy and felt around it for a moment until he twisted his wrist and the door opened into a small doorway just large enough for two men to stand abreast in.  He looked through the door way but there was nothing but blackness.  The man dove into the shadows and, after a moment, he saw the night about twenty yards on the other side and the man was standing on the other side motioning towards him.
  Eric ran through the tunnel and into the nighttime breeze on the other side.  The man sprinted towards the high grass and Eric followed him.  When they had both reached the grass they crouched and moved as fast as possible to the forest of thick trees about one hundred yards away.
   They reached the trees at a run.  He and the stranger walked for about thirty minutes until they came to a small cottage designed to be hidden in a grove of pine.  The man walked inside and began to strike flint onto a prepared bundle of wood and kindling.  Eric stepped up to him and made a quick link to the sticks.  He sent a surge of heat energy through the link and the wood burst into flame.
   The man stood up and stared at him for a long time, and then he lowered his cowl to reveal dirty blond hair in a ponytail, and then undid the turban and revealed the face of the prince of Vervadia now grinning at him in the light of the fire.  
   “Well that was exciting” the prince said as he looked into Eric’s stunned face.


































 
Chapter 11: a proposition


   Eric stood and looked nervously at the prince, he had been expecting a Trojian spy and he wasn’t sure how to handle the situation.  He had killed countless Vervians just the day before and the prince now stood before him smiling.  “I have someone for you to meet.”  He turned and called out into the room adjacent to this one for someone to wake up and after a moment there emerged a woman who looked to be about one or two years older than he was.  The woman looked questioningly at the prince, and when he motioned towards Eric her eyes widened for a moment.  The prince smiled at her as she mouthed the words…is it him??  The prince nodded and she turned and gave Eric a cold hard gaze.
   “What name do you be called?”  She asked in a rough accent that was distinctly recognized Frandian.
   “Errr…well I’m Eric.”  He stuttered and when she was silent he asked “And what are you called?”
   “I am called Adelia of the Winged Tribe.  My partner is regrettably not here, but I am sure she would be happy to meet you also.”  She smiled at him.  Adelia was about five foot eight inches, with a lustrous build.  She was not slight or slender, but neither was she large.  Her hair was very blond and her face very white, she was not tan like the prince but had rosy cheeks the likes of which he had never seen before.  Her face was slightly rounded and she had a soft jawline, and the way she walked suggested that she was incredibly fit and used to handling a weapon.
   “Well it’s…very nice to meet you Adelia.  Where have you traveled from?”  She put a finger on his lips and said.
   “Shush now, we have much to speak about and all will be revealed soon.  I believe that Lief would like to speak with us.”  The prince smiled as Adelia said his name and Eric looked at him expectantly.
   “Why don’t we all have a seat and I’ll make some tea okay?  We can talk while the tea is brewing.”  Eric nodded and sat down on one of the cots in the room and crossed his hands over his knees in calm patience.  Adelia immediately sat down on the floor out of the way and lay on her crossed ankles with a sigh, muttering about crazy customs.  “There we go now in just a moment I’ll be with you, oh I forgot my manners.  Eric do you prefer peppermint or ginger tea?”  Eric looked at him and shrugged and the prince laughed.  “Peppermint it is then, I always loved peppermint myself.”
   Once the pot was settled over the fire he came and took a seat on a bench with his back to the flame and looked expectantly at Adelia.  He seemed to be waiting.
   “Oh no no no Vervian prince I am still confused myself, you must be the one to explain this to him I know not the correct words to explain well enough.”  She looked at him and locked her jaw waiting for the prince to stop his chuckling and get on with what he had to say.
   After a moment the prince collected himself and then solemnly looked at Eric.  When he spoke it was in a calm level voice that was completely emotionless.  “Eric the reason that Adelia and I have gathered you here is because of the power you have inside of you.”  He made a sweeping gesture to Adelia and himself.  “Both myself and the lady have powers eerily similar to yours.”  Eric looked at him questioningly as the prince continued.  “Adelia is the former princess of Frandia; even though she may not look it she is very royal.”  He chuckled as she bared her teeth at him, and then continued “As you know I am the current prince of Vervadia, even though I sometimes prefer the life of a beggar.  We both possess a certain amount of power inside of us, similar yet different to your own.  Myself I have distinct power over the land itself, its subtle changes are clear to me and I am one with the earth.”  He smiled “it is how I became invisible to your eyes; I am completely linked to this earth and am allowed exceptions to the rules that govern all our actions.  I can blend in with any environment; erase all trace of my existence, walk on water, swim in the air.” He said all of this with a completely apathetic face as he continued.
   “The princess has powers over the spiritual realms, she dream walks, communes with spirits, and calls upon them for aid.  She can also do certain things such as scrying, and communicating with the mind telepathically.”  Adelia lowered her gaze and spoke up.
   “I am what your kind calls a sorcerer.”  She said with disgust and stared back to the floor.
   “Now now Adelia you know just as much as I do that you are completely unique.  You are not a sorcerer you are THE sorcerer.  Being the descendant of Frand himself means you are the only one with access to this power and it owns you.”  His eyes shone for a moment “We all must accept our path or it will mean disaster for all of us.”  
   They both looked at Eric and he slowly spoke, chewing every word.  “So why am I here then?”  He asked.  Lief looked at him and exhaled slowly before he spoke.
   “Because I believe that you are the descendant of one of the six children of Mercola as well.  That you are a child of the land like I and Adelia.”  He smiled at Eric comfortingly.  “Don’t worry neither of us knew at first either, we just knew something was different.  I researched, and she dreamed and the answer came to us both.  We both found the secret of our ancestry and have been changed ever since.”  
   He grunted and stood up to pour the tea into three separate cups.  He added two spoons of honey to each of them then returned to his seat after handing each of them a cup.  He settled himself into his seat as Adelia spoke.  
   “I dream dreams of a darkness creeping over the land, and in every one I see the land fall unchecked by anyone.”  She looked omnisciently into his eyes.  “But in one dream I see hope.  In one dream I see the descendants of the kingdoms gathered together in the final defense of the land, and they are tied to the land and the darkness himself appears in the form of a great beast and smites them all.”  Eric flinched and she continued.  “But where there are ashes there was fire and Mercola will reveal his son and he shall rise from the ashes of the land and destroy the beast, and the beast will leave no ashes.  The beast will have no rebirth, no reincarnation, and no existence.  The son of Mercola will terminate his existence from this world forever and he will exist in the nothingness between.”  
   Eric sat stunned at the words, he knew they were true.  The words struck him to his core and he saw in his mind’s eye the plains, and the battle that would take place.  He saw armies that reached to the horizon, and the skies were filled with knights and even the pegii themselves rode in the air.  He saw the cavalry of Vervadia, the archers of Mercia, the great war machines of Rema, the barbarians of Frandia, and the shield-wall of Trojia and thought that none could stand against a land united until he saw the enemy itself.  
   Great beast stalked, and living shadows flew in the skies.  The bodies of people stolen and bent to the will of the Beast fell in step with the demons that were more beast than human.  The dead left their graves and shambled along the lines, their piercing cries joining that of the beasts.  Then he saw the Beast, it was a thing of shadow and blood.  It flew in the sky, and it walked on the earth and its face smote the ground before it.  The lifeblood of the Beast tore at time and space, and all around it was a void of darkness.  It stared at him and motioned with its hand a small growl building in its throat as it laughed at its prey.
   Eric shook his head the vision had passed and the faces of Lief and Adelia were still looking at him.  No time had passed.
   “So what I propose is that we follow the direction of our lady’s dream and assemble the descendants of the six kingdoms.  Only then will we stand a chance against the Beast in the final battle.”  Eric looked at him and nodded his head.
   “Alright Lief, it sounds a bit fantastical but I agree to follow you to the end.  How do you propose we go about this ‘gathering?’”  
   The prince smiling his largest grin yet and began to tell him of a plan concocted by an old man in Thumbria, and Eric sat down comfortably because he knew better than to interrupt.














































Chapter 12: a long, but not quite lonely, road


   “But we could do it in…maybe half the time if we split up.  We could meet here in two months and go from there?”  Lief shook his head.
   “No we can’t do that.  We’d have no way of knowing what was going on, plus alone we’d have no way to keep watch at night.  We’d have to sleep in trees, or with no campfire and that could really affect our ability in the field.”  He shrugged at Eric and grinned.  “And maybe I’d enjoy the company.  Life on the road goes by much faster when there are people who’ve got your back.”  
   Eric had to laugh at this, Adelia would’ve laughed too he guessed but she had fallen asleep about an hour before.  Her long golden hair masked her face.  She had begun snoring a few minutes ago, and she let out a loud grunt when Eric laughed.  
   “I think she’s got the right idea, sleep is starting to sound quite seducing at the moment.”  Lief chuckled as he said it, his eyes were dark around the sockets and he looked like he was squinting when he looked at something.  That sparkle never left his eyes for a moment however.   “I think we’ve hammered down the major points in our little excursion, a little sleep may just be the thing we need.”  He looked at Eric as if he was making a suggestion, and surely he was.  The way the man worded his words he made every suggestion laced with just enough practical logic that one would only make a fool out of himself to argue.
   “I agree, I feel like I’ve…” He grinned at the other man.  “I feel like I’ve escaped from prison.”  Lief burst into a laugh that came from his diaphragm.  He laughed so long that Eric began to laugh with him.  The laugh built until Eric thought he would burst.  All they did was laugh at each other laughing, it seemed as if their bodies were losing their rebound because after five minutes when they finally calmed down, wiping the tears from their eyes, they both simultaneously collapsed on their respective cots and fell asleep.
   Eric woke up a little after dawn to the sound of footsteps by his head.  He cracked his eye open just enough to see Adelia slip out of the house with a change of clothes and a small jar of goat milk soap.  He brought himself to a sitting position and looked around the room.
   Lief lay in the other cot across the room with his back towards him.  The sun was barely shining through the window on neatly swept floor of the house.  He yawned and ran his hand through his hair and frowned as it got caught in the tangles.  He felt dirt covering his scalp and the rest of him was covered in filth.  The rest of him wasn’t much better, sweat mixed with dirt covered his arms and legs and he was sure that his face wasn’t any better.  The last time he had bathed was when he was at the inn near Tora.  He wondered if there was a stream near the house and remembered Adelia and her soap.  She must’ve had the same idea.  
   He decided to venture outside the house and try and find the stream, his half-awake mind lumbered through the grass until he found a path that led down into a valley.  He followed it for a few minutes and came through the brambles into a pool in the stream.  He gazed at it as his mouth watered, licked his lips he was also ravenously thirsty.  He stumbled down the path and fell to his knees at the edge of the pool, as he brought his head out of the water he sensed another presence near him.  He turned as he heard a chuckle off to his left.
   “And you call me a barbarian.”  This was followed by a snort from the woman as she laughed at him.  He turned his head to offer a retort, but when he did he saw that the woman was topless.
  “Hey I didn’t…Oh God!!” He turned his entire body away from her and covered his eyes with his hands.  It wasn’t as if he had never seen a pair of breasts before.  He had had his stabs at romance in the past, and often because of his looks and battle prowess he succeeded in bedding a few women, but he had not expected this.  
   “Hahaha, you seem afraid of me crafter.”  He could tell she was smiling.  His cheeks were burning, but he didn’t turn.   “Have I found a new weapon to use against men of this land?  Are you all frightened of my body?  I feel offended, am I not worthy to look upon?”  Now she laughed aloud.  She knew well the customs of this land and she delighted in tormenting him.  He began to work his way back towards the house reaching out with his hand for the bushes.  “Stop, you may look upon me I am covered.  I am no longer unworthy to be seen by you.”  She mocked him and then began to gather her things.  In no way whatsoever had she been unattractive, in fact he doubted he had seen a woman more luscious than this one, his eyes could’ve poured over her all day.  He slowly turned until he could see her.  She was covered in a simple shift, she was still barely decent and he didn’t look directly at her but he could have a conversation with her.
   “I’m sorry I was very tired I didn’t know you were bathing.  I was wondering if I could…maybe borrow some of your soap?”  
   She looked at him seriously for a moment then replied with a flat “no.”  He wasn’t sure what to say so he just nodded his head and she laughed.  “I will not let you borrow this one, but I will give you the other soap.”  He brought his head up than grinned at the woman.  “I would not feel right using it after you have, I have no wish for the smells of men to be on me.”  The grin left his face and he looked at Adelia warily.  She reached down into her bag and removed something wrapped in a cloth.  She tossed him the bundle, picked up her bag, and began to walk back to the house.  He stood speechless then, after a moment, shouted a thanks to her.  She waved a hand, shrugging him off, and continued on the trail.
   He bent down and after he had washed he also proceeded towards the house.  When he got close he smelled bacon cooking and that quickened his step.  He walked through the door, his mouth watering, and saw that Lief and Adelia had already begun eating their plates.  A third plate sat on the table and he brought a chair to the table to join them.
   As he ate he sensed something in the air between them, he looked up and they both were eating their food.  Lief raised his head to look at him, his lips pursed like he was restraining a smile, and then wholeheartedly returned to his food.  Eric raised his eyebrow and followed suit.  
   After a minute or so Lief looked up from his food and, with a barely restrained smile, asked “So Eric did you have a nice wash?”  Eric raised his head and looked at the man with his mouth still full of food.  His eyes widened and the other man burst into laughter.  Eric blushed and ignored him, hoping that he could hide his blush.  Adelia had kept silent the whole time, and continued to do so but her body jerked once with laughter and she began to choke on a piece of the meat.
   Immediately both men turned at this and tried to assist her.  After a moment when she had ceased coughing they returned to their food.
   There was silence for a moment until Lief whispered, to Eric’s horror and Adelia’s irritance; “Probably not the first time she choked on a piece of meat.”  
   It would be a long, but thankfully not lonely, road.




























Chapter 13:  A Productive Afternoon
   
   Later that day Lief decided to go hunting, something that was extremely foreign to Eric.  Lief had two bows, along with matching quivers, arm guards, and leather covers for the fingers, stored in the basement of the small cottage.  He removed them from their leather sleeves and inspected the wood for any weakness.  Satisfied that they were adequate he passed one of the hunting sets to Eric.  Adelia had decided not to come with them, she would stay at the house and gather the water and other supplies that would be needed to clean whatever animals were hunted that day.  
   Out in the yard Lief was teaching Eric how to shoot a bow, and Eric found that this was a horribly difficult task.  No matter what he did something always was wrong when he released the string and it ended up soaring past the target.
   “It’s the way you’re drawing it, don’t bend your back backwards like that.  Draw using your shoulder blades, straighten your back, and then just shoot the target.”  It was as simple as that he had always said.  Some people tried to teach men to line up the arrow and the target with their eyes, but Lief had learned that the easiest and most efficient way of firing accurately, was to focus your eye on the target not the arrow.  Then simply shoot it, make the bow part of the body and line your entire body up with the target.  Eric still couldn’t get the hang of it though, he could do things with a lance that some people thought were impossible, but he could not put an arrow into the stump from twenty yards away.
   Well let’s rephrase that...He could not put an arrow into the stump from twenty yards away with a BOW.
   After about an hour Lief told him.  “Alright just watch me do it, see how before I shoot I look at the target.  Then, without taking my eyes off of it, I raise the bow, draw the arrow, and release.  Easy as pie.” Eric watched as he stared down the stump, then in one exaggerated movement he raised the bow, drew and fired in less than two seconds.  Eric swore as the arrow struck dead center of the stump just like the five others he had put there.
   “That’s it, I could be spending this time working with my crafting.  I can hunt just as well as you do with a rock.”  And to prove his point he picked up a rock from the ground, linked it with the energy inside him, and sent it flying forward with a burst of energy in mechanical form.  The rock flew, faster than any arrow, and with a loud “thunk” sunk a foot into the stump.
   Lief just shrugged and said, “So be it, you’d never make even a mediocre archer anyway and there’s no pretending otherwise.”  
   They returned to the cottage, Eric fuming, so that he could return his archery packet to the cellar when he heard a snicker from Adelia.  She had apparently been watching them from the house and had a hidden grin on her face.  “What’s so funny?”  Eric asked, his tone was full of irritance.
   “Nothing really, you make a bad stick shooter is all.”  Then she gave him a dazzling smile and returned to work.  
   He ignored her and went outside to gather a handful of small pebbles no larger than his fingernail, and put them in a satchel carried around his waist.
   “Ready to go?”  Lief asked, and he nodded.  “Alright follow me, we’re going to follow this trail up and over this ridge and then into the plain.  It’s about twenty miles across so there should be a couple herds of deer if we look closely.”  Eric nodded at him, then held out a hand for him to lead the way.
   Lief strung his bow and walked a few paces ahead of him with an arrow already nocked.  He had already proven that he could nock, draw, and fire in less than three seconds but maybe it was more of a comfort to keep it drawn.   In addition he carried two knives at his belt, and more hidden away in various pieces of his clothing.  Eric’s small supply of pebbles seemed almost pathetic compared to this man’s assortment but they both knew that he was more than capable with what he had.
   They followed the trail through thick woodlands, and then up a rather steep hill.  In no less than fifteen minutes they came over the hill and into the open view of the plains.  The woodland broke off and relented to grass that was almost as high as his chest, he could smell the wheat in full bloom and feel the wind stronger than he’d ever felt.  They soaked it up for a moment then Lief gestured to him and whispered.  “You see where the trees meet the plains?  That’s where we’re most likely to see a herd.  Now our main target is deer, most anything else is too small, and bison would be too large for us to preserve fast enough.  Although if you see a large jack, or turkey don’t hesitate to take it.”  Eric nodded at him and then they spread out, Lief went about two hundred yards into the plains and they walked parallel to each other along the edge of the woodland.
   After thirty minutes of traversing through the long grass he literally stumbled onto a small herd of antelope, which immediately bounded off towards the open plain.  He shouted to Lief and he saw the man dash until he was maybe fifteen yards from the herd, then he simply…disappeared.  One moment there he was sprinting through the grass and then a second later he simply wasn’t.  Eric was dumbfounded, and watched in awe as the herd ran past the spot he disappeared and then a flash, and one of the antelopes stumbled and began to fall behind the group.  It could barely move faster than a walk and he watched as Lief sprinted towards the animal, fast as an arrow and slit its throat with one of his hunting knives.
   He motioned to Eric and when he arrived he saw that the man’s hands were covered in blood.  “Alright after we gut it it should weigh about thirty pounds less.”  He looked at Eric and he realized that this was his queue and knelt down next to the animal and put a smooth cut from the bottom of its rib cage, all the way to the anus.  He opened it up and the smell of meat and dung hit him like a wave.  Something about it was pleasing to him, he took his hands and gathered the intestines into a ball and removed them from the cavity of the animal.  He placed them on the ground a few feet away and proceeded to remove everything that had to do with digesting food from the animal into the neat pile on the ground.  Then he and Lief walked to a stream about two hundred yards away and washed their hands before returning to drag the animal back to the cottage.
   They began to drag it, but Eric had a burst of intuition and found a long, thick branch and put it through two slits he made in the thin membrane of the back legs.  This made dragging it much easier and they doubled their rate until they reached the cottage after about half an hour.
   Adelia came out of the house and told them to follow her over to a tree that had an iron hook hanging from it, and three basins full of salt water underneath it.  He had seen similar equipment at the butchery in camp so he knew the general idea.  They hung the animal by its jaw and spent the rest of the day cleaning the kill.  One of the basins they took inside the house and the others they took into the storage cellar where they would be dried for the journey ahead of them.  
   They had a nice dinner of greens, potatoes, and antelope that night and, he must admit, he didn’t know if he had ever eaten better.




































Chapter 14: The Message


   They spent the next few days following a routine that consisted of mostly smoking the meat from the antelope, gathering water to fill their canteens, making regular trips to Tora for provisions, and most importantly waiting.
   They didn’t know what they were waiting for, whether a man, or a dream, but Lief said they would receive instruction of some kind once his message reached his acquaintance.  He never said anything more of this mysterious man other than that he could be trusted when everyone else couldn’t.  That he took no sides, and made no alliances, other than with the land itself.  He was a druid of sorts Eric assumed, someone close to the land and who possibly even worshipped the old gods.  
   Druids believed that the gods shaped this land, they believed in the six stewards and the almighty.  The almighty came first and he became sorrowful at his loneliness, the almighty is the world itself.  You could say the spirit of the world, he had a very vague idea but he knew the six stewards were the children of the almighty, or at least called that.  He made them so that he wouldn’t be lonely or something like that, whatever they believed he suspected that this man Lief spoke of was one of the last of a dying breed.
   So they waited, and after a week they waited longer.  At the end of the second week as they were seated at their evening meal they heard a small sound at the window, a light tapping.  Adelia went over to examine the window and gasped, she ran outside and returned with a dove perched on her hand.  Around its leg was tied a small piece of paper maybe ten inches by ten inches folded into a small bundle.  Lief removed the paper and the bird quickly flew out the open door.
   “Ahh dammit, I forgot about the door.”  Adelia cursed as Lief stared at the letter.  A thoughtful look came over his face as he passed the paper around.  It said: 
   May each evening throughout my Everett’s always tire tediously hopeful underneath my bragging ragged insisting affection.  
   I hope this old man’s rambling doesn’t confuse you, I’ve been living more in the past lately than the present.  Our visit with the Trojian’s has been on my mind more than anything else.
   Give the bird woman my love
   Eric and Adelia both looked at it dumbfounded.  “Alright what’s this about?”  Eric demanded from him.  Lief took the paper from him and read through the message again briefly, then with a look of decision on his face he took a piece of charcoal from the fireplace and ran it over the back of the paper furiously.  Before either of them could stop him Eric began to see the words appearing and stared stunned as the other man finished his work.
   After twenty seconds Lief held the paper out for them to see.  It said: 
   Gretta’s Medicinal Herbs near the western gate.
   They were extremely confused now, Adelia spoke first.  “What is that old man speaking of, has his brains finally curdled into mush like the rest of him?”  Lief laughed aloud as he spoke.
   “That man will still be scheming fifty years after he’s in the ground, no look at the beginning letter of each word in the first part of the message.”  She did and after a moment a smile broke out on her face.
   “Meet me at Thumbria, what about the rest?”  Lief showed her the back of the paper rubbed over with charcoal. 
   “This was the way we communicated across the hallway when we were imprisoned in a Trojian prison, we would eat the paper after we read the message.”  He smiled smugly, not seeming boastful at all at their ingeniousness.
  “What’s that about a bird lady?”  Eric asked.  They both looked at him, then glanced at each other with a look that suggested they knew something he didn’t.  Adelia nodded and Lief turned to him.
   “Well the bird lady is Adelia.  She’s a pegii rider, he always called the thing a pigeon.  I’m surprised he didn’t send the message with her, but maybe she’s still recuperating after what happened at Flanning.”  Adelia nodded in agreement with what Lief said.  “But you’ll get to meet her when we get to Thumbria I’m fairly certain she’ll be there waiting for us.”
   Eric looked stunned, “But I thought they were all killed when Gunthred ended the conquest in Fiorra?”  Adelia shook her head slowly.
   “When their riders were killed in battle the pegii have always known that there were safe havens spread throughout Fiorra for them to seek.  But when the last haven was destroyed they sensed an old haven in Frandia that had been placed there at the beginning of our treaty.  The pegii flew there and found Frandian monks willing to care for them.”  Eric seemed stunned as she continued.  “Those pegii could never choose again, but the young colts and fillies that were born chose Frandian warriors as new riders.  A new legacy of Frandian knights has started and is under command of my nest mate Traven, there are not so many riders as there are your sky knights though, we have maybe fifty riders able to fight and more in the next year or two.”  Lief laughed
   “Well now the riders are more of a match, seeing as half of the sky knights were wiped out in the battle at Tora.”  He smiled and Adelia laughed aloud.  Eric was silent and Lief noticed before Adelia luckily and suggested that it was getting late.
   “In the morning we’ll gather our supplies and set out, hopefully before midday.”  Adelia nodded and Eric followed suit and they cleared the table before crawling into their various cots to sleep before the beginning of a long journey that would begin tomorrow.
Chapter 15: The Touch of Madness


   A shriek pierced through the peaceful dusk in the town of Delta.  The obvious pain behind it evident by the cries, and the people of Delta could feel it deep in their bones.
