A baker stopped rolling dough and screamed into his hands cupped around his face.  Children playing in the streets just before their time for bed stopped and fell to the ground writhing.  A midwife preparing to deliver a baby fell to her knees and watched as the mothers screams became so shrill that the woman collapsed, later that day the baby would be born dead and more tears would be shed.
   Deep in the catacombs beneath the shrine of the Y’marr the shriek originated, sending waves of energy echoing through the bowels below the shrine.  A mold of a man made out of stone lay on a slab of rock.  The details were so rich one would have thought that the man was turned to stone through sorcery.  One could say he was partially right.
   U’mar burst through the rock with a roar and stood in the rubble breathing deeply.  He let himself breathe deeper and deeper until his heart slowed to a more reasonable pace and he surveyed his surroundings.
   The room was bare, and the only light showing was the glowstones, they cast their eerie red light on the pedestal U’mar stood upon.  A raised platform stood in the center of the room with ten steps leading up to it.  It stood tall above a room that contained around thirty chairs.  A shattered pulpit had once stood in front of his prison, but now it was twenty feet away shattered to splinters on the ground.
   Three men burst into the room and he looked at them curiously, they wore blood-red robes and carried long thin knives in the sleeves of the robes.  When they saw him they froze and stared blankly up at him in awe.  He chuckled and took a step forward, weariness shot through him and he thought he might trip, but they couldn’t see any weakness so he shrugged it off, he would demand a sacrifice at the proper time, more important matters needed to be discussed.
  He stood menacing at six foot five, lean but not slim.  His skin was pale white, and his eyes as red as spilt blood.  He wore nothing, but the shadows clung to him and he walked down the steps slowly, entertained by the groveling priests.  He walked to them and placed a hand on one of their heads, the man froze and he could feel the sweet nectar that flowed through his veins, more tempting than anything else in the world.  Though he had meant to hold off  he could feel the saliva building in his mouth and tensed himself, when the man looked up U’mar could see the terror in his face as he brutally threw him into one of the chairs ten feet away. 
   The man landed and lay on his back groaning as U’mar bounded through the air and drew the knife from the man’s sleeves.  The man was speechless as U’mar grinned, the feeling was similar to the anticipation before bedding a woman, and was almost as sweet as the act itself.  Almost.
   He pinned the man down with his knees, just like bedding a woman foreplay was essential to productively spending an evening.  He sent pain through the man’s limbs and felt him tense up, U’mar shuddered at the thought of the blood now richer than before, but more concentration was needed.  He stabbed the man through his robes and pierced the liver, he twisted the thin blade sharply and his hairs stood on end as the man screamed.  The knives were made for this, when a sacrifice was offered the more pain the subject was in beforehand the more potent the offering.
   He continued torturing the man, healing him right before he died to prolong the suffering.  He destroyed everything until the man’s eyes glazed over and he could no longer bring him back to consciousness.  Then he ripped his rib cage wide open and cut a major artery with his knife.  He put the Artery to his mouth and sucked until he felt like he would burst.  For about two minutes he pleased himself with the man, when he could suck no more he turned to the other two men standing at the door still.  They had watched him calmly, they were of his cult so they were used to watching blood be spilt in such a manner.  Others had joined them, now they numbered fifteen.
   He walked over to them reveling in their fear, he turned to the high priest and asked.  “You are leader of this coven?”  The man nodded quickly and prostrated himself before U’mar mumbling his reverence.  Goddamned priests, what use was pointless worship, it sickened him.  
   He reached out his hand and the high priest was lifted into the air.  He began whimpering until his mouth shot open, his head was thrown back, and his neck was broken.  U’mar let him fall to the floor and walked over to one of the journeymen acolytes, the one who was smiling as he watched the priest die.  “You” U’mar said to the man who bowed deeply and then looked him straight in the eyes.  This one could prove useful.  “What’s your name child?” 
   “My name is Bledhreft my lord.”  The man said, and showed him his wrist.  It bore five scars the size of a fingernail all burned on, yess very useful this one will be, a full bloodletter.
   “Rift in the blood, interesting are you royalty my servant?”  The man shook his head.
   “No my lord, I was given that name because I am also a bishop in the service of Vervadia.  I use my position to my advantage.”  The man smiled, and U’mar felt his mouth twitch into some sort of a smile then said to the man.
   “Tell me child, can you manage things around here while I’m away?”  The man’s eyebrows raised, and then he nodded slowly.  “Good, because I have some business to attend to.”


































Chapter 16: A Necessary Distraction


   “Alright let’s see what we have here.”  Lief had just returned from scouting the road ahead and all was clear.  “Thirty arrows, two hunting bows, three weeks of preserved food, weapons, three sets of clothes a piece, five bars of soap, three pairs of extra shoes, canvas for sleeping, and two ponies to carry it all.  I think we’re ready to go.”  Adelia grinned at him.
   “If your mouth would stop flapping then we could really leave, I swear you people do not think right.”  Adelia had mock-seriousness in her voice that made Eric grin.
   “Well then, let’s stop talking and get going.”  With that they got the ponies moving and they were off.
   They had decided to take the quickest way possible and follow the eastern coast.  If the weather held, and no obstacles stood in their way, they should make it to Thumbria in less than two weeks.  They were simply going to follow the coastal road up through the city of Delta and then continue on to Thumbria.
   The road was eventless and they made it two days without any sign of trouble ahead.
   But that changed.
   They came over a ridge and saw a small town ahead that was besieged by a squad of bandits.  The town seemed to be having trouble keeping the bandits out and this seemed to be the breaking point of the skirmish.  The bandits poured into the city through the broken barricades and proceeded to battle the lowly trained men-at-arms inside the town.
   “I’ll be damned, that doesn’t look very good does it?”  Eric spoke as if he was talking to himself but Adelia answered anyway.
   “You are good at pointing out the obvious, we should be moving on though we’ve been staring long enough, and I have not the stomach to watch anymore.”  The two men reluctantly nodded, but as the first sounds of the women in the town being raped they abruptly changed their minds.  Adelia protested, but there was no way she could stop them so she went with them drawing her sword and running to catch up.
   They were wordless, three simple travelers with no names and they entered the blood and chaos of the town without speaking a word.  Two men who had their pants around their ankles taking turns at one of the barmaids, turned around when they heard them and hastily began to pull up their pants.
   In a flash both man fell, one stopped breathing when Eric shattered his windpipe with a quick link, and the other fell suddenly with one of Lief’s knives protruding from his neck.  They fell to the ground writhing as they approached the barmaid.  She was whimpering and was hastily redoing her blouse from where they had torn it off, luckily they had left her skirt on.  
   They continued through the town killing small groups of bandits, everywhere was ransacked.  In the center of the town a group of thirty men were trying to hold off the twenty-five remaining bandits.  Although they were outnumbered, the bandits far outmatched the men of the town in skill and cruelty.
   Eric, Lief, and Adelia ran towards the right side of the bandits and killed five men within the first twenty seconds.  
   Eric turned his head as blood spat towards his face from the man he had just killed, he had plunged a knife into his temple and the area seemed more full of blood then others, because it kept gushing.  He turned and ducked underneath an axe swung by anoher bandit, he stepped forward and broke the man’s arm by locking the joint with one arm, and a swift palm to the locked elbow.  The man yelled and Eric punched him in the windpipe staunching his protest mid-breath.  Then moved to the next man, and then the next, and the next until they had punched through to the men on the other side of the bandits.  They looked confused and wondered how they could be winning when they were so outmatched.  There were now only ten bandits and twenty-three men-at-arms.  The bandits made to run away towards the gate of the town, Eric, Adelia, and Lief were the only ones who could outrun them however and they faced down nine of the bandits and drug the last one to the men of the village.  They stared in awe at the three of them, none knowing what to say.
   After about ten seconds the three simply turned and began to walk away back the way they had come, nobody stopped them, until they heard a shout from the barmaid they had rescued from before.
   “Hey you three, stop you, we must give you something.”  They stopped and the woman ran to them.  Panting she made to hand them a sack of food but Lief held a hand out stopping her.  
   “My lady I would not take food from you now, I’m sure that others could use it much more than us.”  He gently pushed the woman’s hands to her chest, as Eric and Adelia nodded in agreement.  “It would be an offense to my conscience, and you’ve suffered a terrible ordeal.  You must accept our help for free and let us be on our way.”  Tears brewed in the woman’s eyes as she hugged the sack to her chest.
   “You three fight like no one I’ve ever seen, even the blademasters that travel through these parts would have trouble if faced with you.”  Then she looked down at her shoes.  “I…I don’t know how I’m going to live now, my father treats me horribly and the only reason he hasn’t made me leave his home is because my purity was worth a high brideprice.  In these parts that’s the only thing a woman’s worth.  My pa will surely leave me on the streets.”  She began to weep and Lief put a hand on her shoulder, and looked at her with his startlingly caring eyes.  
   “My lady I want to tell you something, I want you to take this money.  No no, you must take it.  I also want you to take this note, It says that I am giving you a position working in the palace of Tora as a maid.  The money isn’t great but you will live in the castle.  Ask around and you should be able to speak with the advisor, tell him Prince Lief sent you.  He’ll understand.”  The woman stared awestruck at the man, and then leapt into his arms in a great embrace.  After a few moments she dried her tears and nodded at them.  Without a word the three left the town the same way they had entered it.
   They travelled out of the town and found their horses where they were tied before and continued on the road.  Several times Eric had tried to say something, but the words seemed to catch in his throat and he would turn away.
   He had been born taught that all royalty were cruel and harsh, and cared only for themselves.  Yet here in front of him stood, not only a kind prince, but the most caring man he had ever met in his entire life.  He was at a loss for words.
Chapter 17: A Prospective Future


                                         FIVE YEARS EARLIER
   Ignis’s breath came ragged as he crashed through the thick brush surrounding his village.  It was a small village in the south at the foot of the mountain range separating Mercia and Trojia.  He had been hunting and come across a boar, it was still chasing him and he plunged straight through the lightly dispersed crowd.  He glanced over his shoulder briefly and noticed that it had stopped at the edge of the clearing.  He stopped and faced towards it, then turned back to the crowd to see the faces of people he’d known his whole life.  There were perhaps one-hundred people living in the area and he knew everyone.  
   Torvus the blacksmith, working in his yards a moment before, bellowed at him from across the yard.  “Boy you been gettin’ yoself inta trouble again you air.  Ya gotta learn ta shoot that thang through the forest.  Don’t matter a bit if you can shoot a hundred yards in a field if ya cain’t shoot in the wood.”
   “I try meister Torvus, I’ve been practicing and I’m gettin’ better.  I can’t track though, and whatever I’m hunting always spots me afore I can get close.”  Ignis pleaded.  The older man grunted, then replied.
   “Boy you’ve gone as f’r as ya can teach yourself.  You’re thirteen summers now, get outta this town.  Go join the rebellion.” His voice got real quiet, and he spoke hurredly.  “boy…ye got te go to vren, down south it ain’t even fifty miles.  I know a man there,  real fine he is and he’s a recruiter for the Du Vron Dezdent as well.  They need archers bad now, they’ve been promising twelve silver a week!!
   Ignis whistled.  “Do they really do that sir Torvus?  That’s more money than anybody here makes.  I could buy property after serving for a year.”  The boy was incredulous, but he had the idea locked in his eye now.
   That boy is to goddamn talented for me to let his talent get broken like another farmer, if his old man had a say in it he’d be old and decrepit like him.  Torvus just patted the boy on the back.  “You do what you want boy.  I’ve seen that skill you have with that bow, you’d be puttin’ anyone in the region  to blazin shame.  You go boy, earn your family some ‘a that honor’n prestige that befits ya.  You’ll do well too, don’t let anyone tell ya ye won’t.  Ye won’t get an income though till ye’ve finished yer trainin’.  Two or three years and you’ll be shootin those bastard Trojians like the best of ‘em.”  Ignis had a glazed over look in his eyes, but he snapped out of it when the patted him on the back again.  He had been hinting at this for a little while.  It was hard to think of leaving home, but money like that…hell money like that could cover the losses they took ten times over, and still leave leftover.
   He walked slowly back to his house, his mind was all fizzy, like his pappa’s beer.  Fizzy and new thoughts kept a bubbling in.  Thoughts about how their fields would be the greatest in town, and how they would have fifty head of cattle, and his family would be livin’ with pride in the outskirt of town.  They would have a house with two levels, he would build it with his pap.  On the bottom would be their kitchen and dining area, one room o’course, and upstairs he would build separate rooms fer him and his sis.  His pap and mammy would share o’course there twas no way of splittin’ them up.
   He arrived at his house with a start, he hadn’t realized he had walked that long.  He’d been away from the house for two days, longer than any time before, and it was past dark now so he crept into the house real quiet like he’d been taught.  This kinda sneakin’ only worked inside buildings and on hard ground.  He wasn’t good enough ta sneak in the grass.  He crept to his cot in the corner of the room and curled up.  He reached under the bed for his blanket and pulled it around him.  I’ll tell pap in the mornin’ I will.  I’ll say it all man-like.  Like this, “Pap I’ve decided to go join the Du Vron Dezdent as an archer.  I’ll be gone fer awhile yet, seeing as I have to be apprenticed and such, but I swear on me life, I’ll bring you and mammy so much money.  Twelve silvers a week pap, that’s more than we bring in in a month, and that’s all combined.  I’ll do our family honor I will, I’ll do ye proud pap.  
   Yeah he’d do it just like that, and then his pap’s eyes would fill all up with tears and pat his son on the back and say, I love ye son ye mus’ know that boy.  Rema’s tits we need money as much as food,and boy I’ve seen ye shoot that blazin bow a yours, and ya shoot with the devil’s goddamn luck.  Then he’d look at me all square fer a moment and then shoo me away.  Alright boy get your useless backside off me porch, you go and make somethin’ outta yerself, and don’t come back with less then ye left with ya hear me?  
   Yeah that’d be right, his pap’d have to insult him to say he loved him.    Well I love you to, you old rotten fart, and I swear to god I’ll make you the richest man in the whole blazin’ town.
   His eyes began to droop, and his head nodded as he dozed, but he kept that image in his mind, fixed there so tight he would never let it go.






























Chapter 18: Sorrow in Abundance


   Ignis woke up and threw hisself outta bed.  It was later then he’d usually be up, but his pap musta let him sleep.  That pap, whatta piece a work.  He’d have to knock some sense inta his cranky brain afore too long.
   He looked around the house for mammy, but she must’ve gone over to neighbor Jane’s house.  They liked to get together and talk about womenfolk things that just made his head get all swirly.  Not like pap, he talked man-like.  
   They’d talk about the weather, and the nosey people at the other end of the field, but most of all he’d talk about those no-good blazin Trojian bastards.  That was the biggest subject of conversation, seeing as pap was always flamin’ proud of his relations that died fightin off the Trojians.  If they hadn’t held ‘em off for the month it took to fortify their Capital, Mercia woulda fallen like everywhere else.  ‘We’re free,’ he’d say ‘Cuz those bastard pot-lickers had more courage than a thousand kings.  ‘Taint ever gonna be men like those, one whole month.  Cain’t hardly understand m’self.  One thousand, that’s bloody it, one-bloody-thousand rag tag bastards held off that mass of knights, and them magickers, and those flier knights ride dragons they do.’  It was told that the Trojian contingent numbered around fifteen thousand armed men.  They were held off by only a thousand hunters and farmers for nearly a month before breaking through.  It was a legend in Mercia, and a shame for Trojia.
  He headed out towards their corn field, blasted old man prolly already finished the mornin’ chores hisself.  He’d give the fart a good lickin’ he would.  He arrived at their field and saw that none of the work was done, and pap was nowhere to be seen.  Well that’s mighty odd, I wonder where the old bag-o-bones dragged hisself out to now. 
   He wandered back to the house, but it was still empty.  He wandered towards town, his old pal Droft would know what ta do.  He was a whole year older, oh and all the things he’d done.  He’d told him one time that he’d put his hand up ole Evaleen’s skirts.  He said she didn’t even to nuthin, just smiled and kept a working.  He was so flamin jealous of that piece a work, he’d been right mind ta give ‘im a lickin when Droft told him, his jealousy burned for weeks.  But he was still the most maturist boy he’d ever know’d.
   He turned to the left and headed straight to that bakery, the one Droft worked at.  It looked close to noon, maybe he’d catch him taking a lazy sit outside eating lunch.  He walked about fifty yards and sure enough that lazy bag-o-bones was a sitting there gnawing on the heel of a loaf.  
   “Hey there you lazy stinker, what nastiness have you been getting’ yourself inta today?”  Ignis yelled at him from across the street.  Droft looked up from his heel and made a vulgar hand motion at him.
   “Well I’ve been getting’ inta considerably more than your rabbit-sniffing behind.  I made a whole three coppers today and I’ve a right mind to buy some of them leather gloves.  Then I’d tan your hide like only gawd knows what.”  Ignis snarled at him and launched hisself across the remaining five feet and tackled the boy into the door.
   “You liver-eater!!”  Droft said as Ignis bit his hand.
   “Pig-snatcher!!”
   “Turd-sniffer!!”
   “Take that bloody back!”
   “Make me you little turd!”
   This continued for the next few minutes until both boys got up outta the dirt and brushed themselves off.  “Now look you little criminal, you tored my bloody shirt.  I’ma take that outta your hide one of these days.”  But even as he said the nasty words he grinned and asked the other boy bluntly.  “so what brings your sorry arse over here inta town, been skipping out on those chores again?  Your pap’s gonna bust you inta next week, I cain’t wait ta see it.”  
   “No that ain’t it, and you take it back!  Don’t go and vex me, you know that be the worst kinda luck.  I cain’t find the ole fart that’s why, I was wondering if you’d seen him?”  The other boy shook his head with a  straight face, then after a moment could hold it in no longer.
   “Alright fine, your pap’s down town arguing with the town council.  Seems like some messenger came a trying to say that you was ordered by the king ta join some riffraff contingent.  I think it’s baloney, nobody’d ever want you no matter how well you can shoot that blazin bow.”  Everyone acted like his archery talent was such a useless skill, but being in possession of archers of the highest caliber is why the Du Vron Dezdent was able to last this long.  They’d done countless raids against the enemy lands and gained more men every year then they lost.
   “Well why the blazes didn’t he wake me up?  I’da wanted ta be there.”  
   “Because he’s been there all night ya loon, fighting yer case.  You’d never want to join them archers would ya anyway eh?”  Ignis looked down at his feet.  And Droft’s mouth dropped.  “No way Ignis, I’ll beat you like a baby.  You ain’t thinking about leavin’ are ya?  That’s crazy talk that is, like ole bart down town, just loopy.”  But Ignis just kept looking at his feet, he still planned on leaving in the next week after the corn was reaped.  “Alright fine, I’ll talk to Evaleen, I know she wouldn’t want ya to go.  I’ll see if she’ll let ya put your hand up her skirts, just once mind you, cuz she’s my woman now.  Yup we kissed to seal the deal.”  Ignis looked up for a moment then sighed and started walking to city hall.  Droft fell into step behind him silent as ever, as they moved closer to the hall.
   Just before he opened the door Droft poked his shoulder and he turned and saw his friend staring at him all hard-like.  Like pap does right afore he’s gonna do some smackin’.  “Alright you,” Droft said.  He really did look all stony now, “If you’re gonna do this, then I can’t be expectin’ ya to go alone now right?  That wouldn’t be the neighborly thing at all.  No that wouldn’t.”  Ignis stared at him confused then retorted.
   “What are ya gonna send Evaleen with me?  She’d keep me warm she would.”  Droft snarled at him.
   “No you flamin crook, every archer needs a man right?  And I’s the best ruffian in the town.  I’ll go with ya, we’ll stick together like a virgins thighs we will.  Whadya say?  Don’t be shy, I know ya cain’t stop me.  It’s done then, I’ll be your protector.  Ain’t no way them Trojian’s is gonna cut off your fingers like they do them other archers, not with me around they won’t.”  Ignis just stared at him awestruck, then a grin broke out on his face.
   “Alright then ya fool, let’s go talk to this bastard that thinks he can come in here all ruff-n-tuff and tell us what we can and cain’t do.”  Droft snarled and they marched inta the hall chests puffed out as they walked.  
   When they arrived in the hall proper they stopped in their tracks.  Along with the city council and ignis’s father, five men stood in the hall wearing the finest plate armor that made them look like gods.  You couldn’t stop a man like that, although he’d heard lotsa men fight that way on the front lines, he was awestruck.
   The men looked at him and the leader, obviously because of his painted plate, asked the men of the council.  “Now which one of these young lads is Ignis?”  They were all silent until pap said.
   “That one there, the short one.  He don’t look it, but he can shoot that bow of his a hundred yards at fifty five pound pressure.  He’s a bastard though, and a runt.  See you don’t want him, right?”  Pap was definitely tired, and the man smiled eagerly.
   “I haven’t even seen him shoot, I have reports about all the competitions he’s won, but I need to see with my own eyes to be sure.”  He looked at Ignis for a moment.  “boy would you fetch your bow please?  Bring it round back and we’ll shoot across the field back there.”  Ignis nodded and ran back to his house to retrieve his bow.
   He grabbed it around the leather sheathe it was in and took his quiver of fine goose-feathered arrows and headed back to town hall.  He went round back and saw that four targets had been set up at varying distances across the meadow.  Targets at, thirty, fifty, seventy-five, and a hundred feet respectively.  He glanced at the man and he nodded for Ignis to continue, so he strung his bow and drew out his four best arrows.
   The man noticed that he only took out four arrows and didn’t know if he was cocky or efficient.  He would see.
   Ignis nocked one of the arrows, focused right at the point in the painted target of the thirty yard target.  Adjusted a little for height and wind, exhaled and loosed.
   The arrow stuck in the bulls-eye, but Ignis had already known it would.  He was excited now, another competition that’s all this was, and he shot straight on the bulls-eye of the fifty yard target as well.  He moved his focus to the target at seventy-five and nocked another arrow.  He drew it and adjusted for wind.  He took a deep breath and willed the arrow to the target.  Subconscious parts of his brain made a million tiny adjustments and he loosed the arrow.  As soon as he let it go, he knew it was a perfect shot and he tackled the one-hundred yard target with the same efficiency as the others.
   He turned and looked at the men standing by the council.  His pap was white in the face, and he just now realized he’d condemned himself to leaving without even thinking.  They must’ve made an agreement beforehand.  His pap had never seen him shoot, never came to his competitions, and now it had bit him on the backside.  Wordless he looked at his son, then he turned to the men and nodded solemnly.  They motioned to Ignis and he walked over to them.  Before he got there however Droft came bounding around the corner.  
   “Oh no you don’t you bastards, you ain’t takin’ him ‘less I go with him.  He needs someone to guide and protect.”  The men raised their eyebrows and the leader asked him.
   “And what makes you so able young lad?  Won any tourney’s yourself?”  Droft nodded.
   “I won the wrestling for three years running, plus the club fightin’.  I been doing some practicing myself for awhile yet as well.”  He looked down at the ground as the men laughed.
   “Alright boy you can come, you can go to camp together and if all proves well I’ll assign you together.  You’ll get your chance to protect this young lad from all types of harm.”  The man laughed, and then returned to the inn for the night.  He took the boys to the tent after letting them say a short farewell to their family.  His pap was all white faced but he looked at him and said in that way only pap could.
   “Now hear me lad and hear me well, you come home you hear?  There ain’t hardly nothing worth living without you and sis for us two old bags, so you come right home after you’ve served.  There’s a home for you waiting here when you get back.”  Ignis nodded tears brimming in his eyes.
   “I will pap I promise, ain’t no man that get between us men aye?”  Pap smiled and a tear fall down his face, he wrapped his son in a embrace and he felt more tears run down his back.
   He didn’t want ta leave this, he belonged here.  But wait, he had to get them a home.  They couldn’t live in this any longer, they needed room, and money.  They could barely provide.  He straightened his spine and looked at his pap.  “Pap I’m gonna come back, I promise a man’s oath like you said, and when I do I’ll bring all that saved money.  Twelve silvers a week.  We’ll have a home again.”
   His pap smiled at him and patted him on the shoulder one last time.  The lieutenants had begun calling to him and the old man put his sack on his shoulder and sent his son off.  Off to a life of pain and sorrow that hardly any would know.
   Out of his pap’s loving hands.
Chapter 19: The Town of Delta


                                       
   “It looks like we’ve only got a few more miles until we reach Delta, we’ll try and find a place at one of the inns in town.”  They were in mountainous terrain about five miles south of the city of Delta, and the rocky hills were slowly giving way to farmland and small settlements.
   “Or…we could ask some of these people for housing and save the money.  We’re going to need some provisions sooner or later.”  Lief looked like he was going to respond but Eric had already started speaking so he stopped and listened.
   “That sounds like it could work, but we have plenty of money, plus we could get much more rest without working for somebody in exchange for a bed, I think we need rest more than money.”  Lief nodded, and Adelia just shrugged.
   “I have no preference, you two decide which is best between yourselves.”  Eric smiled and they continued walking until they reached the wooden palisade that surrounded the town.  It stood about fifteen feet tall and was made of the trunks of trudgeon trees.  The trees were twice as thick as a man and, if left to harden, would eventually become as hard as stone when covered in the sap of the getilia tree.  They were the perfect way to erect a quick, strong, defense that could easily defend against everything but siege weaponry.
   They walked up to the guard stationed at the gate and, as they approached, he stood up and allowed them to enter through the gate.  The town was designed very plain, with all the houses made up like log cabins.  The buildings were designed the same way as the palisade.  They were soaked in the sap and were fireproof as well as being hard as stone.
   They walked through the packed dirt that served as the road in Delta, and they saw people staring at them with curiosity as they walked through the streets.  They were slightly disturbed at first, but they shrugged off the feeling and continued through the town.
   After about an hour they came across a tavern that was named The Drunken Dragon.  Eric looked at Lief and he nodded so they walked through the screened door to the bar area.  It was a fine area, with room for drinking and carousing, as well as a small platform for performers to play the jigs that were often played in the evenings.  It was empty now, except for six men on a break from their labor who had decided to come for a quick drink.  The town had little water supply so the only drinks available were alcoholic.  Wine was most common, but ale, beer,  and whiskey were all available as well.
   “I think we could enjoy ourselves a bit tonight, so long as we can keep our heads tomorrow.”  Lief said, and the other two nodded in agreement, so they approached the innkeeper to ask about a room.
   “Well what can I do ta help a couple fine meisters such as yourselves?”  The innkeeper was very large, but seemed very kind as well.  He had a large smile on his face and bowed deeply as they approached him.
   “We are in need of a room tonight, preferably a suite but we aren’t worried so long as there’s room for three.”  Lief, always savvy when it came to bartering, began to negotiate the cost of the room with the larger man.  Loud laughter and comments sounded while the two men enjoyed themselves bargaining over the price of the room.  After twenty minutes or so the older man held up a hand.
   “Alrighty then my young word smith, we can do this two ways.  You can accept my offer, or we can make things more interesting with a little contest eh?”  It wasn’t so much that the men disagreed on a price of the rooms, but that they both desired some sport and Lief motioned with his arm for the man to continue.
   “I’ll let you have the room for half of what I offered if you can beat my eldest son in a wrestling match.  I warn you he has never lost, and he is much larger then you, but it could turn a dull evening into a thing of interest no?”  The man was chuckling but Lief looked intrigued.  
   “I’ll take your offer.  I am talented in that area myself and would enjoy the challenge.”  Lief said this completely calmly and watched as the man’s face turned into a slight frown.
   “Boy my son may hurt you, I give you one last chance to retract the challenge.  You should take it, it would be wise.”  Lief laughed.
   “No sir, I shall not.  I am looking forward to this evening, it should be rather enjoyable.”  The bartender just shrugged and gave them the key to their room.  He wished them well and went back to his work of preparing for the night’s festivities.
   The three walked to their room and deposited their belongings in the drawers provided.  Their mounts were provided a place with hay and oats in the stables.  They collapsed on the cots and fell asleep.  Not even thinking about what lay ahead that evening.




   U’mar walked through the city of Delta, blending in with the citizens.  He wore the regular garb of the area.  The town was only seven or eight miles from his shrine so it had been easy to kill a traveler to acquire the disguise.
   He was following a feeling, something creeping at the back of his mind that was guiding him.  He knew his purpose.  He would not be awake now if there wasn’t the presence of at least two active descendants in the area, so the objective was clear.  The path to success, however, was as clear as his blackest cloak.  He would feel if any of them invoked any of their abilities, but it was unlikely that they would do so where there was many people around.  More than likely he would have to follow the scent-like trail of fragmented energy that they left in their wake.
   He had picked up their trail at the gate, and followed it through the town.  There was at least two of them and no more than four.  He had to tread carefully here, the presence of four descendants was unlikely, but not impossible.  He could not afford to waste his efforts until he was sure that the odds were in his favor.
   As darkness crept over the town he saw that there was ring of people outside one of the taverns in town.  As he got closer he saw that two men were wrestling in the dirt outside the building.  One of the men was hopelessly outmatched against another twice his size.  He watched as the larger man picked up the other with one hand, but just before he threw him to the ground, the smaller man punched the other square in the nose.  The larger man howled and the fight continued.  U’mar was just about to head on his way when he felt a shift in the earth.  
   He turned back towards the fight and watched carefully.  The large man had the other in a submission hold and seemed like he was about to finish him off.  The small man exhaled and slid out of his arms like he was coated in butter.  There he thought, he felt the shift again.  He eyed the small man and took a sniff in the air.  
   Ohh that’s one down I’m sure he’s got others with him.  He watched as the fight finished with the larger man on his back and the crowd cheered for the small man.  The bartender walked up to the small man and grudgingly nodded.  The small man paid him a few silvers and they all stepped inside laughing at the good fun.
   U’mar chuckled to himself, this would be all too easy.  Once he established who the young man was with he could take care of them quickly and efficiently.  The people of this town were weak minded, it would be easy to force his will into many of them easily.  He would just wait until they were asleep, he had a loyal group of followers waiting for his call within the city as well.  It had been established as a sort of trap by the bandit/founders of the town long ago because it was the only real town on this whole stretch of coastline.  Everyone travelling through the area would have to stop there at some point.
   He walked into the tavern and immediately saw the young man dancing with a young lady in the middle of the dance floor.  There were a few other couples but these two outdanced them all so most of the others were just watching.  He settled into a seat and watched as the man finished his dance, bowed to the woman and walked towards one of the couples standing at the edge of the room.  He watched as the woman yawned and waved to the men.  He could sense the blood of Mercola in her, as well as in the two men.  There was no one else though.  
   Three was troubling, there hadn’t been three descendants gathered in one place for a long time.  He had hoped that it was only one very strong person, but three was more troubling.  It meant that they had found each other, and if they had done that then they were aware of their abilities.  They may have even trained themselves a little.  He could probably kill them all within ten minutes, but he couldn’t take that chance.  He would have to gather some of the people.  He would set the trap, a travonian binding should do the job.  That kind simply made the people hostile to the point of madness.  They would kill whoever he set the binding on, and they wouldn’t even realize they were bound until he let loose the energies behind the spell.  
   He would spend the rest of the night binding as many people as possible, he could set the energies and balance them on the door of their room.  He wouldn’t even have to release the energy behind the incantation.  They would pull their own noose in the end.  
   He left the tavern and began to make his way around the town.






















Chapter 20: An Unusual Wake-Up Call


   What woke him up was the silence.  He lied in bed with his eyes opened, staring at the dimly lit ceiling.  There was an unlit lamp beside the bed but that wasn’t needed now, sunlight was just creeping in at the edges of the windowsill.
   He swung himself out of bed.  He was wearing a pair of pants and he slipped on his white tunic before he opened the door to the hallway.  When his hand touched the handle he felt an  odd tingle in his arm, he dismissed the feeling, and opened the door.  He continued down the stairs, but nobody else was there. 
   He wandered through the tavern and looked out into the street, nobody walked about at all.  The streets were completely empty, and he felt the hairs raise on the back of his neck.  Something wasn’t right at all.  He heard a shuffling sound in the street and saw a man running towards him with a crazed look in his eyes.  Eric stared for a moment, then when he saw the man was not planning on stopping, he turned and ran back to his room, quickly locking the door behind him.
   “What the blazes is wrong Eric?”  Lief was rubbing his eyes and his hair was ruffled from sleep.  Eric began grabbing bags and piling them up in a corner of the room.
   “Are you crazy southlander, tell me what it is that has turned you into madness?”  Adelia was in a state similar to Lief’s, only her hair was even wilder than his.  She had only worn a shift to bed, but Eric didn’t even notice anymore.
   “We have to go, something is seriously wrong here.”  He kept stuffing the bags, and finished buttoning the last bag.  He ran to the wall where his lance was hanging, and began putting on his leather armor.  
   Before either of them  could ask, they heard as something smashed into the door.  It hit so hard that dust fell from the ceiling onto them.
   Without question both of them stood up and grabbed their weapons from the corner, they stood facing the door and didn’t see as a woman’s hand broke through the window.  They whirled around just as the man broke through the door.  Three men barreled down upon them, it seemed like more of them had joined, and they fell into Adelia’s whirling blade.  They were dead before they hit the ground.
   Eric set a link to the woman and sent her flying thirty feet into the street below.  He waited to hear the thud, but he heard nothing.  He glanced out the window  and felt his knees go weak.
   The street below was filled with hundreds of the things.  They were people, but the life seemed to have come out of them, and been exchanged with the mind of a rabid animal.  Before either of the other two could ask, a mass of bodies came through the doorway.
   “I know what this is southlander, these people are bound!”  One of the men, filled with frustration from being behind a man grabbed his jaw and broke the other man’s neck.  Adelia plunged her blade into its mouth and another body piled in the doorway.
   The people were having a difficult time getting through the wall of bodies arrayed at the doorway, so Adelia had a moment to speak.  “I thought I felt something last night, something odd.  There’s a sorcerer here, and he’s sent them after us.”  Eric’s jaw dropped, and Lief yelled.
   “Adelia how strong is this sorcerer?”  Lief was a blur, hacking slashing and stabbing at the people who came through the door.  One of them was a child no more than six, but who possessed the same look of anger the others had.  Lief ruthlessly plunged his dagger into the child’s sternum.  Adelia was speechless.
   “Dammit Adelia, how strong is the bastard!”  Eric yelled at her.
   “He’s stronger than any I’ve ever known,” she stuttered while she spoke.  “He’s…he’s a lot st-st-stronger then I am.  I couldn’t bind more th- then fifty people at once, even if I spent a week preparing.”  Silence crept over them.  Even the people running towards their death were just thumps on the floor.
   Something changed and the people stopped coming.  There were bodies strung out all the way down to the tavern as the bodies had been drug away by the maddened people.  They bent over panting as a loud voice boomed out over the city.
   “DESCENDANTS OF MERCOLA!!!”  The voice was sharp, and filled to the brim with rage.  “YOU WILL DIE SLOW, PAINFUL DEATHS THIS DAY.  KNOW THIS, YOU WERE FELLED BY U’MAR, STRONGEST OF THE Y’MARR!!  YOU WILL DROWN IN YOUR OWN BLOOD BEFORE THIS DAY IS THROUGH!!”  
   Eric looked out the window and saw a black shape on the ground below, it looked like a hooded man, but from the top he could hardly know.  He motioned to the other two and they nodded.  They ran through the hall and into the tavern.  The people outside had made a rough ring around a hooded man, and as he drew his blade they began to moan and chant words in a foreign tongue.  They stepped out of the tavern and the man motioned to them.
   The three stepped out into the circle and the bound closed behind them sealing them in.  The man who had called himself  U’mar threw his hood back and stared into their faces.  He drank their faces in, a look of pleasure spread onto his face as he launched himself forward with inhuman speed.
   Before Eric could even raise his lance to block he had been thrown to the ground with a thud.  He was unable to move, and watched as Lief was held the same way.  Adelia, however, was held only for a moment until she shuddered and slashed at the Y’marr that stood ten feet from her. 
   He raised his blade and twisted his head to the side, like a puppy watching a bug.
   Adelia barely got her arm up in time to block the blow from his blade, and then roll away from the punch he had just thrown.  She fell back a few feet and regained her footing when she felt her blood run cold.
   She looked down at her skin and saw the blood running to the surface, it was like she was covered in one large bruise.  She felt her body grow limp and she fell to one knee.  She stared up at the man helplessly as he drew his arm back in a high arc.  She turned and saw Lief and Eric, neither of them was watching.  Lief was blurred at the edges like he kept fading in and out of reality, and Eric’s eyes were locked on the man who called himself U’mar.  
   The man put one hand to his temple and staggered for a second.  Adelia noticed and drew the ward of agrophia, the ward that would release bonds.  She felt no resistance until the last second, then she fought for every inch of ground she made towards finishing the ward.  The man was almost at her level.  He was being weakened every second by the two men and she could even see the people in the circle around them stumbling as the bond U’mar placed on them faltered.
   He took a step back and Adelia stepped forward, she saw the wards he would start to make and she would try and make them deflect.  The air was full of them, small rifts and ripples in this world.  Every pulse wore her down a little bit more, as she weakened.
   She stumbled as a Thoric ward burst in front of her in a shower of sparks, and she fell to one knee.  U’mar walked towards her and she traced an infernic ward and loosed it towards him.  He blew it off easily and continued walking towards her.  
   She had fallen to both knees and knew her life was going to end any moment.  She turned her head, with tears in her eyes and stared at Eric, who’s eyes were flowing with tears.  She saw the tension in the air around him.  Sparks were popping into existence where the energy leaked out of its tracks, but the only effect it was having was to make the man walk a little slower.  Lief was vibrating violently and U’mar flinched for a second, but continued to walk towards her, drawing his sword back for the stroke.
   There was nothing she could do, she closed her eyes to await her fate when suddenly a knowledge entered her mind.  She had never had a teacher, the wards and sigils had been naturally entering her memory for a long time. There was another ward she knew, one that held power over shadows.  She felt her hand drawing the symbol and saw the man stop the sword and stare.  She finished the ward and loosed it upon him, she fell to the ground from the massive amount of fatigue the spell had cost her and blacked out.
   Lief and Eric flew to their feet and threw themselves at the Y’marr.  He had been cut off from his source of power, the torrent of power that had been pouring out of him had been cut off, and he had no power to wield now.  The only thing he had was his body, but even that was enough.
   He met their swings with a parry and crouch, then fell into a dance with them.  Swinging, ducking, dodging, blocking, they flew around the circle in a flurry of blades.  The Y’marr hadn’t expected them to be this skilled, they never were before.  They were always pompous fools with no real skill.  But these two fought with the skill of ten knights and he felt himself go into a panic.  He had not been able to cut off their power any longer and he felt the tide of the battle slipping from his hands.  
   The battle continued to wage for another five minutes and U’mar was desperate.  He had to anticipate their responses.  He moved to the side and decapitated one of the women standing in the circle then flung the blood towards their faces, like expected they turned for a second and he kicked the one with the knives in the temple.  He collapsed to the ground.
   You bastard, die already Lief had just fallen to the ground and Eric was the only one left standing.  He had no choice now, there was a resolve in him.  He knew that the mysterious man would die, and he knew he would kill him.  
   He launched himself into the man, knocking away his sword, and watched as the man stumbled to the ground.  He raised his lance to the man’s throat and saw the pure terror and astonishment in his eyes.
   “Who are you child?”  The man’s voice was like quicksilver, smooth beyond measure.  Eric stared at him for a second and then spoke clearly in front of everyone in the town.
   “I am called Eric, Y’marr, I was called son-of-none but I will call myself such no longer.  What is your purpose for being here?”  The man swallowed hard and spoke quickly and quietly almost in a whisper.
   “I am U’mar, Y’marr of the bloodbinding coven, I am one of six, eternal enemy of the Vervadian descendant of Mercola.”  Eric stared at him calmly.
   “There are more of you out there?”  The Y’marr nodded quickly.
   “There are six now and there will always be six, when one dies another is born elsewhere.”  The man smiled and ripped open his shirt, a red spot appeared on his chest that faded on and off, like a heartbeat.  “I send my heartstone to the oblivion to await its return.”  The red spot on his chest began glowing brightly and the man began shaking.  His features wrinkled, he looked like he was aging a lifetime within a minute. 
   For a moment the light rose to a fever pitch, then abruptly died as the man fell to the ground lifeless.  Eric bent down and looked into his eyes, but when he touched the corpse it fell away into dust.  
   He jumped back and retched into the soil that had been trampled.  He heard whispers beginning to start from the people standing in the circle and he saw that they were angry.  Almost one-tenth of the people had been killed from the sorcery casted by the man, and they had heard that he had been after the three guests.
   The angry talk began to buzz with fury and he ran over to where his friends lay.  He drew from his source and picked them both up onto his shoulders and ran to the stables.  He took a moment and got the ponies ready and led them towards the gate, followed the whole way by the angry crowd.
   The gates were shut behind him, and he turned to the road.  He began leading the ponies towards the north.  It was only another two or three days walk until they would reach Thumbria.  He now had more questions than answers and he was eager to have them resolved as soon as possible.








































Chapter 21:  A New Device


   “Dammit Gunthred this is foolishness!!”  The Onyx knight’s voice rang throughout the meeting hall.  Fifty senators, and generals had gathered in The Trojian court and the only decision that had been made was that the rebels needed to be extinguished, which they had decided on over fifteen years ago. 
   “Now, now J’harra your title permits you some…leisure here in the court, but swearing is a bit low by our standards, even for you.”     The Onyx’s nostrils flared.
   “Senator Methlin I advice that you watch your tongue when speaking to your betters, it may just run away from you.”  Methlin smiled, his perfectly even teeth were just another sign of his life of luxury.
   “If it does I can surely count on you to return it to me unharmed.  Now where were we, ahh that’s right, you were calling the king a fool?”  He paused with his mouth opened and looked at the king.
   “Silence both of you, if you wish to continue with your witless banter I’d appreciate it if you leave the court now.”  The two men glared at each other across the table, just at that moment a loud knock sounded through the courtroom.  The king motioned to the guards to let him in.  It looked like a messenger, likely news from the front.
   The man walked up to the king and knelt to him.  Gunthred motioned for him to stand.  “Speak messenger, if you aren’t too weary.”  The man shook his head.
   “No your majesty, I have news from Warren from the Jagged Pass.”  He looked up and when nobody said anything he continued.  “Trojian forces have lost a little over one legion from the rebel…”
   “A LEGION?!  How the hell did they manage that?”  The man was speechless and the king’s face purpled.  “Speak up man, I don’t have the time for games!!”
  “A-a-alright sir, er the losses yes.  The rebels are estimated to have lost around three hundred me-“
   “Three HUNDRED!  Are my tacticians completely witless?”  He glared at the man vehemently.
   “Your majesty I think this news only amplifies my previous statement.  Our men will continue to be slaughtered by the Mercian’s guerrilla tactics unless we approach this problem from a different angle.”  J’harra practically shouted the words.
   The king sighed.  “And what do you propose we do Sir J’harra, please tell me your plan.”  J’harra grinned.
   “I’d be more than happy your honor…”






   “THIRD SNIPERS!!”  Ignis threw his quiver and pack over his shoulder.  He joined the other fifteen men in his division and they ran towards the officer’s tent.  
   “Ha!  Whaddid I tell ye, third time this week.  I’ll be goddamned, it looks like we’ll be doing sometin’ more than shootin’ fish in a barrel this time.”  Russel was a barrel-chested man with a crooked grin.  His missing teeth were evidence of his battle experience.
   “Eh fine you ole bastard, I didn’t think that other gen’ral would be dumb enough ta attack again.”  Russel grinned his toothless grin.
   “Ne’er did I sprat.”  They had arrived at the officer’s tent and General Finnick was eying them.  Once the men had stopped their chatting he began to relay orders.
   The men all straightened their backs and fell into place to listen to the General.  “Snipers you’ve seen a lot of action this week, I know that.”  None of the men nodded, none agreed with the man.  He grinned “But if I let that stop you then I might as well give you some sweetbread for your stomach’s, or pads for your boots.  We don’t expect comfort, as you all know.”   Now the men nodded and the General laughed.  “Hahaha, well live with it.  Focus on the mission and remember that your life is for the greater good eh?”  The men all nodded, Ignis stayed passive, he was used to this.
   “You all are gonna skirt along the edge of that valley where they’re encamped at.  I want you to go up there and stay hidden until you can see some of their officers.  I want you to kill as many as you can then haul your arses outta there.” The men all nodded.  “Well off with you then.”  
   The Third Snipers hoisted up their bags and weapons, shouldering them with what seemed like an effortless grace.  Though the bags weighed close to eighty pounds they looked as if they weighed ten when the snipers carried them, long days with the bow had worked their muscles to the point that they could bend iron with their hands.  It had been said that one of them had even escaped from a prison cell by prying the bars apart with his hands.  Everyone else thought this was ridiculous, the snipers knew otherwise.  The kind of bow they were required to use was around 160 pounds of draw strength.  They could shoot four hundred yards and hit a target the size of a frying pan dead-on.  They could hit a man-sized target in the chest from six hundred yards.  Nobody knew about this though, it was a well-kept secret that the Third Snipers were really much more than average bowmen.
   If the Third Snipers were the body of inhuman ability, than Ignis was the head.  He outdid the rest of the archers hands down.  If they shot a target in the chest, he could have hit it in the head.  If they could draw 160, he could draw 180.  His torso was broad, his arms as thick as normal men’s legs, and his back almost twice as wide.  He held a bow is his hands made out of a mixture of steel, and Chromatis, the alloy was as bendable as a fresh yew, but strong enough to shoot something over eight hundred yards.  The weakest draw strength for such a bow was 120 pounds, but then it would wear.  It had to be under good pressure to keep and that was what Ignis did.  He fired it at 180 pounds of draw weight and that, coupled with his Chromatis broadheads, could punch a hole through steel three inches thick.
   “Could be worse, he coulda sent us to go scroungin’ fer food.  At least we get ta do some killin’ eh?”  Ignis nodded.
   “Yeah, that or takin’ care o’ his woman eh Franco?”  Jake was a tall man with a thick, red, beard.  His features were sharp and all angles.  He was the broadest of all the snipers.  
   Franco scowled.  “Hmmph, she wasn’t nearly as good as the bastard claims.  She aint never did nuthin no tavern wench couldn’t have.”  Franco was built the scrawniest, he only fired a 140 pound, but even the smallest of the snipers was twice as strong as any footman.  He was not thin by any standards.
   “Ha!  That’s def’netely not what ole Brendan says about her.  ‘Every night, and half the days, she’s there.  Ready ta put a “lip-lock” on my “big-rock.”’  Tell me Franco, did she give yeh a little kiss down there eh?”  The men roared with laughter as Franco blushed.
   “I tell ya she didn’t do half a what that fart claims.  He might as well say she could walk on walls, and pull a fish outta the dirt!!”  The men laughed harder.
   “Now don’t start talkin’ ‘bout no goddamned fish Franco, mind I’ll beat you harder than that ole bitch ever did.”  Russel tried to hide his grin by coughing.  “Now lookit, you got my stomach all worked up.  I oughta tear you a new arsehole!”  He leapt towards the smaller man who scrambled out of the way.  The men around them were likely to bust a gut.
   “Sorry, ow dammit! Stop that, oww fine you win.  I give, I GIVE!”  Russel had Franco in a wrestler’s submission, and the smaller man was squirming like a catfish.
   Russel let him go and they continued down the path.  Franco kept rubbing his shoulder and giving Russel scowls, but the older man took no notice.  Before long they had crossed the battle lines and were headed up the slope to the side of the battle.
   Up ahead there was a line of turned up soil, it looked like it had been dug a few hours before.  “Hey Jake, you notice anything outta place up there?” Jake looked at it and shrugged.
   “Them bloody Trojians musta finally lost their minds, diggin’ in the dirt like that.”  He stepped over the dirt, as well as the rest of the men.  They all made sure not to touch it, afraid it may be sorcery of sorts.
   They came to the cliff edge, and they were able to see the entire Trojian encampment.  Close to twenty legions lay in the valley, each with a thousand men.  It was a foreboding sign to the Mercian rebels.  The rebels had possibly eight divisions assigned here.  Each division was made up of five hundred infantry, two hundred archers, one hundred calvary, and twenty five medics, making eight hundred and twenty five men for each division.  The Third Snipers were one of two special force squads assigned here.  There was also a special force blademaster unit, with twenty five of the best swordsmen in Mercia.  They used swords made with Chromatis but unlike the bows, the swords were cooled right after forging so that they would not break, or dull.  The swords could also be infused and all of the blademasters were proficient channelers.  They couldn’t manipulate the energy in any way other than energy reserves.  The swords were a reservoir of energy that could be tapped into if the swordsman was weary.
   The snipers stared out into the Trojian forces and trained their hawkeyes onto the enemy.  The hawkeye was a device with an adjustable lens inside of it.  The hawkeye could make things that seemed far away look much closer.  Ignis did not use one however, he had no need.  The men said he had the eyes of a hawk himself, but that was wrong.  He had eyes better than that of a hawk.  He could focus his eyes on things that were impossibly far away, as well as things right next to his face.  He could also shoot nearly anything he could see.
   The Snipers spent that afternoon watching the enemy.  They had papers that they recorded people on, and counted officers.  They had found a general walking through the camp, but the army had begun to stir.  He found out why when he saw a force of Mercians marching into view from the south.  
   Perfect!!  He thought, that would separate the Officers from the soldiers while they were receiving orders from their superiors.  They could fire an incendiary tipped arrow into the tent and watch as they ran out like ants.  They could pick them off easily, especially since the commander’s tent was only four hundred yards away from their post.
   He told the other men his thoughts and they quickly agreed.  Jake would fire the incendiary into the tent, and the rest of them would pick off any officers that exited.  The men attached the hawkeyes to their bows, and pulled out the partner piece to it.  An item called the trueflight.  It, partnered with the hawkeye, made it possible to shoot targets hundreds of yards away.  They attached it to the marked spot on the string and then lined it up with the target in the hawkeye.  Many other factors were involved, but it enabled them to line up the trueflight with the hawkeye and shoot objects at impossible distances.
   Ignis did not draw his hawkeye, or trueflight, he needed neither.  He simply strung his Chromatis bow, and prepared to kill some Trojians.
   Once the snipers were ready, and the generals had entered the tent, Jake readied the incendiary.  He counted down from ten and released it into the tent.  It flew true, and a burst of smoke came out of the entrance of the tent.  Men came running out with their hands over their faces.
   The snipers began to pick them off.  Ignis fired a shot into the sternum of a nearly bald man that wore a white uniform.  He fell to the ground with the white uniform turning red.  He killed another man, he was fat and looked like a politician.  He smiled as he watched the arrow shoot clean through his throat leaving a hole.  The man fell to the ground and his mouth opened repeatedly.  Like a fish out of water.  He killed one other man, and saw that nearly fourteen of the officers had been killed.  Doubtlessly there were more, but this would definitely put a damper on their routine.  He relaxed his shoulders, this was a very successful mission, they would probably get a bonus for this.
   “Haha!  Goddamned Trojians, they’ll not forget this soon.  Ha look they’re looking in the woods down there for archers, they don’t even know we can shoot this far.”  Franco spat over the edge onto the trees below and turned to unstring his bow.  
   They were all putting away their weapons when Russel shouted something.  “Hey lookit, over there.  The bastards have learned something!”  They all went to get their hawkeyes but Ignis just stared.  There were devices down there on a slope.  They looked like long metal tubes, and every minute or so a large plume of smoke would puff out of them and a tremendous boom would sound through the valley.  There looked like there were five of them, and they were about eight hundred yards out.  
   Ignis could see them clearly, he was wondering what they were for.  Were they a distraction?  He wasn’t sure.  Then he saw that one was aimed towards a line of infantry and that when it shot it splattered ten men into pieces.  
   Without hesitation Ignis redrew his bow.  He had seen the five men operating each device and he noticed that one of them was giving commands.  He aimed in onto him and, instinctually, he sent an arrow towards him.  It flew for about five seconds before tearing through his chestpiece.  The man fell to the ground and the device ceased firing.
   The other devices were too far away, even for him, so he unstrung his bow and looked back at the valley he had watched for so long.  After a moment he noticed that one of the men from the first device had run all the way over to one of the other devices.  They were speaking, then the man gave orders, and…what were they doing?  Ohh God, they were turning the device and pointing it for the ridge where the snipers were hiding.  Ignis called out to the men and they broke into a run down the slope the way they had come before.  
   They ran through the woods at a breakneck speed, not even pausing when they heard a boom, and the sound of rock shattering where they had been before.  They continued to run through the woods, and Ignis was about twenty yards ahead of the rest of them.  He jumped over the line of dug-up dirt and then stood about twenty yards away to catch his breath.  
   Through the trees men came flying, they had begun to jump the dirt as well.  They had all come through except Jake, who lagged behind.  He came crashing through the weeds and tripped on a rock near the dug-up dirt.  He fell on his face right on the dirt and, just as Ignis had begun to laugh, the ground erupted underneath of him.  It sent gore flying towards them as Jake was blown to pieces from the explosion.
   Ignis turned his head back to where Jake had lay and saw only a blackened scorch mark, surrounded by random pieces of his body.  There was nothing left of him to take back to camp.
   None of the snipers said anything, they just stared mouths open at the spot their friend had lay only seconds before.  Too stunned for words.
   After a minute Russel patted him on the back.  “We…got te get going son.  It…it’s what he woulda wanted.”  He wiped a tear out of his eye.  Men like him didn’t shed tears for not.  There would be a hole in their heart left by the explosion just as large as the hole in the ground.
   Franco trembled, he had been close to the man.  “Those goddamned bastards, they…they can’t just take ‘im away like that they…”  He covered his face in his hands and wept.  “I’ll kill every one of the bastards, meself if I need teh.”  He wept into his hands, as Ignis put his hand on his shoulder and comforted him.
   They had all been together since the very start of Ignis’ training.  A Sniper dying on the battlefield was a foreign notion to all of them.  None of them shirked bonds with the others to make it easier if they died, they were brothers.
   “Yeh won’t need ta do it by yerself man, we’ll kill ‘em together.  All of us.  Itn’t that right boys!”  At Ignis’s words they all roared defiance towards the Trojians.  “All of us, naught no one else.  We’ll make ‘em pay for what they did ta Jake.  Didn’t even get a buriel, just like that.  Well we’ll kill all the fuckers and piss on their corpses.  He don’t get a buriel, well neither will them or their get!”  The men roared again and they picked up the only thing they had left of Jake.  His cracked bow.  They buried it in the ground near where he’d died.
   “At…at least we could bury his bow.  Tha…that was his ‘ole life anyway.  I been with him nigh from the start.  ‘At was all we did was shoot.  Shoot, eat, an’ sleep.  Gawd why’d they have to take ‘im away?”  Many of them wept.  They had spent years together, and it was no shame to cry over a comrade fallen in battle.  After almost an hour, after they had buried his bow, they returned to camp.  Their hearts heavy, and their legs weary.








Chapter 22: The Eclipse


                         999 YEARS, AND 11 MONTHS AGO


   Wren was six years old.  He ran up to his mother, grabbing ahold of the folds in her dress, reaching for her to hold him.  “What is it Wren?  Hahaha” she laughed at him, her laugh was the most joyous sound he had ever heard.  It made him think of happy places, a warm blanket, his little sister, his pup scout, and all the things good in life.
   He didn’t say anything, he just kept reaching for her dress.  Eventually she bent down and lifted him up onto her hip.  She was cutting vegetables for their dinner tonight.  He breathed in the scents of potatoes, and garlic.  His stomach growled.
   “Mmmm, mommy dat smellths good!!”  She laughed that joyful laugh again.  
   “Well that’s a good thing!  It would be awful if you had to eat something that didn’t taste good wouldn’t it?”  He nodded his head enthusiastically.
   “Yeth it would mommy.”  He had a look on his face, his mother looked down at him thoughtfully.
   “What are you thinking about Wren, surely not those dreams again?”  He shook his head.
   “Them not dreams mommy!  I saw dat bad man outside da house again, he wath talking to me thith time!!”  She looked worried now.  She was afraid that her son was wrong in the head.  If the village council found out they would cast him in the firepit this very evening.  She mustn’t let them know.
   “Hush child, don’t speak of such things.  You mustn’t tell anyone else about the bad man okay?  You must promise.”  She looked into his eyes and saw the reluctance.
   “He thaid I mutht go tonight and tell all da people ‘round da fire.  He thaid it was my dethtiny!!”  She looked at him horrified.  How did a child as young as him know a word like destiny?  As far as she knew he had never heard that word in his life.
   “You just ignore that man, tonight’s fire will be great.  There’s an eclipse in the sky today, we must listen to the spirits alright?  No telling anyone anything understand?”  He nodded his head.  “Alright now go feed scout.  He looks hungry.
    “Alright mommy!!”  He ran outside as fast as he could with the pup yapping at his heels, like a regular child.  
   She shivered, what if he was seeing a spirit?  It was possible, some people were chosen from a young age, but why would they do such a thing when it only gets people killed?
   Wren was outside when he heard the voice.
   What did your mother say about the fire tonight Wren?  The man was there again, his skin was pale white and his lips didn’t move when he talked.
   “She thaid not to talk to you no more, she thaid you’re bad!!”  He turned away from the apparition, playing with the pup on the ground.
   Ohh Wren, why would she say such a thing?  She must be trying to keep you from being a man yes?  Like your father, she wants to turn you into a girl.  Wren’s eyes grew wide.
   “No, she dothen’t want me to be a girl!  She calths me her little man!”  The spirit chuckled, his ghostly form rippled as he did.
   Why of course she does, but she doesn’t want you to go to war she already told you that.  She doesn’t want you to tell the elders about me tonight because she doesn’t want them to take you away.  But if they take you away then you will be rich.  Rich enough to buy her enough potatoes that she can make potatoes and garlic every single day!!  His head shot around and he gasped.
   “Every day!!”  The spirit nodded.  “Well of corth she would want that, she jutht dothen’t know.”
   That’s right, now tell them tonight.  And she’ll be so happy she could burst.
   “Ohh thank you very much spirit, my mommy’ll be tho proud when she thees them pertatoes.  I muthen’t tell her though, not till tonight.”  The spirit nodded, then faded into oblivion.  “But wait thpirit, I have one question.”  He faded back to reality and cocked his head.  “I was jutht wondering what your name wath.”  The spirit chuckled, what harm could it do?
   My name is U’mar child, make sure to tell them that.  
   “Alright, U’mar da ghotht what a wonderful name!”  Now he faded, and disappeared into the air.
   That night the fire was roaring in the village square, the elders had stepped up to the bonfire and laden it with their offerings.  Gifts of meat and cloth had been given to the sun god so that they would be spared another year from the volcano.
   There was silence so that if the sun god spoke than someone could hear it.  “Did any one person hear the voice of the sun god, or one of his spirits?”  He looked out among the silent crowd and frowned as a small boy stepped forward.  He was small and had bright red hair, he must have been only five or six.
   “I hear one of the thpirits thir!!”  The boy had a lisp and was hard to understand.  It was forbidden for the children to hear spirits though, it meant that they were evil.  He knew of all the spirits, only evil ones visited childred.
   “Hahaha” the man tried to sound like this was trivial.  “I’m sure you just had a dream young chap.”
   He shook his head.  “No I did not have a dream, there wath a thpirit that thpoke to me.  He thaid to come here and tell you that he did.”  The village was still, they all knew where this was leading.  If a child listened to the voice he was trapped forever and must be mercifully put down.
   “What was his name son?”  He feared the worst, Ba’alshamel or Sha’aron were most common.
   “He thaid hith name wath U’mar thir!”  The boy stood loud and proud, the crowd had no clue what the boy was speaking of.  Inside the chief was screaming.
   “I see…”  He locked eyes with one of the village soldiers and nodded.  
   The man marched up and grabbed the child under his arm, a woman screamed for him in the crowd.  In truth the chieftan was terrified.  U’mar was the devil himself to these people, he terrorized them in the form of animals and beast.  The child had to die quickly before he appeared.   
  “I have judged that the boy is touched by demons!!”  The crowd moaned.  “He must be thrown into the flame, otherwise he will never walk the chambers of the sun god with us.  It is an act of mercy!”  The crowd all shouted their agreement.  The woman was crying, but watched without interruption.
   The guard held the child up as he started to scream.  “U’mar come thave me!  Thith ithin’t what you thaid would happen!”  He was crying, and shouting.  Then a shape appeared above the fire, it had eyes red as blood and an open mouth with eternal depth.
   “Throw him in!!”  U’mar laughed as the guard threw the child in.  He clapped as the child screamed, the scream travelled far and the child was losing his voice as he thrashed around in the flames.  They were hysterical, pleads of mercy, loud cries to his mother and father.  Then a pop in the flame and he froze with his back arched in pain and his mouth open.  All hair had been burnt from his body and he stayed like that for a few seconds.  
   His back loosened and he twitched as the flames burned on, the smell of burnt flesh was so strong that some of the villagers began to retch.
   U’mar laughed again and caressed the boy’s face.  He knew the monster that the villagers had created.  His soul would be placed in oblivion for a thousand years.  It was an eclipse, and a Y’marr was present.  He would go to oblivion and suffer the worst pain imaginable for a thousand years.  Then after that he would wait.  The pain would drive him mad, remove every good emotion from him and tend to the carnal aspects of his nature.  He would be driven insane, but then he would be driven sane.  The pain would be so great that, after driving him insane, something would kick-start his brain into action.  It would unlock his potential and make him able to receive a fragment of the beast’s heart.
   He caressed the spirit of the boy and drew it out of his body like a breath.  He held it in his hands for a moment looking at the monster he would make, and then he threw it down through the earth.  Where it would wait out it’s sentence in oblivion.
Chapter 23: A Thumbrian Greeting, And a Legacy Discovered


   It was two days since the events of Delta.  Eric had nursed Adelia, and Lief back to health.  They had begun walking the rest of the way to Thumbria.  They picked up some horses at a small town and made it to Thumbria after about five days of soft riding.
It wasn’t near as large as Tora, but still had close to a hundred thousand residents inside and around its walls.  They walked through the open gate and into an open thoroughfare.  People crowded in the streets looking for the best sales they could find.  The sound of all the people was a like a loud buzzing sound, and gave Eric a headache.
   They worked their way through the crowd, and headed towards the Western Gate, where Brazen kept his apothecary.  It was called Gretta’s Medicinal Herbs, and would be among the main roads in the city.  Lief knew where it was, and he lead them to a building with a large sign on it.  It said simply Gretta’s Medicines, and Herbs and it looked like it was painted on the front of the building.  
   They entered the shop and an odd mixture of smells enveloped them.  Many of them Eric couldn’t even identify, some were pungeant, and others were sour.  He shook his head as he followed Lief to the counter where an old man sat, his nose in his book muttering something.
   “Hail old man, you look like the sort of delirious dingbat that takes too much of a liking to opium.  Tell me, where do you place your orders?  Is there a drug lord in town, or do you travel to the city?”  The old man didn’t even raise his head.
   “Runts and bastards shouldn’t try to tell their betters what actions they are partaking of.”  The man raised his head and stared at Lief with eyes as green as the wood.  “Especially one so……unruly as yourself.”  The two men stared at each other, both keeping their faces expressionless.  Then Lief’s face broke out into a smile.
   “How long has it been Brazen, two years?”  The old man chuckled.
   “Ha, and I thought I taught you simple arithmetic.  One year, ten months, and twenty-two days.”
   “Well then my arithmetic is evident enough that it was YOU who taught me.”  The old man’s nostrils flared.  Then he laughed a deep laugh, coming deep from his diaphragm.
   “It would seem so, let us go downstairs.  We have much to talk about, I see you have a third with you.”  He gazed at Eric intently.  “This one is an interesting case, I am eager to hear his tale.”  Lief laughed.
   “Ohh yes he is interesting enough, he’s already killed a Y’marr.”  Brazen’s head shot around.
   “What is this foolishness I hear?  A Y’marr would far outmatch YOU Lief, and you have trained under me.”  Lief didn’t even flinch at the comment, he was almost utterly selfless.
   “I know, I had thought there was no hope.  I don’t even know how he did it, me and Adelia were both unconscious, I think you should ask him about that yourself.”  Brazen’s gaze was battering Eric’s eyes.  Eric turned away.
   “Let us take this conversation downstairs, there are eyes and ears in the city.”  They followed him downstairs.  It looked like the man lived in his basement.  It was furnished, and the walls looked almost…shiny.  They gleamed unnaturally, it was barely noticeable but Eric sensed it nonetheless.
   “Ahh much better, shut the door and no eavesdropper will be able to hear us.”  Adelia shut the door, Eric was wondering why she had been so quiet lately.  She hadn’t said a word since they entered the city.  She looked like her face was deep in thought.
   “Where is Lyra, old man?  Don’t keep me waiting any longer.”  Brazen glance at her then looked away.
   “I have her stabled outside the city, in my protected grove.  You can see her tomorrow if you like.  The damn pigeon needed some air.”  Adelia frowned first, then nodded still looking thoughtful.
   “Well then let’s hear it!”  Brazen nearly shouted at Eric, who jumped.
   “What do you mean?”  His voice was shaking slightly, he didn’t trust it too well at the moment.
   “Everything, I want you to tell me everything you remember.  Your parents, siblings, childhood friends, training, lovers, everything about yourself that you know! I shall need to know it all, no secrets.”  Eric frowned.
   “I…I’ve had no parents, or siblings, my training was in the Trojian army.  I had one friend for a few weeks, then he was reassigned.  As far as lovers go, there are very few women in the army, and all the women at the taverns were whores.  I…I’ve never been in love.”  He stumbled through the words.   “I joined Vallech’s sky knights a little over a month and a half ago.  I trained as I travelled to Tora with them.  I was given a….”  His face was blank, could he really have forgotten?
   “Well out with it boy!!  You were given what?”  Eric’s face was still shocked.
