   “I was given a wyvern hatchling, It had been with the army when I was captured.  Oh shit, I can’t believe I left him…”  The old man was perplexed.
   “We don’t need another stable for a wyvern, that damned pegii is enough to drive a man insane.  Good riddance!”  Eric looked up at him and snarled.
   “Watch your tongue old man, I….I’ll go no further until I have him back.  God, he must be almost full grown by now.”  Eric was talking to himself now, mumbling.  Being a sky knight had been his dream, getting caught up with these children of Mercolas had confused him.  He had to go get his partner.  Would he still remember him?
   Brazen frowned.  “Really do you want him back?  It’ll mean that much more food, and you’ll have to be more discreet when walking through towns.”  Eric snarled at him.
   “That’s not even a question!  I want him back more than anything else.”  Eric glared at the man.  Brazen shrugged then said.
   “Well I don’t see how that’s possible, I couldn’t call any animal that wasn’t bonded to you.  The sky knights don’t bond their riders, that’s partly why they’re so efficient.  Adelia’s bonded her steed, but with no connection between it and you I’m afraid it’s lost.”  
   Eric opened his mouth to protest, then paused.  He took off his overshirt so that only his tunic was showing.  Then he took that off.  The red mark on his chest was still clearly visible from where the hatchling had shared his energy with him.
   “What the blazes are you doing, put that back…”  The old man’s eyes were drawn to the spot on his chest.  He walked over to Eric and placed his palm on it.  Eric shivered at his touch.  First he felt nothing, then he felt like he was being sapped, his energy continued to leave him and he almost passed out.  Then, without thinking, he tapped into his source and renewed himself on it.  When he did this Brazen’s eyes widened.  “Gods above…”  He whispered, but did not stop.  After a few minutes he pulled away, looking tired.   “Your wyvern will come to you in three days.  It seemed like it was near the town of Shan, near the border of Mercia.  He was headed back to Trojia with the rest of the army.  He seemed wary of me.  The beast seems almost…intelligent.”  
   Eric stared at him.  “Of course it’s intelligent, it understood everything I told it to do.  I think it understands common tongue.”  Brazen frowned.
   “Alright then, it should be here in three days or so.  Now that I’ve activated the bond you should be able to tell when he gets within ten miles or so.  Should be convenient, now let’s hear the rest of that story.  Just skip to the part where you killed U’mar.”  He looked deeply into Eric’s eyes, daring him to disagree.  Eric began to relate the story of the battle.
   “Then we ran out of the tavern, as soon as we got out there Lief and I were forced down.  Some sort of powerful binding.  I tried to break it, but it was too strong.” He looked down in shame at remembering it.
 Lief nodded.  “I was expending all of my energy into the bond, though Eric was channeling hundreds of times as much force as I was.  I was perplexed.”  Nobody said anything, then Eric continued.
   “All we could do was watch, then Adelia did something strange.  It was like one second he was there, and iron wall.  Then the next he was simply gone.  Like he couldn’t channel any longer.”  Brazen looked confused he turned his gaze to Adelia.
   “How did you do this?  I’ve never heard of such a thing.”  Adelia shrugged her shoulders.
   “The knowledge came to me, I can’t reproduce it.  I don’t know exactly what I did, not a binding…but sort of an UN-binding.  At least that’s what it felt like.”  Brazen frowned.
   “That makes no sense, I know of no such binding.  The Y’marr have a connection to the beast that is supposedly unbreakable.  I don’t know much of the connection, but to break it  would require tremendous power.”  Adelia looked at the floor.  “Tremendous power indeed, so what happened after Adelia cut off U’mar from the beast?”  
   Eric continued.  “Lief and I were released, Adelia had passed out, so we attacked him.  He was more skilled with a blade than any man I’ve seen.  He disabled Lief, and he attacked me.  Somehow I fought him off.”  There was a silence.
   “One doesn’t just ‘fight off’ a Y’marr.  How do you know he died?”  
   “After I disabled him, he opened his shirt.  Underneath there was this…thing on his chest.  Like a heart.  He said it was a piece of the Beast’s heart, and that he sent it to oblivion that the next Y’marr could have it.”  Brazen nodded his head.
   “Yes you killed it alright, although I wonder why.  If you’d have stabbed him, he could have reappeared.  He must have been afraid for the heart.”  Eric answered quickly.
   “Yes I…felt the heart.  It looked invulnerable, but it wasn’t.  I think that, if I had tried hard enough, I could’ve destroyed it.  It was strong, but whatever’s inside of me was stronger.”  Brazen simply nodded.
   “Yes, he must have been afraid for the heart, so he sent it on.  In a month or so another Y’marr will appear, and he’ll be set on killing you.  The only chance you have at killing them completely is by killing this heart.  Every Y’marr has one, and they’re all different.”  He paused for a moment, then continued.  “I don’t know of any other descendants yet, just you three.  If we could find more then we may be able to hunt them down.  Three of you may have been enough, with the element of surprise, but now that one of the Y’marr has fallen the rest will be weary.  They will be prepared for an attack from these descendants.”  The three of them stared at him perplexed.
   “You mean to tell me old man that we should actually LOOK for these Y’marr?  They will simply gather together and outnumber us.  To do such would be folly.”  Adelia looked, somehow, frightened.  This coming from her was unusual.
   “That is not a possibility, the Y’marr must stay in their respective territories.  They are mighty, but that is their weakness.  We have hunted them for a long time, but now we have a chance to kill them forever!  We must quickly gather the rest of the descendants.  Our time is short, and even one more could turn the tides in a battle.  U’mar was average strength for a Y’marr, not weak, but not strong either.  The others will be comparable in strength.”  The three of them simply stared.
   “But how will we find the other descendants?  We don’t even know where to look.”  Eric said this, and Brazen quickly responded.
   “That’s untrue.  Every descendant must be born in their respective country.  Whichever land they are the descendant of.  That should give us an idea of where to look.”  He continued.   “Lief is the descendant of Vervan, and Adelia Fran.  That eliminates two of them.  I’m not yet sure about you Eric, but it seems that your powers are similar to that of Trojja, lord of Trojia.”  
Eric’s jaw dropped.  “You…you can’t be serious?”  
Brazen nodded.  “It was said that Trojja never tired, and that he could ignite anything without touching it.  He could run forever, and fight for ages.  He once held the gate of a keep for almost a week before he grew too weary to defend it.  It would seem that he had some sort of energy source inside of him.  This is eerily parallel to your power, and with more observation I think we could place your ancestry with Trojja, mightiest of the Stewards.”  Eric was stunned.  It was almost a relief to know, but if his homeland was Trojia then how could he oppose them?
   “That sounds…almost exactly like what I can do.  When I was fighting U’mar, for a moment I knew who I was.  I knew I was no longer ‘son-of-none’ I knew I had been claimed.  I felt, deep in my bones, that I was part of a bloodline.  Of a great bloodline, and I think you’re right.”
   Brazen smiled.  “well then, you are a very interesting character.  I think we will learn much about each other.  The Sky Knight, orphaned son of Trojja.  Very interesting indeed.”
   Eric smiled weakly, it would take some getting used to.






Chapter 24:  The Gathering


   Revlin crouched low over his workbench.  It was late at night and the only light he had to work by was a few candles.  The device lying before him looked haphazardly designed, with random parts sticking out of it, but Revlin was never haphazard.  Never.
   He held it up to the light, it was plainly colored.  Simply silver where he had used steel, and copper.  The parts of the machine that were important were hidden from view.  
   He smiled as he remembered stealing the Chromatis from the foundries.  The Trojians didn’t even think to suspect a boy.  Not even sixteen summers yet.  The Chromatis had a certain property to it that was unique.  No other metal came even close to possessing what Chromatis did.  The war-mongers were only interested in the stuff because it made good swords.  It did make good swords, the best he supposed, but it could also hold EXHORBANENT amounts of energy inside of it.  It didn’t even feel hot until it held enough energy to obliterate a pile of rocks the size of a house.  It was actually used in the design of a Reman key, the key was supposedly given to an ancient king as a show of friendship.
   The king was supposedly the only person who could use the key.  Even common channelers couldn’t.  It would take over a hundred channelers, over a year to fill up a piece of Chromatis the size of one in the key.  The piece in his machine would need almost three times as much energy.  When it ran though, if it ran as desired, it would cause the energy to run through a circuit (his own design) inside the object.  It had a long round rod about two feet long sticking out of one end, and the energy running through it could enable them to weld things quicker than any man had thought possible.  It could even make enough energy that they could forge Chromatis objects, without leaving them in a fire for a month.  
   If only there were someone who could channel, well even then it couldn’t work.  I’d need a team of channelers, plus a safe environment.  I don’t have either.  Sigh…  He blew out the candles and crawled over to his mat on the floor.  
   He wasn’t asleep long before a strange dream came upon him.
   He was walking towards a townhouse, there was a sign above the door.  Gretta’s Medicines it said, he shrugged and continued inside.  He walked through a shop that smelled odd.  Many things lay out among ruckus of the shop.  “Come upstairs.”  He heard a voice say, and he walked up the stairs.  The dream was quite vivid, things didn’t twist or warp like they normally did, he pushed the thought aside and walked through a door at the end of the hallway.
   Inside there was an old man sitting upright on his bed.  Around him were herbs being burnt, the smell was seductively sweet so he sat down.
   “I see you’ve decided to join me, would you like some tea?”  The man spoke quite practically.  Every syllable perfectly pronounced.
   “That would be wonderful meister…er what was your name again?”  The man chuckled.
   “Brazen man, and speak up.  I’m a bit hard of hearing.”  Revlin nodded.
   “Well then meister Brazen, it seems like you have quite a fine place here.  Why did you ask me to come up here though, I confess I’m mightily confused.”  Brazen laughed.
   “All in due time, we must wait for the rest of them to join us.  Go ahead and help yourself to whatever you see around the shop, ahh wait.  Another joins us.”  He gestured towards the door where a man walked through.  He had reddish hair, and a hard face.  His eyes were soft however.
   “Ahhh Brazen, one has already heeded the call.”  The man took a seat on the bed beside the old man.
   “Yes Lief, and I’m expecting more.”  The red-haired man chuckled, then went to pick out a tea bag from one of the shelves in the room.   “They are on the way now I believe. Unless I’m wrong.”  The man called Lief raised an eyebrow.
   “I hardly doubt that, now we must wait for them.” 
   “Yes we do son, shouldn’t be long now.”




   Sarai crept out of her hiding place.  She wandered through the wreckage where she had spent her whole life.  Everything was burnt, and the smell of the bodies, it was horrifying.
   No tears came to her eyes, she had cried them all a long time ago.  Five days she had spent in that hole.  Only a gallon of water to satiate her thirst.  Now she stumbled to the well and began to pull up the bucket for water.  When she had the bucket in her hands, trembling from thirst, she looked at the water.  It was colored red, and smelled metallic like blood.
   She retched, but couldn’t throw anything up.  She was too dehydrated, she looked at the bucket.  The water was stained red from the blood of people, no doubt, thrown into the well.  She looked around the town and saw nothing else standing, no other water source.  It was the well water or death.
   She made herself drink it, then she drank more.  She filled her two canteens with the blood-water and decided that it was better than death.  Barely.
   She gathered up one of the ripped tents left by the Trojian raiders that came through there.  She folded it and threw it over her shoulder, at least she would have shelter.
   Her town had been a small fishing village, on the coast of Frandia.  She was Fiorran by blood, but her family had made their home with their allies after Fiorra had been attacked.  They had decided to live their lives out there.
   Now the tears came, they came thick but she made herself stop.  They wouldn’t want her to waste water on them.  She needed to keep the water until she found some more, and she didn’t know when that would be.
   It was dusk, so she rolled down in the sand outside her house.  The salt from the ocean tasted so much like tears to her, she stood up and walked away from it.
   She rocked herself to sleep, the only survivor of one of the most brutal attacks seen in the history of Mercolas.  It was not a surprise that sleep found her quickly.
   She was walking through the gates of a town, it was night time and she saw that just up ahead there was a house with candles flickering in the window.  She felt herself being drawn there, and without thinking walked up to the door.
   She didn’t bother to knock, she simply walked through the doorway.  The floor she was on was full of clutter, many boxes of herbs and medicine’s lay out.  She heard a voice whisper to her. “Upstairs, come join us quickly.”  She didn’t hesitate, she walked up the stairs and down the hallway.  She opened the door and saw four men and a woman sitting in the room.  They had drawn up chairs and two seats were empty.
   She took the seat furthest from the old man, he gave her the chills, and settled down.  They all looked at her with interest, but asked no questions.  The old man just smiled.
   “Not much longer now, only one more to go.”  The people around her looked anxious as the clock on the mantel ticked away. 




   Ignis rolled onto his mat heavily.  They had lost another man today, Travar his name had been.  They buried him six feet deep in the ground, with all his possessions lying neatly on his chest.  His wife had been with the army, washing clothes when she heard.  She was a strong woman, and didn’t let any see her weep until they covered his face with the dirt.
   “How many more lives until you are satisfied?”  He whispered to himself.  “How many families must be destroyed before you leave us in peace.”  He knew he spoke in vain.  The Trojians would not stop, they would be lucky if they were able to hold them off for another month.  It could all be over after then.
   Ignis ignored the men sitting by the campfire, he heard his name but he didn’t care.  Nothing mattered now but his ever growing list of dead Trojians he kept in his head.  He didn’t know when the number would be high enough that he would feel at peace, he just knew that he needed to continue raising it.  Every day, at least one man he sent to the grave.  It was his resolution, his choice.  He didn’t know when he would stop, maybe after they did.  He didn’t know.
   He needed sleep, he closed his eyes and fell into a deep doze, the men couldn’t have kept him awake even if they had tried.


   He breathed deeply the smell of a thoroughfare.  It was night time and he walked through the empty streets of a small city.  He didn’t know where he was going, but something was pulling at his heartstrings.  He headed towards the western gate.  That was where it was coming from.
   The feeling grew stronger as he neared an apothecary.  The sign marked it as a medicine shopped owned by someone named Gretta, he had no idea why but he walked through the front door.
   The feeling tugged him up the stairs and through a dimly lit hallway.  At the end there was a door, whatever was calling to him lay behind that door.  He could feel it.
   He opened the door and saw six people looking up at him respectively.  Two were women and the rest were men.  One of the women had blonde hair, her lips perfectly proportioned and her hips were tantalizing, the other woman was slight.  She had brunette hair, and had torn clothes.  Wherever she had been, she had been run through the mill a few times.  One of the men had red-hair and sat with a smile on his face, his eyes told him that this was a man he could trust with almost anything.  Another man was short and slim.  He had many pimples on his face and looked almost frightened to death of the other people there.
   Of the two remaining men one was old and looked weary so Ignis stared past him to meet the eyes of the remaining man.  He had wavy brown hair, not quite curly, and an average face.  Nothing stood out about his features except for the shape hidden underneath his clothes.  The eyes unsettled him, they threw him off his balance.  His eyes were hard, unyielding.  This man has killed before, he instantly knew that, because it was the same look in the eyes of every veteran on the war front.  He was very wary of this one, from the eyes, and the way his muscles pressed against his clothing he knew that he should keep an eye on this one.
   The old man spoke to him.  “Ahh, it’s nice of you to join us. Please have a seat, get some tea and relax.  I think that we are finally ready to talk.”  Ignis took the remaining seat and looked expectantly at the old man.  He smiled at them all, then he opened his mouth to speak.
















  
Chapter 25:  A Plan Unfolds


   It was nighttime and the room above Gretta’s Medicines in Thumbria had more people in it then Brazen had seen in years.  Technically though, there was only one person in it, laying in his bed snoring. 
   “Well, before we get to more formal matters I think we should all introduce ourselves.  Yes I think we could manage that.”  He looked at the red-haired man to his left.  “Why don’t you start Lief, we could go around the circle if you’d like.”  The man laughed.
   “Well it looks like you all know my name, who I am is a much different story however.  I am Prince Lief of Vervadia.  I am a descendant of Mercolas, and have acknowledged my place as Vervan’s heir.  I trained here with Brazen from the time I turned eight, until not quite two years ago.”  Everyone in the circle looked perturbed, no that word was too mild, they looked mightily confused.
   The woman sitting beside him, the one with the brunette hair and the shredded clothes fidgeted with her wardrobe.  Her skirt barely covered her thighs and she hastened to cover them.  She spoke in a strong voice though, that was smooth as water.  “I am Sarai Truvard, son of Marion and Mary.  I am the last living person from my village, it…it was attacked by Trojians almost a week ago.”  She looked down at the floor, no one said anything, so Brazen nodded towards the young Reman lad seated beside her.
   “Er, me?  Ohh alright, I am Revlin Grace I was born in the Grand city of Remes almost fifteen summers ago.  I lived there long enough to learn my numbers and letters, then left home when I was twelve.  I travelled for years around the country, even with all the Trojian soldiers there.  I stole from their foundries and built things.  I’ve sold them to the officers under an assumed name, and played the delivery boy for my own devices.  I’m not sure why I’m so good at making things, but I am.”  Everyone nodded, but the man sitting two seats down looked abashed.
   “You sold weapons to those bastards?!”  The boy looked shocked, the man’s muscles were chorded and he looked very threatening.  “What kind of weapons?”  The boy was silent for a moment.
   “I sold them the designs for a device that could fire projectiles at a target.  It could be very effective in siege warfare, and they gave me enough money for it to live off the rest of my life in solitary.  Even told where they could mine the powder for it, I…didn’t really think about it, I just needed the food.”  He looked down, and Ignis opened his mouth with a snarl on his face.  He was interrupted though.
   “Enough man, the boy’s barely fifteen years old.  If that.  If the rest of us were judged by the things we did at that age than you should be directing your anger at me.”  It was the threatening man, the one with the wavy brown hair.  His jaw was set and he looked Ignis right in the eye.  “When I was fifteen years old, only two years ago, I killed my first man.  I’d fought and killed any man disloyal to the Trojian empire up until two months ago when my eyes were opened.  I’ve probably killed as many rebels as the boy has, and by my own hand.”
   Ignis calmed himself, he could probably kill this man easily, but something told him not to try.  He mumbled an apology to the boy and dropped his gaze.
   “I suppose I’m next in line, though I’ve done enough talking already.  My name is Eric-Trojjason, I was orphaned as a babe and raised by the Trojian military.  They prefer raising their troops no sooner than the babes are off the tit.  I am the heir of Trojja, steward of Mercolas, and he recognizes me.”  He had felt that in his bones, a new connection with himself.  “I’ve killed a Y’marr, with Lief and Adelia, and matched Lord Vallech himself in combat.  I’ve stormed the keep of Tora, and have been a prisoner of war.  I was saved by my enemy, and betrayed by one I called friend.  I’ve learned by now not to take anything for granted, because you can lose it in a heartbeat.”  He met the eyes of the golden-haired girl seated beside Brazen and she gazed back at him fiercely. 
   The next person was Ignis, and he paused with his mouth opened for a moment before speaking.  “I…I was raised in a wee border town in Mercia.”  He stumbled trying to match the peoples foreign accents.  “When I was a sprat…er lad, me pap planned fer me ta work the fields.  Actually I planned fer me ta work the fields too, ‘twas a good life with little catches but one day a ‘cruiter came ta me town lookin’ fer some fresh meat ta fight the Trojians.  They ‘twas workin’ their way down the border see, and they was lookin’ fer good men.  I was naught but a lad, and I joined ‘em.  Me and me ole pal Droft joined the rebels.  I don’t know wha’ happened ta that bastard.  That’s beside the point though, I ‘twas assigned to the Third Snipers, special forces see.  I been real good at shootin’ things since I was wee, and they saw it.  Up ‘till now I just been shootin’ Trojians wherever I been told.”  
  Everyone nodded, they all seemed interested now.  The last person was the golden-haired woman, she spoke little and said it quickly.
   “My name’s Adelia, I met Lief almost a year ago and he introduced me to this old man.  I travelled with them for awhile and liked them well enough to decide to stick around.  I…I’ve been recognized as the descendant of Fran, and I…well yes that’s it.”  She looked nervous and she looked at the old man who laughed.
   “Look at us in here, who would’ve thought that a Trojian, a Frandian, a Vervadian, a Mercian, a Fiorran, and a Reman could all come together and NOT end up with a war starting.”  His eye twinkled.  “Now that the pleasantries are out of the way, have any of you guessed why you’re here yet?”  Ignis, Revlin, and Sarai shook their heads.  “You are here because before I went to sleep in this bed I invoked deep magic.  I sent out a call to a certain bloodline, a bloodline tied to the land itself.  Every one of you here is a descendant of one of the Stewards of Mercolas.  Tied to the land in mysterious ways, every one of you is the living embodiment of the stewards, in a sense you ARE the stewards of Mercolas.  Your birthright dictates.”  Eric, Lief, and Adelia nodded solemnly.  Revlin seemed perplexed.
   “Wait one second, I was only a tenth leveler.  I’m not even close to being royalty, let alone…anything else.”  No one said anything for a moment, the boy’s face was scrunched up.  He looked conflicted.
   “When Trojia attacked everyone else the first actions they took were to assassinate their royal families.  This action scattered the descendants from the royal households.  The magic that is tied to the descendants went to the next person with the closest tie to their land’s steward.  If the person with the purest bloodline, that was a descendant of a steward then he was recognized.  The power has no discrimination between rich and poor, peasant or king.  Only the blood of the stewards can call the magic.”  The boy looked confused, no he looked skeptical.  
   “How do you know I’m not just smart?  What if I’m not a child of Mercolas like you say?”  The boy was attempting to disguise his fear, he didn’t want to be renowned or important.  He just wanted a simple life, that’s why he’d left home.
   “You would not be here if you were not the recognized descendant of a steward.  Only they could have heard the call.”  Brazen looked impatient now.  The boy opened his mouth, but was interrupted.  “For now though we have more pressing matters to discuss.  Trojia threatens us all, and we must be prepared to fight them.”
   Murmers of agreement came from some of them.  “We ARE fightin’ ‘em, we Mercians be the only free people now.  Why don’ ye all join us down south?  If yur as blazin’ talented as ye say ye are, than many o’ the men fightin’ out there migh’ have a hope a winnin’.”  Ignis had hope in his eyes, he really believed what he was saying.
   Brazen held up a hand.  “You are right my friend, Mercia must be preserved.  But there is also another threat, I was interrupted before I could tell you.”  He raised an eyebrow at Ignis who shrugged.  “The Y’marr have discovered that the descendants fight together.  U’mar sent word to oblivion as he passed, and they have all raised their defenses and extended their networks of spies, it is only a matter of time before you are tracked down and assassinated by the Y’marr.  We must come together to prevent such an attack from taking any of our lives, before it is too late.”  Ignis, Sarai, and Revlin looked confused.
   “Wait, what are Y’marr?  They are something that I’ve never heard of?  Explain yourself please.”  Revlin was eager for knowledge, and it showed.
   Brazen sighed, “Just as the descendants are Mercolas’ hands in the world, the beast has hands as well.  The Y’marr are the opposites to the descendants, they sleep until a descendant enters their land and then awaken.  They hunt down the descendant and kill them.  Luckily for us, the Y’marr cannot leave their territories.  The territories the beast made for the Y’marr are the same Mercolas used for the Stewards.  One Y’marr, one descendant from each country.”  They all nodded.
   Sarai was silent while the old man talked, but after he finished began to talk quietly, they could hardly hear her.
  “What would you have us do Brazen?  No more talking in circles, if this is really worth considering tell us why.  What can the Y’marr do other than kill us?  I must confess, if they only hunt one person they do not seem so dangerous to me, even if I am one they hunt.”  She had hardly spoken at all so far, but her words carried weight.  Everyone looked at Brazen now, they waited for what their leader had in mind.
   “I…the Y’marr are normally not much of a threat to us.  Each of them carries a heart fragment of the beast, and they are held apart by the territory lines between lands.  Lately though I was dream walking, I felt a tremor there.  Strings are being pulled and the fabric is beginning to fall apart.  The beast is tired of being imprisoned.  He begins to plot, and to tug at the knots in his prison.  He searches for a way to gather the hearts together so that he may terrorize the world again.”
   “What will happen if he does…escape?”  Adelia’s eyes were wide now.  She had felt it too.
   “Such things should not be spoken of, it would be worse than the death of us all.  A solution has presented itself though, a beacon in the distance.  We have discovered the secret of killing the Y’marr.”  Revlin scowled.
   “What is the secret?”  He blurted out.
   “The secret to killing the Y’marr lies in their heart.  They only exist because of the heart of the beast inside of them.  Without it they will die like normal men, so the secret to destroying the Y’marr, without worrying about them coming back, is to destroy the heart.  I have studied this for many years and I have never found an energy source strong enough to kill a Y’marr…”  
   It must be Chromatis.  It could surely hold that much energy.  Where could they get the energy though, that was the real question.
   “But now I have found it!!  In the heart of the Trojian power lies energy.  Trojja could run for days, fight for weeks, throw men and burn them with his mind, this is the power that could destroy a heart.  I discovered it after I heard the account of the death of U’mar.  Lief, Adelia, and Eric all fought him.  The only time he gave up was when Eric used his power on him.  Then he killed himself to preserve the heart.”  Brazen looked tired, pleased with himself as well.
   “Could you not simply stab the heart?  Surely a solid stab would stop it.”  Brazen sighed.
   “Alas, it is not so.  The heart is much more, or less, than a physical heart.  It is more alike to a pocket of power inside the Y’marr.  It would need energy pumped into it for it to be destroyed.”  Revlin began to speak, but Ignis spoke first.
   “Makes sense, dunno why but it does.  What if you found a way to carry it?  Like…”  He was interrupted by Revlin before he finished.
   “Chromatis!!  That’s what you need.  I’m sure enough of it could hold all the energy, plus extra.”  He was erratic, if only he could show them his device.  Ohhh if only…
   Brazen shrugged.  “Maybe it is possible, we’ve never discovered someone who could fill it up before.  I had thought about it, maybe it would allow somebody other than Eric to destroy a Y’marr.  Good idea Revlin, I knew I was right about you.”  Revlin grinned.
   “Let’s do it then, we should all meet up.  This dream talking is rather limited.  Those of us near the south and west could all go to Shan.  Then travel on to meet up with you near Tora, or some other town.”  They all nodded.
   “I agree with Revlin.  Every one of them is vulnerable while they are alone.  The more of us that are together the safer we are.”  Eric was set now, Brazen knew him well enough to know that nothing would stop him now.  Agreeing was the only option.
   After they had finished voicing their assent Brazen raised a hand.  “Are we to meet in Tora?  Is that pleasing to everyone here, I think that you three should join together as soon as possible.”  They all nodded in agreement.  Adelia bit her lip.
   “What if we met them in Ford, Brazen it’s only three or four days longer for us to go there than for us to go to Tora?  I think it could save us a few weeks by taking that route.”
   “Whichever way is the quickest at getting us together.  Ford sounds like a good idea.  All in favor of meeting in Ford?”  Every hand went up.  Lief grinned, “Ford it is then, I think you should all work on refining and discovering your gift along the way.  We won’t have more than a couple weeks before the Y’marr will hunt down a group that big in a territory.”
   No one said anything for a moment.  Brazen straightened his back and groaned.  “I’m afraid I can no longer hold on to the dream.  I will call up another if you are not in Shan when we arrive.”  They all nodded, and the dream flickered.  “I shall see you all soon, goodnight.”  The dream flickered again, then went black.  Seven people woke up suddenly at the same time.  Many of their lives would never be the same again.




















Chapter 26:  Training In the Courtyard


   Eric woke that morning to the sound of voices in the street, and the light of the sun peeking through the curtains.  He remembered the night before and sighed audibly.  Just as soon as they got off the road they were headed right back too it.  The ray of sunshine in all the muck of promises was that his wyvern was on its way.  Wouldn’t be long before evening that it’d show up, he’d head out to the fields around Thumbria so that he didn’t panic anyone inside the walls.  That surely wouldn’t go over well with the guard either.  Calling just to have the wyvern shot seemed like a miserable waste of effort.
   He rolled out of his cot and trudged towards the kitchen.  White spots appeared in his vision as he walked into bright sunlight that shone through the window.  There was a furnace burning in the corner that provided heat to the apartment, a chimney kept the house from filling with smoke.  He made some tea and deposited three spoons of sweetsalt into it.  He drank his tea slowly, waking his mind up.
   After finishing the tea he washed the dish then went back to the shop to retrieve his lance.  He had to keep in shape, and running some forms would do just that.  He winced as his aching muscles protested to the stairs he was walking down.  After he had his lance in his hands he walked out the door at the back of the shop.  
   Behind the shop a neat area was fenced in.  The fence was built high, higher than any of the others in this area, and stood perhaps ten feet tall.  One couldn’t see through it if they tried, the wood planks were nailed tight together, some people must enjoy their privacy.
   He began warming up his muscles.  He ran around the courtyard for ten minutes, worked the muscles in his arms and legs with stretches, and practiced making a few links on the stone path Brazen had made in his courtyard.
   Now he sat on the ground and closed his eyes, he could feel energy flowing around him.  He shut out his other senses, let the energy perception come over him.  He could feel the energy of the people on the street outside the fence, a dog two houses over, a couple making love, especially the furnace in Brazen’s house.  It was a fairly new experience, the details were fuzzy though.  Because energy is always being transferred things took on a flowing effect.  Outlines were blurry, he could still generally make out the shape of something a reasonable distance away, assuming it had energy to emit.
   He stood and walked over to the wall where he’d put his lance, he picked it up and walked to the center of the courtyard.  He began to run through the beginning forms of Stahl’s Omission, this combination required him to exercise his dexterity with sweeps, and flowing strikes.  The combination was about five minutes long, and he performed it effortlessly.  He launched straight into Trojja’s Fury, this combination was a test of speed and skill with a lance.  Quick strikes pummeled the air in front of him, and he sent out kicks like lightning.  He finished the combination by stepping back, then threw the spear into the air.  Many people who practiced left out the last part, it required an enormous amount of skill.  Eric confidently jumped into the air, thrust the butt end of the spear to the ground and balanced by holding himself straight up in the air.  His legs made a perfect line with the spear on the ground, he held it for a moment then bent his elbows and pushed off into the air.  He landed with a flourish and caught the lance that came down a split second after he did.
   He stood breathing heavily for a moment, damn it’d been awhile since he’d done that.  He turned his head to the sound of someone clapping.  Brazen walked out into the courtyard slowly, looking straight into Eric’s eyes like he was going to laugh.  “Well done warrior, have any other tricks to show?” 
   Eric shrugged, he hadn’t known the man long and he wasn’t overly comfortable with showing him what he could do with his gift.  “Maybe some other time, I’m a bit tired now.”  Brazen laughed loudly.
   “You expect me to believe that eh, I may be old as dirt but I’m not nearly as dumb as shit…at least not now anyway.”  He raised an eyebrow.  “Go ahead, we don’t have time to pussyfoot around.”  The old man walked over and picked up a crate that was by the house.  He opened it and Eric saw that it was full of apples.  There was perhaps fifty inside.
   “What do you expect me to do with those, eat them?”  Brazen ignored him and threw one of the apples at him.  Eric caught it deftly, considered it for a moment, and took a bite.
   Brazen gave him a look that made him spit the apple out, it didn’t taste good anymore.  “Why don’t you tell me what you can do with them, instead of expecting me to show you.  Which I can’t do by the way, just in case you were wondering.”  Eric threw the apple into the air. 
   He made a quick link with it and sent apple mash all over the yard.  Brazen licked the tart juice from his lips and threw him another one.  “This time don’t just blow it up, do something interesting eh?  How about……smash it into the ground?  Yes that should work.”  Brazen crossed his hands in front of him and looked at him expectantly.
   Eric threw the apple into the air, this time he converted the energy into mechanical form and applied it at the top of the apple.  A chunk of it flew off, but the rest shot towards the ground.  Exploding on impact and splattering them with juice again.
   “Dammit boy is that all you can do, blow things up?  My wife was better than that.  Show me some manipulation, do something fancy.  Work the energies the same way you worked with that spear of yours.”  He put his hands on his hips and wiped a piece of apple from his face.  He threw Eric another apple and braced himself, probably for more carnage.
   Eric held his hand out, instead of throwing the apple he gazed into its energies.  The subtle flow made it seem almost invisible in that world of shifting shapes.  He reached out with a link towards it, he saw as a ripple seemed to travel through the air wherever the link was made.  He tried bending the link, making it into a circular shape.  He could see it now, he made the energy in the air coalesce until it was moderately solid, then he tried to fit it around the bottom of the apple.  
   The apple wobbled, but he couldn’t seem to control the way the energy moved.  No matter how much he put into the link the energy just moved.  He could shoot energy straight through a link, but now he needed it to come from every bit that touched the apple.  
   He tried again, this time he put more into it.  The apple rose two feet, then fell back to his hand.  He grinned, he did the same thing again and again.  Then he moved his hand and bounced the apple using only the link.  He moved the link further up into the air, and reduced the amount of bounce he was giving the apple.  After a minute or so he was able to mostly stabilize it, with only a little wobble every once in awhile.
   He grinned at Brazen, but the old man looked unimpressed.  “Did you really just figure this out?”  Eric’s grin soured and he let the apple drop.  “The last Trojian heir could’ve done that in a heartbeat.  He was strong enough to juggle ten men and not even blink.  We’ve got a lot of work to do before you’re fit to battle another Y’marr.  Even a fringe bane might be able to best you.”  Eric grunted.  “Eh you think you could just blow its head off, or overload it?  I suppose about five of you could do that, if you wanted to kill one.  They travel in groups of three by the way, never alone.  Masters of manipulating energy, ever convince one to teach you and you could be the strongest man in the world.”
   “What’re fringebanes?  I’ve never heard of them.  What do you mean by masters of manipulation, if they have no energy how can they beat me?”  Brazen sighed.
   “Stupid boy, always thinking roundabout, you think you’re the only one that can manipulate energy like that?  There are many people that can manipulate energy at one degree or another.“  He remembered Gerik, didn’t he say something about how every sky knight was a crafter?  “Only thing about fringebanes is this, they’re not natural.  These people are nearly extinct, live on the edge of the blasted world they do.  Been hoarding all the knowledge they can find and keeping it from the rest of us.  I hear they have knowledge on nearly everything anyone could ask for, but every one of them has the strength of one-hundred crafters.”  Eric’s jaw dropped.  “I hear they can craft rocks into gold, water into metal, and coal into jewels.  They can heal people with crafting, and find wells underground.  They can make crops grow, and make people fall in love with each other.  Only problem is, YOU CAN’T FIND THE BASTARDS!!”  Eric flinched.
   “Well where could they be?  Must have a city of some sorts somewhere, you’d just have to look hard enough.”  Brazen laughed, cold and sharp.
   “Ohh I’ve found them before, only they move.  Never in the same place twice, and never in one place more than a month.”  Eric rubbed at his stubble, thinking.
   “Well guess that’s that, if we can’t find them we better not worry about it.  I can still kill Y’marr with my crafting, and I can work with Revlin to make the keys.  Pretty soon all of us descendants will be able to kill them.”  Brazen grunted.
   “Hmmph, better make damn sure.  Ohh yeah I’ve been doing some research, and I just might have found something worth finding.”  He reached into his pocket and pulled out a scrawled note.  He handed it to Eric.
   He looked down at it, “Luxerne” was hastily scrawled on it.
   “What does it mean?”  Eric asked.  The word stirred him, he couldn’t place the feeling.  
   “Means lightwalker.  It’s what the descendants used to be called.  It sounds a lot nicer than walking around calling each other ‘descendants’ all the time.”  Eric gazed down at the parchment again.
   “Lux…Luxernee.  Hmm I like it.”
   “Luxer-NAY boy.  Old language follows different rules.”  The man rubbed his elbows.  “I better get inside, gotta mix up a tonic for this damned arthritis.  Don’t get old boy, it won’t do you any good.”  With a grin he walked back to the house.
   Eric sighed and carried the apples over by the edge of the fence.  He picked one up and lifted it clumsily with a link.  Determined, he worked all morning until he heard shuffling steps come out of the house.
   Wordlessly Adelia came and sat next to him.  His concentration slipped and he dropped the apple he had been balancing, he swore and she looked at him grinning.
   “Ahhh you must work less on study, and more on concentration.  What if that happened in a battle.”  He snarled at her and she giggled.
   “In a battle you wouldn’t be wearing a skirt.”  She was wearing a skirt, he was surprised.  That had been the direct cause of him dropping the apple.  Well, maybe not just the dress…
   “Ha, crazy lowlander.”  She picked up the apple from his hand and took a bite out of it.
   “Hey, what was that?”  She laughed.
   “An apple, now it is only part of an apple.  Goodbye lowlander.”  She walked back into the house.  
   Eric swore, his eyes followed her as she walked back to the house.  Her hips swayed perfectly when she walked, and the luscious curve of her spine held hidden promises.  No dammit, look away.  After he did he regretted it, and his eyes took her in again.  When she passed through the doorway and closed the door a little creeping feeling passed through him, why should her hips matter anyway?
   He pushed the thought from his mind and grabbed another apple from the box.  














Chapter 27:  Flight


   The apple spun through the air.  He ran it through figure-eights and loops for a few minutes before letting it drop back in the box.  Yes!!  I’ve got it.  He thought to himself.  After nearly all of a day he had managed to mostly master that type of link.
   He let out a deep breath and lay back into the grass, the sounds of the city washed over him.  All the people walking through the streets.  He heard snippets of conversation.  “Five gold for that, they must be insane!” from one man, “You have to tie it tight, else the machine’ll knot up” from a woman.  He lay there listening for awhile until he felt something.
   It felt like a pressure entered his mind, he could feel a new source of energy far away.  To him it blazed like a beacon, but he could sense that it didn’t have an abnormal amount of energy.  He focused his perception on that point of light.  For a moment he saw a shape, than he shot up from the ground.
   His wyvern was coming at last!  He ran through the house, grabbing the rolled up makeshift saddle he’d made with Vallech, and flew out the front door startling one of Brazen’s customers in the process.  Ignoring the shouts from the old man, he ran towards the western gate.  It wasn’t far, in two minutes the guard had motioned the gate open and he walked out into fields around Thumbria.  
   He could smell the wheat that was recently harvested.  The sharp scent tingled at the edge of his senses as he walked along the main road leading out of the city.  It was quite worn from the hundreds of feet that walked over it every day.  He walked a mile out, then turned ninety degrees towards the higher grass away from the road.
   He ran.  He ran like he had never run before.  In all of his exercising during his time in the military never before had he felt the desire to run.  To fight yes, to use his lance yes, but never to run.  After running for awhile he still felt the desire unstated.  He drew from his source and flew forward through the field.  Yes, this is what he was looking for.  The grass parted before him in a blur as he flew through the field at an inhuman pace.  The energy flowing outward from his source was like a white-hot river pouring forever outward.  He let it fill his senses and continued through the field.
   After what felt like an hour, or was it just a minute, he couldn’t  tell.  He felt another presence nearby, it had the same glowing energy he had sensed before in the city.  It was close now, close enough that when he looked into the sky he could see it with his eyes.  It was small at the moment, but it was increasing steadily.
   He slowed to a stop and raised a hand to the sky waving it back and forth.  The figure came towards him, and as it neared he began to make out its features.
   The wyvern, for it was most definitely a wyvern, was bright red.  This was its most unique characteristic.  It hovered for a moment above him, then lowered itself down about ten yards away from him.  It was more muscular than most of its kind, considerably large for its age as well.  How long had it been since the egg had hatched, not more than two months.  Yet the creature before him was easily as large as most of the fully grown mounts he had seen with the company.  Its face was less angled as well, the eyes seemed to be more noticeable than they were on most others.  The eyes were pitch black, it had no white there.
   It was difficult to fully take in all of its features.  Was this really the same creature he’d held in his arms that night around the fire?  This being looked completely different, and the amount of energy it emitted was enormous.  Eric shied away from it momentarily at the assault on his sense.  The wyvern blazed with an enormous amount of internal energy.  No wonder he’d been able to sense it from so far away.  
   The vibration of energy being emitted changed pitch.  Changing from high-pitched with exertion, to a more low-pitched “pleased” sound.  It padded over to him and arched its neck to look down upon him.  Eric reached a hand out towards it and began to run his palm over its chest.  Its energy hummed even louder and he smiled.  The wyvern must be able to sense his energy the same way, because it bent down and nuzzled into his chest at the feeling of his pleasure.  
   After a moment Eric pulled back.  He was thoughtful and the wyvern cocked its head slightly at the feeling.  “You know I’ve been wondering about something.  None of the knights ever really named their hatchlings did they?”  From what Brazen told him the creature surely understood common tongue by now.  It wrinkled its brow at the words.  It looked like it understood him, just wasn’t sure of the meaning behind the words.  It must have very little experience understanding speech.  “A name yes?  I think that would be good.  What do you think?”  
   If a wyvern could shrug that’s what it would have done.  It seemed of very little concern to him.  “Alright then, what if I just threw out some suggestions and you just nod or shake your head?”  The wyvern nodded this time, letting out a snort of amusement.  
   “I suppose I could use some of the ancient tongue, that might make it leave a bit more of an impression don’t you think?”  Without even looking at the creature he continued.  “How about……Khu’lir?  That means knife.”  The creature thought for a moment then shook its head.  “Hmmm maybe Phyros, Brazen said that’s kind of like a fire that doesn’t burn out?”  The wyvern thought for a moment, then shook its head again.
   Tellaire, Umbraix, God’their, were some of the suggestions turned down by the creature.  None of the names he suggested seemed to fit.  He shrugged impatiently and jumped onto its back.
   “Who cares about names anyway, we can work that out later…”  He reached into his pack and pulled out the rolled leather saddle.  After making a few minor adjustments, for his wyvern was nearly the size of the full-grown one he’d ridden before, he fitted it neatly around his mount.  He jumped onto its back and tied down his leg straps.  He patted its neck, “C’mon let’s go!”  Immediately it shot into the air.
   The ground fell away from them quickly.  Almost instantly he lost all sense of direction and decided to concentrate on just holding on to the straps.  After a moment they leveled off and he was able to find his balance again.
   They were quite high, he could see far over the mountains on the northern side of Thumbria.  It’d been quite a long time since he’d flown and he felt at home in the open skies.  They flew for nearly an hour before he guided the creature down towards the forest.  Brazen had a small stable there for any travelers passing through the area who didn’t necessarily want to be seen about the town.  This was usually where anybody associated with the du vron dezdant visiting on business was housed.  It had plenty of room for the wyvern to stay, though it would be difficult to persuade it to do so.  
   They landed and followed the path he’d been shown, and arrived at the stable just before sunset.  He found the most spacious stall, usually where the carts were stored, and placed his hand on the wyvern.  He opened a link and impressed upon it the need for subtlety.  The creature hesitated, then proceeded into the area he’d prepared.  He left the door open, it would need the air and he didn’t think a door could stop it anyway.  Then, after a moment’s hesitation, he walked the path back to the Western gate of Thumbria.  
   By now night had fallen, but he had no difficulty finding the apothecary.  He walked through the front doors and up the stairs to the guest room.  He saw Lief already asleep on his cot and wasted no time collapsing down onto his own.  


















Chapter 28:  Travels in Rema


   It was just before dusk and the city of Remes and Revlin had just left his hovel.  During the day he stood out too much, he had travelled primarily at night for the larger part of his life.  Always dodging Guards and street urchins, he’d made it by himself for a long time.
   Earlier that day he’d gathered up his pathetically small pile of belongings.  He had two sets of clothes, both were in need of a wash, and a small box that held his tools.  Mostly all he carried was the basic blacksmith’s array of tools, a hammer, some pliers, a small bucket of nails, and a couple other small acclimations he’d acquired over the years.  There was no way he’d be able to carry his whole set with him, some of his special creations weighed over twenty pounds.  
   He did take his flame bow though, he was most proud of this tool he’d made a year or so back.  It had an iron canister on the back that held a mixture of corn oil, coal dust, and a type of mineral he’d dubbed phorys.  The mineral, when mixed with a liquid, would convert it into gas form.  The gas would gain pressure inside of the iron canister, until it would have enough power to propel itself into the air when he pressed a switch located behind the canister.  He’d tapered the end, like a nozzle, so that he could direct the flow.  Although he couldn’t directly melt any metal with it, he could start a fire with ease almost anywhere.  He also knew how to build a temporary ford, and knew what minerals made good anvils.
   He glanced at the city gate, he lived just outside of the city in an old torn down house where there was barely room inside to have a fire.  He could just see the morning patrol leaving the gates headed towards their assigned routes.  He’d memorized the guards’ routes by heart, and he knew side-alleys.  He calculated and cross-referenced their paths, and chose the safest way through the forests that surrounded the city.
   He walked along the road for a bit, then turned into the woods to avoid the outpost a little ways down the road.  He tripped on a tree root but trudged on as if it hadn’t happened.  After some time he came to a ravine that ran deep in the forest.  He analyzed it cautiously and decided it was safe enough to just slide down and climb up the other side.
   Holding his sack to his chest he slid down the mossy slope on his backside, probably looking very out-of-character by doing so.  He never would’ve done this before, his clothes needed to last him awhile and there was no reason to risk tearing his leggings, but the rebellious notion had streaked through his mind without warning.
    Well that was quite exhilarating, he thought.  Next time why don’t I jump off the waterfall, or swim through the river.  Think Revlin, use your brain.  But he still couldn’t help but grin when he remembered the slide down.  He shook his head, appalled at himself, and continued through the woods to the point where it was safe to get back on the road.
   He adjusted his repeater crossbow into an easily accessible position, he had heard of bandit attacks in this area.  Thieves, cutthroats, and deserters often found the travelers here loaded with coin.  Many Trojian caravans passed through Remes before moving forward into the plains of Vervadia.  They made easy prey for the scoundrels.
   Luckily the rest of the day was quite eventless, he walked for seventeen miles which was one mile more than he needed to walk to arrive at Ford two days early.  He liked giving himself plenty of extra time.  One never knew what might happen along the road.
   He found a nice clearing off the road and decided to pack it up for the night.  He trudged off the path, bones weary from the hours of walking.  One might never guess how tiring simple walking can be when it is done for long amounts of time.  He had a hard time sitting down, his back seemed to be stuck in its upright position from walking.  Eventually he managed to lie down on his mat, after easing down slowly he found its warmth refreshing.  He tucked his hammer into his belt, and made sure his weapons were within arm’s reach before he fell asleep, he at least had the sense to do that before falling to sleep.
   He hadn’t had time to dream before he was awoken by someone dragging him by his hair out of his mat.  He didn’t pause to think, bandits had been known to capture and rape young travelers, he jabbed the man behind him in the neck with the knuckle in his thumb and dove towards his crossbow.
   He rolled on the ground and scooped up the weapon, then turned to face his assailants.  Two men stood by the fire with knives in their hands, they began walking towards him slowly.
   “Go on kid, just put that down and we can ‘ave us a little chat.  Doesn’t that sound nice?”  His partner chuckled.  Just then the man on the ground gave a shudder and stopped making the choking noises.  “Blimey ‘e killed Travis.  Blasted idiot ‘e was” The man shrugged.  “More for me then,” and the two men began to circle him like wolves.
   Revlin grinned on the inside, let’s see how they like this.  He flipped off the safety on his repeater and sent a bolt into one man’s foot.  He bellowed as the other one rushed him with a triumphant look on his face.  Revlin paused until just before the man swung, downward and to the left if I was betting, he dropped to his right knee and hit the man in the ribs with his palm.  
   He should bend like...  The man absorbed the blow and his hip was forced to slide, Revlin’s palm shot towards his ear.  And now he’ll fall to the knee…  When the man’s left knee hit the ground Revlin stepped on the man’s foot with one leg, and dislocated his knee with the other.  Now when his face hits the ground…  He gave a forceful stomp on the back of the man’s neck and heard a satisfying snap of bone.
   He immediately turned towards the final assailant, but saw no one.  He had slipped away while Revlin had been killing the second man.  No matter, he shan’t bother me again.  Not able to get back to sleep he lay on his back, gazing up at the stars until morning came.  Then he packed up his sack, and headed back onto the road.
   The next night he lay awake for an hour, in case the final assailant returned.  But he did not, nor did he return any of the three nights after that.  It had seemed that he was outside of that particular group’s authority.
   The next day he came to a bridge that provided a much easier route for traveling people than the path.  He began to walk across the bridge without a suspicious thought in his body.  I’m making quite good time, I shall most likely be the first one to arrive in Ford at this rate.  He was so deep in thought that he did not notice the men in the bushes that he had passed at the beginning of the bridge.  He saw six men walk out of the bushes in front of him.  
   He pulled out his crossbow, aimed at one of the men, and fired towards him.  He had, however, forgotten to load the crossbow at his camp that morning so nothing at all happened when he did this except for a small click from the device.  He swore and drew his flame bow, but that was no good either.  It needed more phorys before it could be fired because he had used it the last few nights to start a fire.
   He turned to flee but was trapped from both sides.  He glanced off the edge of the bridge, but that was much too far of a drop, he’d be lucky if he lived a fall like that let alone escape his pursuers.  The only choice he had was to try and negotiate with them.
    One of the men held up a hand for them to stop.  Revlin moved towards him with his hands raised, “Please, I have little money but all I have is yours.”  The leader tilted his head to the side so that he could listen to a man whispering in his ear.  A shiver ran through his spine, that was the man who survived his first night out.  A cold sweat broke over his whole body as the leader spoke.
   “I hear you are responsible to the deaths of two of my men.  Is this true?”  Revlin nodded at the man.
   “Yes, three of your men attacked me in my sleep.  I killed two of them and shot this man in the foot.”  He gestured towards the man beside him.  
   The leader shrugged.  “Then that is all we need to know.”  He whistled sharply, turned his back, and swirled his hand in a circle.  
   The bandits pounced on him, he didn’t even try to resist, it would have been a futile attempt if he had.  
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Sarai woke with a start.  She was sitting up now, with her hands covering her eyes.  Flashes of memories shot through her, of her family lying dead in a pile of rubble, of days of searching just for something to eat, of a strange gathering, and now the boy of that gathering being captured by bandits.  
   She shook her head as if to clear it out, that boy was one of them wasn’t he?  She could remember the gathering quite clearly, but the dream of the boy was in fuzzy detail.  She could see his face, but none of the others, she could tell they were on a bridge, but everything else was out of focus.
   The boy had said he was from Remes hadn’t he?  If that was true than he shouldn’t be very far away from where she was now.  She had stayed at an inn about three days walk northeast of Remes.  If they had started at the same time he should only be a day or so ahead of her.  
   Troubled, she washed and gathered her things.  Instead of going down the stairs on her left that lead to the common room, she walked along the corridor until she found the hatch that lead to the cellar.  As she made her way down the ladder a sliver of guilt shot through her mind, but she pushed it aside, she had barely had enough gold to pay the man the deposit on the room.  She surely couldn’t have paid the full price of the room.
   She made her way out of the cellar and onto the street.  It was a very small town and everybody around her seemed to be related in some way.  She had to leave town without any of them seeing her, or they may tell the innkeeper before she had time to get away.  
   She decided to cut through the woods, it was probably the safest way, and made her way back to the road.  She walked until about noon before finding a well along the road to drink from.  As she held the bucket filled with water she glanced at her reflection in it. 
    She was a slight girl, smaller than most of those around her, and all of her features amplified her smallness so that she looked smaller than nearly every other woman she’d encountered.  The water reflected a travel-stained and weary face back to her.  She almost didn’t recognize this woman staring back at her.  Perturbed, she drank from the bucket and filled her canteen full to the brim when she was done.  
   She took her map and compass out of her pack to check her position, she was glad her father had taught her how to use those tools.  She noticed that there was a bridge marked not far ahead of her, probably only five or six miles, maybe it was the same bridge she’d seen the boy at.  She supposed she would find out.
   She rounded a corner in the road and the bridge came into sight.  It was definitely the same place she’d dreamed about.  She walked to the place where she’d dreamed the boy and knelt to examine the ground.  There were many pairs of footprints, more than what would be on a seldom-crossed bridge.  She didn’t know how to read the other signs though.  It had been Revlin, she no longer doubted that, but she only remembered up to the part where they drug him into the woods. 
   She walked to the other side of the bridge and saw the path they’d taken, it led straight into the heart of the forest and she was wary to travel it.  She was deep in thought, but before she decided what she was going to do she heard a voice behind her.
   “Following them too?”  She spun around and saw a rather tall man standing behind her.  He had a full beard and was sporting leather armor.  He had a battleaxe through one of his belt loops and a crossbow strapped behind his back.  He had an enormous grin on his face as he listened to her response.
   “Who do you mean by ‘them?’”  He snorted.
   “I thought it was obvious, the Hellhounds are such a large group they usually attract a lot of ambitious understudies.”
   Her eyes widened, “No that’s not… I mean I’m not……They’ve kidnapped one of my friends!”  
   At hearing this he let loose a deep growl.  “Damn bastards, should’ve known they wouldn’t stay low.  They’ve been hiding out for awhile, they probably ran out of supplies and started raiding the routes again.”  He shook his head.
   Sarai wrinkled her brow.  “So how far into these woods do you think they are?”  He just shrugged.  
   “Who knows, I would recommend heading down that road back there across the bridge.  After five or six miles you should find a small settlement.  Maybe you can enlist some help from the locals.”  He patted her on the shoulder.  “Good luck little girl, I hope you find your friend.”
   He began to walk away as a wave of hopelessness washed over her.  How was she supposed to undertake a rescue by herself?  She would be hopelessly overpowered, and after leaving the inn without paying she doubted anyone would help her anyway.  She made up her mind after a moment of thought.
   “Hey!”  He didn’t turn around.  “HEY!”
   This time he turned towards her.  “What do you want, I thought I answered all of your questions?”  
   She ran towards him, almost tripping more than once.  “I…I forgot to ask you what your name was?”  
   He looked down at her with an amused smile.  “It would also seem you forgot to tell me yours.”  She blushed and after a few seconds he said.  “My name’s Hart, Hart Treeman.”  
   She looked back at his face, she hoped she wasn’t blushing.  “My name’s Sarai Truvard…”  She broke off before finishing.
   Anticipating her unasked question he grinned and spoke again.  “You want me to come and help you?”  Speechless she nodded quickly.  “I thought so, girl like yourself always needs help with something.”  She opened her mouth but nothing came out.  She looked down at the ground without saying anything.  “Uh, well I’m not sure if I’ve got the…”  She looked up at him with tears in her eyes.
   “P-Please, I c-c-can’t d-do it alone.  And they’ll k-kill him if we don’t do anything soon.”  
   Hart blinked.  He wasn’t sure what to do.  “I…I suppose I could maybe help out.”
   She looked at him quickly.  “Ohh could you?  I’m so sorry, I must be a huge bother.  You don’t have to, I can always…do it myself.”
   He blanched when he heard this.  “No, I can definitely help you.  Truth is I’ve been looking for the Hellhounds for quite some time now.  It’s no problem at all, I can help find him.”
   “Thank you!  What should we do first, I’ll help you however I can.
   Hart grinned.  “The only thing I ask,” she nodded quickly.  “Is that you stay out of my way…”


































Chapter 29:  An Old Friend


   Ignis awoke with a start.  He rolled onto his back and stared up at the sky while rubbing one of his palms against his forehead.  He’d had such a strange dream, there had been seven people there.  He tried to get the image out of his head but nothing he did could make him forget.  They’d said something about the Stewards…and about Y’marr.  
   It was still nighttime, or at least close to dawn, so he stood up off of his mat.  His joints cracked as he stretched himself in the morning silence, the sound seemed to travel for miles.  He stood there staring at the mouth of the valley for awhile.   
   He made up his mind, one person couldn’t help much here, even if they were as talented as he was.  He would leave here, though he doubted he’d ever be able to return.  Deserters were punished heavily, usually by hanging, and he did not think he would be much use hanging by a rope.  He would travel to Shan, meet up with the boy and the girl, then travel on to Ford to meet the others.  He could tell that these people were talented like him and he was anxious to hunt these Y’marr they spoke of.
   He packed his bag quickly, shoving all of his belongings into the bag as fast as he could.  He put his bow in its sheath across his back, and his knife at his belt.  He threw his pack overtop the bow and began to walk towards the edge of the camp, towards the mouth of the valley.  
   Before he had walked ten steps however he heard someone shout behind him, he hissed in frustration and turned to silence them.  As he turned he recognized Russel getting to his feet.  “Where the hell do ya think yer…”  He was cut off when Ignis made a cutting motion across his lips, indicating silence.  Russel made a confused face, and walked closer to him before whispering.
   “Ye can’t be desertin’ can ye?  I shan’t believe me own eyes, what the blazes do ye think yer doin’?”
   Ignis sighed, he wasn’t sure what to tell him, could he tell him about the dream?  No Russel would think he was crazy.  “I…mind yer own business Russ, I can take care o’ meself.  If-if ye mus’ know I’ve been reassigned.  Up north, there’s some new front and the gen’ral assigned me specific-like, so you jus’ go back te sleep and don’t bother yer mind with it.”
   Russel’s jaw dropped.  “Bu-but jus’ wait one minute you, I’m leader o’ this regiment and I didn’ get no messages from no commanding officer.”  
   Ignis restrained from cursing.  “Russ, I jus’ have ta.  You understand I can’ tell you everything, it’s too sensitive.”  
   Russel puffed up his chest.  “Well I’m not lettin’  ye leave here.  I’m sorry, but you’ll have to get the commander ta speak ter me himself.”  His jaw was set, there was no moving past him.
   Ignis looked at the ground.  Sorry Russ, he thought as he made to set his bag down.  While setting it down he picked up one of the fist-sized rocks lying on the ground.  He saw Russel’s eyes grow large, but he wasn’t fast enough to stop the rock that hit him in the side of the head which knocked him out cold.
   He drug him back to his mat, it should be a few hours before he was found unconscious.  They would send out a search party, but no one would guess that he had run towards the enemy lines.  They would assume he’d tried to escape home.  Yes, he was home free.
   He picked up his sack but, once again, he heard a voice speak his name.  This time however it was in hushed tone.
   “Hey you, Ignis!!”  Ignis shot around and looked towards where the voice had come from, but when he saw who it was his jaw dropped.
   It was Droft, his childhood friend.  “Droft?”  He was in disbelief, he didn’t know he’d been posted here as well.  “What the blazes are you doin’ here?”
   Droft grinned, he remembered that grin from his past.  There hadn’t been a day that they spent apart till they joined the Du Vron Dezdant.  “Same thing as you I s’pose, killin’ some Trojians.”  He glanced at Ignis’s pack and frowned.  “Been reassigned?”
   Ignis’s mouth went dry.  “Well…er not exactly.”  
   Droft looked perplexed, “Well than why…are you?”  He seemed to be searching for what to say.  “You’re not deserting are ye?”  
   Ignis was at a loss for words, should he tell him about the dream?  He’d always been able to tell him everything, and it would be hard to go on alone.  But he couldn’t expect him to desert his post just because of some dream he’d had, it would be the same as betraying the man.
   “You are aren’t ye, no don’t answer.”  He let out a shuddering breath, and Ignis noticed the blade strapped across his back.  He didn’t have a shield, but something about the blade jogged his memory.
   “Jus-just don’t stop me Droft.  You know I wouldn’ be doin’ this if I didn’t have ta.”  
   Droft snorted, and just then he recognized the insignia on the blade.  Two crossed blades on the hilt were barely visible above his shoulder.  Droft was a blademaster.  “Stop yeh?  Stop yeh, naw I wanna come with yeh.”  He was grinning now, and Ignis was at a loss for words.  “We started this together didn’t we?  I ‘spect we oughta end it together too.”
   Ignis almost told him no, that he couldn’t let him leave just for him but then he remembered.  Droft had joined just for him, and he had risen through the ranks at an astounding rate.  He examined the man that had once been his childhood friend and saw the same boy staring back at him.  The same boy, maybe with a somewhat larger and more intimidating physique, but still the same person he’d known before.
   “Droft…are-are ye sure?”  
   He snorted, “As sure as I was when I firs’ said it.  You need someone to cover your arse so ye have time ta get that bow o’ yours ready.”  He grinned sheepishly, “Plus things are dull here, the fighting’s simple, and we’re at a standstill.  I need ta stretch meself out too.”  
   Ignis grinned too, “Alrigh’ then, I ‘spect your almost as good at killin’ as I am eh?”  They both sat there grinning, like two schoolboys about to get into mischief.
   “Well if we’re gonna go we might wanna do it soon.  Them guards’ll be makin’ round afore too long, and we don’t wanna be anywhere near here when they do.”  
   Nodding, Ignis led the way out of the camp along the same path they’d used on their mission the previous day.  Instead of stopping at the lookout point they continued on until they reached the other end of the valley.  They left the opposing armies behind them after only a few hours.
   They would be able to reach Wystland by tomorrow and, if they made good time, in another two days meet up with the other two in Shan.
   That night around their fire Droft was quiet, he seemed to be searching for the right words to say.  Ignis spoke first, already knowing what he was going to ask.  “I suppose you’re wondering why I left aren’t ye?” 
   Surprised Droft nodded.  “I s’pose I was a little interested yeah.”
   Ignis sighed, “Well I’m not too sure meself actually.  It all kinda starts with me bein’ good at shootin.”  Droft shrugged, obviously he was curious though.  “See there’s this group forming up, they got special gifts see.  They’re all related ta them Stewards.  Bit confusing though, I figure we’ll find out more when we meet up with some o’ them in Shan.”
   Droft wrinkled his brow, “You mean you’re related to the Stewards?  That’s…somethin that is.”  His voice trailed off in thought.
   “Yeah, that’s what them people think.”  He left out the part about him seeing them in a dream, he thought that would be a bit over the edge right now.  “We’re meetin some of them in Shan and the rest in Ford.”  
   Droft nodded, “And what are yeh planning ta do after you form this little…group?”
   Ignis scratched his head.  “See this is the part that I’m confused about, they say there’s these things out there that are tryin ta bring the Beast back ter life.  Y-Y’marr they called em I think, and supposedly the only ones that can really fight the Y’marr are the descendants of the Stewards.  Bit complicated, but we’ll find out more when we meet em in Ford I suppose.”
   After hearing this Droft smiled, “Then I guess I’m in the right place then.  There should be plenty o’ action where yer goin.”  He nodded, “And I’ll be good in a fight, can’t wait ta meet these people.  Strongest warriors in the land no doubt.”  He was nodding repeatedly now.
   “well…not really.  One’s naught more than a sprat, and there are two lasses.  Two of them looked like they might stand five or six minutes in a fight, but otherwise they don’ really look much.”
   Droft blanched, “Maybe so, all the more reason ta give em some protection eh?”
   Ignis nodded.  “Yup, they’ll be happy ta see ye.  Think they may be recruitin fer the Dez too.  I dunno but that ole man made sound like he was high up in the rebellion.”
   Droft shrugged.  “I guess we’ll find out when we get there eh, I don’ know abou you, but I think I’m gonna call it a night.”
   Ignis laughed, “G’night then, ya ole peckerfish.”  They both laughed for a minute, then layed out their mats.
   Ignis lay there awake for awhile, he was happy to have Droft with him.  It’d be a big help having a blademaster in the group, and they need all the help they can get.  He rolled over and fell asleep, thinking about the long road that lay ahead of them.








Chapter 30:  A Daring Rescue


   “Alright Sarai I want you to stay in this patch of brush, you still have that knife I gave you right?”  Sarai nodded vigorously.  Her curls bobbing against her shoulders as she did so, sending her scent towards him like a cloud.  It was sweet and subtle, like the smell of dew in the morning, or the way a flower smells.  Not a strong smell, but a nice one nonetheless.
   He shook his head to clear it out and put one hand on her shoulder.  “If anything happens to me you’ll have to run alright?  No don’t give me that face, if they’ve killed me they’ll be smart enough to know that I was trying to rescue the boy.  They’ll kill him just for spite.”  Sarai had looked at him incredulously for a second, but then set her jaw in a way that simply looked resigned.  Whatever happened she would do what she had to do.
   They both nodded at each other and Sarai moved into the brush.  Hart removed his bow from his shoulder and strung it, he set off into the forest with an arrow already nocked onto the string.  The woods that surrounded this twenty mile stretch of road were thick, and the trees grew tall.  He was weary for any sign of an outpost hidden in the trees.
   He walked for nearly a mile before he smelled the smoke of campfires over the next ridge.  It was nearing sundown so the bandits would probably be getting ready for their evening runs, assigning groups of three or four to different stretches of road.  He inched his way over the rest of the hill and looked down at the encampment in the valley.
   There were nearly a hundred bandits living in the valley, many of them were dressed as simple commoners to lure people towards them on the roads.  Their tents were forest green, and seemed to blend in with the shade of the valley.  Hart watched as nearly half of them gathered their weapons, readying themselves for an evening of robbery along the main routes.
   Almost too late he realized that they would be walking right into him when they passed, he crouched low and circled around to the other side of the camp where none of them would be walking past, just in time to see a group of eight parade past the spot he’d been hiding before.  He wiped the sweat off his brow and waited for the rest of the highwaymen dissipate, leaving twenty or so behind.
   He watched as they all gathered around the fire in the center of their camp.  They were roasting a hog over it on a spit, and he noticed one of them take a plate over to one of the tents in the camp.  He returned from the tent empty-handed.
   If I wagered a guess I’d say they were hiding him in there, he’d see if he was right.  Hart made his way down the edge of the valley and took cover behind one of the trees on the edge of the camp.  He peeked around and saw that nearly all of them were seated around the campfire, talking heartily as they ate their dinner.
   Hart slunk to the outside of the nearest tent, careful to make sure that no one was inside, he settled down to wait.
   A few hours past, it was well past dark when he heard a man walk by the tent.  He walked past the tents towards the woods and stopped.  Hart listened until he heard the splash of liquid hitting the ground, then peeked his head around the corner of the tent to make sure that the man was alone.  He was.
   The man was nearly fifteen feet away and slobbering drunk, not exactly his most difficult target.  He advanced towards him with his knife drawn and held behind his wrist parallel to his forearm.
