That could buy them some time that was needed to continue fortifying the palisade.
   “More than I ever expected Gundleas, it seems that no man has ways with words like you.  What you said back at the Dezdant inspired them, even though you said very little.  With me I brought two regiments, about fifty blademasters.  However, every blademaster that heard you that day decided to join us.”  This was interesting, Gundleas tried to remember if he’d seen many Blademasters that day, but he couldn’t remember.  After a moment, Hordrar seemed to think his reaction was a little under-enthusiastic.  “Gundleas, the entire Order has come with me this day.”  He felt lightness in his chest, the entire Order?  That must be more than fifty surely.  He had never been sure of how many Blademasters existed, they were rather spread out and hardly ever gathered.
   “How many are with the order Hordrar?”  The corners of the man’s lips twitched.  
   “There are one-hundred and forty-nine Blademaster journeymen with us, as well as all three Heads, including myself.”  He let the silence speak after that, this was excellent they could hold here even longer then he had thought.  Gundleas started to speak but the other man held up a hand.  “There is more, we came into contact with a group of Snipers that was posted at the front.  These men met with us and added nearly twenty snipers to the regiment that had already decided to march with us.  We have fifty-nine Snipers that are ready for duty.”
   Gundleas nodded, it was surprising to have so many special-force regiments with the army.  This could have a very positive effect on the battle outcome.  “I thank you Hordrar, your news has brought me great reassurance for the upcoming battle.”
   Hordrar nodded, and they smiled at each other for a moment before they heard a messenger approaching them.  Gundleas knew what the message was, before the man could speak Gundleas shouted for the men to get their shields up.  
   The message was clear, the enemy was approaching their position.  Now that he listened closely he could hear the sound of hundreds of footsteps approaching them.  He shifted nervously where he stood.  The moment was closing in, it wouldn’t be long before this battle would be starting.
   Hordrar said nothing, he nodded to Gundleas and walked away to get the Blademasters into their place at the heart of the Shield wall.  None of the Blademasters used shields, but they would be perfect for mounting a counter-surge, or for being partnered with the front man.
   There were many parts to a successful shield-wall.  It was the most important tactic used in warfare, and often the end result of a battle was decided by whichever side could hold together the longest.  Each man holding a shield had two others supporting him.  One spearman that stabbed over his shoulder, and an axmen behind him, the axmen would be able to swing over the front man’s left shoulder to help cover his left side, while the spearman would watch for an opening in the opposing soldiers defenses.
   The enemy had begun to enter their field of vision, Gundleas watched as they began to slow and then, finally, stop less than one hundred meters away.  It was only a small portion of the army, and he watched as shouts of indignation began to arise from soldiers further back in the mass.  For a few minutes he waited as a team of officers began to gather in front of the army.  After a few more minutes a group of five men rode to the space between battle lines, with a banner of truce raised.
   Gundleas had been prepared for this, and he motioned for his group of officers to come with him.  With him also came Hordrar, Brent, and Whit who were the leaders of the Blademasters.  There were also two Snipers, Trevon who lead them, and also a man called Russel who led the Snipers from the front.
   The group of twelve walked to the area where the Trojian commanders waited, they all seemed underdressed compared to the fine linens the Trojians wore.  They also rode fine horses, of the finest bloodlines, while the Mercian commanders walked on their own feet.  It was almost a representation of the two armies in general.  Fine, civilized Trojians pitted against the Mercian trash of the south.
   Gundleas stopped ten yards away from the Trojians and waited for them to speak.  After about twenty seconds one man dismounted and walked towards Gundleas.  He was a very tall man, with a straight beard that came down to his chest.  The beard looked strange, there was almost no curl to any of the hairs.  The hair on his head was tied at the nape of his neck, and came halfway down his back.  His eyes were bright green, and his features were sharp.  On his back there was a curved blade that seemed to add to the strangeness of his appearance.
   This man had eyes only for Gundleas as he walked forward.  Gundleas met his gaze with a cold, tempered glare.  He realized that his hand was on the hilt of his sword, and he pulled it away once he did.  The two men stood staring at each other for a moment, like the way two dogs stare at each other, hair bristling before a fight.
   The other man spoke first, his voice was cool and measured, every word seemed like it weighed a ton.  “It’s been a long time Gundleas.  Tell me, how is your family doing?”  He was speaking to him like an old friend.  Or at least that would be how it appeared to most of the others around them.
   “They’re quite well J’harra, I’ll be sure to tell them you inquired of their safety.”  The Onyx Knight said nothing.  His gaze shifted to the sky for a moment, like he was sending some thought to the heavens before he turned back to Gundleas and whispered something to him.
   “You can leave Gundleas, my lord’s fight is not with you.  I have no quarrel with you, every one of your men can walk away.  Make this peaceful Gundleas, it doesn’t have to end with blood being spilt today.”  Neither man spoke for a few moments, they both knew how this conversation would end.  They were both, in essence, the same man.  Both great generals of their time, both had an irrevocable sense of pride for their cause.  Neither would give, neither would surrender.  That was fact.
   “You know as well as I do J’harra that doing such a thing isn’t even an option.  This land is ours, always has been.  The reason you’re here is to eliminate the rebel threat, and blood must be spilt for that.  I do recognize the effort you put forth in minimizing your own casualties, though I think you should have known I would never agree to such a thing.”
   The Onyx Knight nodded solemnly, his face betrayed nothing.  He said nothing as he turned and walked back towards his officers, and in truth they were his officers.  None of them could even dream of commanding men as well as J’harra, or Gundleas for that matter.  Before he got to his horse he turned his head slightly, so that one eye was visible.  His voice was so quiet that Gundleas had to strain so that he could hear it.  “I wish it wasn’t so Gundleas, I wish we’d fought under the same flag.  Before, and now.”
   Both groups returned to their lines, it was getting dark and Gundleas doubted that either side would wish to attack the other in the dead of night.  One could never be too sure though, he would set up a watch and send a runner to the raiders in the hills.  Once the fight started the next day they would have orders to attack the rear of their encampment.  
   Gundleas scratched his scalp as he walked back to their battle lines.  Whether he was ready or not the battle was upon him.  Every person in his country depended on what he did tomorrow.
It was hardly a comforting thought…






   Meanwhile Brazen led the group east along the road towards the Glandraung mountain range.  In the five days leading up to the Trojian’s breaking through the pass into Mercia they had made it past Wystland and nearly to the trail Brazen had spoken of in the mountains.  
   “Shouldn’t be much farther along, if I remember correctly the pass is about at the seventy-second mile mark from Wystland.”  They had just passed the seventieth square-shaped stone that marked miles along the road.  In two more miles there should be an unmarked road that led south through the Glandraung mountain range.
   In a little over an hour they came to the seventy-second mile stone.  There seemed to be no trail that led off to the right, had Brazen been mistaken?
   Eric walked over to the stone and ran his eyes over the forests that lay between the road and the mountains to the south.  There didn’t seem to be any break in the trees and brush that he could see.  He turned to Brazen and saw that the old man had walked to the edge of the woods and was motioning for them to follow him.
   Ignis shook his head, “Are ye sure ye wanna be bringin’ that wagon through the brush there?  Don’t look like it’d be too good fer the wheels.”  Sarai bit her lip, and looked like she would object but, instead, shrugged and started away from the road towards the woods.
   Everyone else followed suit and, after a few long minutes of crashing through the woods, they came into a clearing that had obviously been cleared by the hands of men.  On the other end of it there was a path that looked like it was covered in seashells.
   The Mercian’s didn’t make their roads the way the other countries did.  The most widely accepted way was to simply start off with a gravel base, then load bedrock on top of that before covering the very top with bricks.  Mercia, on the other hand, added an extra layer of crushed seashells to their roads.
   Though it made very little difference whether or not there were seashells on the road, it was an obvious marker that this road had been made by the Du Vron Dezdant as an alternate route for their traders to get into Vervadia.  It was actually an ingenious idea for them not to tie it directly into the main road.  That way nobody would stumble onto it.
   Solvyri had a little bit of trouble making his way through the trees, but in the end he emerged into the clearing behind everyone else.  He shook his body to rid himself of the burrs that clung to his underbelly and walked over to where Eric stood.  He nudged him with his head and Eric put his hand to the mark on its chest.  It glowed slightly, and Eric felt the familiar draining sensation he always did whenever he touched the mark.  Eric tapped into his source to make up for the difference and closed his eyes.
   They had decided to camp in the clearing for the night.  Eric started a fire quickly before walking over to Brazen.  The older man was mixing some sort of tonic for Droft, who had had a horrible headache all day.  “Brazen I’m going to fly with Solvyri again tonight, the two of us would like to explore the mountain range.”
   The old man just nodded and continued his work.  Eric ran to Solvyri and settled himself onto the saddle it wore all the time.  He would ride the wyvern during the day sometimes, flying high above the group while they walked slowly.
   They took off and the familiar sensation that he was going to fall hit him hard again, but not as badly as before.  He was indeed getting used to riding this larger wyvern.  Solvyri’s movements were stronger, he could change directions much quicker than his smaller relatives, his tail was much thicker and actually had something resembling a fin on the end of it.  Most wyverns didn’t have this, nor did they have wings as large as Solvyri’s.
   As soon as they took off Eric saw the mountains to the south, they were very large and went on as far to the east and west as he could see.  They rose high in the sky, and the tops of them seemed to disappear in the clouds far above the ground.  Solvyri inclined steadily for a few moments, before leveling off just below the bottoms of the clouds.
   They flew through the mountain range and Eric caught his first glance of Mercia.  Its rolling hills carried on to the end of his vision.  He could see the small path that they were following winding below him as he flew over it.  He would follow it for a few miles and then turn around and head back to camp.
   Just as he was about to turn around he saw smoke in the distance.  It was coming from a patch of woods about a mile away.  He started to turn towards it, but hesitated.  He had no idea if these people were his allies or not.  Even the Du Vron Dezdant might not be comfortable with a sky knight landing in the middle of their camp.
   Before he could make up his mind he heard the sound of beating wings coming from behind him.  He turned and saw Adelia flying towards him on her pegii.  In a way it was a remarkable, if not a little clumsy, beast.  Its coat was completely white, like unturned snow on the ground, and its wings were wide and glorious.  
   Laughing, he shouted at her, “What do you think you’re doing bringing a horse to the skies where the wyvern reign?”  Adelia scowled.  “You should’ve stayed back at camp and brushed its mane a little more, I think I see a burr.”
   That comment sparked a snarl on her face, “I haven’t seen a lizard as fast as my Lyra.  I’ve killed dozens of them because they just couldn’t keep up.”  
   Eric laughed with exaggerated fervor, “You think so do you?  Why don’t you try to keep up with this.”  Solvyri shot towards the ground at a tremendous speed.  Everything around him was a blur as they descended, the wyvern had tucked its limbs and wings close to its body while in the dive.
   They pulled out of it magnificently and shot up towards the clouds, his momentum from the dive made his ascension that much quicker.  He risked a glance over his shoulder and saw Adelia not far behind him.  She’d been right, the pegii was fast.
   Not fast enough he thought, and touched his hand to the back of Solvyri’s head.  He made a link from his source to the wyvern’s heart, funneling it at one end so that he wouldn’t overwhelm him.
   He felt the wyvern’s energy resonate through the air around him as they flew even faster through it.  He glanced behind him to check Adelia again and noticed ripples in the air behind them.  Adelia noticed them too, she slipped inside the stream and began to catch up to them.
   He snarled and sent out mental probes towards the trail behind them, it wasn’t energy that he could manipulate.  It was like an echo, or a ripple.  And it was allowing Adelia to catch up with him.
   He and Solvyri weren’t communicating through touch and voice like normal, they were communicating with energy.  It pulsed and thrummed through them like a river, carrying with it thoughts and emotions.  They shot towards the ground again, this time weaving in a zig-zag pattern so that it would be harder for Adelia to stay in the stream.
   She followed him closely, matching his every move as they wove through the air.  Adelia obviously had experience at this, she was keeping right on his tail no matter how much he swerved and dodged.  He decided to pull out one of the tricks Chala had taught him a few days ago.  He hardened the air in one spot with mechanical energy, then filled it to the brim with raw energy.  
   He let the crackling ball fall behind them, and then burst apart letting loose a huge burst of light.  He heard a cry from behind him as the light blinded Adelia.  He banked hard to the right and watched as Adelia blew past the spot where he’d turned and flew on towards the ground.  She was able to pull up just before crashing into the treetops.
   “Damn you Eric!”  She shouted at him as she flew to him, shaking her head as she did.  “That was cheating, we weren’t dueling.”  He could hear the simmering anger in her voice, and it was quite obvious on her face.
   “Maybe you should be ready for anything, that surely shows whether or not you can adapt to whatever’s thrown at you.”  She snarled and Lyra shot forward in the air towards them.  Solvyri dropped just quick enough, and Eric hardened the air in front of her.  She smashed into it with a loud crack and hovered where she was, trying to regain her bearings.
   Without waiting Eric and Solvyri leapt forward and Eric cracked her in the back of the head with the butt end of his spear.  Solvyri stopped with Lyra’s neck held in his jaws.  The pegii did not struggle, nor did Solvyri bite down, it was a delicate moment as they hovered there in the air.  If Adelia could’ve killed him with a look then the glare she gave him certainly would have.
   Solvyri let go of Lyra’s neck, and they flew a small space away from each other.  For a moment Adelia’s face was completely expressionless, had he gone too far in beating her?  She stared at him for another moment before laughing loudly.  The tension in the air evaporated as quickly as it had come on.  Eric joined in with her laughing until they were both holding their stomachs.
   As she wiped a tear from her eye she smiled and nodded at him.  “I will have to get used to air-dueling a Trojian, I should have known they would resort to anything in order to beat me.”  She gave him an indignant look.  “No matter, I will beat you next time.  I can be equally persistent.” 
   They began laughing again, but then they heard a noise to the south.  Eric had heard the sound a hundred times, it was the sound of an army marching.  The sound of hoofbeats, and boots hitting the ground was like thunder.  People who had never heard an army marching likely wouldn’t understand what he meant, or why he could hear it.  But thousands of small sounds added together and could travel when added up.  
   He glanced nervously at Adelia, they shared the same question in their eyes.  Should they go and discover what such a large group of men was doing marching towards the west.  Had the Trojians broken through the pass?  Last they’d heard the pass was at a complete standstill.  
   “Adelia, you fly back to Brazen and tell him what we’ve heard.  I’m going to go and find out where they’re going.”  She narrowed her eyes at him.
   “What if they are Mercian and they shoot you down, what then?  I think that I should be the one to go and discover who it is that is leading this army.”  Eric shook his head.
   “No Adelia, I would have speaking power if it was the Trojians.  But the Mercian generals already know that we’re on our way.  They wouldn’t shoot a sky knight out of the air, and even if they do I could defend myself better then you could from arrows.”  
   She made like she was going to object again, but Eric flew off toward the sound without waiting to hear what she had to say.  As much as Adelia had fought in small raids, she did not know much about war.  She had never stood on the ground in a battle, and she definitely had no idea how closely generals communicated between forces.  Every small thing was always accounted for, they would know that the descendants were coming.
   The forces came into view after about five minutes of hard flying, there wasn’t as many as he had thought before.  About twelve hundred men were setting up camp in a valley below him.  They let the Mercian flag fly freely above their tents.  This was definitely Dezdant forces.
   He landed in the middle of their camp, next to the general’s tent.  He was immediately swarmed by Mercian soldiers.  They pointed their spears and bows at him.  One of the men stepped forward, he was dressed in the sharp outfit that marked him as an officer.
   “What is your business here Sky Knight?  State it quickly, my men have been itching for some Trojian blood all day.  I cannot wait to satisfy them with yours.”  Some of the men around the circle shouted insults, but Eric stayed passive.  The trick was to act in command, if you thought you were on top of the situation then so would they.
   “I’m here to speak with the commander of this regiment, I have sensitive information for him.”  
   “More like a knife in the back!” One of the Mercians shouted, and the crowd surged forward towards him.  Solvyri growled at them and they faltered.  
   “I do not wish to kill him, I am here to offer up my assistance in the war effort here in Mercia.”  Eric was calm and passive, but not submissive.  He did not hold his spear in his hand, but he held his chin up and spoke to the men like they were his subordinates.
   “And what assistance might one man offer him?”  The officer sneered at him, obviously he had never seen a sky knight before.  Otherwise he would have recognized how different Solvyri was shaped from those wyverns.  His dull colors were exchanged for a blood red, and his pupil-less black eyes blazed with intensity.  He was also much larger than the wyverns, he was nearly twice the size and continued to grow every day.
   “That information is for him, step aside so I may have words with him.  I do not have time for this.”  Eric began to walk towards the tent that the officer was blocking his way towards.  The man drew his sword and sneered at him again.
   “Take one step closer and it’ll be your head, I mean it.  Don’t test me, your words here mean nothing.”  Hearing the threat in his words caused Solvyri to hiss at him, loud and sharp.  The fins along his head, back, and tail stood on end as he bared his teeth.  
   Eric did not stop, he continued walking forward complacently towards the tent.  “So be it,” said the officer as he leapt forward, swinging his blade overhead.
   The blade never met Eric’s skin.  It had met rock-hard air and broke as sparks jumped from where it had struck.  The officer stared at the broken blade in shock as Eric walked right by him, treating him as if he didn’t even exist.
   He strode into the commander’s tent and bowed to the man who sat at his desk there.  He wore a robe and stood as Eric finished his gesture.  “What is it that you need soldier, as you can see I am quite busy at the moment.”  
   Eric stepped forward.  “Greetings commander, I am here to offer up the assistance of myself and the Descendants of Mercolas.  I am sure you received word of us from Brazen.”  As soon as he heard this the commander’s eyes widened.  
   “Yes I have, is what they say true?  Are you as mighty as they claim?”  Eric nodded.
   “Yes sir, we have much to offer for the resistance.  We claim independence, we owe allegiance to no country, but to the continent of Mercolas in its entirety.  But we believe that the Trojians should not be allowed to make such a serious advance against the people of Mercia.  We are here to assist you in the war effort.”  
   The commander nodded and stepped forward, his hand outstretched.  Eric took it, and they shook hands.  “Call me Jared son, no need for titles here.  I’d be honored to have you fight with us against the Trojians, from what I’ve heard we need all the help we can get anyways.”  He shook his head.  “I wouldn’t turn any man aside right now, let alone one with as much to offer as you.”  He glanced over Eric’s shoulder.  “Where are the rest of you?  There are more right?”  
   Eric nodded.  “They will be here in the morning, we’re camped on the other side of the Glandraung Mountains.  I will fly them here as soon as the sun rises on Solvyri, as long as there is a place for the two of us to sleep in your encampment?”
   Jared nodded his head, “Of course there is, the two of you can have a place with the Runemasters on the eastern side of camp.  Umm, would your companion like to step inside for a moment, I’m sure I would love to speak with him as well.”
   Eric smiled.  “I think Solvyri would have a difficult time fitting inside this tent, you’re welcome to come outside and greet him yourself if you like.”  Jared nodded.
   “Alright, let’s go.”  The two of them walked towards the flap of the tent, when Eric reached it he held it open for the other man.  Jared nodded to him and stepped out of the tent.  His eyes widened as he saw the wyvern standing outside.  The Mercians still pointed their spears and bows at him and his fins were still sharply raised at them.  They looked like two cats preparing to attack each other.
   The shock left the man’s face, and he ordered the men to stand down.  Solvyri relaxed a little and turned towards Eric, with a questioning look.  He examined the commander with a scrutinizing gaze, before settling down on the ground.
   “He is marvelous, truly marvelous!”  Eric heard one of the robed men around the circle shout as he walked forward to examine him.  Solvyri bared his teeth as the man walked closer, but he did not attack him.
   Eric watched as the man examined Solvyri.  Jared finally caught his tongue.  “You are a Sky Knight?  I had no idea!”  Eric nodded.  
   “Before I joined up with the other Luxerne I was part of Vallech’s Sky Knights.  That was before I realized how misguided it was, when I was younger.”  His eyes were glassy, like he was in some faraway place.  Like he was remembering times before life was complicated.
   “That time is over now, I am Luxerne and that is all.”  He stared into the commander’s eyes, Eric’s frightened the man.  They were cold.  “I would hope that you would not distrust me because of my past, it is not where a man was raised that decides who he is.  It is what he has done with his life.  Remember that Jared.”
   The man nodded, then turned to the robed man examining Solvyri.  “Rhiverra please show our guest a place with the Runemasters, give him some food as well.”  Jared put a hand on Eric’s shoulder.  He nodded his head at him.  “In the morning, if you would like, I could brief you and your companions on the details of our resistance effort.  I am glad to have you with us, it will inspire hope in the men.”  
   They nodded to each other politely as the commander withdrew into his tent.  Eric walked beside Solvyri as the man led them to the fenced off section of the camp where the Runemasters slept.  The man was chatting away the entire time.  
   He explained that the Runemasters had been practicing the ancient arts for longer than any other group alive.  They were in essence, historians, researchers, alchemists, mathematicians, architects, brewers, metallurgists, scribes, physicians, physicists, veterinarians, and many other things.  They devoted their entire lives to the study of pursuing knowledge in its entirety.  Their libraries were said to be the most complete in the entire world.
   As they sat around the fire Eric was barraged with questions from the Runemasters.  They wanted to know everything about his life, from Sky Knight training methods, and Trojian culture, to shoemaking procedures.  He found it extremely overwhelming and quite irritating.
   After a stretch of time, just as he was falling asleep, another man walked over and sat beside him.  He didn’t say anything for a few minutes, only looked at Solvyri sleeping next to Eric.  Finally, after a few moments, he asked.  
   Where did you find this beast man?  I never thought I’d live to see one in my entire life.”  Eric sat up and looked at the man questioningly.  
   “What do you mean?  Wyverns aren’t that rare in the wild.  They still roam the Frandian coasts quite commonly.”  The man shook his head indignantly.
   “That’s no wyvern, the head’s all wrong.  And look at the size of it!  It’s much larger than any wyvern I’ve ever seen, and I’ve seen tons of them.  And it knows things, look even now its listening to me.  No wyvern does that.”  
   Eric scowled, what was the man getting to.  “I’m not sure what you mean.”  
   “I’ve spent my whole life looking for one of them, the Sky Knights nowadays pale in comparison to the Knights of Legend.  The connection is weak, and so are the crafters.  I’ve been researching it for a long while, and I’ve come to a conclusion.  History has many accounts of Sky Knights carrying five or six refugees apiece on their mounts.  How could they have done this if they rode wyverns like today?”
   Eric was confused.  “Get to the point man, I’m tired.”
   “There are two possible explanations.  One, is that the wyverns are much weaker than they were in the past, which is completely viable as an explanation.  All the time there are species that are doing that, changing over time.  Or there’s another explanation, that they didn’t ride wyverns.”  
   When he heard this Eric raised his eyebrows.  “Oh, and what did they ride if not wyverns?”
   The man rubbed his hands together, like he’d been saving the best for last.  “They rode a much more ancient breed, as similar to wyverns as a warhorse is to a pony.  They rode dragons!  That’s the only other explanation.  And if they made the connection with them like the Knights do nowadays then they would’ve been much stronger as well.  That’s what I believe your beast is, it’s a dragon!”
   Eric bit his lip, dragons were myth and that was it.  According to legend they grew six or seven times as large as a wyvern, and a wyvern was larger than a warhorse.  He glanced at Solvyri, he was already twice as large as an adult wyvern.  Could it be true, could Solvyri be one of those ancient beasts?
   “I have no idea what you’re talking about, and it doesn’t matter what he’s called.  Solvyri is what he is, I’m not going to worry about it because we have much worse things to worry about.”  He rolled over onto his mat as the other man sighed and walked away.  
   He put his hand on Solvyri’s chest and felt the familiar thrum inside as they shared energy between them.  He fell asleep that way, next to his partner.






   Zradist coughed as he breathed in the air along the Trojian coast south of Granadad.  It was too sweet for him, too full of life.  He spat as he walked towards the city in the distance, he could see the palace standing tall at the far end of the city.
   Some men called it the most glorious city in the world, others would refuse to go near it.  In truth Granadad was surely the largest city in the world, inside its inner walls were the finest buildings and merchandise that one could ever hope to see.
   But outside the inner walls was where the true character of the city was.  The suburbs of the city stretched for miles in either direction.  Nearly covering the entire point of the Trojian peninsula, they were like the sea beating on an island when compared to the Inner City.  While the people dressed in fine clothes, and dined like kings on the inside, on the outside the people were more like rats.  They fought over the trash that was dumped in their streets.  Shit and piss filled the alleys, and blood running down the roads was a common sight.
   That was where Zradist would build his kingdom.  
   As he crossed into the suburbs the air became less clean.  There was a stale taste to everything, like moldy bread.  He smiled and inhaled deeply, the smell of decay in the air was sweet.  His mouth watered for blood, he was a Bloodletter at heart.
   He twitched in annoyance as the old magic pressed in on him, the Beast had made Zradist special.  He was the crown jewel among the Y’marr, marked when he had been initiated.  He had made him his reh, his hand.  Zradist shared his eyes, his ears, and his nose with the Great One.
   The Beast had been growing in power, and he had used the death of the previous Y’marr to boost his strength enough to create Zradist.  Zradist did not share his brother’s restrictions to the territories, he could walk among all of them.  
   He walked through the streets like a shadow.  No man could have seen him in the darkness that was spread over the city.  He made himself a part of it and prowled the streets like a cat would, hunting his prey.
   Finally he found what he was looking for, a shrine of innocence in the city of darkness.  He smiled wickedly as he closed in on the abbey.  Technically he could have satisfied his bloodlust with any beggar, but destroying something so sacred, so tremendously selfless in their beliefs, tasted sweet on his tongue.  He didn’t want to simply satisfy himself, he wanted to revel in the blood of the innocent.  He wanted to destroy something that was inherently good.  That, even more so then satiating his thirst.
   He slid through the crack under the doorway, and through the halls like a snake.  He found a room with a woman lying in her bed asleep, she was positively luscious, and she wore the chain of chastity on her neck.
   Hissing with pleasure he ran a sharp finger along her spine, she shivered as he did.  He heard her breathing speed up, and her heart pumped faster.  He could hardly control himself now.
   He needed to sweeten her though, to terrify her and make her blood run cold.  That was what his instincts told him.  He rent her robes off of her and picked her up by the throat.  Her eyes darted left to right wildly, like an animal caught in a trap.  He must have looked terrifying to her as he slowly slid one claw into her, when he felt the tightness of her flowers he grinned sickeningly.
   He raped her ferociously, purposefully extracting every ounce of terror he could from her.  The blood song was filling his every thought, his every emotion.  It ran through his head like sweet nectar.  
   He was so enthralled in the woman that he failed to notice his hair prickling on the back of his neck, and the small voice in his head screaming to run.  He was enjoying himself too much.  It was almost too late when the door was thrown open, and he felt a crossbow bolt pierce the back of his thigh.
   He screamed and turned towards the intruders, only to be blinded by light.   “Back demon of the night, you are NOT welcome here.”  In the doorway were two men, they wore armor and clothes of pure white.  One of them held a repeater crossbow and the other, who was crafting the light that blinded him, held a burning blade in his hand.
   Zradist hissed and leapt at the man holding the sword.  The man threw his hand up and a blast of light as bright as the sun threw him back into the wall.  The other man stepped forward to take the girl, he hissed as he saw how badly she was bleeding.
   “By the light of the divine, do you see her?”  The man holding the sword nodded.
   “I see her Devin, take her away.  I shall deal with this filth.”  The man called Devin carried the woman out of the room as Zradist stood to his feet.  He stepped forward with his blade held at the ready.  “Do your worst hellspawn, you face the might of the divine.  I am Gabri, wielder of the flame of Mercolas.”
   Something screamed inside of Zradist, he needed to escape from this man.  He melted into the shadows and slipped out the window into the night.  That man held power, and he had known that Zradist was near. 
   His instincts led him deeper into the city, down into the sewers where he could rest and recuperate.  Fucking bastard he thought, as he crawled inside a grate to sleep.




   Bledhreft lounged in his chambers at the royal palace of Granadad.  He had done as U’mar ordered and acquired a place on the council of war.  It hadn’t been hard, he’d already been an established politician with a name for himself.  Bledhreft, of course, was not the name he went by in public.  He had kept his father’s name for that.
   A servant entered the room, she carried a steaming plate of fine salmon fillets.  “Here you are Lord Harris, your salmon fillets just like you asked for.”  He smiled at her, it was so easy to fool the people in the palace.  They all wanted to believe that he cared for them, it was just a matter of letting them think what they already wanted to.
   “Thank you very much Rebeka, it looks delicious.”  She smiled at him as she bowed and left the room.
   He ate in silence, thinking over his campaign.  Trying to find ways to get as deep as he possibly could so that when he, Lord Harris, died and Bledhreft acquired the King’s blood, that there would be as little suspicion that the attack came from inside as possible.
   He chuckled to himself, he was going to be king of Trojia one day. 
   The thought amused him.




Part Two:  War


    “Get to the fucking lines!” Gundleas shouted at a group of men who were standing at the camp.  He tore his helmet off of his head and wiped his bloody fingers on his tunic.  He smelled like gore and shit as he walked towards the council’s tent that they’d just set up in his encampment.  He had told them his plan, and he would not be waiting behind while a group of Goddamn politicians gathered their treasures on the retreat.  
   Before he even reached the tent he could smell the roast beef that was being served inside, it smelled like someone was having a feast.  He burst through the tent flap and saw the group of five robed council members sitting around a table, dining with fine silverware.  He strode to the table and flipped it onto the ground, spilling all of the food and drinks onto the ground.  
   “My orders were to stay at Headquarters during the campaign, you voted me as General over this campaign and I demand that you allow me to do my blazing job!”  One of the councilmen, who had an extremely bushy beard, stood to his feet.
   “Gundleas what is the matter with you?  Have you succumb to battle-madness and lost your mind?”  His face was bristling with anger.
   Gundleas rose to his full height, squaring his shoulders.  He must have looked absolutely terrifying covered in the gore of battle, even more terrifying than normal.  He drew on that intimidation right now.  “At some point in time in the next forty-eight hours every person in this camp will drop everything, grab a horse, and ride as fast as possible to our second point of fortification.  I am giving you notice now, because when we leave I will not give you any more notice than the other men.  You are not dressed for riding, and you are not carrying a weeks worth of food with you on your back.  You will be left behind if you cannot keep up, that is how it is.”
   Just as abruptly that he had entered he left, every one of the politicians looked like they had been struck.  The man with the bushy beard followed him out of the tent, he ran up to Gundleas as he was walking away.  “What the hell was that about, why did you say that?”  
   Gundleas stopped, but did not turn his back.  He was bristling with anger, and his voice cracked as he talked.  “Neither you, nor any of those other men in that tent have ever had to fight for your keep.  You’ve never had to stand on the front lines and see your brothers dying beside of you.   You’ve never had to kill another man, and it’s not easy.  Poets talk about it like it’s the easiest thing in the world, but it’s not.  Killing a man.  It’s not easy at all.”  He was quiet for a few seconds, and his eyes took on a sunken look.  “Just don’t be here when I sound the retreat.”
   Then he walked away without another word.  He picked up his helmet from the ground and headed back towards the front lines.




   Eric, Lief, and Ignis flew west over the Mercian forests.  Earlier that morning they had met with Jared and his officers, and decided that the three of them would fly on to the lines while Brazen and the others stayed behind to help with the fortifications.  They were only about an hour away from the front.
   “So what’re we going to do when we get there?”  Lief was shouting over the wind that was roaring in their ears.  “Meet up with Gundleas and move from there?”
   Eric nodded.  “Yeah, once we find Gundleas he should be able to show us where we need to be.”  
  None of them spoke for the rest of the flight, and the palisades entered their view just past midmorning.  They could hear the sounds of the two armies clashing below them in the valley.  They landed behind the Mercian trenches.
   After a few minutes of asking around, they found Gundleas sitting on the ground drinking deeply from a wineskin.  They approached him, and he stood up to greet them.  “How can I help you?  Make it quick, I was just about to head back to the line.”
   Eric bowed deeply, “Greetings General, we are reinforcements from the east.  We are Luxerne.”  When he heard this Gundleas raised his eyebrows.
   “Luxerne eh, you here to fight then?”  He looked curious, surely he’d heard about them from the letter Brazen sent ahead but he must’ve been wondering how they fought.  When they nodded he shrugged.  “Alright then, follow me you can fight with the Blademasters in the center.”
    Just before they left Ignis raised his voice, “Gundleas sir, if it’d be alrigh’, I’d like ter fight with the Snipers.  My bow’d be a lot more useful there.”  Gundleas shrugged.
   “Fight wherever you’ll be the most help.”  Ignis nodded and ran off to the pickets set up for the archers.  Lief and Eric followed the General.  It was difficult to keep up with his pace, he walked very briskly through the camp.  Eric could tell that this man was very zealous when it came to his troops, to the point where there was almost no disorder among the camp.  Everything was perfectly ordered.
   Eric chanced a question, “So what should we know about the battle up ahead, anything in particular that we could’ve missed?” 
   Gundleas grunted.  “Nope, just normal Trojians making a normal fucking mess like usual.  They’re fighting well, the Onyx Knight is leading them.  I assume you know who he is?”  Eric nodded, everyone knew that name.  The Onyx Knight J’harra was well-known all over the continent for his prowess in battle for the Trojians.  He was feared by his enemies, and adored by his allies.  Not exactly the man that you wanted to be fighting outnumbered.
   “Have you thought of any way to get rid of the General?  Surely if he was gone it would take a heavy toll on enemy morale.”  Eric was trying to learn the man, figure out how he fought.   
   Gundleas turned sharply to look him in the eyes, there was something in the older man’s features that hadn’t been there before.  “I win my battles by being the best leader I can be for my men.”  He squared his shoulders.  “Not by getting rid of the competition, if I had to resort to that than what makes us any better than them?  Nothing.”
   Gundleas turned back around, and they followed him all the way to the area where the Mercian troops were holding the Trojians at.  There was a long, deep ditch that divided the armies, the Trojians had been attempting to force their way through sporadically over the entirety of the morning.  So far they hadn’t been able to break through the Mercian line.
   The General directed them to the division forming up to replace the one in front of the ditch, it was hard to switch off because the Trojians would rush them as soon as they did.  They were expecting a heavy assault as soon as the divisions changed.
   Gundleas ordered the men into formation, shieldmen in the front, spearmen on their right shoulder, and axmen on their left.  Each shieldmen had a saxe, a short blade slightly longer than a man’s forearm, because they would be fighting the closest to the enemy.  In a shield wall men were pressed together very tightly, with bodies pushing against you on all sides.  No man would be able to swing a long blade in those conditions, which is what made the saxe so deadly.
   Eric and Lief stood with the Blademasters, they would stand at-the-ready a little ways behind the shield wall and look for any weak areas in the line.  When they saw one they would rush to that area and divert it as quickly as possible, then pull back to the area just behind the shield wall to wait.
   Eric could feel the tension in the soldiers as the lines prepared to switch, like a bowstring ready to snap.  The shieldmen were ready and Gundleas shouted, as loud as he could, the order for the division-change.  
   Not five seconds after the words left his lips the Trojians were charging the line.  The ditch slowed them down just long enough for the Mercians to be mostly in position, though there was still a dangerously high level of empty spaces in the line.  
   Eric fidgeted as he watched the line struggle to force off the Trojians, they had the high ground and a high morale but the vast Trojian numbers threatened to overrun them.  They managed to hold the line, and the archers began to fire volleys into the Trojian forces, weakening their assault.  There were a few moments that looked like they would break through, but the Mercians proved their worth and pushed them back into the ditch every time.
   After about a half-hour Eric saw Gundleas remove himself from the shield wall, the older man walked towards them hastily.  When he arrived he was out of breath, but composed.  “Alright you two I was thinking before and I realized that I have no idea what you can do, I trust Brazen’s word but I also need to know where you’ll be the most useful.”
   Lief nodded.  “I understand General, what did you have in mind?  Would you like us to tell you, or would you prefer a visual demonstration?”  He shrugged before adding, “Personally I’ve been feeling rather testy all morning, I could use a bit of a warm-up myself.  If that’s what you thought was best of course.”
   Gundleas nodded, “I have just the task.  Hordrar, the leader of this Blademaster regiment, is going to take a small group of Blademasters to the northern edge of the valley to make a raid on the enemy supplies.  It will be quick, and precise.  I would like one of you to accompany him on this raid, I trust Hordrar and I know he will be able to judge your skills well.”  He gestured towards the smaller man in black leather behind him.  “I would like the other to stay with me and the shield wall, the spearmen behind me fell in the Trojian surge and I need someone to cover my right side.”  
   Lief and Eric looked at each other and nodded.  “Sounds like a plan to me, I’m in.”  Lief said, he really did seem like he was anxious.  Hordrar motioned to Lief and the two of them walked back the way they’d come before.  
   Gundleas motioned to Eric with a gloved hand, “Can I assume you know how to fight in a shield wall?  This isn’t your first time?”  
   Eric nodded to him and picked up his lance.  “I’m ready to face the Trojians General, lead the way.” 
   Gundleas grinned, it seemed an alien gesture, like it was out of place on him.  “Let’s go.”




   “What was that Reshi?  I’ve never read about anything like it before.”  Devin fidgeted with the straps holding his crossbow to his back, he was a young man of eighteen with a dark complexion.  He had not been born on Mercolas, but in the lands across the sea.  Two monks had found him there and had taken him back to the Divine Light headquarters.  His skin was dark like coffee, but his hair was snow white.  Just another side-effect of being touched by the Flame, it would turn red like his master’s after a few more years.
   “That is a problem, one that must be addressed as quickly as possible.  Have you read about the Y’marr in your studies?”  Gabri was a man of average height, slightly larger than average build, and with plain features.  He would have blended in with the crowd perfectly, nothing stood out about his face or body except for his hair, his hair was red as flame.  Not red like most people would think of when they thought of red hair, but truly looked like fire, it was a lighter orange at the base and darkened the longer it went out.  It came to his shoulders in a style that one would not have expected from the Wielder of the Flame, it seemed very unofficial and lackadaisical compared to the monks that he lived with.
   Devin flinched openly, “Yes I’ve read of them Reshi, is that what you think that was?”
   Gabri bit his lip, “I have my suspicions, but first I need to speak with the minister.  He should be able to help answer the questions I have.”  Questions like how a Bloodletter Y’marr could possibly have entered into the Trojian territory, and if it wasn’t a Y’marr what else it could’ve been.
   They had covered themselves with their cloaks on the way back to the monastery, the white garb of the Divine Light was not a welcome sight in these parts of the city.  They traveled the mile or more back to the gate of the Inner City, Gabri showed the guard his passport and he let them in.  The monastery wasn’t far from the gate.
   The large doors to the cathedral creaked loudly as the Gabri opened them, the doors opened into the largest religious building in all of Trojia.  The ceiling itself rose high enough for shadows to cover the top of it, and it was lost in blackness.  There were stained glass windows all along the sides, and a large circular window showing the Flame of Mercolas burning brightly in the center.
   The two of them walked briskly to the far end of the hall and through the doors that led to the monk’s living quarters.  There was a library there, a feasting hall, three or four common rooms, and dormitories for the monks.  They walked past all of these to the minister’s quarters, it was late in the evening and he would probably be asleep.
   Devin was nervous at the thought of Minister John, they’d had somewhat of a strained relationship while he’d studied in the seminary.  When he’d been chosen to be the next Wielder of the Flame there had not been much enthusiasm from anyone in the monastery.  Prejudice had always existed from the others there, mostly because he’d been born across the sea, so they’d been just as surprised as he was when the Flame had enveloped him as he stood with the other initiates in front of the congregation that day in the cathedral.  It had covered him completely, without burning his skin, he’d burned for a full twenty-four hours afterwords.
   Gabri didn’t bother to knock, he just walked inside uninvited.  Sometimes Devin thought he enjoyed his complete lawful immunity, no door was locked to the Wielder of the Flame nor action forbidden.  He saw his master smile as he walked into minister John’s study where the old man was studiously poring over a manuscript.  
   The old man glanced up from his book and his lips twitched, Devin couldn’t tell if it was irritation or amusement.  “It’s rather late, I was just heading to bed.  Isn’t this something that could wait for tomorrow?”
   Gabri shook his head, “No minister, we have big problems.”  
   The old minister bit his lip, “How big?”
   “Big,” he said simply then motioned for him to sit down.  John sighed and took his seat on the other side of his desk.  Gabri and Devin took the two seats opposite of him.
   After shuffling his papers a little bit the minister crossed his hands and leaned forward towards them.  “I’m ready for the worst, go ahead Gabri.”  
   His master sighed, “I woke up in my bed about three hours ago, the Flame called me and was leading me outside the Inner wall.  Devin and I followed the call to that old abbey a mile south of the walls, same place where we gave food last week.  The call grew stronger as I entered the doorway and it led me down the hallway to the sleeping quarters of the chaste.  Then the Flame filled me and I knew something was on the other side of the room.” 
   They were silent for a moment, the look on the minister’s face held little interest.  Gabri continued, “I could hear sounds on the other side, I tried the knob but it was locked.  I kicked in the door and saw a demon of the night raping one of the chaste.”  The old man’s eyes widened momentarily, but then he returned to the same way he’d been before.  After a moment of not saying anything, Gabri seemed to think his reaction was a little under enthusiastic, so he added one last sentence to his account.  “It was a bloodletter, with the mark of the Y’marr on its forehead.”
   He got the reaction he was looking for, the minister’s jaw dropped and his eyes widened like a scared horse.  He was breathing fast when he finally spoke.  “That-that can’t be possible!  You must have been mistaken, no bloodletter can enter the Trojian territory.”
   Gabri shrugged, “It was a bloodletter, there’s no doubt about that.  And it’s not U’mar, this Y’marr is new, fresh from Oblivion.” 
   John slammed his fist onto the table, “Then explain to me what it is doing outside of its territory, and why Golath has not destroyed him the second he arrived?”
   Gabri snarled, “I don’t have the answers, the best I can do is pay Golath a visit.  He’s the only one who would know if there were any changes in the old magics.”
   John was aghast, “Consult a Y’marr?  That-that’s ridiculous, he would tear you to pieces!”
   Gabri stood and motioned for Devin to do the same, “Obviously you know nothing about the power you worship.  You would be wise not to underestimate it.”
   Two men, garbed in nothing but white, exited the monastery and began walking towards the Trojian royal crypts.




   Lief was accompanying the small group of blademasters preparing to raid the Trojian supply caravan, they had walked almost a mile along the ridges and the white-canvas wagons began to show through the trees below them.  Hordrar motioned for him to come closer.
   “Alright Luxerne, do you see those wagons down there?”
   Lief nodded and grinned, “I see them quite clearly, is that the supply train?”
   Hordrar nodded, “Yeah that’s it, have any ideas for destroying them?”  Lief gazed down at them thoughtfully for a minute.  What had Brazen always taught him, If the rules don’t suit you then change them.  
   Change the rules, how could they render as much food as possible inedible?  They couldn’t simply walk up and destroy it with their swords, the men could still eat it.  They couldn’t steal it because there wasn’t nearly enough men to carry a substantial amount of food away.  He scanned the floor of the valley, there was a river that ran along the southern side of it.  
   He grinned, “We could push the wagons into the river.  That might work.”  
   Hordrar studied him for a moment, then raised his eyes thoughtfully.  He contemplated the idea for a moment and then shrugged.  “That is a good idea, better than burning them.  But we would have to push them through that entire stretch of forest, over two-hundred yards.”
   Lief bit his lip, “do you have any better ideas sir?”
   Hordrar gave a halfhearted shrug, “possibly.  Have you heard of the new weapons the Trojians have?”  He tilted his hand from side to side quickly, as if to say a little.  “They call them devastators, because they are so devastating when fired.  They can tear a hole three horses long in a line of men.  They use a rock called phorys, which they grind into powder and light on fire to shoot them.”
   Lief furrowed his brow, “you want to find that powder and use it to start the fires quicker.”
   Hordrar grinned, “yes that’s what I was thinking.  The wagons with red canvas are the ones carrying the devastators, they should also have the powder inside of them.  I’m dividing the blademasters into three groups.  One to gather the powder, one to start the fires, and one to protect them from the Trojians.”  He motioned with his hand, “any preferences?”
   Lief grinned, “Let me fight them.  I still need to prove myself remember?”  
   Hordrar laughed, “fair enough.  Don’t get yourself killed, that’s all I ask.”  
   They shook hands then walked over to where the other blademasters stood.  They gathered into their groups and set off down the slope towards the Trojian caravan.  Lief walked near the back of the fifteen Blademasters that made up his group, he seemed under-armed compared to the others.  They all carried a hand-and-a-half sword on their backs, and all around their waist was a line of wicked looking exotic blades.  Some curved, some hooked, and some had barbed heads, and every one of them had a different set of them which added to their diversity.
   They reached the end of the forest and crouched low while they looked towards the Trojian caravan.  Lief moved to the left side of the group and peered stealthily through the bushes that were there.  He could see that there were hundreds of covered wagons that made up the supply train of the Trojian army.  He scanned the caravan quickly and saw that there was a group of wagons covered in red canvas near the front of the caravan, it must’ve been placed there so that it would closer to the lines for the Trojians to use.
   He heard shuffling sounds behind him as the other two groups of Blademasters caught up with them, they all gathered at different groups of bushes to peek out at the Trojians.  Lief motioned to Hordrar to come close, then whispered to him and told him where the Dragon carts were. 
   Hordrar nodded, and relayed orders to the group Lief was in.  They would be leading the raid, and they would secure the carts so that the other two groups could begin burning the supplies.  Hordrar’s group would begin to secure as many of the white wagons so that they could be burned as quickly as possible.
   Lief ran with the rest of his party to one of the white wagons that was closest to them, they used it as cover for a moment.  Lief turned to the squad leader, “I’ll go ahead of you, no offense but you all make about as much noise as a bison.  You follow after you see the soldiers die okay?”  The man nodded, and told the rest of them what to do.  
   Lief stood and snuck around the corner of the wagon, when he was out of the Blademaster’s sight he closed his eyes and swept the light away from him.  He pulled lightstuff from the aether and covered himself with it, partially removing himself from the physical world.  Everything around him lost color, except for blue, the only color that stayed was blue.
   He reached his hand out and tunneled the space in front of him, he moved from spot to spot by pulling himself through the tunnel.  It took about half a second between jumps, and he could jump twenty feet with each one.
   He found a group of Trojian guards and dove through the air towards them, he waved his hand and removed the lightstuff from his skin so that he could contact the Trojians during the fight.  He threw the lightstuff into the air as he appeared above them, now he was no longer pulled downward like normal.  
  He shot lightstuff at one of the soldiers, and shot towards the man as fast as if he’d fallen off a cliff.  When he hit him he sunk a blade into the back of his neck and rode him to the ground with the force of the pull.  He kicked off of the ground like he would if he was swimming and broke another man’s windpipe with his elbow.
   The last man stumbled and fell to the ground, Lief turned from his crouched position over the second soldier and hurled a blade into his eye socket.  The man began to convulse as blood and other liquids gushed from the wound, Lief bent space in front of him and shoved his arm through twenty feet of air that had been pulled closer to him, his knife stabbed into the man’s temple three times which silenced him quickly.
   He cleared a swathe through the rest of the guard and led the Blademasters to the red wagons.  His vision clouded and stars popped in front of his eyes from his use of the lightstuff and he almost fell over.  It had been a long time since he’d exerted himself so much, and he saw the skin below his gloves beginning to glow from it.  The lightstuff wasn’t from this world, it was matter that made up the aether and was the most useful weapon in his arsenal.  It canceled out all the rules of this world, but he could also change them.  He could make himself be pulled to the ceiling, or walk on walls.  He could make himself invisible, and slow down time.  He could bend space and warp from place to place, and he could stop sound from travelling through the air.  
   But using the lightstuff came with a price, it exacted a heavy toll on his body with every use.  His body rejected it very strongly, and would try to force it out as hard as it could.  That was why his skin glowed, because it was being forced out of his skin.
   He saw something out of the corner of his eye, a hole in the aether.  A flow of uether in the midst of a Trojian caravan?  He sent a tendril out to feel the flow, to identify it.  When he touched it he jerked back in shock.  It couldn’t be…
   The Blademasters began carrying bags of phorys out of the red wagons and loading them into the white ones.  Just as they began to light the wagons on fire a squad of nearly a hundred Trojian infantry charged towards the Blademasters with their spears leveled at chest height.  
   Lief saw them and, before they came upon the Blademasters, he shot a line of lightstuff through the ground in front of the Trojians.  When they stepped on the line they fell through the ground as if it were made out of water, the entire charge faltered as man after man fell into the person in front of them.  
   With that last effort Lief collapsed on the ground.  The last thing he could remember hearing was a rushing sound like a waterfall before the world went black.

   Back in the Dezdant fortress Adelia, Sarai, Brazen, and Sa’shaset had begun to aid with the defenses of the fortress.  Brazen, with Adelia’s assistance, had begun to present new ideas to the engineers that orchestrated the fortress’s repelling power.  There were traps, spikes, barbed wire, ditches, pikes, and a network of underground tunnels that ran along the field outside of the Dezdant Headquarters.  There were also catapults and trebuchets that ran the length of the wall, and cauldrons of lard lined the wall.  The lard would be melted whenever they were threatened, and the fat was much more expendable then oil.
   Sa’shaset had begun to craft some of the weapons and armor that the Mercians possessed.  She herself couldn’t do much, but the core guard and archers she helped however she could.  She grinned as she made an ever-sharpening link to one of the guard’s spears.  Links like this were tricky, and required more finesse then force.  That was why she hadn’t begun to teach Eric how to make a loop in the link, how to make it feed back into itself.  The hardest part was saturating the inside of the link with energy, but then sealing the edges using a welding or cauterizing effect.  It wasn’t permanent, but it would last months or sometimes years depending on the skill of the Drunair.
   Brazen was helping the medics and alchemists mix more medicines and salves for the injured that would surely come in droves, his knowledge astonished them at every turn.  He taught them better how to set a broken bone, how to cauterize a wound while leaving the smallest scar possible, how to properly stop an infection, and countless other useful pieces of knowledge.  He also mixed a potent poison for the arms master, the tips of arrows and spears would be soaked in it before battle to be more dangerous against the Trojians.  He hoped that his efforts would prove useful in the days to come.

   Sarai plunged one book deeper into the towering bookshelves that fill the library of the Runemasters, she had decided the day before that she would not be any help if she didn’t learn to master her gift.  She had studied the Histories of Fiorra and found very little about the powers of Firra herself.  Though she did find one particular passage interesting:
   “While it is unclear what part Firra played in the defense of Mercolas during the Phylax Invasion, it was said that she possessed a power vastly different from the other Stewards.  Their abilities were suited to war.  Trojja, Vervan, Mercan, and Fran possessed abilities that terrified the men that fought against them.  Rema supported them, engineering designs for weapons that were far superior than the Phylax invaders.  There are no accounts of Firra being present on the battlefield, nor any journals that included references of her support to the Mercolan cause, yet she was said to be the closest to Mercola himself.  Hers was the gift held back, it was near six months after the other five had received their gifts when Mercola invested this last bit of himself into his youngest child before disappearing from this world.  Afterwords she said little, hardly ever making public appearances with her older siblings.  She would walk the streets sometimes, never taking a guard with her, and not say a word to anyone.  This is where her title as the “Silent Steward” originated…”
   Then it began to babble about her being silent because she was somehow shorthanded by her father, this didn’t ring true to Sarai.  When a father holds something back for one child specifically like that it’s usually not because what he’s holding back is somehow worse than his other gifts.  It was like the way her father would sometimes bring home sweetbread for her and her other siblings, only he would tell her to wait and take a walk with him.  At first she would feel disappointed, and her siblings were too engrossed in their treats to care about where Sarai went.  Then, when they were out of sight, her father would slip a small chunk of chocolate in her hand.  But he would make her promise not to let the other children know, because then they would be jealous of her gift.
   She wondered if the same could be said of Firra.
   She stood from her seat in the library and put the book she was reading back on its shelf.  Her hand paused on the shelf and she heard a voice behind her.  “Having trouble finding something dear?”  She turned and saw a man in no more than rags standing behind her, he was middle-aged and somewhat good looking in a rugged sort of way.  His hair was blazing red as well as his beard.  She said nothing, but tilted her head to the side, examining him for another moment before he spoke again.  “What book do you have there,“ he reached up to the shelf where her hand had just been.  “Let’s see, ahh the histories of Fiorra yes, a fine read, if you are looking for a watered down version of events that is.”  
   He shook his head, “This particular gathering of books was issued to the public because it was overly general and skimmed over most of the battles and details.  Now if you were looking for something more…” he made a gesture with his hands, “tangible then I would recommend this.”  He jumped onto one of the chairs and reached onto the topmost shelf, after fumbling for a moment and almost losing his balance he pulled out a rather thick and dusty book that had a leather binding.  He jumped off the chair and held the book out to her triumphantly.
   “What’s that book called?”  Sarai asked, she couldn’t read the name of the title because it had worn off over the years.
   “It’s called The Gathering of Fiorran Histories.  It’s the most detailed collection that we have here of the Fiorran culture and court lifestyle before they were conquered by the Trojians twelve years ago.”  
   Sarai glanced up at him quickly, then back to the book.  “Do you have anything about the Stewards?  That’s what I was really looking for, Firra in particular.”
   He frowned, “Why are you looking for that?”
   Her features hardened, “I’m the Descendant of Firra, but I have no one to show me about my gift, or even what it is.  I was hoping to find some sort of clue in some of the accounts of Firra.”  She paused for a second and watched his eyes widen, “can you help me or should I look somewhere else?”
   He blanched at that, “Of course I can help you!  There’s not another library of this size anywhere else on Mercolas.  The Library of the Runemasters is the most complete collection of knowledge anywhere on the earth.  There are accounts of Firra by the dozens, we just have to know where to look.”  
   He headed off towards the catalogs at a brisk pace, but stopped after a few steps.  He turned back to her, with his lip between his teeth.  “Dear, I…thought of a better idea.  I happen to be one of the……higher ranking Runemasters here in Mercia.  I have a great deal of knowledge about almost everywhere in Mercolas, but I happened to write my graduating thesis on the gifts of the Stewards themselves!”  He was grinning like a kid now, “It was one of my dreams to get to speak with one of the great Luxerne themselves, and I would be more than happy to tell you everything I know about the Steward’s gifts.”
   Sarai studied him for a moment, she had never really talked to one person for a long amount of time before.  She found that she was rather shy in many of the public places she’d been with the others, but she smiled at the man’s rather eccentric behavior.  
   She held out a hand for him to shake, “Sarai.”  She said.
   He returned the gesture fervently, “Runemaster Gerik at your service, I think I’ve met one of your companions before.  A lad by the name of Eric right?”  He saw her eyes widen as his words sunk in.  “I was undercover with the Sky Knights while Eric was with them.  I taught him a little crafting after his wyvern hatched.”  His eyes glazed over for a moment, “He was some lad…”




   Adelia walked briskly through the training fields outside of the Du Vron Dezdant headquarters, her pegii Lyra was trotting along beside her.  The fields stretched very far, as far as the eye could see, and she could see the Blue Wings running drills in the sky above her.
   She jumped onto Lyra and took off into the sky, she flew towards the man sporting a red coat that told her he was the leader.  She was wearing her traditional yellows, which told them that she was a rank higher than the recruits, and had already passed training.
   She saluted the squad leader and came to a halt about ten feet from him.  He looked down at her smoothly, “Hello Adelia.”  She knew him, he was a bald man who was covered in scars.  He had an eyepatch over his left eye, and always seemed to wear an annoyed expression on his face.  His name was Brendon Revlon.  He led the Blue Wings.
   “Greetings Sir Revlon.”  Adelia swiftly lowered her head to him as an acknowledgement to his authority.
   “What do you want here, are you tired of those Luxerne already?”  His voice came out like a growl.
   She blushed briefly, “I’m inquiring of how I could help the Blue Wings, I did not lose my position among their ranks for my reassignment to the Luxerne, I’m sure there’s somewhere I could fight.”
   He made a dismissing motion with his hand, “Go where you want, a yellow doesn’t need to be here anyway.  I don’t know why you came, there was no need.”
   She  looked him in the eye, “You wear the red coat, I naturally would come to you first.  I wanted your approval.”
   He didn’t say anything else to her, he flew towards a group of recruits on his black stallion to correct them for something.  
   Only slightly disheartened, but used to his attitude, she flew to the yellow quarters where she would try and get assigned to a quad.
   Blue Wings formed squadrons of varying sizes depending on the rank.  A division of Blue Wings was made up of one squadron from each varied color rank.  Blue were the first level to be able to fight on the battlefield, they were formed into squadrons of sixteen and would follow orders given to them.  Green made up squadrons of eight, they were a little more specialized then the Blue, and could begin to learn their own weapon specialization, but they were essentially the same as the Blues.  Next were the Yellows, they formed quads of four and were the field officers of the Blue Wings.  They would lead different groups of Blues and Greens into battle under orders of the Red.
   There was always one Red assigned to each division, they were the commander and were responsible for the entire division.  Brendan was a Red, and there were three others.  She had learned under Brendan because she had heard he was the toughest instructor and yearned to make a name for herself.  It had been a difficult two years, but at the end of it she had come out of the program as an extensively mature Yellow.
   She spoke with one of the organizing officers and enlisted into the Blue Wings as a temporary transfer from the Luxerne division, only until she received orders from them that would mean she would rejoin the Luxerne.
  She walked into her room and threw her things onto the bed.  She took Lyra to the stables and then decided to take a nap for the afternoon.
   She figured that she would soon need the sleep…




   For what seemed like the twentieth time, Eric plunged his lance under the ribs of a Trojian soldier.  He held onto it tightly and blasted the man off of his spear with a burst of energy.  He tapped into his source and parried a strike aimed at the Mercian to his left.  
   The Trojians had been attacking for three straight hours, whenever their lines became even a little weary they were replaced by fresh troops.  The Mercians had to fight longer between shifts because they were fewer in number, which was disheartening to most of the men.
   Eric hadn’t tired at all though, he was still tapping into his source and didn’t feel weary in the least.  He made a link to the air around a group of Trojians and watched as it exploded, spewing gore and smelling of burnt hair.  
   He heard Gundleas shouting and turned to see the Mercian lines falling apart.  The shield wall had broken at a point twenty yards to his left and the entire area had turned into a slaughter house.  Men were dying left and right, and the entire ground was so slick with blood that it made it almost impossible for anyone to keep their footing.  Still, Trojian after Trojian rushed through the gap and Eric knew that they wouldn’t stop unless he did something.
   He sent out a beacon of heat from his body to signal Solvyri and drew heavily from his source.  
   All at once the world slowed, his vision sharpened and every motion he made felt effortless.  He’d never drawn from the source so deeply before and it was the most wonderful sensation he’d ever felt.  Better than any drink he’d had, better than any woman he’d bedded.  It was like one endless climax, that feeling of endless energy and release was very similar to this.  Only amplified.
   He snarled and dove through the Trojian shield-wall, he was a demon and no one could stand against him.  He kicked the shield and the Trojian holding it was thrown thirty feet into the air, he backhanded a man beside him and decapitated him as swiftly as he would have with an axe.  The energy was leaking out of him and was burning the closest men without Eric even opening a link.  
   He dropped his spear and began to burn a path through the enemy forces, he didn’t bother conserving his energy, he just hurled it deeper and deeper into their lines.  Fire erupted in shades of orange and blue in lines thirty and fort feet in front of him where he threw it, and men by the dozens were hurled into the air by the force behind it.  He knew why Trojja had been named the Steward of Battle, he could imagine how men must fear him now as they did his ancestor.
   Solvyri roared above him, and hovered fifteen feet above the battlefield where he stood.  He leapt the distance and landed on his back, the wyvern grunted with surprise and turned his head to stare at him with one black eye.  Energy thrummed out of him, but Eric couldn’t understand it.  He raised his hands, palms extended, and made two enormous links to the ground below him.
   To the naked eye Eric looked like a god pouring fire down among the Trojians, Gundleas had retreated and begun the second stage of the defense.  The Trojians were fleeing by the thousands, but Eric poured more and more flames down upon them.  The entirety of the battlefield had been blackened, and thousands of Trojians had been roasted by the flames.  
   The majority of the enemy had retreated to the trees, they must think I’m afraid to burn the whole forest down.  He laughed and began to direct his funnels of fire towards the forest. 
   All at once he was completely human again, the flood of energy that had been pumping through him had stopped violently.  He felt light headed and very weary, he tried to tell Solvyri to take him back to Gundleas but he was too weak to get any words out.  
   He began to slip closer and closer to unconsciousness but held on for a few minutes until Solvyri landed next to General Gundleas’ tent.  All the soldiers were already beginning to flee, luckily though Gundleas himself had not yet left.  He had been ensuring the survival of the Mercian council members that had come to the front.
   When Solvyri landed in front of him Eric slid out of his saddle and collapsed onto the ground.










































Part Three:  One Will Prevail


   Lief awoke to the pain as his head smacked into a rock on the ground.  He jerked away from it and realized that he was being drug by a large man with a sword strapped to his back.  He blinked quickly then began to shout and struggle to be let go.  Startled, the man let go of him as if he’d been a venomous snake.  Fear filled the soldier’s eyes as he backed away from the fallen Luxerne.
   He drew a knife and had nearly thrown it when he realized that the soldier was one of the Blademasters who’d walked with Hordrar earlier that day.  He quickly returned the knife to its sheath in his sleeve and stood up from the ground where he’d fallen.  “What happened back there?”  Lief said, as he brushed bits of leaves off of his shirt and pants, “I don’t remember a thing.”
   The Blademaster returned to his senses, but Lief could still detect a hint of fear and reverence emanating from him.  “We were able to light the phorys in time to get out of there with…” he glanced at Lief warily, “minimal injuries.”  
   He nodded, “Where is Hordrar, I need to speak with him now.”  He hated acting so uppity to the man, but the Luxerne were feared by a great many of the Mercian soldiers and he used that to his advantage.
   The man bowed his head slightly, “Absolutely, follow me.”  The Blademaster led him along a trail and through some tangled bushes, until they emerged in a small cope of trees where five or six of the Blademasters had sat down to rest.  Once they saw Lief shock and surprise shot across their faces.  None of them stood so Lief walked forward.
   “Where is Hordrar?  I need to speak with him about something quickly.”  All he received were shrugs and a few “I’m not sure”s.  He walked to the man who had been carrying him for the past four hours.  “Alright I need you to give Hordrar a message when he returns, I need you to tell him that I have pressing Luxerne duties that must be attended to as fast as possible.  I will go my own way from now.”
   The Blademaster nodded and said he would tell Hordrar what he’d said.  Lief thanked him and walked off into the woods towards the way he’d come before. 
   When he was out of their sight he wrapped himself in lightstuff and prepared to jump to the Dezdant headquarters.  The power of the aether flowed through his veins and blue wisps of smoke began to appear out of his skin.  He’d used a lot of lightstuff that day, if it were any other time he would rest at least a few more hours, but this was urgent.  He’d discovered something in the Trojian encampment, and Brazen needed to hear about it as soon as possible.  If he died in the process then it would have been worth it in the end.
   He forced all of the lightstuff in his body to a point at the end of his index finger, then cut a line in the fabric that separated this world and the aether.  When it was as tall as a man he reached his arm through it and made a wrinkle in the aether.  The aether plane is much less tangible than the worldly one, which is how he can wrinkle it using much less effort.  This was a technique he’d invented himself.
   After cutting through the fabric on the other side he stepped through the newly-made doorway and into the walled fortress of the Du Vron Dezdant.
   He stumbled and fell to the ground as wave after wave of weakness slammed into his body.  He writhed and vomited as his body reacted violently to the huge amount of lightstuff in his system.  It rushed out of him in the form of a heavy mist and lay like a mist over the ground ten feet around him.  He tried to push himself up onto his hands and knees but wasn’t strong enough to.
