   He moaned as his trembling turned into convulsions and chills flooded his system.  At this rate he wouldn’t be able to live long enough to tell Brazen about Wyr’fen, and the threat he was posing towards the Mercians in the Du Vron Dezdant.
   He faded into semi-consciousness as he lay on the street, he remembered shouting, and shuffling sounds.  Then someone picked him up and carried him to a soft bed.  More vomiting, this time blood came with it.  He reached to wipe off his face and it came away wet, he heard someone shout and turned his head towards the dimly lit room around him.  His vision faded in and out of focus as he made out a mirror on the other side of the room.  In it he saw himself, blood was draining out of his ears and eyes onto the bed he was laying on.  He opened his mouth and realized that it was full of more blood and weakened more as a mouthful of it gushed onto his lap and the sheets around him.
   He reached out a blood covered hand to the woman standing next to him.  “Get….Brazen.”  Was all he could manage before he coughed and sprayed a bloody mist onto the wall in front of him, staining the walls with red globules.  
   A few minutes later the woman returned with Brazen, he ran to Lief’s bedside and crouched there next to him saying something he couldn’t understand. 
   Lief shook his head, “Bra..zen…Wyr’fen is....powering the Trojians.”  He coughed and more blood spilt onto the sheets, he was almost completely covered in it.  “He’s made……a blood pact.”  With that he collapsed onto the bed unconscious.  
   Brazen’s eyes widened with what he’d heard, why would Wyr’fen - a Y’marr of bestial qualities – ever make a blood pact with any human power?  It just wasn’t done, the Y’marr wouldn’t even make a pact like that with each other.  What on earth could Wyr’fen gain by making such a violent commitment to anyone other than the Beast himself?
   He stood up quickly, fear flooded his features.  The Beast had named a reh, then a blood pact would be expected to be made to him from every servant the Beast had in this plane.  That was why Wyr’fen let himself be bound to someone, but why the hell did the Trojians have the Slip?  
   A Slip was an object that was used to hold and focus the uether, dark matter that made up the plane of oblivion where the Beast resided.  The uether bound two beings together as closely as they could be, one would always have unwavering loyalty to the other.  The lesser would never be able to lie to the greater, and the lesser would obey the greater even if it led to his own death because, if he disobeyed, it would be his death anyway.
   If Wyr’fen was working for the Trojians now, and his blood pact was with the reh, then that must mean that the reh was controlling the Trojian armies.  Which meant that Gunthred himself had been replaced by the reh, no, the reh would want mobility so he would place a figurehead on the throne instead.  
   This changed everything.
   Brazen stood from his chair and walked the fifteen minutes back to his laboratory in the fortress.  He began throwing books and scrolls around like bits of trash, searching for something important that he knew must be around here somewhere.
   Finally, he pulled a book of the topmost shelf in his bookcase.  It was covered in dust and was more than a little moldy from the period of time spent unread.  That didn’t matter though, Brazen sifted through its pages one after the other searching for something he knew he’d read before.
   Just before he found it he heard a gentle knock on the door.  “Go away dammit, whatever it is can wait.”  There was no other sound for a moment so he dove back into his book.
   The door opened slowly, and with it the smell of death and decay so strong he almost vomited.  Brazen turned, eyes wide with shock, to see a figure walk through the doorway.  
   The Y’marr stood seven feet tall, with muscles that bulged with inhuman strength that could crush boulders.  He seemed to be made of the forest himself, bits of moss and grass clung to his body in bits and his skin was covered in soil.  He wore a loincloth and a metal mask that left his mouth and bottom jaw free to bite.  His teeth jutted straight out of his mouth like a sharks, and four rows of them filled his mouth.  His eyes were black as night, like two onyx marbles placed in his eye sockets.  He carried no weapon, but each finger ended with a jagged claw that the Y’marr could rip and tear with.
   Brazen stood up straight, this was a creature out of a nightmare come to life.  Wyr’fen walked towards him, his breath made fog in the air even now, those eyes never left him for a moment.  
   Brazen laughed, he couldn’t help it, here he stood staring death itself in the face and he laughed like a child.
   What else would you do if you were about to die…


   
   “The first thing you must understand is that you must not call upon your power, you must not coax it, force it, or focus it in any way.  It is alive, it knows your will, and you must trust it in order for it to work the way it is designed.”  Gerik spoke to her slowly, instead of rags he wore traditional Runemaster garb, a robe of sky blue.  His blazing red hair contrasted it sharply.
   Sarai nodded, “What is the first thing I must do?”
   Gerik bent over and picked up a potted plant that was sitting on the floor next to him, it was a lily flower.  He set it on the table in front of her.  “Want this flower to die, that is it.  When you have done this I will give you further direction.”
   Sarai furrowed her brow, but tried to do as she was told.  She wanted the flower to die…but why did she want it to die?  What had the flower done to deserve death?
   As if reading her mind Gerik held up a hand, “You must want it dead, it is easier than you would expect.  Focus your mind and the power will follow, it is like a baby and must be shown what to do.  Show it what you want.”
   Sarai emptied her mind and stared at the flower.
   It came easier than she expected, there was something inside of her that got pleasure out of seeing something die.  The same satisfaction she got when she smashed a bug, or built a fire.  She truly wanted to watch the flower wilt.
   Gerik nodded, “remember that feeling.  It’s the basis of how you control your power.  No matter what it is you want you must want it just like you want the flower to wilt.”  Sarai nodded, she understood. 
   Gerik took a deep breath, then continued.  “Now you must search inside yourself for something, I don’t know what it is, that is trapped.  It’s been with you your whole life, possibly a feeling, or an emotion.  Whatever this trapped thing is must be released, relax whatever is holding it back.  Then, when it is released, the power will do your bidding.  But you MUST NOT try to control it, you must trust it to do as you wish.”
   Sarai focused again on wanting the flower to die, she was able to do it within seconds and so continued on to the more difficult task.  She closed her eyes and searched her mind for what Gerik described to her, something trapped that she kept hidden like an emotion. 
   It didn’t come to her quickly, she spent twenty minutes delving her mind trying to find the prison Gerik had spoken of.  But it did come, she found it where she didn’t expect.  It was hidden behind the temperance of words, the urge to say something only to please someone else or to look better.  She knocked down the wall and focused hard on wanting the flower to die.
   She felt a small ripple in the air around her and opened her eyes to see a wilted plant that had shriveled up where the healthy one had been before.  There was no explaining it, and she had no idea how it had happened.  She turned to Gerik, “How is this possible?”  Her voice was hard and cold.
  “I’m not sure, you will have to find that out for yourself.  I read the technique in Firra’s diary, there was no mention what the power was that she commanded.”  Sarai shrugged and a thoughtful look came over her face.
   “What if I wanted to bring the flower back to life?  Could I do that?”  Gerik scratched his beard thoughtfully.
   “As far as I know you can do anything, the power has no limits or cycles.  You’ll have to learn yourself what is or is not possible, I’m afraid I’ve taught you everything I know of Firra’s power.”  Sarai nodded and thanked him for helping her so much, she finally felt like a Luxerne, like she could actually offer something to the group.
   Just then a messenger came bursting into the room, “Sarai….Sarai Truvard.”  He was breathless and she allowed him to catch his breath for a moment.  “Brazen is…dead, they found what was left of his body in the laboratory downstairs.”
   Sarai’s eyes widened, and she stared at her shaking hand in shock.  Brazen, Dead?  It didn’t seem like it could be possible, he was so confident and in control.  She hadn’t known him long, but the time she had known him he had been so kind to her.  It was impossible to think that he could be dead.
   She sat down in the chair and cried.  She cried for the people who had died at the Trojian’s hands, and for the people that would die by the Trojian’s hands in the future.  
   After a moment she stood up, she was resolved now.  She knew why she would fight as a Luxerne, to stop suffering.  Death was natural, everyone died, but all this war was causing nothing but pain for everyone involved.
   She would do everything she could to end it.






   Lief stood up from his bed a little too quickly, stars swam in his vision and he had to grab the bedpost for balance.  He steadied himself and then immediately set off at a dead run through the fortress.  He knew what he had to do, because there wasn’t time left for anything else, there wasn’t time to send messengers to the rest of the Luxerne he needed to speak to them now.
   He burst into Brazen’s laboratory, there were bloodstains on the floor and walls and many of his alchemical tools had been smashed to pieces.  He’d known Wyr’fen wouldn’t let him live long, Brazen was all that was left of the old magic in the world.  The type of magic in fairytales, and his presence would’ve attracted the Y’marr like flies to honey.  He found the shelves that held what he was looking for and slipped the herbs into his purse.  He made his way down to a secluded area in the basement and began setting everything up like Brazen had taught him.
   It was going to be a long night for six Luxerne.






      Six people sat around a table in a world made of clouds and fog, the table was made of stone and the chairs around it were simply rocks that were crudely shaped into something possible to sit on.
   “Sorry guys, I’m not nearly as good at holding an image as Brazen was.”  Lief looked uncomfortable and sounded like he was further away than he was.
   Ignis shifted uncomfortably, “ ‘ats alrigh, never cared much fer comfort anyways.  Why we here eh?  And where’s the ‘ole man?” 
   Eric and Ignis looked around at them quizzically as Lief, Adelia, Sarai, and Revlin sighed deeply.
   “Brazen died this morning, he was killed in his lab.”  Revlin fidgeted at his sleeve as he talked, and couldn’t make eye contact with either of the warriors.  Eric’s eyes widened maddeningly, and Ignis looked like he’d been hit over the head with a cane.
   Eric spoke first, rather frantically.  “How did he die, did he leave any sort of message?  How could this have happened right under your noses?”  Nobody said anything, there was still an unanswered question in the air though.  It had been Brazen that had mostly formulated the plan for stopping the Y’marr, it had been him that held all the knowledge they needed about their strengths and weaknesses.  They were blind without him.
   Lief bit his lip, “We were all busy, and I was injured.  No one expected this to happen in the middle of the fortress.”  He paused for a moment, “But I do know who did it.”
   Revlin turned to him in shock, “Who, who did it?  He was supposed to teach me how to refine Chromatis and the old man never got the chance.  I think I’d like to kill whoever was responsible for this.”
   Ignis, Eric, Adelia, and Sarai all nodded in agreement.  “Yes, Brazen must be avenged.  He saved me when I was little and taught me about my gift…I owe him more than my life, I owe him my freedom.”  Adelia blinked back tears and set her gaze to the ground.
   Lief sighed, “Yes I will tell you who killed Brazen, but it will make no difference.  We all vowed to kill him anyway.  His name is Wyr’fen, he is the Y’marr of Mercia.  He killed Brazen because of the knowledge he had that could have transferred the Slip to our control.”
   Adelia furrowed her brow, “A slip?  That could only mean…”
   Lief nodded, “Yes Adelia, Wyr’fen has made a blood pact with the Trojians.”
   Eric blanched, “Why the hell would he do that?  I can’t see a Y’marr taking orders from anyone?”
   Everyone was quiet for almost a minute, they either didn’t know or didn’t want to say what they thought was a possibility.  Eventually Sarai raised her quiet voice against the stillness.  
   “The Y’marr wouldn’t make a pact with anything unless they were afraid of it.  What is higher than a Y’marr?”  She turned to Lief and Adelia in turn and asked them, “What is stronger than a Y’marr?”
   Neither wanted to answer, Lief closed his eyes and took a deep breath before answering her question.
   “Only one being, apart from the Beast himself, could have power over the Y’marr.  The Beast must have found a way to turn the Y’marr that took the place of U’mar into his reh.  That would explain the blood pact, the reh would have received one from every foul being the Beast had ever employed.”
   None of them looked like they had any clue, not even Adelia.
   Lief continued, now that he’d admitted the possibility of one existing it was easier to focus on it.  “The reh is the Beast’s most resourceful weapon.  He is literally the eyes and ears of the Beast in this world.  This being is tied to the Beast closer than any other could possibly be, and everything he experiences is as if the Beast experienced it himself.  He does the Beast’s bidding to a “T” and has extraordinary powers gained from being attached to him.  Normally it is a regular soul sent from Oblivion, just a normal person who is tied to the Beast in that way.  But the Y’marr would not make a blood pact to one such as this, the Beast wouldn’t wish it.  He would rather be discreet than powerful.  But they would make a blood pact to a Y’marr if he was also reh.”
   Ignis shook his head quickly like he was trying to avoid a bee, “wait one blazin’ minute, why the hell would ‘e want to be powerful now if ‘e didn’ before?”
   Lief stared straight ahead then closed his eyes, “There is only one reasonable explanation.  The Beast is making a power play, with the Trojian army behind his back he’s going to try to take command and conquer the rest of the continent.  Then, when the Trojians are weak and unsuspecting, he will attempt to gather all six pieces of the heart in the shrine of the Beast and resurrect himself there.  Then he will take the world for his own.”  He shook his head, “I can think of no other possible reason for his actions so far.”
   Eric frowned, “Well that ends if we start killing off Y’marr.  Now that we know Trojja’s power can destroy the hearts for good we can stop him from being able to resurrect himself.”
   Revlin nodded, “Yeah we shouldn’t change our plans, if we kill off the Y’marr than he’ll have no heart to resurrect himself with.”
   The rest of them nodded and sounded agreement.  Lief nodded with him, “I agree.  We know the whereabouts of Wyr’fen, Brazen told me while we walked here from Ford.  He lives in a cave along the Glandraung Mountain Range, where the mountains meet the ocean.”  He paused for a moment to ponder something.  “Eric, how many people could Solvyri carry that far?”
   Eric frowned thoughtfully, “Probably three, any more and we’d have to take a lot more rest, and the smaller the better.”
   Lief nodded, “I think Eric, Adelia, and I should fly there as soon as possible.  We can destroy Wyr’fen and be back in as little as four days.  The rest of you can stay and aid with the defense of the Dezdant.” 
   Adelia shook her head immediately, “No I will not go.  I must be here in the city when the Trojians arrive, I must assist the Blue Wings, I have promised them…”
   Lief bit his lip, “I had chosen the three of us because we at least have some experience with fighting Y’marr.  Who would like to join us?  Ignis?”
   Ignis shook his head firmly, “I also will not leave the fortress.  These archers know nothing, I must train them.  If the Dezdant falls there won’t much of a world worth saving I don’ think.”  He glanced at Revlin who was fidgeting again, “Take the boy he could use the experience.  We’ve all got to learn some time.”
   Lief turned to Revlin, ”is that alright with you?  Do you want to hunt Wyr’fen?”  
   Revlin jumped out of his seat, “Of course I want to hunt him down!  I have just as much reason as any of you t want them dead.  I want to go with you.”  His eyes blazed with intensity.
   “That’s settled, the three of us will leave as soon as possible.”  Suddenly the dream wobbled and Lief gripped his side in pain.
   Sarai stood up, “You forget that you are injured, I saw the blood and I know you cannot be strong enough for this.  I will go, and Lief will stay.  It shall be Eric, Revlin, and I who will kill Wyr’fen.”
   Everyone stared at her, Lief knew the truth behind her words and he nodded quickly.  No one else dared argue, because no one else could go.  In truth they had all viewed her as the weakest, though none would dare speak of their thoughts.  As long as Eric went then Wyr’fen could be slain.
   The dream wobbled again, this time more violently.  “I…can’t hold it anymore.  Eric you must fly here as soon as you wake, don’t forget.  The rest of you, do what you will to help defend the city.  Our duty is to Mercolas, I know you’ll all succeed.”
   He smiled at them all before closing his eyes and letting the dream collapse.




   When Eric woke up he practically leapt onto Solvri’s back, all his things were in the saddle so it only took a moment to get it on and be off into the sky.
   He flew all morning and arrived at the Dezdant headquarters a little after noon,  he fed Solvyri energy from his source but he continued to notice a strange vacuum that hadn’t been there before.  He hoped that he hadn’t damaged himself in some way when he’d routed the Trojians in the pass.  
  They would keep fighting, he hadn’t killed nearly enough of them.  They would bury their dead, gather supplies, and be on their way in less than a day from now.  He hoped the Mercians and the three Luxerne could hold them off long enough for him to kill Wyr’fen, because if they did he didn’t think they would do to well if Eric hacked at them again like he had before.
   Revlin and Sarai stood on the top of the central tower of the fortress, it was the highest and they waved to him so he knew where to go.  He landed on it, secured the other two passengers, and took off as quickly as he’d come.  
   He flew as fast as he could, stopping only twice more that day for them to relieve themselves, they ate on dragonback that day.  By the time the sun had sunken over the horizon they were already half way to the coast.




   “Goddammit, no I don’t need help I’m fine.”  Lief held his head in his hands and shooed away the soldiers who tried to help him.  Blood had begun to leak from his eyes and he wiped it away with the sleeve of his shirt.  He had work to do.
   If even a little use of aether provoked such a violent reaction he would have to do it the normal way, or use uether……no, he would only do that if the only other choice was death.
   He walked briskly from post to post, critiquing weapons and checking arrow supplies.  It amazed him how often he found soldiers with less than five arrows apiece, one had none at all.  Then he went to the staging room and talked battle tactics with Jared, until Gundleas arrived from the second blockade they would continue to gather food, wood, and anything else from the towns surrounding the fortress.  Jared thought they had a week, Lief expected less than three days.
   So they gathered, and they talked, and they waited.  None of them had any idea how long Gundleas could hold the blockade, and no one dared to think of the day when it would fall.  Because it would, there was no doubt.  It would fall.
   That night the Trojians overran Gundleas…




   Gundleas stood in shock, no one could have imagined the power of the Trojian weapons.  The devastators had truly destroyed their picket set up and left them powerless, the men had fled and Gundleas stood in the wreckage with nothing but his blade and his armor.
   “J’harra!” He shouted towards the Trojian army coming towards them.  He began to walk towards them with his sword drawn, but he didn’t care anymore.  He shouted again, this time louder.  “J’HARRA, come fight me you bitch!”  He heard muttering as some of the Trojians recognized the fearless Mercian general.  “You’re a BITCH J’harra, a fucking stain!  You’re not even worth fighting, show your face so I can send your head back to your woman in the city.”
   Finally the soldiers parted and the Onyx Knight walked through the opening in his forces.  He wore his exotic armor and held the curved katana in his hand, he wore no shield.
   Gundleas strode towards him, gold plate no longer gleaming from the blood and grime spread on it.  J’harra looked like something out of a story, compared to the stench and dirt that was Gundleas he looked strangely out of place.  
   The Mercian general held his blade out at eye level, he stared the other man in the face.  Like two dogs they stood like that for almost a full minute, neither wanting to make the first move.  Tension shot between them like bolts of lightning as they visibly bristled.
   Gundleas lowered his blade and shook his head, “You’ll die J’harra, if that’s what it takes to save Mercia you’ll die.  I swear it.” 
   The Onyx Knight said nothing, he didn’t move at all.  He watched as a snarl covered the other man’s face and was still as he leapt towards him.
   The Trojians gasped as they saw Gundleas leap towards their leader.  Then they were quiet as they watched J’harra wiping the blood off of his sword on Gundleas’ trousers.  It had happened so quickly, none of them had even seen the strike that took the Mercian general in the throat, or the second strike that severed the tendons in his sword arm, it took less than a second.
   They buried his body honorably, the Onyx Knight had commanded it and none of the soldiers wanted to cross him after what they’d seen that day.




   Gabri burst into the crypt with his sword aflame, Devin entered just behind him with that crossbow of his nocked and held at eye level ready to shoot.  
   There was a puff of dust in the air and the sound of mice fleeing the strangers in the doorway.  The light of Gabri’s sword parted the darkness like curtains and the two of them began to walk into the crypt.  The sound of their footsteps seemed like a series of avalanches to their ears, and their heartbeats like cannons.  Almost at the same time they took a deep breath to calm themselves for what they would face ahead.
   They made their way through the first chamber and entered a long hallway, it turned out of sight to their right about thirty feet ahead of them and blocking their way was a sight that would have sickened the Beast himself.
   Children hung by their feet, they were all naked and obvious signs of whippings and torture covered their bodies.  There were bloodstains on the thighs of the girls, and the genitals on the boys had all been mutilated in one way or another.  Their throats were all cut, and many of them had had their bottom jaws removed, or their rib cages rent open.
   Devin closed his eyes and tried to fight the nausea that hit him like a brick.  This is what he was fighting, this is what he had to stop.
   They walked through the hall, trying their best to avoid the corpses hanging from the ceiling.  Devin tried not to look down at them, but one girl’s face caught his attention.  There was a gleam in its eye, like a small flash of light.  He looked closer and pissed himself when the girl’s eyes widened and a snarl came over her face.
   “By the Flame!” he shouted as the girl reached towards them, her hands were more like talons and blood began to flow from her wounds, piling on the floor below her.  She was making an odd hissing sound, and her eyes were as wide as they could go.  Her jaw kept opening and closing, like she was a rabid animal trying to bite somebody.
   Gabri spun around and saw the rest of the hallway come alive behind them.  All twenty-so children were gnawing at the bonds holding them to the ceiling and making the same hissing noises as the girl.  He grabbed Devin’s arm and pulled him away from the closest girl, just in time to cut her in half with his fiery blade before she got a hold on him.  He held the blade in front of him like a torch hoping it would frighten them off.
   It didn’t…
   It only showed them where he stood…
   Gurgling roars erupted from the throats of the children and they ran at him with a bestial fury, fiercely intent on the flesh that stood in front of them.  Their hands reached towards him, and some of them crawled on the walls to get around the ones in front.  He heard two quick clicks and hissing sounds as Devin shot two of them off the ceiling.  Gabri killed another two of them on the ground with his sword, then fell as one of them leapt on his chest.
   “Nooo!” Devin screamed as he brought his mace down on the creature’s head, splattering bits of brains and bone onto the wall.  Gabri didn’t have time to stand up so he lay there killing every child that came towards him, with Devin firing bolt after bolt into the horde.
   A minute later the last one fell into a pool of blood, one of Devin’s bolts sticking out of its eye.  Neither of them had the heart to stand so they leaned against the wall for a moment.  Then they collected themselves, Devin gathered any bolts he could reuse, and they continued to make their way deeper into the crypt.
   It was quiet as they walked down a staircase, Devin found that he couldn’t stop shaking.  “M-m-master, what were those things?”  He shivered as the image of blood spurting from the little girl’s as she tried to bite him shot into his head.  He’d never seen anything like it before.
   “Don’t think about it, trust the Flame you hear me?”  Devin nodded and tried to collect himself, Gabri could still see the fear in his eyes.  “We’re going to be fine, Golath is just ahead.”
   Devin nodded and steeled himself, the stairs ended with a heavy door in front of them.  Gabri didn’t bother knocking, he stabbed his flaming sword through the door and stepped back as it was burnt to cinders by the magic in the blade.  He picked it up off the floor, it was surprisingly cool, and stepped over the cinders into Golath’s lair.
   The Y’marr sat at a desk in the corner of the tomb, a candle gave him enough light to see what he was writing.  Everything was organized, there was a bed in the corner, a dresser beside it, and bookshelves that lined the walls of the room.  He had knotted black hair that hung to his knees, and hawk-like features.  The Y’marr ignored them, even after Gabri shouted his name.
   Gabri shouted again, “Golath!”
   With an elaborate sigh the Y’marr stood up, his back towards them he spoke in an even voice.  “What do you want Flamewielder, I have nothing you desire.”
   “Wrong,” in two strides Gabri was across the room and had the Y’marr held to wall by his throat.  “What is the Vervadian Y’marr doing in the streets of Granadad?”
   In a flash the air between them exploded outward with force, throwing Gabri across the room.  Devin fired off two bolts from his repeater crossbow and they stopped in midair like they had hit a wall.  Golath snarled and walked towards him, Gabri was on his feet in a heartbeat and stood between them.  His entire body was on fire with bright rainbow-colored flames, and the Y’marr backed away with his hand covering his eyes.
   “Don’t touch me Flamewielder, you’ve already intruded on my studies and killed my guardians, don’t push me.”  As Golath snarled Devin thought he recognized him from somewhere, he couldn’t place where though, surely he wouldn’t have seen a Y’marr walking the streets, he must be imagining things.  
   Gabri walked towards him slowly, letting the light cast from the flames push the Y’marr back to the wall.  “I came here for one reason, and I’ll leave as soon as you tell me what it is.”  As he spoke the flames around him swelled with each word, Golath was on the floor now with his hands still over his eyes.
   “You…you don’t understand, I can’t tell you Flamewielder.  There are some things that cannot be done and this is one of them.”  Then the Y’marr looked into Gabri’s eyes and saw his death there, he sighed deeply and raised his left forearm for them to see it.
   On his arm there was a red spot, about the size of a large coin branded into his arm.  The purple veins extended outward from it, making it seem that the mark had recently been made.
   Devin didn’t recognize the mark, but as soon as Gabri saw it he hissed through his teeth.  “Golath…is that what I think it is?”
   The Y’marr closed his eyes, “I couldn’t tell you even if I wanted to.  That should tell you enough.”  He breathed deeply and opened his eyes to the two of them, Devin was still very confused.  Gabri’s flame had gone out and he turned his back to them and began to walk away with his hand over his eyes.
   He turned again, resolved, and drew his sword as he briskly walked towards the Y’marr.  Golath hissed and backed into the corner as the flame lit up around him again, its light filling the chamber with dancing colors.  “I can’t let you live Golath, not after what I’ve seen.  I know another will come to take your place, but at least this will buy us some time until then.”
   Golath stood up, there was sorrow in his eyes so deep that Devin had to look away.  Why should he feel sadness for this monster?  After what he’d seen in the corridor he should want to kill the Y’marr himself.  “Let me die with my spear in my hand Flamewielder, it is all I have.”
   Then Devin recognized him, it had been about two years ago when King Gunthred had been making a proclamation to the kingdom.  It was nothing serious, something about taxes, but this man had been by his side when he made it.  
   Vallech the king had called him, the leader of the Sky Knights.  
   Devin shouted, “Vallech!”  The Y’marr turned his head towards him and smiled.
   “You recognized me, how special.  Did you idolize me?  Did you want to be just like me?  Many people wanted to be like me…and often I wanted to be more like them.”  He sighed deeply, “honestly the mantle of Y’marr has been growing harder and harder to bear over the centuries.  I have lived here almost three hundred years as a scourge on humankind.  In the beginning I relished it, but now I’m just tired.”  He looked up at them with eyes that had seemed to sink even further into their sockets.  “I don’t want to die Flamewielder, but I also don’t want to live.  I don’t want this accursed title anymore, and I don’t want to go back to Oblivion when you kill me.”  He stared at the ceiling now, “He would be angry with me, The Beast, for knowing that the Descendant of Trojia was within my grasp for almost sixteen years and I did nothing about it.  I loved the boy in my own way, he reminded me of myself.  I couldn’t bring myself to kill him.”  He laughed openly now, “how funny is that?  I’ve killed countless of innocents for years, but I cannot find the strength to kill one of my greatest enemies.”
   Gabri paused just before bringing down the blade.  “How did you travel beyond the border?  Y’marr can’t do that.”
   Golath/Vallech laughed loudly, “One of my greatest accomplishments I think.  I found a way to separate myself from the heart, I don’t even have it now.”  He pointed towards his spear in the corner, “The heart is isolated inside the Chromatis head of my spear.”  He smiled, he seemed to take pride in his accomplishment.  “As long as I live it is still tied to this world, but when I die the heart will travel to Oblivion with me to be reborn into another vessel.” 
   Gabri shook his head, “that shouldn’t be possible…”
   Golath/Vallech just smiled.  “But it is possible, I’ve done it.  And I’d be more than happy to take it to Eric for it to be destroyed if I didn’t have this accursed spot on my arm.”
   Gabri wrinkled his brow, “Eric…that’s the name of the Trojian Descendant?”  Golath/Vallech nodded.  “Where might I find him?”
   The Y’marr shrugged, “when I last saw him he had been captured by the Vervadians when we attacked Tora.  I can tell he’s still alive, but I’m not sure where he is.”  The Y’marr bit his lip.  “That’s not entirely true, I know he is in Mercia at the moment.  Don’t ask me how I can tell.  He’s been in Mercia for a little over a week.”
   Gabri straightened himself, “Golath I have a proposition for you.”  The Y’marr raised an eyebrow.  “What if I told you I could free you from the blood pact without killing you?”
   Golath/Vallech blinked three times.  “You can’t…”
   Gabri snarled and brought the flat side of the flaming sword to Golath’s/Vallech’s arm.  The Y’marr screamed as a hissing sound came from where the flaming sword made contact with the arm.  “Mark of the Beast I deny you power over this being!”  He snarled as a red light began to bleed into the rainbow colors shining from the sword.
   “Stop, please I beg you.  It hurts!”  The Y’marr screamed again as Gabri pushed the flat side of the blade harder against his arm.
   “This being is claimed by the Flame, he is no longer yours to hold.”  He closed his eyes and the light grew even brighter, drowning out the red that had been coming from Golath’s arm.  
   They both fell to their knees from exhaustion, Devin ran to his master’s side and checked him over for any injuries.  “Don’t worry about me…check Vallech.”  Devin ran to the Y’marr and put his ear to the other man’s mouth, he could feel the other man’s frantic breathing against his ear.  “Is he…alive?”
   Devin nodded and watched as Vallech clumsily stood to his feet.  He furrowed his brow and snatched at empty air in front of him, like a blind man who could finally see.
   “What…what did you do to me Flamewielder?”  He stumbled and fell to his arse as he stared at his left forearm where the red spot had once been.  All that was left was a small bruise.
   “I gave you freedom Y’marr, against my better judgment maybe, but it is not my duty to judge those who would choose to be free of evil’s grasp, it is to free them.”  The Wielder of the Flame stood to his feet, now more ominous than ever before.  The light still danced in his eyes, and true fear flooded over Vallech’s features.
   “Why…would you do such a thing?  If only you knew the things I had done, then I don’t think you would have made the same decision.”  Vallech stared at the floor for a moment, “I have lived longer than any Y’marr before me, and only now have I realized how truly evil I was.  I was made a monster by The Beast, and I fear I can be nothing else.  This…was my purpose.  My reason to exist…”
   Gabri shook his head.  “No Y’marr, my power was not given to me so that I could judge the wicked, it was given to me so that I might free the hostages.  That’s the nature of true evil, when one being enslaves another, and you do not strike me as someone who is in the habit of enslaving anyone.”  
   A single tear ran down the face of the Y’marr, he looked up at Gabri with glistening eyes.  “In all my years, never have I met one with as much power as you Flamewielder.  If you wanted the world I doubt any man could stop you.”  He shook his head and closed his eyes.  The Y’marr breathed the air like it was a feast, like each taste was the most beautiful thing in the world.  “You have done a truly wonderful thing, I was not one who deserved the luxury of freedom.  Not after the things I’ve done…”
   Gabri walked slowly to where Vallech sat on the floor, and stared at him for a moment.  Then he sheathed his blade, let the Flame of Mercolas that surrounded him dissipate, and extended a gloved hand to the Y’marr.  “I would have you as my friend, Vallech, I don’t care what you’ve done in the past.  I see the potential you have to do good, and the opportunity for you to right some serious wrongs.”  Vallech stared at the extended hand in shock, his mouth opening and closing like a fish out of water.  “What do you say?  You’ve got power, there’s no doubt of that, will you use it to help those around you?  Freeing the hostages, just as I have freed you this night?”
   Another tear fell from Vallech’s eyes…
   And for the first time in the history of Mercolas, a Y’marr and a Wielder of the Flame joined forces together to work towards the same goal.
   God save whoever would stand against them.






   “Sir Jared, I have a message for you from the Trojian commander.”  The messenger was frantic and from the way he was breathing it sounded like he’d been running to the point of exhaustion.  Jared nodded and motioned him forward to the table where they were conducting plans for a defensive stand.  The messenger bowed his head to them and then began to read from a piece of paper he held in his hand.  “General J’harra, Onyx Knight of the Trojian army extends an open hand of peace towards any Mercian who would accept it.  There is no need for more blood to be spilt, your supplies are low, your General is dead” – There was a gasp around the room as he said this – “and we outnumber you ten to one.  Lay down your arms and the only people who shall meet our blades are the ones who are in command of this rebellion.  You have until tomorrow evening.”
   The room bristled with tension as he finished the message, Lief could see the cowardice in the face of almost every politician at the table.  There were five empty seats from the men who had followed Gundleas to the front, but that still left eight men who cared more about their own lives than they did for the people of Mercia.  Lief glanced at Jared, he didn’t think the man had the tact to deal with them the way Gundleas had.  He feared what would happen if the defense fell apart because of a lack of leadership.
   As a Prince of Vervadia, he figured he was the most qualified person for the job…
   He thanked the messenger and wrote a note that he quickly sealed and handed back to him, giving him orders to return it to J’harra as fast as possible.
   Just after the messenger left the room the senators immediately set upon him, asking him what the letter said.  Jared turned to him quizzically.  “What did you tell the Trojian general Lief?”
   Lief shrugged, “I told him he could rot in hell.  And that I would sooner eat my own shit than allow the Mercians to forgo their honor in the face of such a blatant disregard to the rules of war Mercolas himself set down.”  His eyes blazed now, what he was saying was almost as much an insult to J’harra as it was to every Mercian who considered flopping sides.  “This is not their land, they have not only invaded this land and cast the rules aside like a used whore, they insinuate that it is US who would be acting foolishly for not letting them stomp all over us.”  He began to crumple up the battle-plans they had laid out earlier and threw them over his shoulder.  “That just will not do, will it?”
   The Council looked like they had just been slapped in the face, but when Lief turned to Jared he saw respect in his eye.  The General grasped his hand firmly.  “I would be honored to fight beside you Prince Lief.”  Lief grinned at him, and nodded.
   He was surprised it had taken this long for the Council members to speak up, one of the robed men shot to his feet, his face was as red as a beet.  “Who gave you the entitlement to answer such a message?  We should’ve all talked it over and THEN decided what to say!”  His mustache bristled with anger now.  “Now they’re going to kill us, every last one of us.  What should we tell our wives and children, that the Vervadian prince has killed us all?”
   Lief slowly turned to the man who was standing, he looked like a man ready to kill.  “Would you like me to tell you something you pompous bastard, If I were to kill you, and every other member of this council here and now, then the only person who would be able to say anything was Jared.”  His eyes blazed, telling them that this was no idle threat.  “You don’t know the Trojians like I do, they give you offers like this but they never abide by them.  They would kill you the same whether you came out willingly or were drug out by your hair.”
   Jared held a hand out, “I was Gundleas’ second in command.  It is my place to decide our next course of operation.”  He glanced at Lief nervously, “I believe what the Prince says.  I say we fight the Trojians while we still can.”
   The mustached council member sighed and sat back in his seat.  He ran his fingers through his hair and sighed deeply.  “So be it, we fight the Trojians.”




   Eric, Sarai, and Revlin sat around a small fire on the night before they would arrive at the lair of Wyr’fen, the night was quiet and they all ate in silence which added to the effect.
   Eric washed his wooden bowl out in a nearby stream and returned it to his bag, he watched as the other two did the same.  When they had returned to their seats by the fire he sighed and laid back onto the grass.  Neither of them said anything, Sarai was reading a book and Revlin was making the final adjustments on the two Chromatis stakes he had made.
   Revlin held out the two pieces of metal, “Done.  Could you fill them up Eric?”
   Eric opened one eye and reached out his hand, beckoning the boy to give him the weapons.  He held them in his hands and they felt very cold to the touch, almost like ice.  “How do I know when I’ve filled them enough?”
   Revlin smiled, “When they feel warm to the touch.”
   Eric laid them down on the grass in front of him and held the first one up at eye level.  He made a link to it, tied the link to his source, and let the energy flow freely into the module.  The Chromatis was like a void, it seemed that no matter how much energy he put into it that it could always hold more.  Vertigo clutched his stomach as the sensation of falling swept over him.  He continued to pour a heavy stream of energy into it until he felt it growing warm to his hand.  
   Revlin stared in amazement at the stake in Eric’s hand.  It had changed from a blueish silvery color, to that of pure gold.  Eric handed it to him and he stared at it in amazement.  “Amazing…”
   Eric did the same with the other stake as Revlin instructed Sarai on the use of the energy module.  “This button on the side will release the energy held inside the stake, you have to stab them anywhere in the torso and then press the button.  The device will do the rest.”  He grinned as Eric handed him the other stake, which glowed gold just like the first.  “This is really quite astounding, I doubt anybody knows what happens when you fill Chromatis up to the brim like this.  Blacksmiths have to keep it under intense flame for nearly a week before it can be shaped, but even then it never glows gold like this, just a coppery color.”  He was grinning like a kid who had just found a bag of candy on the street corner.
   “Well there’s a first for everything, I suggest we get some sleep.  Who knows what’s in store for us tomorrow.”  Eric rolled over on his mat and the other two followed suit.  














Part Four: A Siege and A Song
   


     
   Ignis stood on the walls of the Du Vron Dezdant fortress, there were men crowded all around him with their bows in hand.  He looked from side to side as they all stared blankly at the wall of men that approached them nearly five hundred yards away.  Ignis peered down into his quiver, he had thirty arrows there and two more quivers on the ground next to him.  He and the rest of the snipers had been spread out at regular intervals along the wall.  Lief had told them of a new breed of Trojian soldiers had started to use their own version of the hawkeye and trueflight, while it was inferior to the Mercian’s, these men still needed to be dealt with as soon as possible.  
   Ignis scanned the Trojian forces for any sign of such men.  His strength was that he could scout the enemy forces without having to raise any sort of device to his eyes, which was a real asset to the snipers and the defense strategy as a whole.
   He spotted a man holding a cylindrical looking glass to his eye, the man seemed to be looking somewhere to Ignis’ left.  Ignis picked up his Chromatis bow, nocked an arrow, and sent it whizzing right into the man’s forehead.  None of the men near him had even seen what he’d accomplished but still they cheered.  Having a Luxerne on their side was a huge comfort to most of them.
   He was sure to put his bow down to his side as soon as he made the shot, that way there was only a period of three or four seconds where an enemy sniper could see him fire and discover his location.  He spent the next few minutes spying out the Trojians before he found a senior officer giving orders to some of the men three lines back.  He killed this man just as he had the first and continued his thorough search of the Trojian forces.
   After a half hour the Trojians had begun to advance towards the Dezdant fortress.  Ignis continued to pick off officers but he’d begun to suspect that they were decoys.  The Trojians had probably noticed how excellent the snipers were at killing their officers and decided to dress regular men in officer uniforms and have them deliver messages to the line.
   Ignis grimaced, that must be why he hadn’t noticed any real delay in their advance.  He sent a runner to the rest of the snipers telling them what he’d discovered.
   The Trojians were close enough now that the regular longbowmen began to fire volleys into their midst.  They somewhat thinned the enemy lines, but after the first volley the Trojians raised their shields above their heads and that shut down most of the potency in the strikes.  At least the barbed arrows would stick in their shields and make them a little more unwieldy and difficult to maneuver.
   All at once the lines parted and allowed nearly forty Devastators to come between them and quickly fire off a round each into the Dezdant walls.
   The effect, like their names implied, was devastating.
   On impact the shots that hit near the top of the wall blew stone and man into the air.  Huge fifteen foot sections were shattered and thrown this way and that, leaving gaping holes where there used to be area for men to stand.  Shots that hit the lower parts of the wall tended to blow it inward, to the point where it looked like the wall was simply going to collapse on itself.
   The volley jarred nearly every Mercian that stood behind the walls.  Man and child alike began to cry out at the destruction of their precious stronghold.  It wasn’t the stone or the caulk that tied them to their fortress, it was the sum of all Mercian hopes and dreams of the last decade that seemed to be crumbling, that was what the wall stood for.
   Lief pulled a whistle out of the pouch of his shirt and blew it three times as hard as he could.  All at once the skies were filled with over two hundred Blue Wings that hovered above the crumbling walls.  Lief waved his hand and leapt off the wall onto Lyra’s back.  “This does not look good lowlander.”  Adelia’s familiar voice was a comfort to him.
   “No, not at all,” he watched as Revlon snatched a sealed piece of parchment from Jared’s outstretched fingers.  “Let’s just hope that our new General has done what I told him to do.”  
   The Red rider opened the sealed parchment and quickly read the orders that were contained there.  He dropped the paper and swung his sword around his head twice, like a man spinning a sling.  Lief nodded and sighed, Jared had trusted him.  Now all they needed was a miracle.




   “Hart, can I have a word with you?”  He turned his head away from his meal and looked up at the Mercian General.  He nodded and stood up from the table, leaving his bowl behind and gathering the new sword he’d acquired after he lost his axe.  He liked the blade a lot more, it had better balance than the axe did.
   They made their way down the hall into one of the more secluded rooms off to the side, Jared motioned for him to sit down at the table inside.
   “What’s the problem sir?”  Hart had very little respect for Jared, not just because of a nasty spat they’d had a few years earlier, but because he was no Gundleas.  Never would be.
   Jared sighed, “I can’t do it anymore Hart, I can’t fucking do it.  And don’t call me that, I’m no General.”  Hart was speechless.
   “What the hell are you talking about?  What do you mean ‘you can’t do it?’”  Hart was confused, and pissed off.  
   “I mean that I can’t be a General anymore, I can’t lead.  I don’t have the knack for it, all I’m doing is listening to the Vervadian Prince because he knows what he’s talking about.”  He looked miserable, like he knew that every word he said was true.
   Hart grimaced, “What do you want me to do about it?”
   Jared laid his hands on the table palms up.  “You were close to Gundleas, his second-hand man.  You deserve to lead more than I do.”
   Hart’s mouth dropped.  “Jared….you’re misunderstanding it.”
   He furrowed his eyebrows, “What’s there to misunderstand?”
   Hart clenched his jaw muscles and rubbed his palms together, if he told Jared the nature of his part in the war then he would probably never look at him the same way again.
   Hart was an assassin.  He mostly worked on the battlefield, dispatching important messengers or officers, but he’d taken hits from sources other than the Mercian army.  Gundleas knew about these odd jobs he’d taken, and hadn’t said a word in exchange for his cooperation with the military, which is exactly why he couldn’t tell Jared, because he might report him to the authorities.
   He opened his mouth to reply, but nothing came to his mind.
   Jared scowled, “was it a secret than?”
   Hart nodded, “I can’t tell you how I worked for Gundleas.”
   Jared shook his head, “but Gundleas is dead now, it doesn’t matter anymore.”
   His eyes widened and he slammed his fist into Jared’s face, knocking him onto the floor.  He stood out of his chair and pointed a finger at the other man.  “If you ever say something like that again, I’ll make you wish you had never fucking been born.” 
   Jared pulled his hand away from his mouth and stared at the blood on it, his face contorted with rage and he tackled Hart before he reached the door, slamming him against the wall on the other side of the room.
   Immediately Hart’s knife was in his hand, in three swift moves he had the other man on the floor with Hart’s knife to his throat.  “What the fuck was that for?  Want me to kill you now, you bastard?”  Jared just shook his head quickly from side to side.  Hart let him go and stood up from the floor, brushing himself off as he did.
   “You can tell me Hart…”  
   He sighed and looked up at the ceiling.  Could he really tell him?  He might be able to be more help if he was doing something more than infantry work, but he didn’t know if he could risk it.  He shrugged, what the hell.
  “I was a battlefield hit-man for Gundleas, he told me to keep it a secret.”  He stared at Jared with a penetrating gaze that forced him to look away.
   He turned back to him and bit his lip, “Ahh…..I guess I can see why you’d want to keep that quiet.”  Battlefield assassins had a terrible reputation because they technically went against the rules of war laid down by Mercola.
   Hart turned and started to walk towards the door, just before he reached it he heard Jared shout something behind him.  “I have a job for you than.”
   His gaze shot straight to the other man’s eyes.  He didn’t say anything, partially because he wasn’t quite sure he’d heard right.  Jared had always been a very “by the book” strategist, who absolutely loathed anyone who didn’t follow the rules of war.  And hiring a hit on an enemy was VERY against the rules.
   Hart frowned.  “Who, your ex-wife?”
   Jared grinned.  “How about the Onyx Knight?”




   
 
  The ocean was beautiful.
   Three Luxerne flew towards that seemingly never-ending body of water.  It was close to noon, and they had been flying all morning.  “Keep an eye out for anything out of the ordinary, we’ll follow the mountains like Lief said.  It should be somewhere close to the coast.”  They flew for another hour, looking for any trace of the prey they sought.
   “There!” Sarai shouted, far below Solvyri there was a stretch of rock that stretched out into the ocean.  At the tip of it was a large gaping hole.
   Eric nodded and guided Solvyri to the spot Sarai had pointed out.  As they neared it a wave of nausea spread over him, almost knocking him out of the saddle.  He righted himself and shook his head from side to side to try and clear it.  He needed to be focused for what was ahead.
   It took nearly ten minutes to reach the tip of the rocky peninsula.  Solvyri landed on a cropping about fifty yards away and they began to walk towards the hole.  When they reached it they were surprised to find that it was much larger than they had originally thought.  It was nearly fifty feet wide, and was pitch black as far as they could see.
   Eric picked up a stone and dropped it in the hole to measure its depth, he counted up to thirty seconds before he gave up and tried again with a larger stone.  The result was the same.
   “This is the place, I can feel it.”  Sarai had closed her eyes when she said it, and now she bit her lip out of anxiety.  Eric knew what she was talking about, the nausea had grown stronger and the air felt wrong.  Like a cloud of smog was hanging over them and it was somehow less…alive than the air everywhere else.
   “Well what do we do now?”  Revlin nervously fingered the golden stake at his belt, it had stained his breeches with a light golden dust as well as his hands.
   Eric motioned towards the pit, “We ride Solvyri in there of course.  I’m sure Wyr’fen will have his lair heavily guarded.  I should hardly think that he would simply be lying at the bottom of a hole in the ground.”
   The other two shrugged and they all piled back onto the blood-red wyvern.  None of them said anything, the light conversations they’d had along the way faded into the backs of their heads as the reality of their task towered above them.  They truly had no idea what they were facing.  An unknown enemy is always the worst kind, and that was exactly what they were up against.  With Brazen dead they didn’t have anyone able to prepare them for whatever horrors they might have to face.  It was tragic that he’d lived his entire life planning and scheming just to die as his plans were beginning to become reality.  A bit like an architect designing a building that he would never see completed.
   Solvyri slowly descended vertically into the pit, using his wings to keep their speed to a minimum.  Eric pulled out the lightstone chala had given him and fed it the weave of Alir it required.  He held the light above his head and took in his surroundings.
   The walls around them were covered in bones and pieces of flesh, obviously to show what happened to uninvited visitors.  The walls had changed to something other than rock, the closer he looked the more it looked like…
   “The walls……they’re alive aren’t they?”  Sarai didn’t stutter the tiniest bit as she said it.  The walls looked like a body that was cut open, like the insides of someone, they were grisly and red like a raw steak.
   Revlin shivered and fingered the stake at his side.  “I think we found the right place.”
   Eric nodded, this was definitely the right place, now he just needed to figure out where they needed to go next.  The answer made itself clear as a side-passage appeared another fifty feet down, the edges of the opening had large spikes protruding from it like the maw of some beast.  He guided Solvyri through the hole, still feeding the lightstone to provide them with its yellow globe of light.
   The hallway narrowed and soon was too small for Solvyri to fit through.  Wincing as they left the protection of him, they gathered their weapons and proceeded down the tunnel, for that was what it had become.
   The walls were sticky and smelled like blood as they walked further and further down into the lair of Wyr’fen.  Everything smelled rotten, like a decomposing carcass, it was enough to make them very sick to their stomachs.  Eric almost slipped and caught himself by putting a hand on the wall, only to jerk it back  and quickly wipe it off on his pant leg.  The sickly red stain it left there looked just like blood.
   Eventually the tunnel opened up into a large chamber.  In front of them there was a huge underground lake filled with the same red liquid coating the walls, they all ignored how much like blood it looked like.  The lightstone could not shine enough light for them to see to the other side, and the sides of the lake ended where the walls of the tunnel were.  There was no way across but to swim.
   Eric frowned and stepped forward to the edge of the lake, it looked like it was thicker than normal water so they could probably swim across if they had too.  Something didn’t sound right about a Y’marr making an obstacle that he would have to pass every time he wanted to return to his lair, there had to be some short cut or roundabout way.
   He glanced along the shoreline, there was about thirty yards from wall to wall, but there was nothing there that looked like it could be used to cross.  He closed his eyes and tried to think about the qualities of the Y’marr ahead of them.  He was the fierce Y’marr, he was brutal and straightforward.  
   Eric paced the shoreline again, this time looking for anything that might be used as a marker.  He stopped at a peculiar dark spot on the ground, it looked like it had been burned by a torch.  He glanced out at the lake and looked closely at the water directly in front of him.
   There, he saw it, the ripples along the surface stopped on either side of the water in a straight line, about five feet on either side of where the burn was.  It was a pathway.
   He gingerly stepped forward and his boot struck stone only two inches deep.  He grinned and motioned for them to follow him as he made his way across the lake of blood.  His boots were soon soaked with it, and he tried to ignore the slimy sensation between his toes as he walked through the viscous fluid.  Bile rose in his throat and, again, he ignored it and continued walking forward until he reached the other side.
   This beach was a perfect mirror of the other side, where the water ended there was a small stretch of sticky membrane that narrowed towards another tunnel entrance.  They warily made their way towards it, Eric’s lightstone casting eerie shadows on the walls as they did.  The sound of their breathing was the only noise that could be heard as they travelled through the winding tunnel.  At first it only wound right and left, but later began to move up and down as well, so that they were constantly changing direction as they traversed the path.
   Soon they had lost all sense of direction, the constant twisting and turning had dissolved every cognitive sense of where the entrance to the tunnels lay.  The only thing they could do was press forward towards what they knew could very possibly be their deaths.
   Eric gasped as the membrane he’d just put his hand on jerked away from him, like a hand would pull away from a fire.  A breeze began to blow through the tunnel, but instead of smelling like fresh air it smelled like a rotting corpse.  They all began to cough and retch from the strength of the odor.
   “Wha-“ Revlin coughed hard as he tried to get the words out, “what is that?”
   Eric shook his head and tried to stop himself from gagging as well.  Sarai was the first to straighten herself and begin to walk forward again, she turned her head to them and spoke softly.  “You mustn’t let yourself vomit, this is meant to weaken you before Wyr’fen can destroy us himself.  That’s what all of this is, I can feel it, he would do something like this.”
   Revlin held his breath for a few seconds, then let it out in a rush.  “Sarai’s right, that sounds like something a Y’marr would do.  Weaken their prey before they catch it, especially if this one is supposedly savage beyond comprehension.”
   The three of them continued through the tunnels until they arrived in a room filled with what seemed like hundreds of little…
   “Children?” Ahead of them in a corner of the room sat a group of children in a circle around a statue of a terrifying creature.  They heard the children’s voices chanting one sentence in a slow rhythm, their voices sent chills down Eric’s spine.
   “Our life for the body, our songs for the body, our souls for the body.”  
   Revlin crept forward towards the group of chanting children.  Eric and Sarai tried to motion for him to stop but he just waved a hand to silence them.  Once again the chant rang though the chamber.
   Our life for the body, our songs for the body, our souls for the body.
   Eric shook his head to try and rid himself of the sound, it seemed to permeate through his being and strike against his soul in the worst possible way.  He felt sick and the lightstone flashed a few times as he struggled against the voices.
   Suddenly the voices stopped and every child in the chamber turned their heads slowly towards the three Luxerne and spoke in one voice.  
   Leave now, the body is displeased by your presence.
   Again the voices seemed to come from inside them as much as from outside them.  They closed their eyes against it and stood firm against the cerebral outburst.  Revlin stepped forward and shouted across the room.
   “The body can fuck itself, tell Wyr’fen to face us like a man and not like some coward hiding behind his playthings!”  Eric and Sarai tried to stop him, to tell him that what he was saying was not the right thing to say if they were going to make it out of this place alive, but before they reached him the walls of the chamber began to convulse and writhe with a sickly rhythm.  Revlin turned around and gasped as one of the children flung themselves across the entire length of the room and landed right behind him.
    “Watch out!” Eric shouted as he blew the thing to pieces with a link.  The lightstone flickered from his lack of concentration, and plunged them into near-darkness for a few seconds until he restored the flow of energy coursing through it.  
   Our life for the body…
   More children began flying through the air towards them, others ran faster than anything he’d ever seen, and still others crawled on the ceiling like some sort of perverse insect.  The effect was that the three intruders were almost immediately surrounded by fifty of the creatures coming from many different directions.
   “To the tunnel” Revlin yelled as he began sprinting towards the way they’d come.  Sarai was closest and dove back into the narrow space just before the other two. 
   Our life for the body… 
   Eric slid the lightstone into his pocket and crafted two flaming swords in either of his hands with a steady flow of Alir from his source.  He shouted for Revlin to shoot around him when the first of the creatures reached the entrance of the tunnel.  Eric let the Alir flow through him like a river and wash away every hint of emotion left inside.  
   Caught up in the battle-calm he began killing the children mercilessly as they approached the tunnel entrance.  He was like a farmer, and the creatures fell to his blades like stalks of wheat before the scythe.  He was aware of bolts firing around his head, and he almost smiled as he saw them falling all around him.  This was easy, it seemed effortless.
   All at once the Alir left him.  His flaming swords disappeared as fast as they had come, and he fell to his knees from the shock of losing his hold on the energy that had been coursing through him only seconds before. 
   Our life for the body… 
   Revlin let out a cry of shock and shouted for Sarai to drag him back away from the path of the creatures that had regained their confidence and began to rush forward again.  He pulled a round device from his belt and crushed the pouch inside by squeezing it with both hands.  He threw it towards the creatures and turned his head.
   He felt a gust of wind and, even with his eyes closed and back turned, his vision flashed s bright that he felt dizzy from it.  He turned and began firing off bolts into the crowd of, now blind, children.  Their faces were menacing as they clawed at their eyes, and some of them still tried to reach him by licking the ground at their feet and tasting where the three had stepped.  Many were writhing on the ground and a few looked like they had been killed just from the light itself.  
   When the bolts in the cartridge had run out he tossed the repeater to the side rather than reloading it, he reached into his pack until he found his flame bow.  He pulled it out, quickly loaded a canister of the phorys mixture into it, and let loose a burst of flame towards the blinded children in front of him.  They howled, and he tried not to retch as the scent of burning hair and flesh reached his nostrils.  He’d never smelled anything so terrible in his entire life.
   After just a few moments he’d killed or at least maimed them to the point where they would no longer be a problem.  Revlin turned back and saw Sarai’s wide-eyes staring at him.  Eric was just now waking up from his fainting episode.  
   “What the hell happened?”  Revlin shouted as he walked towards the other Luxerne.  “You almost got us killed, what did you do to yourself?”
   Eric just shook his head. “I-I don’t know what happened, it was like I was cut off.  It happened once before, I think I’ve just overexerted myself.”  
   Sarai still hadn’t let go of him, her large brown eyes stared at nothing on the wall in front of her.  Eric shook himself out of her grasp, “Come on, we need to finish this.  Wyr’fen is just weakening us more and more the longer we stay in here.”
   Revlin gathered up his repeater and gestured forward with it, “Let’s go than.  We’ve got work to do.”




   Lief tried to remember his lessons to aid him in the battle:  Your gift allows you to change the things that make up this world, to alter the normality of it in a way that suits your favor, he tried not to think of Brazen as he thought back to the words the old druid had told him.  When you change something you can do one of three things to it.  You may change its position in space, you may change its mass, and you may change the laws of the universe around it.  To do this you must harness an aether material called lightstuff…
   Yes, he remembered correctly.  Brazen had taught him the theory of all three disciplines, but only the application of the last two, of how to change something’s mass and how to change the universal laws in a small area.  
   It was the first discipline, to change something’s position in space that he needed to accomplish now.
   He pulled lightstuff from the aether and let it fill him, not to the brim, but just so that the edges of his senses were bathed in a bluish light.  A tingling sensation spread over his entire body, this was a good sign.  He was completely rested.
   He reached into Lyra’s saddlebag and pulled out a perfectly spherical rock that had been made at his request.  It was polished smooth and fit nicely in his fist.  “Okay Adelia, get me on the ground at the top of that rise in the plains.”  The plains made a sort of hump right between the Dezdant stronghold and the Trojian forces so that there was a slope on each side.  He crossed his fingers as Adelia hovered eight feet above the ground to let him jump off onto the ground.
   He stood up straight and set the sphere onto the ground in front of him, already he heard shouts from the Trojians who were only a couple hundred yards in front of him.  Ignoring them, he fed the lightstuff into the sphere as fast as he could.  Once it held enough that it began to glow blue he touched his hand to it and wove together three streams of the lightstuff to match the second discipline.
   Immediately the sphere began to grow at an alarming rate, he continued to feed it lightstuff as fast as he could, but the stone was taking it as fast as he could give it.  Within twenty seconds it was larger than a house, after thirty as tall as two houses.  He watched for the signs that the sphere could grow as large as it could, most things couldn’t grow more than their original matter could handle.  The denser the object, the bigger it could grow. 
   It was perfect, the marble was working wonderfully and just began to lose the blue glow after it reached close to eighty feet high.
   He stopped the flow of lightstuff and the towering boulder began to roll towards the Trojian forces laid out before him.  It didn’t take long until it began rolling over the men that were splayed out on the field.  He heard shouts and screams as it rolled over  more and more of them until it came to a stop almost at the other side of the army.  
   Now came the tricky part, he wove the lightstuff into the pattern for the third discipline and attached it to two places, right in front of him, and on the stone he’d just sent rolling towards them and stepped back as the weave took effect.
   For a second he thought it hadn’t worked, until the air in front of him rippled and regurgitated the same marble stone that, once again, rolled towards the Trojian army that had just begun to fill in the gaps where it had gone before.
   He heard shouts from behind him as the Mercian counterstrike flooded out of the fortress.  Within twenty minutes every man that lived within the city had taken his place on the field in front of their beloved stronghold.  They weren’t yet organized, and the Trojians had begun to advance towards them.  In the twenty minutes that it had taken for the Mercians to get out of the city he had sent the marble ball of death rolling towards the Trojians twelve times.  Every time he would place it at a different point on the rise so that it would roll towards a new section of the army.  He had probably killed close to a thousand of the twenty-thousand Trojians remaining from the conquest eastward.  The size of their army had been more than cut in half from the five-thousand Mercians that had fought them every step of the way.  
   Now they were down to something around nineteen thousand men, against nine-thousand trained Mercians and a rabble of another eight-thousand.  Gunthred must be furious…
   The longbowmen began firing volleys into the Trojians while the Blue Wings acted as supply men, bringing more arrows to the lines from the fortress.  Every woman and child had worked for close to two weeks making as many arrows as possible, and had emerged with something close to two-thousand quivers of them.  Each quiver held fifteen arrows in it.
   Lief was exhausted from the energy he’d had to put into using as much lightstuff as he had, and he stumbled as he walked back towards the cheering Mercians.  He raised his head and shouted, “don’t cheer yet, the fighting’s just begun.”


   
Our life for the body, our songs for the body, our souls for the body…
   Eric, Sarai, and Revlin emerged into an antechamber filled to the brim with people all facing the same direction.  At first the three Luxerne had prepared to fight, but at a second glance, the glassy-eyed humans seemed like they were only interested in what was happening at the front of the room.
   A hundred yards in front of them was a huge platform that rose out of the floor, a sickly red light shone up from it onto countless skinned bodies that hung from the ceiling by ropes attached to their hands.  A voice boomed through the chamber.
   “WHO DARES ENTER THE LAIR OF WYR’FEN?”
   Eric let a cone of flame burst forth from his open hands to burn a path through the people that blocked their way to the podium, none of them screamed or shouted, and they burned quickly like parchment.  Revlin and Sarai held the golden Chromatis stakes in their hands as they advanced towards the platform right behind Eric.  
   There were stone steps that led up towards Wyr’fen, and they walked up them with grim determination.  They had not been able to see what lay at the top, so whatever happened would likely be a surprise.  The air in the chamber grew thick, as if the world around them was clenching its fist in preparation for a blow.
   They reached the top of the platform and immediately a large bestial form flew into Eric, tackling him backwards down the steps while letting out a snarling noise like a dog.  A burst of light and heat shot out from the two of them as Eric made a link to the Y’marr and sent it flying back onto the platform.
   Their plan was for Eric to use as little of his power as possible, in case Wyr’fen would become afraid like U’mar had.  That didn’t seem to be an issue though once they reached the platform and saw the roughly humanoid figure picking itself up from the ground in the eerie red light that bathed the chamber.  His muscles looked sickeningly enormous, and his shoulders were so large that it made him hunch forward when he stood up.  His entire body was covered with matted hair, and he snarled with needle-sharp teeth as he began to run towards them again.
   Revlin pulled out his repeater and began firing bolts towards the Y’marr as fast as he could.  Wyr’fen dodged most of them with inhuman speed, and the few that struck him didn’t seem to do any damage at all.  Eric ran forward with his spear leveled to meet the beast but it swerved around the point at the last minute and tackled him to the ground again on top of the platform.  
   Eric was struggling to fight off the creature, and to protect his neck from its fangs.  Sarai rushed forward with the Chromatis stake held high above her, poised to stab the Y’marr.  Just before she brought it down between his shoulders, Wyr’fen turned and backhanded her twenty feet across the chamber where she struggled to try to stand back up while clutching her head in her hands.
   Revlin shot off three bolts into its back and gritted his teeth as it howled.  The Y’marr turned its gaze towards him and roared furiously.
   While it was distracted, Revlin saw Eric reach towards the Y’marr with his fingers outstretched.  He placed his palm directly over its heart and lit up the chamber from the enormous energy being transferred from the link between them.
   Eric fought against the tremendous force emanating from the Heart as he loosed his source on it.  Rather than making a link and forcing it through that, he lowered the walls that held the source in and let it flow through his hand and into Wyr’fen.   It was a glorious flow, and the feeling of it sent shivers down his spine.  This was true power, he didn’t think anything could stand in his way.
   Our life for the body…
   Revlin and Sarai turned sharply towards the staircase just in time to see a mass of transient humans rushing towards them with a primal fury.  “Hurry up Eric, we don’t have a lot of time!”  He had pulled out his flame bow and was shooting a steady stream of fire towards the creatures.
   Eric heard him shout and turned his attention away from the Y’marr above him for just a second.  When he turned back, Wyr’fen lay on the ground in front of him panting from exhaustion.  Eric saw that the defenders were getting closer to Revlin and flung himself towards them wielding two flaming swords.  
   He was afraid as he approached them, afraid that his power would be cut off again and leave him helpless.  He closed his eyes briefly and closed off his emotions.  No fear, no pain, no love, no concern.  He was doing his job, and that required no feelings.
   Immediately he knew that he had found the balance, the reason why he’d been losing control of the source.  Yes he had kept his concentration, but wielding something that powerful required an empty mind so that it could flow.
   He entered the midst of them with a steady resolve.  
   Our life for the body…
   He caught the first one in the head and turned his eyes away from the flash that came when the brand met flesh.  The smell of burning skin wafted over him as he stabbed another one of them through the eye socket, sending brains and gore into the air from the mini-explosion.  
   He felt hands gripping his left shoulder and made a link to the creature, sending it flying over a hundred feet to collide into the wall.  His swords killed them one after another as he flowed like water from stance to stance, always starting one as soon as the earlier ended.
   Sarai had finally made it to her feet and saw the Y’marr lying on the ground in front of her.  Eric and Revlin were doing their best to hold off the horde of creatures that threatened them.  She ran her finger over the Chromatis stake in her hand, she would finish off the Y’marr.
   As she ran towards Wyr’fen he began to stumble to his feet, this time he stood on two like a normal person instead of all fours like a dog.  Sarai braced herself to counter the attack she was expecting but saw the terrified look in its eyes.  It was eying the edge of the platform and began to walk towards it as fast as it could.
   He must have realized our intentions, oh no… She ran at him as fast as she could.  He was getting closer to the edge and he started to jump just as Sarai leapt towards him with the Chromatis stake held in her hands.  She felt the grim satisfaction as it drove through his flesh, and gritted her teeth as it grinded against his scapula on its way through.  She felt it stop in a thick, meaty area right where his heart should be.
   Wyr’fen screamed and threw himself off the platform before she could press the button to release the energy, it would be for vain if she didn’t finish the job now.  She let herself be pulled off the platform with him and squeezed the button on the Chromatis stake as she felt her feet leave the ground.  




   The apprentice, the Wielder of the Flame, and the Agent of Darkness walked towards the palace of Granadad together.  After close to an hour of conversing Vallech had told them about the impersonator in the palace, Bledhreft he was called.  He was a bloodletter impersonating senator Harris of the council, and working towards acquiring emperor Gunthred’s blood.  
   Together they would kill him.  Simply that, they wouldn’t try to reason or question him.  Gabri was the Wielder of the Flame, and his position allowed him complete lawful immunity in every case, except for killing the emperor.  
   The guards opened the palace doors at the wave of his hand, and they briskly walked to the dormitory suites where the senators lived when they weren’t attending council meetings.  They were approached again by two guards who Gabri brushed aside, before walking to the directory and looking up where senator Harris lived.  
   “East courtyard, eighth suite.  Let’s go, weapons ready.”  The three of them walked down the passageway towards the East courtyard.  There was quite a contrast between the three of them.
