In the lead was Gabri, with his snow white cloak trailing behind him and his black leather armor making squeaking sounds as he walked.  To either side was Devin, wearing the gray robes of an apprentice monk, and Vallech suited up in all black chainmail that clinked as he walked.  Gabri’s sword was flaming in his hand, making rainbow lights dance off the walls.  Devin carried his mace, and Vallech his ornamented lance.  Altogether they looked quite formidable.
   They reached the door that had a number eight marked on it and braced themselves for the confrontation that they knew would come as soon as they stepped through the doorway.  Gabri turned back to the other two and nodded affirmatively, then turned and kicked the door in.
   Inside senator Harris turned towards them from his lounging position on a couch, pulled his hand out of his pants, and shouted at them furiously.  “Who the fuck let you in?”  A look of terror covered his features as he stood up from his suggestive position on the couch.  “No, no, please!”  He screamed as Gabri walked towards him, the Flame of Mercolas shining around him.
   He turned to Vallech, “This is Bledhreft?”  The Y’marr nodded, disgust riddling his features.
   “That’s the bastard, a bloodletter of the first.  He’s after the emperor.”  Bledhreft stared at Vallech openmouthed, and then screamed at him.
   “The Dark One sees you Y’marr!  He sees your betrayal, and he gnashes his teeth with his anger towards you.  You will suffer more than any other once you are in the clutches of Oblivion.”  He grinned and shed his skin like some sort of sick insect would its carapace.  He slid out of it and unfurled his wings, flinging droplets of blood all over the walls.  The bloodletter was a bluish color and had a squished face like a dog, it had no hair, and its fingers ended in sharp claws.  Its skin had a slimy texture that always looked wet, and it hissed furiously as the thing flew straight at Gabri.
   Light shone out from the pores of his skin and sent the creature flying back to the wall, and a concussion rippled through the air.  Furniture fell over, and glass dishes shattered against the walls.  Vallech rushed forward with inhuman speed and leapt into the air, hovering for a split second before bringing his lance down to stab into the wing of the creature.  Its scream was earsplitting as blood spurted from its wing, the bloodletter kicked Vallech in the chest, sending him flying across the room to land in a pile of broken dishes that had fallen off the shelf.
   Devin’s mace slammed into the bloodletter’s jaw, making a loud cracking noise and knocking the creature onto its back.  It lay on the floor without moving as Devin turned and walked back to where his Master stood, it had all happened in a split-second, almost before any of them had blinked.
   “That wasn’t difficult at all.”  Devin grinned as he watched his master sheathe his sword.  He heard a groan from the corner.
   “Fucking bastard had some energy to it.”  Vallech said, as he pulled himself up from the wreckage of the room.  He brushed off his chest and walked over to where his lance had fallen to pick it up off the ground.
   Gabri walked over towards where the bloodletter lay and examined the creature closely.  It had stopped breathing and it lay in a pool of a black ichor-like substance that stuck to his boots when he stepped into it.  Vallech stooped down beside him for a moment before standing up and moving towards the door.
   “You should burn that, and we should get moving as quick as possible.  You remember those Luxerne I told you were gathering in Mercia?”  Gabri nodded and called the Flame of Mercolas to set fire to the bloodletter.  Hissing and popping sounds came from it as the fire devoured the corpse.  A stinking smell like rotten meat filled the air, and black smoke began to rise from the body.
   Together they left the palace, commandeered three horses from the palace stables, and began riding towards Mercia.
   






   Adelia dropped from Lyra’s back with her sword drawn and landed on the back of a Trojian pikeman, driving the blade between his shoulders and slamming him into the ground.  She wove a Thoric sign and sent bolts of lightning towards the line of shields in front of her, knocking back four men that made up the shield wall.
   She waved her hand to the ten Blademasters standing behind her, “Go now!”  They raised the monstrous swords they carried and rushed forward through the gap in the shield-wall, leading a group of Mercian infantry behind them.
   She whistled for Lyra and thrust her sword into the groin of a Trojian soldier who had been running towards her, jumping inside his guard and catching his arm on her shoulder instead of the blade.  The man screamed and she slammed her gauntleted fist into his throat to silence him.  He fell to the ground, clutching his ruined throat with his hands.
   The pegii neighed and hovered just above her, its wings sending little gusts of wind onto the back of her neck.  She turned and grabbed ahold of Lyra’s saddle, pulling herself into it and tucking her sword into its sheathe as she did so.
   Adelia had spent the entire battle flying to different points on the front and breaking through the Trojian shield-wall to allow the Mercians to push them steadily back towards the forests.  So far things had been going well, they had begun advancing around the left flank thanks to her leading the way through the shields that opposed to them.  
   She saw a group of Trojians break through the Mercian shields off to her left, she immediately flew towards it as fast as she could.  She checked the Yinnis ward that protected her from archer fire and dropped off of Lyra’s back once she was close to the ground, calling an Extensia sign as she hit the ground.
   A shockwave and a burst of bluish light shot out from her, sending the enemy soldiers around her flying into the air.  She wove three Infernic signs and made a wall of fire that blocked off the Trojian advance.  She shook from the effort of holding it up as the Mercians reformed their shield-wall around her, and warned them before letting the flames die.
   Trojians ran forward as soon as the fire was gone, but the Mercians were ready and held back their sudden advance.  Adelia whistled for Lyra once more, now feeling the strain of the castings quite clearly.  When she was on the pegii’s back she guided them back to the Dezdant fortress where they rested against part of the ruined wall.  One of the servants brought her a flask of water, and another brought a bowl of soup from one of the houses.
   She sipped the soup and ran her fingers through Lyra’s coat.




   Droft bashed his shield into the face of a Trojian soldier off to his left, sending him sprawling to the ground holding his ruined face in his hands.  He drew from the energy stored in his Chromatis sword and felt all of his weariness wash away to be replaced with a renewed awareness and reflex.  He held his sword out in front of him and fell into the swan stance with his left foot in front of the other.  
   He was standing just behind the Mercian shield-wall with a squad of eight Blademasters and twelve Mercian elite infantry.  He glanced at Hordrar, who was leading them, and heard him shouting for the squad to form up.  Droft took his place at the front of the boar’s tusk shape they were forming.  The boar’s tusk was shaped like a V-formation that birds used when migrating, only the white-space in the “V” was filled with regular infantry.  The edges of it were where the Blademasters stood, and Droft was at the point.
   The Mercian shield-wall separated at Hordrar’s command and Droft rushed forward, drawing energy from the Chromatis blade and turning the blade bright orange from the heat.  Just before he reached the Trojians he dove into the shieldman’s knees and opened up the wall for the rest of the squad.
   The man underneath him struggled for his knife, Droft pulled his sword up and slammed the hilt into the man’s forehead.  Blood spurted into the air, some of it landing on him, and the Trojian screamed at the dent that was left in his skull.  He began to thrash as Droft slammed the hilt into his head again, and again, opening up his skull and breaking off bits of bone until snot, blood, brains, and vomit had all coalesced together into a stomach-curdling mix.
   Ignoring the screams of the dying man, Droft rolled forward and brought his hissing blade up into the groin of another soldier.  He quickly pulled the blade out and sunk it into the man’s skull, smelling and hearing the sword burning his brains before he pulled it out.
   They punched through the Trojian shield-wall like a hammer through a sheet of paper-thin aluminum.  Mercian infantry flooded through the breach, forcing their way farther and farther into the Trojian army.  They were on the right hand flank of their forces, closer to the middle than the sides though.  Just that initial charge had let them cut their way almost one-eighth of the way through to the backside of the army.  If they could effectively divide the Trojians into two separate entities then they might have a better chance in destroying them.
   He bellowed loudly and pressed forward harder, cutting through the enemy as fast as he could.  He could feel Mercians behind him, fighting their way forward just as he was.  




   Adelia was sipping her soup slowly, savoring the meaty flavor that caressed her taste buds.  Just as she was raising the bowl to her mouth to finish it, she heard someone sprinting towards her from the courtyard.  
   “Adelia come quickly, a group of Blademasters and elites are forcing their way through the Trojian army.  They need as much support as we can give them.”  Lief was frantic, if they were able to accomplish what he said then routing the Trojians would become a definite reality.  She set her bowl on the ground and rushed to gather her weapons to put them in Lyra’s saddlebags.  The pegii neighed in irritancy, but Adelia silenced her with a quick slap on the flank.  She jumped onto the pegii’s back and turned to Lief, holding out a hand for him to climb up as well.  He grinned and ran forward to climb onto Lyra’s back.  
   They took off into the skies above the fortress, the rush of wind against their faces eliminated any weariness they may have been feeling.  They flew over the battle, searching for the breach in the Trojian line.
   “There!”  Adelia shouted, as she guided Lyra towards the Mercian right flank.  Below them there was furious movement and a group of Mercians fighting their way through the Trojian soldiers that stood in front of them.
   She guided Lyra above the Mercians and hovered ten feet above them; the men below her looked up and began cheering Lief’s name.  She turned to face Lief in the saddle.  “Are you ready for this lowlander?”
   He gave her the most dazzling smile he could muster.  “You better fucking believe it.”
   They jumped…




   Hart strode with an officer’s confidence through the middle of the Trojian camp.  He wore the clothing of one of the minor field-officers that he’d come into contact with, and switched the infantry outfit he’d had previously acquired with that one.  It fit him well enough, and he had plenty of room in the loose sleeves to hide his blade.
   The curved knife that he carried had been dipped in nighthawk venom, and sharpened until it could cut a ribbon by simply running the fabric lightly over the sharp side of the blade.  And the nighthawk poison was potent enough that one-tenth of a drop could kill a horse.
   He was careful not to cut himself as he cleared the rest of the distance to the General’s tent, making sure that he walked with the arrogant strut that most of the officers carried.  He blended in with the Trojians effortlessly, smoothly fitting in to the mentality of the assassin he had been hiding for so long.  Everything fit into place, every detail came sharply into focus as his mind woke up from its long hibernation.
   General J’harra had a grand set of quarters, the tent he slept in stood nearly thirty feet high, and had a diameter of something close to the same as its height.  It was probably filled with furniture and drawings.
   He fingered handle of the poisoned blade, drawing his thumb along the polished wood of its handle.  He could almost feel its thirst for blood as he neared the Onyx Knight’s tent.  Two guards stood outside.
   They were nothing.
   He walked towards the opening between them, ignoring their presence like an officer would.  When they stepped in front of him he had already pulled two of his other knives from his pant legs and hurled them into the Trojian’s throats.
   Ignoring their gurgling moans, he quickly drug their bodies around to the back and stepped into the tent and put his knives away.  He wouldn’t need them.  Inside he saw J’harra sleeping on his cot; his curved sword lay on the floor next to his sleeping figure.  
   He heard a loud shout behind him, and the Trojian general began to stir from his slumber.  Hart blended into the shadows of the interior of the tent just as the flap was violently yanked open.
   “Lord Jharra, lord J’harra!”  Two armed men ran into the ten and jerked back as the Onyx Knight jumped to his feet, blade in hand.
   He blinked a few times, trying to rid the eyes of sleepiness and spoke to the soldiers in a low voice.  “Why are you here, is it an emergency?”
   They both looked around, “We saw that there were no guards outside, is there anything wrong?”
   J’harra scowled.  “Nothing’s wrong, don’t worry, go back to your posts.”  The two soldiers shrugged and left almost as quickly as they’d come in; J’harra sighed and lay back down in his cot.  
   Hart counted out five minutes in his head, any longer and he would’ve been afraid that someone would find the guard’s bodies outside.  He slid the poisoned knife from its sheathe and left the shadow of a closet that he had been crouched behind.  
   He smiled as he cut the Onyx Knight’s throat, it turns out that he bled like every other man.




   “Sarai!”  Revlin screamed as she plummeted off the top of the stone platform in the middle of the antechamber while clinging to the Y’marr.  An unearthly scream resounded through the living chamber, echoing strangely of the membrane-like walls.  
   A sound louder than thunder blotted out the scream and the sounds of the dying possessed, and a light flashed through the chamber; blinding him and making him drop to his knees.  All the sounds around him had ceased, he tried to fight the pain in his head as he slowly opened his eyes.  
   He felt a hand on his shoulder, “What happened Revlin, where is Sarai?”  Eric’s voice was shaking, there were many cuts on his face, and his clothes were drenched in blood burnt black from the heat of the flames he’d been crafting.  He raised his hand and pointed to the edge of the platform.
   Eric ran to the edge and peered down, it was a long ways down but there was no sign of anything at the bottom.  He drew from the energy still coursing through him and leapt off the edge.  He applied mechanical energy to himself near the bottom to stop the fall from killing him, he’d never done it before but anything seemed possible after that fight and it just felt right.  He landed roughly, and scuffed his knees badly, but he stood up and brushed the dust off of his knees.
   The only thing on the ground was a small writhing mass of what looked like liquid darkness.  The ichor-like substance was no bigger than his hand, it looked like it was boiling because there was steam coming off of it and there was a loud hissing sound.  
   He heard Revlin shout, and then the sound of his hurried footsteps coming down the stairs.  There was no sign of Sarai or Wyr’fen, but there was a large pile of rocks on the ground around him.  He looked up and saw a large gouge in the side of the plateau, it had been made from the force of the energy inside the Chromatis stake.
   Revlin had arrived at his shoulder and was examining the scene in front of them, Eric saw him look upwards and gasp as he saw the obliterated section of rock that the explosion had caused.  It seemed that he also noticed the absence of their third party member when his breath caught in his throat and his fists clenched tight by his sides.  He closed his eyes for a bit longer than a normal blink and his nostrils flared.
   Eric’s throat was tight, and he tried to say something to honor their fallen comrade but nothing came but blankness.  Was that the cost of destroying a Y’marr, the life of the one destroying it?
   The black ichor on the ground caught on fire, and a profane shriek resounded through the chamber, echoing and re-echoing off the walls until it became almost unbearable.  Black smoke was shooting out of the fire in a plume that grew until they could see nothing else.  
   Eric sent out tendrils of energy through the air and felt his way to the burning substance.  It was sending out heat like a mini-sun.  His instincts were guiding him as he poured more energy onto the flame.  The screaming grew higher in pitch and the air seemed to vibrate with the energy coming from the flame.  Eric could feel the hairs on his eyebrows burning from the overwhelming heat.  
   All at once the flame collapsed on itself, compressing all the energy into ball the size of a pin for a split-second before exploding with a force one-hundred times what it had been before.  Eric and Revlin were thrown back towards the wall and landed with their hands covering their eyes.  
   Other than the ringing in their ears the hall was eerily silent.  Not even the sound of wind was in the chamber, the only sound that could be heard was that of the two of them breathing, and that seemed to be ten times as loud as usual.  Revlin stood up and offered his hand to Eric, who took at and stood to his feet as well.  
   The two of them stared at each other and then at the empty chamber in front of them.  The fires that had lit it were out and the only source of light was the lightstone in Eric’s hand.  Revlin took a deep breath and flashed his boyish grin, it occurred to Eric that he was only fifteen years old.  Eric wasn’t even to his eighteenth year yet, and they had both played their parts in killing one of the most ancient and evil beings that resided on the continent.  He grieved for Sarai, but she would not have wanted them to forget their quest.  Brazen had said that the Beast was bending the bars that held him in Oblivion, they only had a short amount of time before he broke free.  
   One down he thought, as the two Luxerne began the long journey back to the surface.  
   Five to go.




   Close to one-hundred shouting Mercians broke through the last line of men that stood between them and the free plains behind the Trojian army.  
   Lief raised his sword to the air, “Go kill them, kill them all!”  Every soldier knew that the only men that stood in the rear of the army were the ones that weren’t expected to take part in the fighting.  The enlisted or drafted men who were only there because they needed the money to send back to their families.  They were the ones who expected long days of marching, but didn’t expect to take part in any real fighting.  Now, those men were facing Mercian elites who had begun cutting through them like they were nothing more than sacks of wheat.
   Seventy Blue Wings, each carrying one extra soldier landed behind the Trojian lines.  With the rider and the extra soldier, either a Blademaster or an elite, that doubled the number of men that had fought through the Trojians.  
   It didn’t take long for the Trojians to close the gap that the Mercians had made, and separate those men from the rest of the army.  With the bulk of the Trojian forces facing the Mercians head-on it didn’t leave many of their veterans to take on the Mercian special-forces.
   Another fifty Blue Wings flew to reinforce the Men behind the Trojian army.  There were three-hundred and seventy Mercians there, and every one of them was either a Blademaster, a Yellow Wing, or an elite.  
   The Blue Wings made two more trips, bringing another three hundred men to the fighting area, some of these weren’t special-forces, but all of them were veterans and strengthened the Mercian outbreak even more.
   The enlisted Trojians fought back at first, but they quickly discovered that the Mercians far surpassed them in skill, and had the momentum and confidence that they’d gained from effectively capturing the ground behind the right flank of the Trojian army.  
   Within an hour the Trojians had lost close to four thousand men, a huge chunk of their army had been brutalized by the Mercian maneuver earlier.  Word also rang through the Trojians that the Onyx Knight had been slain, giving the soldiers even less confidence than what they’d had before.
   Seemingly all at once the Trojians lost heart.  Men took to the woods and, once they realized that their allies had deserted them, the veterans on the battle lines lay down their weapons and surrendered the fight to the Mercians.
   Lief watched as the Mercian officers walked the line of the Trojian men and beheaded any man with authority.  The rest were stripped of any currency or weapons they had and sent away with their right hands cut off.  Unlike Trojia, Mercia honored the rules of war.  Most of them looked happy that they were leaving with their lives.
   He was feeling lightheaded, and he didn’t even need to look down to know that lightstuff was leaking from his hands.  He walked over to a chunk of wall that was lying on the ground and leaned against it, sighing deeply as the tension was taken off of the sore muscles in his back.  
   The walls of the Du Vron Dezdant fortress had been shattered and almost completely destroyed in some places.  Many of the houses and structures behind them had been crushed by the rubble and were uninhabitable.  This fortress didn’t offer them any protection anymore, it would take months to rebuild the wall, and then it wouldn’t offer nearly as much protection because the stone that had been used before had been drug there from the Glandraung Mountains.  It had taken years just to get the materials.
   His vision started to blur, and he slid to the ground to rest his head on his knees.  They would work out what to do, other people could worry about it.  For now he needed rest.




                                             






























Epilogue:  The Trumpets Will Sound


   Eric looked around the war room of the Du Vron Dezdant, Jared had called a number of individuals there for a special meeting.  All Luxerne had been summoned and, against their better judgment, they had agreed to attend.  He sat between Ignis and Adelia at the circular table that was in the middle of the large chamber.  Jared stood at the far end of the chamber with Hart, they were talking in low voices to each other.  
   He rubbed his eyes to try to rid them of the tiredness that was there, the five Luxerne had stayed up late the night before deciding their next course of action.  They’d decided to travel to Rema and search out the next Y’marr there, not only was it the closest and easiest to travel too, there was no conflict going on there.  It was nothing but ghost towns and rubble, so that was where they would start.
   Jared stepped forward and leaned against the table in front of him, his brown hair was still a mess from the battle and it didn’t look like he’d had a lot of sleep since then.  When he spoke though, his voice was strong and sure.  “Allies, I have gathered you here because you all played a pivotal part in our victory against the Trojians.  We have successfully defeated their Capital Army, and we did it with minimal losses.  General Gundleas, Prince Lief, and myself have done our best to maximize enemy losses and minimize our own.”
   The people sitting around the table all nodded in agreement, they were still reveling in the aftereffects of their victory.  Servants brought spiced wine and food, while Jared continued his speech. 
   “We must recognize that, while we have suffered few losses, we have lost many fine men.  And the precious walls of our fortress were destroyed in the Trojian assault.”  People began to mutter, Eric bit his lip.  He still felt slightly guilty for not being there when the Trojians had assaulted the Dezdant fortress.  They’d estimated that the three of them would’ve been back before the assault had really begun.  “Which means that we must stop our hiding, we have no more walls to hide behind and, come next spring, the Trojians will attack again with what’s left of their armies and they will decimate us.  We must unite with the Vervadian, and Frandian armies and settle this fight with Trojia once and for all.”
   Some of the people around the table shouted their affirmations, some shouted, some raised their glasses to toast.  Others, however, remained dutifully put off by the idea and shook their heads.  The supporters were more numerous than the disdainers, and many of them scowled at the more pessimistic guests.  
   Jared raised a hand to silence them.  “I have news to announce before we come to a decision.”  He motioned for Hart to come forward, the reluctant man crossed his arms and looked down at the ground.  Like a child made to stand in the front of the room.  “I will not be leading the Mercian forces in our assault, I have chosen Hart as the new General of the Du Vron Dezdant.”  Surprisingly no one objected to the move, many of them knew Hart and thought he would’ve made a better replacement for Gundleas in the first place.  If he hadn’t been in Trojia at the time of Gundleas’ death he probably would’ve gotten more votes from the council than the other man.
   Jared motioned for Hart to speak and stepped back behind him out of the way.  Hart stepped forward and began to talk in his rough voice. “The first thing we’re going to do is send three messengers to each of the remaining Dezdant fortresses, if they respond then maybe we can request a meeting in the Reman rally point.”  He bit his lip and furrowed his brow, as if he was trying to remember something.  “Luxerne, I don’t know how much you want to be involved in this campaign.  Do you plan on travelling with us to Trojia?”
   Lief stood up to answer him.  “We hold our allegiance to no man or country, and we will stand by that.  If the Trojians had declared war in accordance with the laws set down by Mercola than we would side with nobody in this fight.”  He shook his head.  “But the Trojians have upset the balance and that balance needs to be remedied.”
   Hart nodded, “Does this mean that you’ll fight with us?”
   Lief shook his head.  “We will not campaign with the Du Vron Dezdant, we have other matters that require our immediate attention, but the Mercians hold our favor.  If we are ever in a situation that requires us to choose a side we will not hesitate to fight with your cause.” 
   Hart seemed satisfied, they had a plan.  The Dezdant had hope.  One of the six fragments of the heart had been destroyed, though he knew nothing about that.  The Luxerne had lost one of their midst, but they knew that the only road left to take was forward, Brazen had showed them that.
   Like the many pieces on a chessboard, the world was maneuvering itself into place.




   In the highest room of the Tower of Transmodim, on the island that lay in the depths of the sulfur fields of Oblivion, the Beast smiled.
